# analog-guest

This is the messaging engine for Analog — a guest recognition platform for independent cafes, bakeries, and restaurants. This repo handles inbound and outbound messages between venues and their guests, plus the AI generation, classification, and routing logic. It also hosts the internal Command Center (`app/admin/*`) — the Analog-staff debugging surface served on `admin.theanalog.company`. A separate venue-operator dashboard (`analog-operator`) is planned but not yet built; bearer-token auth scaffolding for that future repo lives in `lib/auth/` (see "Auth boundary" below).

---

## Keeping this file current

This file is the source of truth for how the codebase works. When shipping a new script, migration, schema convention, file-naming convention, library pattern, or workflow rule, update this file in the same commit. If a future-you (or a future Claude Code session) would have to rediscover the convention, document it here.

---

## Product principles (do not violate)

- **Recognition, not loyalty.** Guests do not "earn" things — they get recognized. Avoid loyalty-program language ("points," "rewards," "tier," "earn") in any guest-facing output, system prompts, or operator-facing UI copy.
- **Speaker framing is per-venue.** Messages to guests can come from a few different framings depending on what the venue approves: from the venue itself (default), from a named person at the venue ("[Name] from [Venue]"), or from the owner directly when the owner approves that framing. The default is venue-level until told otherwise per venue.
- **Every venue is its own isolated block.** Own messaging number, own voice corpus, own config, own data. Cross-venue queries are Analog-internal only and never exposed to operators.
- **The product is messaging-only.** There is no guest app. Do not assume a frontend for guests. The guest's only surface is the iMessage/SMS conversation with the venue.
- **Voice is the product.** The agent's response style, register, and judgment are what we sell. Treat voice quality as a first-class concern, not a polish-later detail.

---

## Tech stack

- **Language:** TypeScript, strict mode
- **App framework:** Next.js (App Router) deployed on Vercel
- **Database:** Postgres with pgvector, plus Auth and Storage (Supabase as the current provider)
- **Messaging:** iMessage + SMS fallback (Sendblue as the current provider). One dedicated number per venue. Inbound webhook: `webhooks.theanalog.company/api/webhooks/sendblue` (configured Sendblue-side; route handler at `app/api/webhooks/sendblue/route.ts`). Future webhook integrations (Square, Stripe, Toast) inherit this subdomain convention. **Instagram DM is a second inbound channel as of TAC-445** (Meta app, Instagram Login path — `graph.instagram.com` and an Instagram User token, never `graph.facebook.com` or a Page token), handler at `app/api/webhooks/instagram/route.ts`, callback `webhooks.theanalog.company/api/webhooks/instagram`. **Since TAC-468 it saves what arrives**, into the schema TAC-467 added (migration 048): a guest message or icebreaker postback becomes an inbound row, and the venue account's echo an outbound row, all with `channel: 'instagram'` set explicitly; the guest is found or created by `(venue_id, instagram_scoped_id)`; the venue comes from `venues.instagram_account_id`, which has **no write path and is set by hand in Studio**. Until it is set, every delivery is skipped as `venue_not_found`, so the feature saves nothing. The value is the webhook's `entry.id`, which no log line carries (TAC-458): get it with the venue's Instagram User token from `GET https://graph.instagram.com/me?fields=user_id,username`, taking `user_id`, NOT `id` (which is app-scoped). Confirm the mapping by the logs: `instagram_event_skipped` stops and `instagram_event_persisted` appears. A read receipt is matched to its row and logged, never written, because no column holds read state. It has the Sendblue route's shape (save, answer 200, hand a new guest message to the agent via `waitUntil`), but **the agent does NOT run for Instagram guests** until TAC-469 builds outbound: `lib/messaging/instagram/agent-gate.ts` holds the gate. Nothing here detects STOP: the only opt-out handling in the repo is the classifier inside that agent run, so Instagram gets it when TAC-469 lifts the gate (ruled 2026-09-18). **The daily webhook-silence alarm (`app/api/cron/webhook-silence`) counts `channel = 'text'` inbound only since TAC-468**, so it watches Sendblue alone; without that filter one Instagram DM a day would hide a complete Sendblue outage. Instagram has no silence check of its own. **The `x-hub-signature-256` signature is ENFORCED as of TAC-458**: a delivery that fails it, and every delivery while `INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET` is unset or empty, gets 403 with nothing from its body logged. That is the route's only non-2xx. A verified delivery gets 200 on every path including a parse failure and an unhandled throw (so does a throw while reading the body, which logs nothing from it), where Sendblue and Square reserve 5xx so the provider retries. **Since TAC-468 that includes a failed save, which loses that event** (ruled 2026-09-18): Meta disables a subscription after repeated non-2xx, and a failure that isn't transient would keep failing until the channel switched off with no alert. Sendblue loses messages the same way in practice, because supabase-js returns a network failure as `{ error }` rather than throwing, so its route 200s on a failed insert too. The same fact cuts the other way for the 403: **if GENUINE deliveries start getting it, Meta will eventually disable the subscription and nothing here alerts** (see the env-var entry). A rotated or cleared secret is the likely cause; fix the secret or revert rather than debug in place.
- **LLM:** Anthropic API via the Vercel AI SDK abstraction layer (model-agnostic from day one)
- **Embeddings:** Voyage
- **Drive integration:** Google Drive + Sheets API for the venue onboarding artifact pipeline (06-09 files described below)
- **Analytics:** PostHog for product/event analytics, Slack for operational alerts

Treat the database, messaging, and LLM providers as swappable. Code should depend on internal interfaces (e.g., `lib/messaging/send.ts`), not on vendor names.

---

## Brand & design

The Analog visual language is documented in `docs/brand/style-guide-v01.html`. Open in a browser to view rendered. It defines:

- Color tokens (clay, paper, parchment, ink variants, stone variants) with hex values in the `:root` CSS block
- Typography stack: **Fraunces** (variable serif — display + italic-display, with brand-default `opsz/SOFT/WONK` variation settings in `--fraunces` and `--fraunces-text`) and **Inter Tight** (variable sans — body, UI, eyebrows with `letter-spacing: 0.22em`, captions). No script face — every italic-display moment resolves through Fraunces.
- Voice register guidance ("Tone continuum" section — Composed / Direct / Quiet, with surface-by-surface mapping)
- Layout primitives (eyebrows, section headers, hairline borders, tabular nums for receipt-shaped data)

The file is reference material, not code. Do not modify it. Brand updates land as a new versioned file (v02, v03, etc.) so the canonical reference for any given moment is pinned.

Token extraction into runtime code happens in `app/globals.css` under `@theme inline` (Tailwind v4 — no separate config file) — set up during THE-198 (Command Center scaffold).

---

## Folder layout

### App and library code

- `app/api/` — route handlers (webhooks, internal API for operator)
- `lib/db/` — database clients and queries (currently backed by Supabase). `lib/db/admin.ts` exports `createAdminClient` for service-role access in scripts and crons.
- `lib/messaging/` — message send + receive + webhook verification (currently backed by Sendblue). `instagram/` (TAC-445) holds the Meta half, in a provider subfolder mirroring `lib/pos/square/` and imported BY PATH, since sub-barrels are not the convention here and a barrel is what lets a future `vi.mock` hand a test a stubbed verifier it needed for real. `verify-webhook.ts` is pure (secret passed in, never read from env) so it is testable with known vectors — **note it does NOT reuse `lib/messaging/verify-webhook.ts`, which is Sendblue's and is not HMAC at all**: Sendblue echoes its secret in plaintext in an `sb-signing-secret` header, so the reusable precedent is Square's. `verifyInstagramSignature` returns ok or a rejection reason and NEVER the computed digest, and refuses an empty secret itself rather than relying on the route (see the signing-oracle gotcha). `summarize-payload.ts` is the non-PII shape summary; since TAC-458 removed the raw-body capture it and `logInstagramOutcome` (below) are the only things the route logs about a payload, so they are what hold "no guest content in logs". **TAC-468 added the handler, in three files.** `parse-events.ts` is pure: a verified delivery becomes typed events in delivery order (Meta batches `entry[] × messaging[]`), told apart by the item's own keys (`message` with or without `is_echo`, `postback`, `read`), with the guest taken from the RECIPIENT on an echo. Everything else (comments and other `changes[]` fields, `standby`, reactions, edits, handover, unsends, unsupported or empty messages, a standalone `referral` whose ref is therefore lost, and any entry key besides `id`/`time`/`messaging`/`changes`/`standby`) becomes an `unhandled` event carrying key names, never values, and is logged and acknowledged, never dropped silently. `handle-events.ts` saves them, one at a time and each in its own try/catch, following the Sendblue order (venue, guest, duplicate check on `provider_message_id` = the `mid`, insert). Only a guest's own message or postback creates a guest; an echo or a read for an unknown IGSID is skipped (ruled 2026-09-18), so staff messaging a supplier from the venue account creates no "guest". New guests are always `created_via: 'inbound_message'`: a referral ref can't tell a QR sign from a shared link. A postback is saved even without a title, because TAC-469 computes the 24-hour reply window from the newest inbound Instagram row and a postback opens it (the row is legal only through the TAC-309 NULL quirk in `messages_has_content`). A guest message with nothing to show (unsupported, or no text and no attachment URL) is logged, not saved, as approved: it creates no guest and doesn't count toward the window, which TAC-469 should revisit if that refuses replies it could send. `created_at` is our receipt time, not Meta's `timestamp`, so the window computation needs a margin. A racing guest insert re-reads the winner on 23505, and a racing message insert reads as a duplicate. Echo rows are shaped like `sendReaction`'s outbound insert (status `sent`, no `review_state`, no `generated_by`). `logInstagramOutcome` logs our row IDs, flags and names, plus a failed save's PostgREST message and code; never its `details`, which carry the failing row's values. `agent-gate.ts` holds `INSTAGRAM_AGENT_REPLIES_ENABLED = false` and `agentMessageIdFor`; the route's `waitUntil(runInboundAgent(...))` call is already wired behind it, so TAC-469 lifts the gate by flipping the constant. Its kind check is NOT part of the gate and stays: an echo, a read, or a duplicate is never handed to the agent, or the agent would answer its own replies once they echo back. **Echoes race TAC-469's own sends, and nothing orders the two.** The send path learns the `mid` only from the Send API's response, and Meta can deliver the echo first. When it does, this handler saves the echo as an outbound row with no agent metadata, and the send path's own write of that `mid` then hits 23505 on `provider_message_id`. TAC-469 must handle that case (for example, treat a 23505 on its own `mid` as "the echo landed first" and update that row with its metadata), not assume an ordering. Before lifting the gate it also inherits three things listed in `agent-gate.ts`'s header: a titleless postback reaches the agent with an empty body, an unsent message stays in history, and STOPs received while the gate was shut were never classified. `testing/db-fake.ts` is the in-memory store both test files use; like `lib/agent/testing/pending-rows-fake.ts` it answers only the query shapes the handler sends, so an update (say, on a read receipt) throws. `fixtures/` holds real Meta deliveries captured on 2026-09-17 (a guest message, an echo, a read receipt, and an icebreaker postback carrying an `ig.me` referral), with IDs, `mid`s and text replaced because the repo is public. Its README and `fixtures.test.ts` record what they show, and each point is a test: **a reply typed by hand in the Instagram app arrives as an echo** (`is_echo: true` inside `message`, sender and recipient reversed), so a handler will see venue staff's own messages. `is_echo` marks the venue's side, not staff: the agent's own API sends most likely arrive as echoes too (uncaptured; verify when the real handler lands); **a read receipt's key is `read`** (`messaging_seen` is only the subscription field name) and it names one `mid`, so read state is per message; the two guest IGSIDs seen were 16 digits where the account ID is 17, so the two kinds of ID don't share a length; a `mid` encodes the account, conversation and message IDs; **an icebreaker tap after an `ig.me` link is a `postback` carrying the referral**, with the link's `ref` unchanged in the one sample and the `mid` inside `postback` (a hand transcription put it outside; copy fixtures from the logs by script, never by hand); and **a thread the guest deleted on their side and reopened through the link got a referral but kept its thread ID** (expected: Instagram deletes a chat only for the side that deletes it, so the venue still had it), so on that path the referral, not a new thread, is what signals a returning guest (other re-entry paths weren't captured). Never commit a delivery's signature beside its body.
- `lib/ai/` — AI SDK setup, prompts, classification, generation. Contains `classify-message.ts`, `generate-message.ts`, the `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`, and the universal voice rules R1-R22. `extract-reported-order.ts` (TAC-323) is a standalone Haiku call mirroring `classify-message.ts`'s shape exactly (`generateObject`, `AIResult`, no `.min()/.max()` on the `quantity` number field per THE-157) — given an inbound body + the venue's menu item names, returns the items a guest is reporting having already ordered, or none. Deliberately carries its own `EXTRACT_REPORTED_ORDER_PROMPT_VERSION`, independent of `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`'s `PROMPT_VERSION` — it never touches the classify/generate contract. `classify-intention-prompts.ts` (TAC-324) is the same shape again for a different question: given a message the venue just SENT plus the caller's currently-open intentions as self-contained `{key, description}` pairs (never a bare key list this module would need its own description lookup for — that lookup lives once, on `IntentionDefinition` in `lib/agent/intentions/definitions.ts`), returns which of those the sent text actually raised. `raisedKeys` is constrained via a `z.enum` built from the caller's open-key list PER CALL (not a bare `z.string()`) — the same structural-constraint fix a prior code review forced onto `extract-reported-order.ts`, applied from the start here. Own `CLASSIFY_INTENTION_PROMPTS_PROMPT_VERSION`, same independence rationale. `verify-grounding.ts` (TAC-350) is the same shape once more, for the knowledge-gap fabrication problem: given the guest's inbound, the generated reply body, and the SAME `venueInfo` + `knowledgeChunks` the generator saw (rendered via the same `venueInfoToProse`/`knowledgeChunksToProse` serializers `compose-prompt.ts` uses — never a separately re-derived summary), returns whether the reply states a specific factual claim not supported by that source material. Own `VERIFY_GROUNDING_PROMPT_VERSION` (v1.4.0 as of TAC-409), same independence rationale. **TAC-409 added two bullets to the prompt's `Do not flag:` list, and the framing matters: the verifier was UNSTABLE and is now LESS so — not "too strict and now calibrated."** (1) **Abridgement.** A reply that omits detail the source contains, while contradicting nothing in it, is grounded. The bullet names its own DIRECTION against rule 3 deliberately: rule 3's three worked examples are all a reply asserting MORE than the source, and this is the reverse case. The two never conflicted logically and the model conflated them anyway, twice, three days apart, drawing a different line each time. (2) **Identity.** Who the assistant is — the name it speaks under, and that it works at this venue — is configured, not asserted. **Deliberately NOT "the role it claims": nothing configures a role.** `speakerFramingProse`'s `named_person` branch renders only "staff at the venue" and `venue_info.staff` is `z.array(z.string())` of free-form lines, so a reply claiming a specific job title, shift or responsibility is an ordinary unsupported claim and stays checked — an earlier draft of this bullet exempted it, caught in code review. Scoped to identity ONLY so it cannot be read against the assistant's-own-text rule, which must survive intact. **On whether context parity would have fixed this instead: on these drafts, no — but do not generalise that.** The name the verifier objected to was already in `venue_info.staff`, which it receives, and on one draft in the guest's own inbound, so context was not the constraint there. The persona block is a separate matter: `personaToProse` renders into the generator's SYSTEM prompt and `verifyGroundingStage` passes only the USER prompt, so `speakerName`/`speakerFraming` never reach the verifier at all. At a venue whose `speakerName` is NOT on the staff roster — a configuration the product allows — an identity flag would be a genuine context gap. The exemption is what covers that case, not TAC-422. Measured by a verifier-only replay, both arms against fixed bodies: 39 promise bodies (19 recovered + 20 fresh, reported as two populations and never combined) held at OLD and still held at NEW, zero regressions, so the rules do not widen TAC-401's prose-promise leak. Both identity drafts went 5/5 and 4/5 held to 0/5. See the gotcha on single-verdict sampling below before reading any of those numbers as deterministic. **TAC-367 added `VERIFY_GROUNDING_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (2000, was an inline 500) and `VERIFY_GROUNDING_TRUNCATED_ERROR_CODE`** — the catch block now reads `NoObjectGeneratedError.isInstance(e) && e.finishReason === 'length'` off the SDK's own error rather than inferring truncation from provider-formatted message text, and reports it as a distinct `errorCode` so the caller can fail CLOSED on that one cause. That constant is imported BY PATH in `lib/agent/stages.ts`, never via the `@/lib/ai` barrel, because `stages.test.ts` `vi.mock`s the barrel and a bare constant arriving `undefined` would make the fail-closed branch silently unreachable in every test — same reasoning as `emoji-cadence.ts`'s deliberate exclusion. Deliberately excludes voice-corpus (`ragChunks`) from what it checks against — voice is how the venue talks, not what's true about it. Consumed by `lib/agent/stages.ts`'s `verifyGroundingStage`, which only ever invokes it when `GeneratedMessageSchema.knowledgeGap` is already `false` — see "AI agent runtime contract → Approval policy gates" below for why self-report alone wasn't enough. `emoji-cadence.ts` (TAC-362) is a pure, `import type`-only module owning per-message emoji frequency — `EMOJI_PROBABILITY`, `resolveEmojiDirective(policy, rng)`, and the canonical `countEmoji`/`containsEmoji` detector shared with `scripts/onboarding/grade-voice-deterministic.ts` (which dropped its own block-range `EMOJI_RE`). Deliberately NOT exported from `lib/ai/index.ts` — `stages.test.ts` `vi.mock`s that barrel, and a pure function the tests need for real must not arrive mocked; import it by path, same as `self-talk-detector`. See "AI agent runtime contract → Per-message emoji cadence" for the measured basis. `self-talk-detector.ts` (TAC-355) is a pure, dependency-free deterministic module — no AI call, no independence-rationale prompt version — exporting `SELF_TALK_PATTERNS` + `matchSelfTalk(body)`, same shape as `lib/agent/comp-backstop.ts`'s `matchComp`. Catches the agent showing its own reasoning in a guest-facing body (a dash self-correction: "...dandelion root — actually wait, no dashes"). Wired into `generateMessage`'s existing per-attempt regen loop alongside `DASH_REGEX`, sharing its attempt budget (the two failures are correlated — the motivating incident WAS dash avoidance) and composing regen feedback when both fire on the same attempt. Patterns requiring a self-referential subject (`my`/`our`/`I'm`/`I was` + instructions/rules/programming/guidelines) deliberately exclude bare "the rules"/"instructions" so ordinary venue copy ("per house rules") can't trip it; "actually wait"/"let me rephrase" are deliberately left loose (a false positive there costs one harmless regen, not a queue) — see the module's own header for the full false-positive analysis. `verify-mechanic-offer.ts` (TAC-355) is the same `verify-grounding.ts`-shaped independent backstop again, for the mechanic-approval self-flag: given the drafted reply plus this turn's `requires_operator_approval: true` eligible mechanics (name/reward/qualification — the exact bullets `formatMechanicEligibility` renders), returns whether the reply offers one of them. `mechanicId` is a per-call `z.enum` built from the actual eligible-gated-id set (never a bare `z.string()`), same discipline as `extract-reported-order.ts`/`classify-intention-prompts.ts`. Own `VERIFY_MECHANIC_OFFER_PROMPT_VERSION`. Unlike every other backstop in this file, its caller (`verifyMechanicOfferStage` in `lib/agent/stages.ts`) treats a call failure as a **queue**, not a pass-through — see "AI agent runtime contract → Approval policy gates" for why this one fails closed while `verifyGroundingStage` fails open.
- `lib/rag/` — embedding (`ingestCorpusEntry`), retrieval, the `match_voice_corpus` RPC wrapper
- `lib/recognition/` — relationship strength scoring, state machine logic (`computeGuestState`, `computeRelationshipStrength`, `loadSignals`, `normalize-signals`), threshold evaluation. `state-bands.ts` exposes `isStateAtLeast(current, min)` for ordered comparisons across the four bands. `eligibility.ts` exposes `isRedemptionActive` + `filterEligibleMechanics` for runtime mechanic eligibility filtering, plus `MechanicRedeemedDataSchema` documenting the `engagement_events.data` shape for `event_type='mechanic_redeemed'` rows. `extract-menu-exploration.ts` exports `normalizeMenuItemName` (trim + lowercase) — shared with `lib/agent/extract-reported-order.ts` (TAC-323) so the menu-item prefilter and the self-reported-order resolver can't disagree about what "the same menu item" means.
- **`retrieveKnowledgeStage(ctx, category, query)` takes its query EXPLICITLY (TAC-367).** The third parameter is required, and there is no fallback — it replaced a derived query ending `?? \`Followup ${reason} for ${firstName}\``, which is the defect TAC-367 began from and was reachable by any caller without a guest message. Required rather than optional-with-a-default on purpose: a new caller on a path with no inbound has to DECIDE its query rather than inherit a wrong one by saying nothing. `''` is the legitimate "do not retrieve" answer and short-circuits to `[]`.
- `lib/agent/` — orchestration layer. `build-runtime-context.ts` (assembles RuntimeContext for a single agent run), `stages.ts` (classify → retrieve → generate stage functions), `handle-inbound.ts`, `handle-followup.ts`. `dispatch-arrival-capture.ts` (TAC-297) is the shared helper called from both handlers to transition a referenced commitment to `pending_ack` (imminent) or stamp `expected_arrival` for cron (scheduled) — never throws, RAGResult-shaped. **`handle-operator-decline.ts` (TAC-299)** is a third orchestrator parallel to inbound + followup, fired from `/api/operator/commitments/[id]/draft-decline` when an operator swipes left on a heads-up commitment card. Mirrors `handle-followup.ts` (synthesizes a `Classification` from a `'manual'` trigger; threads the operator-decided "we can't fulfill: {description}" instruction via `FollowupTrigger.metadata.hint` → `buildAiRuntime` → `operatorInstruction`) but with TWO structural deviations: (1) NEVER imports `scheduleAndSend` / `sendMessage` — persist-pending only, structural invariant asserted by an import-set check in `handle-operator-decline.test.ts`; (2) SKIPS `applyApprovalPolicyStage` entirely because the operator's swipe-left IS the approval. Calls `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` with `primaryTrigger='operator_decline_initiated'` (an operator-initiated `messages.review_reason` distinct from the `APPROVAL_TRIGGERS` auto-policy set — see `lib/operator/queue.ts` `ExtraReviewReason`). Returns the standard `AgentResult` discriminated union. The agent module owns the per-request lifecycle. **TAC-308 adds a fourth orchestrator plus the timer it serves.** `handle-holding-message.ts` generates and SENDS the holding message for one knowledge-gap card — the only outbound in this repo with no operator and no gate behind it, acceptable only because the message is content-free by construction (asserts nothing, commits to no time, names no fact). It runs the real gates and treats a `queue` verdict as a FAILURE, because the card already owns one of this guest's pending slots: generate, retry once, then send `FALLBACK_HOLDING_BODY`. **TAC-367: it retrieves NO knowledge corpus at all** — `ctx.knowledgeCorpus = []`, unconditionally, like `handle-operator-decline.ts`. It used to call `retrieveKnowledgeStage`, and that FIRED: this path builds context with `followupTrigger.reason='manual'` and no `currentMessage`, so `shouldRetrieveKnowledge` returns TRUE and retrieval falls through to the synthetic-query branch, embedding the literal string `Followup manual for {name}`. Measured against Le Mil's live corpus that returns a FULL slate every time (4/4 above the floor on every variant); two of the four chunks were `mechanic` entries. **On when this exposure existed — the obvious reading is wrong, and a first version of this entry got it backwards.** The highest score across EVERY measured variant is **0.4977** (`Followup manual for guest`), the rest 0.31–0.38; all are BELOW 0.5 and `filterByRelevance` is `>=`, so at the old floor this path retrieved **zero** — and `manual` has no `CATEGORY_TO_PRIMARY_TAG_PREFERENCE` entry, so no untagged-retry branch rescues them. The real history is three-step: the exposure predates TAC-350 (before it there was no relevance floor at all, only `lib/rag`'s `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` of 0.3, which every one of these clears), **TAC-350's 0.5 floor closed it by accident, and TAC-358's drop to 0.30 reopened it** — so TAC-358 is the proximate cause of the live exposure even though the underlying synthetic-query defect is older than both. `[]` not `null` is deliberate: an empty array renders TAC-242's explicit "No specific venue knowledge matched this query… do not invent specifics" framing, while `null` omits the block — for a message whose whole guarantee is that it asserts nothing, being told it has no venue knowledge beats being told nothing. It suppresses on `guests.opted_out_at` (read fresh, fails CLOSED), on `venues.hold_all_outbound` (a venue that reviews everything can't be handed an ungated send), and — TAC-307 — when `approval_policy` holds its category (`'manual'`) or everything. **That third check MUST live at the suppression point, not at the gate**, and the reason is the trap: `tryGenerateHolding` DOES call `applyApprovalPolicyStage`, but it treats a `queue` verdict as a FAILURE, and the failure ladder ends in `sendFallback`, which bypasses generation and the gate entirely. A policy hold enforced at the gate would be converted into an ungated send one line later. `knowledge-gap-timeout.ts` is the processor — third sibling of `commitments-due.ts` and `followups/engine.ts`, still concrete-not-generic — doing scan → CAS claim → send → regenerate-card. **`pending-slots.ts` (TAC-394)** owns the two-slot rule: `pendingSlotOf`, `loadPendingRowsBySlot` (the only per-guest pending-draft read), `decideSlotAction` (the one decision table shared by the gate, persist race recovery, the crash card and the decline), and the knowledge-gap card predicate, moved out of `stages.ts` (which re-exports it) because `schedule-and-send.ts` cannot import `stages.ts` without loading Voyage. `testing/pending-rows-fake.ts` is its in-memory `messages` table. `pending-question.ts` loads the outstanding question for the `## Unanswered question` prompt block and exports `loadInboundQuestion`, shared with the processor so the two can't disagree about whether a question is readable. **`extract-reported-order.ts` (TAC-323)** is a fire-and-forget side effect fired from `handle-inbound.ts` via `waitUntil` right after classification succeeds — a slow or failed call can never delay or block the reply, at the accepted cost that the extracted order is NOT available to `generateStage` (the reply can't reference it). Exports `bodyMentionsMenuItem` (pure prefilter — zero DB/LLM calls on a miss; matches on individual significant WORDS drawn from each menu name, not the whole name — see the word-level-matching gotcha below), `resolveReportedItems` (pure, maps the LLM's extracted names back onto the real venue menu via `normalizeMenuItemName` exact-match against the WHOLE name; unresolvable names are dropped, never stored as freeform), and `extractReportedOrder` (the orchestrator: gate → call `lib/ai/extract-reported-order.ts` → resolve → INSERT). Gate is three conditions, ANDed: prefilter hit, zero existing `guest_reported` transactions for the guest (once one exists, permanently disarmed for that guest — Command Center's delete action on the row is the only re-arm path), and within 7 days of `guests.created_at`. Never throws; every branch (including DB/LLM failures) returns a typed outcome and is logged, matching the `updateGuestContext` failure-handling posture already in the same file — no red alert, since this isn't part of the voice/reply contract. **TAC-325 adds ONGOING order capture alongside this, not on top of it**: enrollment's gate 2 (zero existing rows) and gate 3 (within 7 days) no longer terminate the run on a miss — either failing falls through to a second write path, `source='guest_reported_ongoing'` (migration 047), which enrollment's own index and gate never see. Ongoing capture collapses to one row per guest per venue-local calendar day: a later same-day report merges its new items into the most recent ongoing row (name-only dedupe against that row's existing `raw_data.line_items` — deliberately not "is this a re-order," so "got another cortado" reads the same as "that cortado was cold," a known simplification costing item/spend attribution, never visit count) unless the model's `continuesRecentVisit` flag says this is a separate trip. `reportTiming` widens from `'present' | 'past'` to `'present' | 'specific_past_day' | 'vague_past'` — a specific relative day ("yesterday", a named weekday) now resolves to a real `occurredOnDate` via `venueLocalInstant` (venue-local noon as a synthetic anchor, always `approximate` precision), while a genuinely vague reference ("last week") writes nothing at all rather than falsely dating itself to the message's own timestamp. `advanceLastVisit` is the one shared `last_visit_at`/`last_visit_precision` advance helper used by both paths — forward-only as before, plus a new guard: an `approximate` write never displaces a `pinned` value anchored to the SAME venue-local day. **`lib/agent/intentions/` (TAC-324)** is the first-touch-intentions mechanism: `definitions.ts` holds the closed `IntentionKey` union (`learn_first_order`, `invite_contact_save`) plus `INTENTION_DEFINITIONS` (each with a `promptLine`, a `classifierDescription` — what the post-send classifier is told this intention means, added on code review so `lib/ai` never carries its own independently-maintained copy of the same fact — an `expiresAfterMs`, an `isSatisfied` predicate, and — TAC-379 — a `satisfactionLabel`, plain-English prose stating how the intention closes. That last one is required, and required ON THE INTERFACE rather than as a lookup keyed on `IntentionKey` somewhere else, for exactly the reason `classifierDescription` is: a predicate cannot be rendered as data, so the read-only Command Center viewer needs the intent stated separately, and putting it here means a new intention fails `tsc` until someone says how it closes instead of silently reaching an admin surface that then has to invent a description for it. Display-only and structurally unable to reach the prompt — `OpenIntention` carries `key` and `promptLine` only — TAC-380 added `eligibleAt`) — the sole extension point for future intent sets, so adding one never touches `derive.ts` or `lib/ai/classify-intention-prompts.ts`. `derive.ts` is deliberately PURE (no DB access, unlike the ticket's own suggested signature) — `deriveOpenIntentions` takes already-loaded facts and applies one uniform rule (open until satisfied, prompted, or expired) with zero per-key branching, mirroring `filterEligibleMechanics`'s pure-filter-fed-by-caller-loaded-data shape; `applyCurrentTurnSuppression` drops `learn_first_order` for the current turn only when `bodyMentionsMenuItem` (imported from `extract-reported-order.ts` above, not reimplemented) hits the inbound body, so the reply answering an order question never gets rendered next to "you haven't heard what this guest ordered yet." `record.ts` is the post-send counterpart — fired from `handle-inbound.ts` via `waitUntil` after `scheduleAndSend` succeeds, using the SENT body (never a queued/dropped/refused draft, since those never reached the guest): classifies which open intentions the send raised via `lib/ai/classify-intention-prompts.ts`, then upserts one `guest_intention_prompts` row per raised key with `ignoreDuplicates: true` (independent per-`(guest_id, intention_key)` writes, unlike `claimFollowupLogRows`'s deliberately-`onConflict`-free multi-row claim, which needs atomic all-or-nothing rollback for a different reason — see that file's own header). Gated to `created_via: 'qr_scan'` guests and to inbound runs only; `build-runtime-context.ts` sets `openIntentions: []` unconditionally on the followup path, because recording never fires there and rendering the block would raise an intention with no row ever capping it. **TAC-380 supersedes the specifics above — see "Intentions, redefined (TAC-380)" under the runtime contract:** seven keys, not two; `load.ts` (new) owns the reads; `derive.ts` gains arming, re-arming, gating, the brake, `deriveIntentionState` and `renderableIntentions`; `record.ts` gains `recordIntentionEligibility` (inserts and re-arms) and retry-then-close-pessimistically; the `qr_scan`-only origin gate is gone. **TAC-385 PR 1 adds `rendered.ts`**, a NEW sibling rather than an addition to `derive.ts` / `definitions.ts` / `record.ts` — those three stay untouched, which is PR 1's structural guarantee that it changes no file deciding when an intention CLOSES. It owns both ends of the `messages.rendered_intentions` carrier: `buildRenderedIntentionsPayload` (write) and `parseRenderedIntentionsForRecording` (read, dropping a retired key or an unparseable anchor rather than throwing, because it runs inside the operator's approve tap). **`crisis-safety.ts` (TAC-348)** is a pure, dependency-light module (`CRISIS_SAFETY_REPLY_BODY`, `CRISIS_SAFETY_REVIEW_REASON`, `buildCrisisSafetyResult()`) consumed identically from two call sites — `handle-inbound.ts` (right after `classifyStage` succeeds, before any retrieval) and `scripts/onboarding/run-test-scenarios.ts` (the TAC-347 harness, same position) — so the harness grades the actual shipped mechanism rather than a full-pipeline generation the code never runs on this turn. See "AI agent runtime contract → Crisis-safety short circuit" below for the full design rationale.
- `lib/auth/` — auth helpers. `verifyAnalogAdminAccess(authUserId)` gates `/admin/*` access via cookie-session (THE-198) — admin layout (`app/admin/(authed)/layout.tsx`) is the primary caller. The bearer-token path — `verifyOperatorRequest(request)` (returns `{ operatorId, allowedVenueIds }` or throws `AuthError`), `getCurrentOperator(request)` (Response-returning wrapper), `verifyAnalogAdminRequest(request)` (bearer + `is_analog_admin = true`), and `withOperatorAuth(handler)` (HOF for `app/api/operator/*` route handlers, TAC-258) — went live with the operator API endpoints. `verifyAnalogAdminRequest` remains forward-scaffolding for the future `analog-operator` repo; the others are active. **TAC-272 split `operators.auth_user_id` into `auth_user_id_phone` and `auth_user_id_email`** so an operator can sign in via phone OTP OR email magic link and resolve to the same row — both `verifyOperatorRequest` and `verifyAnalogAdminAccess` do an OR-match across the two columns and lazy-link via `linkOperatorByAuthUser` (`lib/auth/link-operator.ts`) on the miss path. The admin magic-link callback at `app/admin/auth/callback/route.ts` eager-links right after `exchangeCodeForSession`; lazy-link still fires for any session that pre-dates that wiring. `authUserPhoneToE164` (`lib/auth/normalize-phone.ts`) bridges Supabase's `auth.users.phone` (no `+` prefix) and `operators.phone_number` (with `+`) at match time. Server-only; uses the admin DB client.
- `lib/operator/` — Operator API building blocks (TAC-258, TAC-277, TAC-297). `dispatchOperatorOutbound({messageId, operatorId, allowedVenueIds, action, editedBody?})` is the shared "send the pending draft via Sendblue and update the existing row" helper for approve + edit; optimistic UPDATE flips `review_state` pending→target, then Sendblue, then second UPDATE for `status='sent'` / `sent_at` / `provider_message_id`. Returns `originalBody` (pre-UPDATE) so the edit route can stamp `response_review.originalAiBody`. **TAC-297 extends this** with a post-dispatch read of `messages.pending_commitment` and a call to `createCommitmentFromPending` to materialize the `guest_commitments` row from the carrier; failures log + don't roll back the dispatch. `listPendingQueue(allowedVenueIds)` wraps the `list_operator_queue` RPC and normalizes the jsonb recent_context into `QueueDraft.recentContext[]` (entries typed as the canonical `ThreadMessage` from `@/lib/schemas`). **TAC-364 added four Contract-locked fields to `QueueDraft`, all ALWAYS PRESENT (empty array / empty string, never undefined, so the client never branches on presence): `reviewReasonCode` (the RAW primary trigger code — colour keys on this, never on the prose label, because shipping only the label is what left analog-operator's `queue-tone.ts` joining on three English strings this server has never emitted, so every production card fell to its default and two of its three colours were dead), `reviewTriggers` (the full set as RAW CODES, **primary included and never deduped server-side** — the field means "everything that fired", and the client renders secondaries as `reviewTriggers` minus `reviewReasonCode`, a subtraction that is only well defined because both sides are codes), `reviewTriggerLabels` (display text, PARALLEL to `reviewTriggers`: same length, same order, index-aligned, with the same `'Needs review'` fallback `reviewReason` uses) and `ungroundedClaims`. Note the wire DELIBERATELY collapses `ungrounded_claims`' three-state column to `[]` — see migration 039: the never-ran-vs-found-nothing distinction is a property of the row for SQL to ask, not a UI state, and neither value renders anything on a card.** **TAC-394 added a fifth, `otherPendingDraftsForGuest`** (always present, 0 when none): how many other pending cards the same guest holds, 0 or 1 under migration 041, from migration 042's `other_pending_for_guest` and normalized to 0 unless it is a positive finite number. Client half: TAC-402. **No card-facing string contains an em dash (ruled 2026-09-14).** `REVIEW_REASON_LABELS` values, and anything else that reaches the operator card, are read fast on a phone mid-shift, where an em dash is a pause the reader has to parse and a full stop or comma is not. Enforced by a test in `queue.test.ts` that runs EVERY key of the label map (plus the `'Needs review'` fallback) through `listPendingQueue` and checks both `reviewReason` and `reviewTriggerLabels`, so a new trigger's copy cannot reintroduce one; a review-only rule would not have survived the next trigger added. Comments and ticket prose may keep em dashes; only strings that reach the card may not. `listHeadsUpQueue(allowedVenueIds)` (TAC-297) is the sibling for the new `commitments` field on the queue response — Contract-locked payload `{ id, venueId, guestId, type, guest: {name}, description, code, expected_arrival, created_at, recognitionState, sourceMessageId }` (TAC-299 added the last two, TAC-364 `venueId`/`guestId`), FIFO via `ORDER BY created_at ASC`. `loadGuestThread({messageId, allowedVenueIds})` (TAC-277; since TAC-395 it returns only messages that reached the guest) is the full-thread-fetch helper behind the operator-app edit screen: two-step lookup (resolve `(venue_id, guest_id)` from `messageId`, then membership check + most-recent-200 select with `ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 200` reversed in JS to oldest→newest per the Contract — CC's `loadConversationData` used ASC LIMIT 200 until TAC-316, which kept the OLDEST 200 rows and went live as a truncation bug the night Mock Sextant crossed the cap; both now use the DESC-window pattern). Failure modes distinguished as `message_not_found | out_of_allowlist | db_error` so future logging/alerting can tell them apart even though the route flattens the first two to a uniform 404. All four helpers are RAGResult-shaped (`{ok: true, ...} | {ok: false, error}`) and consumed by the routes under `app/api/operator/*`.
- `lib/ui/` — Brand primitives consumed by `app/admin/*`: `<Eyebrow>`, `<SectionHeader>`, `<HairlineRow>`, `<StatusDot>` (plus `<StatePill>`, `<SignalBar>`). RSC-compatible. Sourced from the brand language at `docs/brand/style-guide-v01.html` — do not introduce decorative variants without updating the style guide first. **`<Card>` was removed here in TAC-306** — the single Card now lives at `@/components/ui/card` (shadcn). The remaining primitives stay because shadcn has no equivalent.
- `components/ui/` — **vendored shadcn/ui source** (TAC-306), added via `npx shadcn add <name>` against `components.json` (new-york style, CSS-vars mode, `iconLibrary: lucide`). Regenerable, not hand-authored — treat as third-party: exempt from the no-`any` / errors-as-values conventions, and prefer re-running the CLI over hand-edits (the two `eslint-disable` lines in `sidebar.tsx` / `hooks/use-mobile.ts` annotate upstream patterns the repo's react-hooks rules flag). Consumed only under `app/admin/*` (CC) today; the operator app is a separate repo. `lib/utils.ts` exports `cn()` (clsx + tailwind-merge) — the canonical class-merge helper every shadcn component and call-site override uses. `hooks/use-mobile.ts` is the shadcn sidebar's viewport hook. See "Admin scaffold → shadcn/ui component layer" for the token bridge.
- `app/admin/` — Command Center routes. Auth-gated by `verifyAnalogAdminAccess` in `app/admin/(authed)/layout.tsx`. Served on `admin.theanalog.company` in production via root `middleware.ts` host gating. **Direct** register per the style guide tone continuum. `app/admin/_components/` holds chrome shared across every admin surface — the shell, sidebar, ⌘K palette, `ContentFrame`, and (TAC-381) **`SectionShell` / `EmptySectionNote`**, the card chrome every Command Center section renders inside. That last one lived at `venues/[slug]/_components/section-shell.tsx` until the intentions page (TAC-379) grew an identical local `Section` and TAC-381's venue sections would have made a third copy; it is now imported as `@/app/admin/_components/section-shell` from 17 call sites. **Put a new section's chrome here, never a local re-declaration** — the promotion reconciled one divergence already (the intentions copy used `CardContent py-5` where the other 14 used `py-4`; unified on `py-4`), and a fourth copy would reintroduce exactly that drift.
- `lib/schemas/` — Zod schemas for venue-shaped JSONB fields. `BrandPersonaSchema` (handles dual-shape `voiceAntiPatterns`, see gotchas), `MessageReviewSchema` (the `response_review` shape from migration 014), `VenueInfoSchema` (with `filterActiveContext` per THE-150, and `classifyContextEntry` — extracted from it in TAC-343 so the admin venue page's expiry queue and the agent's own active-context filter share one per-entry active/expired/malformed classification instead of two), `GuestContextSchema` + `GuestContextPatchSchema` (the persisted shape and the agent-emitted patch shape for `guests.context` per TAC-296, plus `filterActiveLifeContext` mirroring the venue-info expires_at filter, and `toParsedGuestContext` for the runtime-ready projection with expired entries dropped + observations truncated to `OBSERVATION_RENDER_LIMIT`=10). `approval-policy.ts` (v1.24.0) owns `venue_configs.approval_policy` — `parseApprovalPolicy` (fail-OPEN to defaults) + `resolveCategoryPolicy` (stored `perCategory` merged ON TOP of the code default, so an empty stored object can't erase the `comp_complaint` route). **TAC-307 adds three things**: `POLICY_EXEMPT_CATEGORIES` / `isPolicyExemptCategory` (categories that can never be held — `opt_out`, for TCPA; the one named place, consumed by the resolver, the admin UI, and the write route alike), and `resolvePolicyDecision`, which returns `{disposition, source}` where source is `'exempt' | 'stored' | 'code_default' | 'policy_default'`. `resolveCategoryPolicy` is now a thin wrapper over it. **The source is what makes a hold absolute or not** — see trigger 8 under *Approval policy gates*. Note the resolver reads the stored entry off the CALLER'S policy, never off the `?? APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT` fallback: indexing the fallback would report the fleet-wide default as source `'stored'` and silently make every degraded context absolute (caught in TAC-307's own tests; there's a named regression test for it). `guest-commitment.ts` (TAC-297) covers `CommitmentEmissionSchema` + `ArrivalCaptureEmissionSchema` (what the LLM emits — all inner optional, no-op shape `{}`), `PendingCommitmentSchema` (the stricter jsonb carrier shape on `messages.pending_commitment`), `GuestCommitmentRowSchema` (full DB row), helpers `isEmptyCommitmentEmission` / `isEmptyArrivalCapture` / `pendingFromEmission` (generates a 4-char verification code for comp/hold/discount when the agent didn't emit one), and `toActiveCommitment` (runtime projection used by the `## Active commitments` prompt block). `VenueServicesSchema` (TAC-301 part 2) is the structured statement of what a venue does and doesn't do for a guest — `aheadOrdering` / `holds` / `reservations` / `delivery` / `catering` as **optional booleans**, plus free-form `alsoOffers` / `alsoDoesNotOffer`. **Three-state, and the distinction is the design: `true` renders a positive, `false` renders an explicit negative, ABSENT renders nothing.** Absent must never read as a denial — the block's closing line says unlisted services "have not been stated either way" precisely because an earlier wording ("unknown, not available") re-read every unstated service as unavailable the moment a venue filled in ONE field, which is the same failure narrowed to partially-configured venues. Named fields rather than `Record<string, boolean>` so `services.holds === false` is compile-checked the day commitment vocabulary is conditioned on capability; `SERVICE_LABELS` in the serializer is a total map, so adding a service fails `tsc` until it gets a label. Parsed `.optional().catch(undefined)` — permissive at the LIVE boundary because the field is Studio-only today and `buildRuntimeContext` throws on a venue_info parse failure, so a typo would otherwise take down every agent run for that venue. `rendered-intentions.ts` (TAC-385 PR 1) owns the shape of `messages.rendered_intentions` — `RenderedIntentionsSchema` plus `parseRenderedIntentions`, permissive at the live boundary (a malformed entry is dropped on its own, keeping its siblings). Deliberately DOMAIN-FREE: resolving a key to a live definition needs `lib/agent/intentions/definitions.ts`, and `lib/schemas` must not depend on `lib/agent`, so that half lives in `lib/agent/intentions/rendered.ts`. `visit-precision.ts` (TAC-377) owns `VisitTimePrecision` (`pinned` | `approximate`) plus `parseVisitPrecision` — the shape behind `transactions.occurred_at_precision` and `guests.last_visit_precision`; permissive at the live boundary (an unrecognized value degrades to null, the "nobody recorded this" state) with migration 036's CHECK as the strict half. `venue-hours.ts` (TAC-301) is the pure interpreter of `venue_info.hours` — `parseDayRange` (one day's free-text range to minutes, tolerating the canonical en dash the venue-spec parser writes plus the hyphen/em-dash/`7am-3pm`/24h variants the admin page permits) and `resolveOpenState(hours, timezone, now)` returning `open | closed | unknown`. **Its governing rule: never claim OPEN or CLOSED on input it did not positively understand.** Every ambiguous branch resolves to `unknown`, which renders no line at all — a wrong verdict fires on every turn at that venue, where the bug it fixes needs a specific arrival phrasing to surface. Three consequences, each caught in code review after a first version got them wrong: (1) **a DESCENDING pair needs explicit half-of-day evidence** (a meridiem on both sides, or unambiguous 24-hour values) before it's read as crossing midnight — `"5:00 PM – 2:00 AM"` is a bar, but `"7-3"` is a cafe writing 7am-3pm in shorthand, and reading the second as overnight reports OPEN at 7:57pm, the exact minute of the incident, now asserted confidently rather than merely unstated. (2) **Absence is never a closure.** `parse-venue-spec.ts` silently drops table rows whose label isn't in `DAY_KEY_MAP` (`Sat & Sun`, `Weekends`) and the admin venue page writes `undefined` for a cleared field, so a missing key means "nobody said" at least as often as "shut" — an earlier version read it as a closure and would have emitted CLOSED all day at an open venue. A real closure arrives through `classifyDay`'s word-like `CLOSED_MARKER` instead, which matches the pipeline's own `"Closed – Closed"` shape, not just a hand-typed bare `"Closed"`. A bare dash is a placeholder, not a closure, and stays `unknown`. (3) **A substituted timezone suppresses the verdict** — `buildAiRuntime` tracks whether `isValidTimezone` fell back to `FALLBACK_TIMEZONE` and passes `{state:'unknown'}` when it did, since a venue resolved against the wrong zone is input nobody positively understood either. Watch the related hazard the code can't see: `parse-venue-spec.ts` and migration 001 BOTH default `timezone` to `America/Los_Angeles`, which `isValidTimezone` passes silently — a venue whose spec omitted the key is confidently Pacific, and that default is now guest-facing rather than cosmetic. Read JSONB fields through these — never directly via SQL paths.
- `lib/guests/` — Per-guest helpers consumed by the agent runtime (TAC-296, TAC-297). `context.ts` exposes `getGuestContext(guestId)` (parsed JSONB read, fail-OPEN on malformed payload → empty context), `updateGuestContext({guestId, update, now})` (deep-merge patch + observation append + identity-column sync in a single multi-SET UPDATE), `deepMergeContext(existing, patch, now)` (pure), and `isEmptyContextUpdate(update)` (short-circuit predicate for the no-op emission shape). `commitments.ts` (TAC-297) exposes `createCommitmentFromPending` (INSERT from the jsonb carrier), `transitionToPendingAck` / `scheduleArrival` / `markAcknowledged` / **`markCancelled` (TAC-299)** (all CAS-gated on prior status — the rowcount=1 result anchors push idempotency per TAC-297 design call #4; `markCancelled` is CAS-gated on `status='pending_ack'` and shaped like `markAcknowledged` for caller parity, but writes NO `cancelled_at` / `cancelled_by` columns because TAC-299 was scoped to no-migration — operator audit lives on the `operator_draft_decline_initiated` PostHog event), `findActiveCommitmentsForGuest` (loaded by `build-runtime-context.ts` for the `## Active commitments` prompt block), **`findEarliestAcknowledgedArrival` (TAC-436)** returns the earliest `acknowledged_at` for a guest, or null — the arrival half of "a visit is confirmed", and the ONE arrival signal that creates no transaction row, which is what makes it usable for arming an intention any transaction closes. Earliest, not latest, because `understand_order` never re-arms and its window runs from its anchor. Reads the column directly rather than through `GuestCommitmentRowSchema`, so an unrecognized `type` on some other row can't drop a genuine arrival. `findScheduledOpenCommitments()` (no time arg — returns ALL `status='open' AND arrival_signal='scheduled'` rows; the per-venue morning-hour + date filter lives in the processor, not the query). **`commitment-expiry.ts` (TAC-341)** is the pure, single derivation site for every commitment horizon — `OBLIGATION_TYPES` (comp/hold/discount, an ALLOWLIST so a fifth type defaults to being left alone rather than inheriting a negation), `deriveExpiresAt`, `escalationDueAt`, and `venueLocalInstant`. That last one is genuinely new logic: venue-local wall clock → UTC, DST-aware, **two-pass** because the offset has to be sampled at an instant that is itself what you are solving for — a single pass is silently an hour wrong for any local time on the far side of a DST transition from the naive guess (mutation-verified against a Pacific/Auckland case; `venueLocalNow` in `venue-hours.ts` goes the OTHER direction and is private, so nothing existing covered this). `commitment-lifecycle-due.ts` (TAC-341) is the processor — **fourth** concrete sibling to `commitments-due.ts` / `followups/engine.ts` / `knowledge-gap-timeout.ts`, still concrete-not-generic. `commitments-due.ts` (TAC-297) is the cron processor — `processDueCommitments(now)` is the single named function for the commitments consumer; **the TAC-123 follow-up engine landed its sibling `processDueFollowups(now)` in `lib/followups/engine.ts` — the two are deliberately concrete-not-generic per design call #3; the shared "find-eligible → side-effect" seam stays unextracted until both processors' shapes have stabilized enough for the seam to fall out cleanly.** All `RAGResult`-shaped — never throws into the agent loop. Last-write-wins concurrency for `guests.context` v1, see "Common gotchas." The orchestrator dispatches `updateGuestContext` from `handle-inbound.ts` + `handle-followup.ts` **between `generateStage` success and `applyApprovalPolicyStage`** so context capture reflects what the agent UNDERSTOOD from the inbound regardless of whether the draft ships, queues, or refuses. The TAC-297 arrival-capture dispatch (`dispatchArrivalCapture` in `lib/agent/`) sits in the same window for the same reason.
- `lib/followups/` — TAC-123 follow-up engine (concrete sibling to `lib/guests/commitments-due.ts`). `engine.ts` exposes `processDueFollowups(now): Promise<ProcessDueFollowupsResult>` — top-level processor called from `/api/cron/followups-due`. Per-venue → per-enrolled-guest scan that runs detectors → Gate 1 (`canSendFollowup`) → claim-before-dispatch → `handleFollowup` → finalize/release. Per-venue local-hour filter against `followup_rules.cron_hour_local` lives in the processor; the cron itself fires hourly UTC (Vercel Hobby caps at daily; sub-daily lives on GH Actions, mirroring `commitments-due-cron.yml`). **TAC-367: `handle-followup.ts` retrieves knowledge ONLY against the operator's own note.** The original defect was never "followups retrieve" — it was retrieving against `Followup {reason} for {name}`, a template with no referent in any corpus that still returned a full 4/4 slate on every measured variant at Le Mil's, because cosine always ranks something highest. Every chunk it produced was coincidence. An operator note ("tell her about the new Panama lot") is real content about a real topic, so `operatorInstructionQuery(trigger)` — **shared with `buildAiRuntime`, never reimplemented, so the text the model is instructed with and the text it is grounded against cannot diverge** — is the query. No note, or any engine reason (`day_*`, `cold_lapsed`, `perk_unlock`), means no query and no retrieval: those runs have no free text at all, and their content is structured (visit history, guest context, the perk's own `reward_description`) and already in the prompt. **Keeping `[]` for a hinted note was considered and rejected**: a note asking the model to be specific, with nothing behind it, removes the grounding while leaving the pressure — on a path that, at the time, had no grounding backstop. `verifyGroundingStage` used to return early when `currentMessage === null`, so no followup had one; the approval gate ran but no trigger keyed on fabrication for an outbound, so at a venue with no hold configured a fabricated fact auto-sent. **Closed by TAC-376**: per the 2026-09-17 ruling, `verifyGroundingStage` now runs on every followup (engine and manual) and the knowledge-gap holding message too — same verifier, same `knowledge_gap_backstop`/`grounding_check_failed` triggers, same fail-open-on-transient/fail-closed-on-truncation posture as inbound, no new trigger or parallel check. The one deliberate divergence from inbound: a claim about the GUEST'S OWN actions ("brought a friend in") is in the verifier's remit on a followup — on inbound the guest made the claim, on a followup the venue does, and a followup must never assert something the guest did that isn't recorded (a fact stated in the operator's own note still counts as supported). `detectors.ts` is pure (`detectPostVisitReason`, `detectColdLapsedReason`, `detectPerkUnlockReason`, `dedupKeyForReason`) — detectors answer "did the calendar/state condition trip?" and never consult the DB; intent-bound dedup lives at the claim layer via the dedup_key UNIQUE constraint. `log.ts` is the `followup_log` interface: `claimFollowupLogRows` (atomic INSERT — 23505 conflict means another run is handling at least one reason, engine skips), `finalizeFollowupLogClaim` (UPDATE message_id post-dispatch), `releaseFollowupLogClaim` (DELETE on refusal/failure so dedup isn't burned), `loadFollowupSnapshotsForVenue` (batched per-venue read of the rolling-7d count + per-reason history + announced-mechanic set). The companion Gate 1 implementation lives at `lib/agent/followup-rules.ts` (`canSendFollowup` pure function — opt-out / quiet hours / recent conversation / weekly cap / time-bound cold dedup; per-reason intent-bound dedup is NOT here, that's the claim layer). Manual followups (TAC-249 Command Center button) bypass this engine entirely — they call `handleFollowup` with `trigger.reason='manual'`, don't write `followup_log` rows, and don't count toward weekly_cap. They do NOT bypass the approval gate (TAC-307). The render-side multi-reason intake landed in the same ticket at `deriveFollowupContext` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) which now consumes `[primary, ...trigger.additionalReasons]` and `buildAiRuntime` now populates `perkBeingUnlocked` from `FollowupTrigger.perkMechanic` (first production wiring of that AI-runtime field).
  **TAC-313 adds two pure, dependency-light modules here.** `split-message.ts` is the surviving DEFENSIVE half of message splitting (`BUBBLE_DELIMITER` = `[[BREAK]]`, `MAX_BUBBLES_PER_RESPONSE` = 3, `INTER_BUBBLE_GAP_MS` = 1500, `collapseToSingleMessage`; `splitIntoBubbles` was retired by TAC-319 when the model's delimiter role ended) — deliberately import-free so it loads in vitest with no SDK init. Its sibling `sentence-split.ts` (TAC-319) is the DECIDING half: `splitIntoSentences`, `stripTerminalPeriod`, `resolveDispatchBubbles`, `SPLIT_PROBABILITY` — pure, imports only `split-message.ts`. `group-responses.ts` is the READ-side inverse (`groupIntoResponses`), folding a response's bubble rows back into one `RecentMessage` so `build-runtime-context` caps on responses and BOTH prompt serializers are fixed at a single site — `formatRecentConversation` and `formatClassifierRecentConversation` are independent one-line-per-element renderers over the same `ctx.recentMessages` array, so fixing either alone would leave the classifier reading rows.
- `lib/analytics/` — PostHog event emission helpers (events fire and forget; failures must not crash the agent path)
- `lib/observability/` — Langfuse SDK wrapper (THE-200). `startAgentTrace(opts)` returns an `AgentTrace` with `span() / update() / flushAsync()`; spans expose `span()`, `generation()`, `update()`, `end()`. No-op when env vars missing or `NODE_ENV=test`. Wrapper never throws — SDK errors are swallowed with `console.warn`. Use this from `lib/agent/` only; never import `langfuse` directly from app code.
- `lib/voice-training/` — Shared voice-data write helpers used by the 08-flow onboarding script (`scripts/ingest-response-review.ts`), the cc-review live-edit API route, and the Voices command-center rail (THE-237). Channels: `upsertCorpusEdit({...}, 'skip-existing' \| 'replace')` for source-ref-keyed cc-review/08-review/voices-commit writes (anti-corpus-poisoning rule applies — only the operator-edited message text is embedded). `addCorpusEntry({venueId, content, sourceType, tags, addedByOperatorId?})` for ad-hoc rail additions (`source_type` ∈ `manual_entry` \| `sample_text` \| `past_message`). `editCorpusEntry({corpusId, content?, tags?})` re-embeds only on content change. `removeCorpusEntry(corpusId)` straight delete + FK cascade on voice_embeddings. `dedupeAndAppendAntiPatterns(venueId, rules, { source, authorOperatorId? })` does read-modify-write on `venue_configs.brand_persona.voiceAntiPatterns` with case + whitespace normalization. `removeAntiPattern(venueId, ruleText)` removes by exact text match (operators delete what they see). THE-236 reshaped the persisted anti-pattern entries from `string[]` to `Array<{ text, source: 'auto' \| 'manual', authorOperatorId?, addedAt? }>`; `BrandPersonaSchema` accepts both legacy strings and the struct shape and normalizes string entries to `{text, source: 'manual'}` on parse. **Asymmetry to know about (TODO):** the cc-review path doesn't currently populate `voice_corpus.added_by_operator_id`; only `addCorpusEntry` does. Track for backfill in a future ticket.
- `lib/voices/` — Critique → regen → commit loop helpers that drive the live half of the Voices command-center playground (THE-238). Distinct from `lib/voice-training/` which owns CRUD: this folder owns inference + persistence around critiques. `regenerateWithCritique({venueId, originalMessageId, critique})` rebuilds runtime context with history pinned to the moment of the original outbound's triggering inbound, classifies + retrieves + generates a fresh response with the operator's critique injected, and returns a single attempt (multi-attempt state lives client-side). **Coupling: this helper deliberately mirrors the wiring in `lib/agent/stages.ts` — if retrieval thresholds, knowledge gating, or post-generation logic change there, mirror them here.** `classifyCritique({critique, badResponse, goodResponse})` is the commit-modal Sonnet call deciding edit_only vs edit_and_rule + synthesizing a candidate ruleText (operator overrides both before commit fires). `persistCritique({...})` embeds + inserts a `voice_critiques` row. `findPatternClusterForCritique({...})` and `findActiveClusters(venueId)` run cosine-search → verification per the cluster-detection pipeline. Pure helpers (`-pure.ts`) split the prompt builders + threshold logic out so module-load SDK init doesn't trip vitest.
- `lib/tunables/` — Hand-maintained manifest of operational tunables (TAC-183). `manifest.ts` re-exports agent / recognition / retrieval / timing constants from their source files with display metadata, consumed by the read-only Command Center viewer at `/admin/tunables`. No registration primitive, no module-load side effects — purely a data file. Read-only by design; editable overrides are Phase 2. Vitest module-load: the manifest test mocks `voyageai` (the only SDK module that trips vitest's ESM directory-import resolver) — adding a tunable that pulls in another heavy SDK at module load may require extending that mock.
- `lib/notifications/` — APNs push pipeline for the operator approval queue (TAC-207) and the commitment-arrival heads-up surface (TAC-297). `apns/jwt.ts` signs the ES256 bearer token using `jose` (5 env vars: `APNS_AUTH_KEY` PEM, `APNS_KEY_ID`, `APNS_TEAM_ID`, `APNS_BUNDLE_ID`, `APNS_ENV`); cached at module scope with a 50-minute refresh window and an explicit invalidation on key-id / team-id rotation. `apns/client.ts` wraps `node:http2` (new pattern — no prior http2 use in the repo) with one-shot `sendApnsRequest(deviceToken, payload)` returning a `RAGResult`-shaped result; one session per request, no pooling at pilot scale. `env.ts` is the pure shape validator for the five `APNS_*` vars (`checkApnsEnv`, layer-scoped via `JWT_APNS_VARS` / `TRANSPORT_APNS_VARS`; never returns key material) — consumed first-call by `client.ts` + `jwt.ts` and ambiently by `/admin/health`. `push-policy.ts` owns the fire/skip decision as a TOTAL `satisfies Record<ApprovalTrigger, PushDecision>` map (deny-list: everything pushes except `previous_pending_held`) — deliberately separate from the context-label map so the two can't drift into one constant again. `send.ts` is the draft-flagged orchestrator: `shouldSendDraftFlaggedPush(primaryTrigger)` (re-exported from `push-policy.ts`) skips only `previous_pending_held` and fails OPEN on unknown triggers; `sendDraftFlaggedPush({agentRunId, venueId, guestId, guestFirstName, draftId, primaryTrigger})` fans out to every operator whose allowlist covers the venue, computes the badge using the same predicate as `list_operator_queue`, POSTs to APNs, handles 410 Gone / 400 BadDeviceToken by nulling the token columns. **`send-commitment-push.ts` (TAC-297)** is the sibling for the commitment-arrival surface — `sendCommitmentArrivalPush({commitmentId, venueId, guestId, guestFirstName, type, code, expectedArrival, arrivalSignal, venueTimezone, agentRunId?})` fired from `handle-inbound.ts` (imminent CAS win) and the `/api/cron/commitments-due` processor (scheduled fires). Same `loadRecipients` + token-invalid handling, but the badge predicate combines pending drafts + pending_ack commitments across the operator's allowed venues — single source of truth across both push surfaces. Payload `{ aps: {alert: {title, body}, badge, sound: "default"}, commitmentId, guestId, operatorId }` — content-free (no commitment description, no inbound text). Body `"{firstName} arriving {context} — {typeLabel}{ code}"`. PostHog discriminator: `surface: 'commitment_arrival'`. Both surfaces never throw. **Helper duplication caveat:** `loadRecipients` + `nullOperatorToken` + the badge count are duplicated between `send.ts` and `send-commitment-push.ts` — extraction to a shared `lib/notifications/recipients.ts` is a deliberate follow-up.

### Scripts

- `scripts/` — top-level CLI entry points. Each is a thin orchestrator that reads args, sets up clients, calls helpers, logs progress.
- `scripts/onboarding/` — helpers used by the onboarding pipeline scripts. One helper module per pipeline step. Helpers may be split into `<name>-pure.ts` (no `@/*` imports, vitest-loadable) and `<name>.ts` (DB-touching wrapper) when tests need to load the pure logic — see "Module split for testability" below. Modules: `airtable.ts` (transcript fetch), `menu-csv.ts` (CSV parser), `parse-venue-spec.ts` (Zod-strict venue-spec parser; the offline boundary referenced in the permissive-schema gotcha; **TAC-343 Phase 0** stops silently dropping `staff[].notes` on the way to the flattened `venue_info.staff` roster string — any non-empty `notes` is routed into a synthesized `staff_<slug(name)>` `knowledge_corpus` entry, appended alongside whatever the extraction itself wrote, so the content survives whether or not extraction independently covered it), `seed-supabase.ts` (helper imported by `scripts/seed-venue.ts`; **TAC-343 Phase 0b** adds a `force?: boolean` option to `seedVenue()` — the already-exists guard still hard-refuses by default; `force: true` deletes and rewrites CONFIG STORES ONLY (`venue_configs`, `mechanics`, `voice_corpus`+embeddings, `knowledge_corpus`+embeddings), never the `venues` row itself and never any guest-relationship table, and refuses outright — even with the flag — if the venue has any `guests` or `messages` rows, so it can never erase real guest history; nothing in this repo passes it yet), `extract.ts` (LLM-extraction orchestrator; exports `buildExtractionSystemPrompt` (TAC-331) as a same-file pure split — no `@/*` imports to strip here, just a network call to keep out of the unit-testable prompt-content path — so the extraction routing/mood rules are covered by `extract.test.ts` without hitting Anthropic; **TAC-343 Phase 0** adds a knowledge_corpus GRANULARITY rule — one entry per self-contained claim, never several combined, each entry must name its own subject since `chunkText()`'s character-count splitting downstream has no idea where one item ends and the next begins; **TAC-346** tightens the voice/knowledge/persona/mechanics/staff placement rules described under "Extraction hardening" below and imports `MECHANIC_TRIGGER_TYPES` from `lib/schemas/mechanic.ts` rather than hand-copying it), `verify.ts` (TAC-346, NEW — the post-extraction verification pass; `runVerification` via `generateObject`, `VerifyResultSchema`, `countVoiceCorpusEntries` (pure, mechanical — never delegated to the model), `formatNeedsConfirmationSection` (pure renderer for the `## Needs confirmation` markdown); own `VERIFY_PROMPT_VERSION`, independent of `extract.ts`'s contract, same independence rationale as `EXTRACT_REPORTED_ORDER_PROMPT_VERSION`), `extract-venue-spec-args.ts` (TAC-346, NEW — `parseArgs` + the pure `shouldRefuseOverwrite` guard decision for `scripts/extract-venue-spec.ts`, split out specifically so they're importable from a test without importing the entry script itself: the entry script's unconditional top-level `main()` call means importing it directly would trigger a real run, and a `import.meta.url === file://${process.argv[1]}` main-module guard is NOT a safe fix — that comparison silently never matches on a path containing characters `import.meta.url` percent-encodes, such as spaces or a curly apostrophe, which this repo's own path has), plus `extract-test-scenarios.ts`, `run-test-scenarios.ts`, and `ingest-response-review-pure.ts`. `run-sheet.ts` (NEW, post-TAC-347) is the pure serializer for the 07-sheet's **`Run` tab** — one row per scenario (`sample_id`, `topic`, `category`, `scenario_source`, `mode`, `guest_state`, `inbound_message`, `outcome`, `route`, `primary_trigger`, `all_triggers`, `voice_fidelity`, `reply`, and all three grades — knowledge/voice/routing, each with verdict + reason — plus `expected_behavior_verdict`/`expected_behavior_reason`, and — TAC-358 — the trailing `retrieved_chunk_ids`/`retrieved_chunk_scores` pair). **Those last two are deliberately two columns rather than one `id@score` column**: ids are stable across runs and are what a run-to-run diff by `sample_id` compares, while scores move on every embed and would make that diff noisy; they stay index-aligned, the nth id being the nth score. `retrieved_chunk_ids` carries the `knowledge_corpus` row id, NOT the `knowledge_embeddings` chunk id — 1:1 on today's data, but the corpus id is the one a human can act on (it's what the admin venues page shows and what a retag targets), and a corpus row chunked into several embeddings correctly repeats its id. **Two limits on how far these columns can be read, both load-bearing for anyone recalibrating retrieval.** (1) They are **post-floor SURVIVORS ONLY** — `retrieveKnowledgeStage` returns `filterByRelevance(...)`, so the smallest score that can ever appear is `KNOWLEDGE_RELEVANCE_FLOOR` itself and a near miss (0.4971 against a 0.5 floor) is invisible here. That is precisely the number a floor recalibration needs, so calibration evidence has to come from a direct probe against the corpus, not from this tab; capturing pre-filter scores would mean changing agent-runtime code. (2) An empty pair has SEVERAL causes and they are not equally diagnosable. The crisis-safety short circuit and a throw predating retrieval are identifiable from `outcome`/`primary_trigger`; but every-chunk-below-the-relevance-floor, zero-rows-clearing-the-confidence-floor, and a knowledge-retrieval **degrade** (an RPC or query-embed failure, which only `console.warn`s and returns `[]`) all render identically on an otherwise-normal `sent` row. **Do not read an empty pair as proof the floor is too high.** What the columns do buy, and what was un-observable before they existed, is the distinction between a reply grounded in retrieved `knowledge_corpus` chunks and one grounded only in `venue_info` — which reads identically in the generated text, and is why the TAC-358 floor miscalibration went unnoticed. Sibling to `report-sheet.ts`'s `Report` tab, different shape: `Report` is aggregates/samples/a capped review list, `Run` is the full uncapped per-scenario detail a run-to-run diff by `sample_id` needs. `scripts/run-test-scenarios.ts` (the CLI) writes it automatically on every run via `ensureTabExists` + `writeTabValues`, same overwrite-wholesale pattern as `Report` — it's a snapshot of the latest run, not an accumulating history across runs.
- `scripts/onboarding/fixtures/` — repo-resident input fixtures consumed by extraction scripts (e.g., `venue-spec-example.md`, `test-scenarios-example.md`)
- `scripts/onboarding/drive.ts` — shared Drive integration. Exports `getDrive`, `findVenueFolder`, `listVenueFiles`, `findByPrefix`, `readDriveFileAsText`, `writeMarkdownFile`, `writeJsonFile`, `writeSheetFile`. Use these instead of inlining Drive calls.
- `scripts/lib/` — shared, pure, testable helpers consumed by top-level scripts that aren't part of the onboarding pipeline (`scripts/onboarding/` owns that one). Plain `.mjs` (no `@/*` imports, no SDK init at module load — these run inside GitHub Actions workflow steps, not this app's Next.js/vitest process, so they colocate a `module.test.ts` the same way `scripts/onboarding/*-pure.ts` modules do, importing the `.mjs` directly rather than needing a `-pure` split). `comment-provenance.mjs` (TAC-396) is the first: `unescapeBrackets`, `isBotComment`, `commentMarker`, `isRulingComment`, `isContextChatComment` — the shared, tested definition of "does this Linear comment open with the `**[FROM CLAUDE CODE]**` prefix, and what's its marker," extracted out of `scripts/slack-rulings.mjs`'s own inline regex so the two can't drift the way `build-ready.yml`'s and `audit-new-todo.yml`'s duplicated jq once did. `isRulingComment`/`isContextChatComment` have no call site in code: their consumers are the CHAT-vs-RULING rule in `.claude/process.md`'s "Comments" section and `.claude/commands/work-ticket.md`'s Phase 0 step 2b, prose read by an LLM rather than code that imports a JS function. Don't read them as dead code to clean up. That rule and the unescape-then-`startsWith` rule landed in the prose in a second TAC-396 PR, after a CI session was refused writes to both files as "sensitive" with no human present to grant it. **`build-ready.yml`'s jq resume check still applies neither**: it anchors on the CC prefix without unescaping, so it can start a session on a plain `[FROM CLAUDE CHAT]` comment or on an escaped CC prefix, and the session applies both rules. That gap is pinned as a `KNOWN GAP` test in `analog-operator` PR #57 and closes in both repos together or neither.
- `scripts/linear.mjs` (TAC-444) — how a CI Claude Code session writes to Linear: `comment <issue> <file.md>`, `describe <issue> <file.md>`, `label add|remove <issue> "<name>"`, `state <issue> "<name>"`. The text of a comment or description comes from a plain markdown file the session writes with the Write tool, so nothing is JSON-escaped by hand; the helper looks up every id, posts comments flat, and exits 0 only when Linear returns `success: true` (1 on a failed write, 2 on bad usage or a missing key). It reads `LINEAR_API_KEY` from its environment, sends it only as the Authorization header, and redacts it from every line it prints. It refuses a comment that doesn't open with the `**[FROM CLAUDE CODE]**` prefix and a marker, using `comment-provenance.mjs`'s `isBotComment`/`commentMarker` rather than a second copy of the rule, because an unprefixed agent comment reads to the automation as human input and would resume a ticket as if Jaipal had answered. The logic is in `scripts/lib/linear-cli.mjs` (pure: `run` takes injected fetch, readFile, stdout and stderr); the entry only wires the process, and imports nothing outside Node's standard library, because the audit workflow runs it without `npm ci`. `scripts/lib/linear-prompts.test.ts` reads both workflow files and `work-ticket.md` as text, and fails if the two Linear blocks differ, if either teaches a helper call the helper rejects, or if the hand-escaped JSON write form comes back. CI only: locally the Linear MCP is the write path. **Both allowlists carry `Bash(node scripts/linear.mjs:*)`, the helper and no other node command, and no python at all (TAC-449).** They carried `Bash(node:*)` and `Bash(python3:*)` until then, both added by TAC-439 for YAML checks (`node -e`, `python3 -c`) before the helper existed, and nothing taught either for anything else; the narrow entry is the one analog-operator ships (TAC-451), and its audit run 35303513132 shows Claude Code admitting it. Operator's `main` never carried python; its TAC-439 review dropped it as too broad. A script a CI session must run gets an entry of that form, never a bare interpreter: a session that can run any program can read every secret in its environment, `LINEAR_API_KEY` included. `linear-prompts.test.ts` parses both workflows as YAML (operator's test, copied) and fails if either allowlist loses the entry, denies it, or carries any other rule naming node, nodejs or python (any version, with or without a path, so the runners' own `python3.12` too), a wildcard that would admit one, or a bare `Bash`. It does not see a rule that reaches an interpreter another way, such as `Bash(env python3:*)` or `Bash(bash:*)`. `npx vitest run scripts/lib/linear-prompts.test.ts` is now a build session's YAML check for those two files (nothing parses the other six workflows). **The narrow entry removes the obvious door, not the exposure**: a session can Read and rewrite the helper and then run it under this entry, a build session runs code through `npx vitest`, `npm run lint` and git hooks (husky shims the hooks that commit, checkout, pull and push run, and each runs a hook file the session can write), and the taught curl read can send the key to any host. Closing it at the credential is TAC-449's open half.
