- `scripts/run-report.mjs` (TAC-447) — what the build workflow's check step says when its session runs into the turn limit. `ending <execution-file> <max-turns>` prints how the session ended: `finished`, `finished-over-limit` (it finished and claude-code-action failed the run afterwards for going over, as run 35288433905 did at 128 against 120), `stopped-at-limit` (the CLI's own `error_max_turns`), `errored` or `no-record`. `notice <ticket> <execution-file> <max-turns>` prints the comment for the two limit endings, `[TURN-LIMIT]` (blocking) or `[OVER-LIMIT]` (bookkeeping), and nothing otherwise. Both report what reached GitHub, read from the runner's own git refs after the session: `git push` moves `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` only when it succeeds, and the checkout fetched every branch at the start. Logic in `scripts/lib/run-report.mjs` (pure, injected `git` and `readFile`, key redacted through `linear-cli.mjs`'s `redact`), which has a test against a real temporary git repository, because a fake `git` answers only the arguments it was written for and cannot tell a wrong range from a right one. Run by the workflow, never by the session, so no allowlist applies, and from a copy the workflow takes before the session starts (`$RUNNER_TEMP/turn-limit-reporter`): by the time it runs, the checkout is the session's branch, which may predate the script or be a ticket that edits it. If it fails, the check step falls back to its pre-TAC-447 behaviour.
- `scripts/claims.mjs` (TAC-448) — the build selection's check that no other session has a ticket before it takes one. The queue step's jq lists every candidate, in priority order and without a limit, with its comments and the time its ruling landed (`newestAt`); `node scripts/claims.mjs` skips each claimed one with a `skipped <ticket> (<mode>, <state>): another session has it: <reason>` line on stderr, then takes `LIMIT` from the rest, so a claimed ticket never uses up the run. It reads claims through `comment-provenance.mjs` (unescaped prefix, marker right after it), branch commits from the checkout's own `refs/remotes/origin` (the checkout fetched every branch), and open PRs with `gh` (`GH_TOKEN` in the step env). It fails the step when it cannot read the branches or they come back without `main` (nothing fetched from GitHub; a shallow checkout of main still lists `main` and would silently drop every commit signal, so a test pins `fetch-depth: 0`, and that pin, not the guard, protects the scheduled run), and warns and carries on without the PR signal when it cannot list PRs. A local `[CLAIM]` edited to `released` holds nothing; one that is not released holds whatever the ruling time. Logic in `scripts/lib/claims.mjs` (pure, injected `git`/`gh`/`now`); its tests carry TAC-396's real thread and branches from the incident, including the half no check can catch. Liveness has one owner: the jq no longer reads `[POLLING-STATE]` itself, and a test keeps it that way, because two liveness rules is how the resume path went unchecked.
- `scripts/reconcile-status.mjs` (TAC-466) — writes a ticket's status from public GitHub state, every build run: a branch `jaipal/tac-xxx-...` on GitHub moves it to In Progress, a merged PR moves it to Ready For QA. Exists because nothing in this repo's own GitHub-Linear integration attaches an analog-guest PR to its ticket (confirmed 2026-09-18: TAC-467's PR merged after the "Public repositories" integration setting was fixed, and TAC-467 still shows no PR attachment), so the "In Progress on PR open, Ready For QA on merge" behaviour `.claude/process.md` and `work-ticket.md` used to describe never happened here. Both files still need that correction (TAC-466's [NEEDS-ACTION]) — a CI session is refused Edit/Write on either as a sensitive file, with no human present to grant it, the same restriction TAC-396 hit; a human, or a locally-run session where Jaipal can approve the prompt, has to apply it. Reuses `isTicketBranch` / `parseRefs` / `REF_FORMAT` from `claims.mjs` so branch matching can't drift between the claim check and this. The queue step runs it over every owner-matched Ready/In Progress candidate, not just what `claims.mjs` selected this run, and that is safe rather than a second claim signal: a derived status is a projection of public state — a branch existing, a PR having merged — and carries nothing a claim doesn't already carry more precisely, and `claims.test.ts` pins that neither `claimOf` nor `pickUnclaimed` reads `.state`. Forward-only (`deriveTargetStatus` compares against a fixed `PIPELINE`, never invents a status outside it, never moves a ticket backward) and **fails SOFT, the opposite of `claims.mjs`'s fail-closed posture**: an unreadable branch list, an unreadable merged-PR list, or a failed Linear write just leaves that ticket's status stale until the next run, because there is no two-sessions hazard here to fail closed against — "reconciled every run" is what recovers a missed write, not a retry.

### Database

- `db/migrations/` — SQL migrations (single source of DB truth). Hand-written. See "Migrations" section.
- `db/types.ts` — auto-generated by `npm run db:types` against the live Supabase schema. May be hand-patched temporarily after a migration lands but before `db:types` runs; the next `db:types` run overwrites the patch with canonical output.

### Tests

- `*.test.ts` colocated next to source files. Vitest is the runner. Run all: `npx vitest run`. Run single file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`.

---

## Code conventions

- Industry-standard TypeScript and Next.js naming. PascalCase for components and types, camelCase for functions and variables, kebab-case for filenames, SCREAMING_SNAKE for env vars.
- Prefer functions over classes
- Async/await over `.then()`
- Zod for runtime validation at all API boundaries
- No `any` types. Use `unknown` and narrow.
- Errors are values for internal functions: return `{ ok: true, data }` or `{ ok: false, error }`. Throw only at outer boundaries (route handlers, scripts). Domain-specific aliases formalize the contract: `RAGResult<T>` (`lib/rag/types.ts`), `AIResult<T>` (`lib/ai/types.ts`), `RequireAdminResult<T>` (`lib/auth/`). New helpers should reuse one of these or define a parallel alias rather than inlining the discriminated union.
- Imports use the `@/*` alias for repo-relative paths in app/lib code. Scripts may use either `@/*` or relative imports; see "Module split for testability" for when relative imports are required.

### Module split for testability

Vitest resolves `@/*` aliases via `vitest.config.ts` (THE-231). The remaining failure mode is **module-load-time SDK init** — importing a module that constructs a Voyage / Supabase admin client at the top level will run that init in the test process, even with `vi.mock` (mocks intercept resolution, not transitive eager init). When this happens, split the helper module:

- `scripts/onboarding/<name>-pure.ts` — pure helpers, no `@/*` imports. Tests import from here.
- `scripts/onboarding/<name>.ts` — DB-touching code. Re-exports everything from `-pure` so the CLI can keep a single import.

Example: `ingest-response-review-pure.ts` (parsing, classification, dedupe) + `ingest-response-review.ts` (Supabase upserts, Voyage embed). The CLI imports from the latter; tests import from the former.

### LLM call patterns

- Use Vercel AI SDK's `generateObject` for structured outputs with a Zod schema.
- **THE-157 gotcha: never use `.min()` or `.max()` on number fields in LLM-output Zod schemas.** Anthropic's structured output rejects these. Use `.refine()` or post-LLM validation instead. **Extends to arrays (TAC-347):** `.max()` on a `z.array()` field also gets rejected — `output_config.format.schema: For 'array' type, property 'maxItems' is not supported` — discovered live when `key_facts: z.array(z.string()).min(1).max(3)` broke the first real generation call in TAC-347's scenario harness. `.min()` on an array is fine (confirmed working in the same ticket); only `.max()`/`maxItems` is rejected. Same fix as THE-157: drop the `.max()`, cap the array length via `.slice(0, N)` after the call returns.
- Default model for production agent work: `claude-sonnet-4-6`. Default temperature for generation: 0.7 (variation in phrasing) or 0.3 (idempotent extraction).
- All LLM-output schemas should have a `prompt_version` string field on the wrapper object so we can distinguish output from different prompt iterations.
- **Bumping `PROMPT_VERSION` requires updating FOUR test files, not three, and sometimes more.** `lib/ai/prompts/system-template.test.ts`, `lib/ai/generate-message.test.ts`, `lib/ai/classify-message.test.ts`, **and `lib/agent/handle-inbound.test.ts`** — the last carries a `promptVersion:` fixture that is easy to miss because it lives outside `lib/ai/`. Ticket descriptions have listed three since at least TAC-308; the fourth bit on both the v1.27.0 and v1.28.0 bumps. The v1.43.0 bump (TAC-356) found two MORE stale-fixture sites this same list doesn't name — `lib/agent/stages.test.ts` (two mocked `classifyMessage` results) and `lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.test.ts` (a mocked `classifyMessage` result) — neither of which failed CI (they're fixture values fed to mocks, not compared against the live constant), so they'd have shipped stale silently if the grep hadn't caught them. The reliable move is `grep -rn "v1\.<old>\.<new>" --include='*.ts' .` after the bump rather than trusting any written list, including this one — and update every hit that's a live fixture, not just the ones a failing test forces you to. The v1.45.0 bump (TAC-301) found a SIXTH site the TAC-356 list doesn't name either — `lib/agent/handle-followup.test.ts`, which didn't exist when that sweep ran (TAC-355 created it). The list grows every time someone adds an orchestrator test; that's the argument for grepping rather than reading. **The v1.47.0 bump (TAC-358) hit SEVEN files** — `lib/agent/handle-followup.test.ts`, `lib/agent/stages.test.ts` (two occurrences), `lib/agent/handle-inbound.test.ts`, `lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.test.ts`, `lib/ai/classify-message.test.ts`, `lib/ai/generate-message.test.ts` (two occurrences), `lib/ai/prompts/system-template.test.ts` — ten sites in total, found in one grep in a few seconds. **The v1.48.0 bump (TAC-362) hit EIGHT files / 12 sites** — one more file than TAC-358's seven, and the list grows on every bump because every new orchestrator test adds a fixture. Which is the argument for the grep, not for reading any list including this one. Note the one hit you must NOT change: the version string inside `system-template.ts`'s own changelog comment block is history, not a fixture. The v1.49.0 bump (TAC-380) hit the same eight files and found no new site. The v1.50.0 bump (TAC-394) found ten sites in seven files by the same grep. **The v1.51.0 bump (TAC-436) hit TWELVE sites in NINE files** — `lib/ai/prompts/system-template.ts` (the constant), `system-template.test.ts` (×2: the test NAME and the assertion), `generate-message.test.ts` (×2, same shape), `classify-message.test.ts`, `stages.test.ts` (×2), `handle-inbound.test.ts`, `handle-followup.test.ts`, `two-pending-slots.test.ts` and `regenerate-with-critique.test.ts` — two files more than TAC-394's list, because `two-pending-slots.test.ts` postdates it. The list grows on every bump; grep, do not read. **Three hits must NOT change, and a blind sed would have broken all three**: `system-template.ts`'s own changelog entry for the OLD version (history), plus comments in `serializers.ts` and `serializers.test.ts` that cite what v1.50.0 decided. (TAC-313, TAC-356, TAC-301, TAC-358, TAC-362, TAC-380, TAC-394, TAC-436.)

## Workflow rules for Claude Code

These are not aspirations. They are hard rules. Following them produces good work; skipping them produces drift.

### Linear-first

All work starts from a Linear ticket. If a request arrives without a ticket ID, ask for one before planning. Drive-by changes without an audit trail are not allowed.

### Audit-first

Before writing any code for a new ticket, read:
1. This file (CLAUDE.md)
2. The relevant existing files the new code will sit next to (e.g., for a new script in `scripts/`, read at least one existing script in the same directory)
3. The migrations that touch the relevant tables
4. Any existing tests for the modules being modified

Cite specific file paths and existing patterns in your plan. Don't infer architecture from filenames; read the actual code.

**High-stakes changes are planned before they are built, and the narrowest of them are never built unattended.** **Hard stop** — auth (`lib/auth/`, `verifyAnalogAdminAccess`, RLS policies), payment/Stripe code, Sendblue webhook handlers, or migrations against `messages` / `engagement_events` / `voice_corpus`. Write the full plan and post it as `[PLAN]`; on approval post `[HUMAN-REVIEW-REQUIRED]` and stop, because the build is a session Jaipal drives. **Plan gate** — the agent runtime contract (voice fidelity floor, retrieval floors, the universal voice rules). Write the full plan, post it as `[PLAN]`, wait for approval, then build, commit, push and open a draft PR. Neither tier is a reason to skip the plan, and neither proceeds via `[NEEDS-INPUT]` alone. A change to guest-facing copy shows the new wording verbatim in the plan and waits for approval of that wording specifically.

### Plan → review → build → review → commit

1. **Plan only on the first request.** Output a written plan covering scope, file paths, function decomposition, sequence of operations, existing patterns to reuse, edge cases, what you intentionally chose NOT to do, and open questions. Stop and wait for review.
2. **Build only after the plan is approved.** A second prompt will explicitly authorize the build per the approved plan.
3. **Verify and report.** After building, run `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run`. Report file changes, test count, deviations from the plan, and anything you'd push back on.
4. **Commit only after review.** Don't commit until the human says go.

### Comment protocol

**Authorship prefix.** Every Linear comment posted via the MCP starts with `**[FROM CLAUDE CODE]**` on its own line at the top. Linear shows Jaipal as the author of all MCP-posted comments, so this prefix is the only way to distinguish CC comments from chat comments (which use `**[FROM CLAUDE CHAT]**`). Applies to every comment — audits, plans, self-reviews, NEEDS-INPUT flags. Going forward; not retroactive.

**Purpose tag.** When a clarifying question surfaces during work, follow the authorship prefix with `[NEEDS-INPUT]` and each question numbered, written in the concrete-case format under "Asking Jaipal a question" in `.claude/process.md`. Add the `Needs Decision` label, leave the status where it is, and stop. Blocked-on-Jaipal is a label, not a status: there is no "Awaiting Input" or "Needs Ruling" status any more. Any human comment newer than the question is his answer. A question on a high-stakes ticket is still `[NEEDS-INPUT]`: `[HUMAN-REVIEW-REQUIRED]` is not a question marker, and is posted once after a hard-stop plan is approved (per "Audit-first" above). Post every comment flat, never as a threaded reply. `.claude/process.md` is canonical for statuses, labels and markers.

**`/work-ticket` auto-polling.** Inside the `/work-ticket` flow, posting a comment that needs a response (plan post, `[NEEDS-INPUT]`) does not exit the session. The command is idempotent and uses `ScheduleWakeup` to auto-resume on a 60s → 300s exponential backoff with a 2-hour cumulative timeout (26 iterations). When the operator replies in Linear, the next wakeup detects the reply and applies a 3-way classification: **Proceed** (advance to next phase), **Modify** (integrate revisions and re-post plan, keep polling), or **Wind-down** (post `[POLLING-CLOSED]` and exit cleanly). Chit-chat / holding-pattern replies post `[POLLING-ACK]` and re-schedule. Terminal states that exit immediately with no wakeup: `[HUMAN-REVIEW-REQUIRED]`, `[POLLING-TIMEOUT]`, `[POLLING-CLOSED]`, and PR-link comments at Phase 5. The operator types `/work-ticket TAC-XXX` exactly once; subsequent harness re-fires of the same prompt are transparent. The agent never auto-closes the ticket — operators close manually (the permission hook enforces this). **Stale-session safety:** each session writes a `[POLLING-STATE]` comment to the ticket and updates it on every wakeup. If a manual re-invocation finds a fresher `[POLLING-STATE]` than the one it would write, it exits immediately to avoid two wakeup chains for the same ticket. This means manually re-running `/work-ticket TAC-XXX` while a session is already polling will appear to "skip" iterations — that's intentional. Outside `/work-ticket`, the default "stop after `[NEEDS-INPUT]`" behavior above still applies.

### Changing the ticket workflows

A change to `build-ready.yml` or `audit-new-todo.yml` is tested on a **fixture ticket**, and what can be tested before merge depends on the workflow: the audit workflow runs whole from a branch, the build workflow runs only its shell steps from one. Its session skips on any branch whose copy differs from `main`, and a green run means only that it skipped. The procedure, including asking Jaipal before creating the fixture, is `.claude/process.md`, "Testing a workflow change". A build run works one ticket and pushes each planned commit as it makes it (TAC-447).

### Claiming a ticket

**Two sessions must never work one ticket, and the status column does not stop them.** On 2026-09-17 a build run resumed TAC-396 while a local session was building it (run 35299836324, attempt 1, `resume: TAC-396 (In Progress)`): the resume path read neither the status nor any sign of the other session, and a human cancelling the run was all that stopped it. The build selection now skips a ticket another session has and names it in the run log (`scripts/claims.mjs`, TAC-448). A ticket is claimed, within the last 3 hours, by a local session's `[CLAIM]` that is not `released`, by a `[POLLING-STATE]`, or by a commit on its `jaipal/tac-xxx-...` branch from anyone but the build session, and, for a start, by an open PR. A claim that is not released holds whatever Jaipal rules meanwhile, because his ruling reaches Linear when the Slack sync posts it; a `[POLLING-STATE]` or a commit counts on a resume only if it is no older than the ruling. The build workflow in turn posts `[CLAIM]` or `[RESUME-CLAIM]` naming its run, including for a ticket named in a dispatch, and a local session leaves the ticket alone while that run is going.

**A local session posts `[CLAIM]` on the ticket before anything else it writes**, whether it runs `/work-ticket` or was started with a plain prompt, and edits it to `released` when it hands the ticket back to wait for Jaipal (`work-ticket.md`, "Claiming the ticket"). Before that comment lands, or the session's first push to its `jaipal/tac-xxx-...` branch, nothing protects it: the selection sees only Linear and GitHub. Also unprotected: a build dispatched with a named ticket, which skips the selection, and `In Progress`, which is set only once a branch exists and is not read as a claim. `.claude/process.md`, "Claims", has the full rules and the remaining gaps.

**A claim is per ticket, not per checkout.** Two local sessions in one working directory share one HEAD. On 2026-09-17 the TAC-458 session ran `git checkout -b` in the checkout the TAC-448 session was using, and TAC-448's next two commits landed on TAC-458's branch, one push reaching its draft PR #209 before both sessions noticed. A second local session works in its own `git worktree` (TAC-440), and a session pushes by explicit refspec (`git push origin <branch>:<branch>`), which cannot push to a branch it did not name.

### Never write `.env.local`. Never run `vercel env pull`.

**Standing rule, no exceptions:** Claude Code does not write, overwrite, regenerate, or restore `.env.local`, and does not run `vercel env pull` (or any other command whose effect is to produce that file) under any flag, in any directory, for any reason. When credentials appear to be missing, empty, or wrong, **stop and ask the operator.** Do not attempt to repopulate them. `.env.local` is operator-owned local state that exists in exactly one place on one machine; it is gitignored, it is not in any backup this repo controls, and there is no safe idempotent command that rebuilds it.

The rule is absolute rather than conditional because every plausible guard failed in the incident that produced it (2026-09-12). `vercel env pull .env.local --yes` was run against a file believed to be blank, and destroyed 29 live keys. Two independent errors compounded:

1. **"I can't see a value" was treated as "there is no value."** The file had been inspected with `grep -oE "^[A-Za-z_]+="`, which prints key *names* only and never prints values by construction. Every key looked valueless because the command could not have shown a value. A verification step that cannot produce the evidence it claims to check is not verification — see "A safety gate's green signal is only evidence if you have confirmed what it fires on" under Common gotchas, which is the same failure in a different costume.
2. **An earlier failed `cd` made the file look absent.** The repo path then contained a curly apostrophe (`Desktop’s`; TAC-425 moved the repo to `~/dev/Ideas/` on 2026-09-16); a hand-typed path silently resolved to the wrong directory, the file wasn't found there, and "not found in the directory I'm actually in" became "does not exist." This is the same character that breaks the `import.meta.url` main-module guard documented in the `scripts/onboarding/` entry and the OAuth-client path documented under Drive integration. It cost three separate things before the move.

Recovery in that incident came entirely from outside this repo — VS Code's local file history at `~/Library/Application Support/Code/User/History/` happened to hold a copy. That is luck, not a recovery path, and it is not to be relied on as one.

Reading `.env.local` is fine and often necessary. **Writing it is not, and pulling it is not.** If a script fails for want of a credential, report which variable is missing and stop.

### Never just acknowledge

If asked to remember, forget, or update something about the user's context, use the `memory_user_edits` tool. Don't reply conversationally with "I'll remember that" — that's a lie if you don't actually update memory.

### File path conventions

- For any new file, propose the path first.
- Match the directory's existing pattern. Don't invent new top-level directories without asking.

### Commit messages

- Lowercase, imperative ("add inbound webhook handler"), no emoji.
- Body explains WHY when the change isn't obvious from the title.
- Reference the ticket: `THE-XXX: <title>` on the subject line.

### When unsure

Ask. Do not guess product behavior. The plan-first rule exists so questions surface before code does.

---

## Git workflow

- Branch protection on `main` is active. Direct pushes to `main` are rejected.
- All changes go through a pull request.
- Branch naming: `jaipal/the-XXX-short-description` (keep it short — Linear's auto-generated branch names are often too long).
- Open PR with `gh pr create`. Title format: `THE-XXX: <imperative subject>`.
- Merge style: `gh pr merge <num> --squash --delete-branch`. Squash matches the linear single-commit-per-change history of `main`.
- **STACKED PRs DO NOT SURVIVE THE MERGE OF THEIR BASE. Expect the child to be auto-CLOSED, not retargeted.** Happened three times on 2026-09-13: #157 was stacked on #156's branch, #156 merged, #157 closed unmerged and had to be replaced by #158 from the same head branch five minutes later; TAC-367's #161 was stacked on #160 the same way and closed the instant #160 merged, replaced by #162. **Reopening is refused, not merely awkward** — `gh pr reopen 161` returns `GraphQL: Could not open the pull request. (reopenPullRequest)`. Budget a replacement PR; there is no recovering the original. **Retargeting wouldn't help even where GitHub offers it**, and that is the part worth understanding: `--squash` puts a NEW commit on `main` that is not an ancestor of the child branch, so the merge base stays at the pre-fork commit and the child's diff still contains the parent's changes — it will conflict on any file both touched. **A rebase is mandatory regardless of what happens to the PR object.** **Recover with the parent's COMMIT SHA, never its branch name**, because `--delete-branch` deletes the local branch too and the name is gone by the time you need it:

  ```
  git log --oneline <child-branch>          # the parent's tip is the commit below yours
  git rebase --onto main <parent-SHA> <child-branch>
  git push --force-with-lease
  ```

  The SHA survives in the child's history even though every ref to it is deleted, which is what makes this work at all. The branch-name form fails with `fatal: invalid upstream` — a first version of this entry gave exactly that command and was found broken twenty minutes later by the person who wrote it, during the recovery it was supposed to serve. Note also that an unrelated dirty file (`.claude/`, `supabase/.temp/`) aborts the rebase with `cannot rebase: You have unstaged changes`; stash first. Verify the REBASED TREE, not just the absence of conflicts — `git diff main...HEAD --stat` must list ONLY the child's own files, and a clean replay can still produce a wrong file when both PRs edited the same one (CLAUDE.md, usually). **STANDING PREFERENCE: don't stack. Branch each PR from `main` and take the conflict at merge time.** Stack only when a PR genuinely cannot be REVIEWED without its parent's changes present — which was not true of any of the three that broke on 2026-09-13; they were stacked out of habit, to keep a diff tidy. The asymmetry is the whole argument: **a merge conflict is visible, recoverable, and in-repo. A destroyed PR object is not recoverable at all** — reopening is refused, and its review history, comments and CI runs do not transfer to the replacement. Documentation makes the recovery cheap; not stacking makes it unnecessary. When you do stack, make it a deliberate exception and budget the replacement PR.
- CI must be green before merge — `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint`, `npx vitest run`, `npm run build` all enforced via `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- Pre-commit hook (husky + lint-staged, configured via `prepare` in `package.json`): runs `eslint --fix` on staged `.ts/.tsx`, then `tsc --noEmit` against the full project, then `vitest related --run` on staged files. Failure rejects the commit. Don't `--no-verify` without a reason.

---

## Database migrations

Migrations live in `db/migrations/` numbered sequentially. Each is hand-written SQL. Workflow:

1. Write the migration file.
2. Operator applies it via Supabase Studio's SQL editor.
3. Operator runs `npm run db:types` to regenerate `db/types.ts`.
4. If the migration adds a column the script needs to typecheck against immediately, hand-patch `db/types.ts` in the same commit as the script. The next `db:types` run will overwrite the patch with canonical output. Document the patch in the commit message so it's not lost.

### Ordering for backwards-incompatible migrations

When a migration drops a column, renames a column, or adds a `NOT NULL` constraint that the currently-deployed code doesn't yet handle, **deploy the code first, then apply the migration in Studio.** The reverse order opens a downtime window — between Studio-apply and Vercel-deploy, the deployed code queries a schema that no longer exists and every affected request 500s.

**Backwards-incompatible → code first, then schema:**

1. Open PR with the new code (and the migration file checked in).
2. Merge → Vercel deploys the new code. The new code may reference columns that don't exist yet — `tsc` and any runtime path that hits the missing column will fail cleanly (compile error / "column does not exist") rather than producing 500s on a live user-facing path.
3. Apply migration in Studio. Run `npm run db:types`.

**Backwards-compatible (additive only) → order doesn't matter.** A migration that only adds nullable columns, only widens a `CHECK` constraint, or only creates a new index can be applied before or after the code deploy without a downtime window.

**Heuristic:** if `git diff` shows a `DROP COLUMN`, `DROP FUNCTION`, `RENAME COLUMN`, `ALTER ... SET NOT NULL`, or a tightened `CHECK`, treat the migration as backwards-incompatible. Apply Studio AFTER merge — never before.

Source: TAC-272 (2026-05-16). Migration 021 dropped `operators.auth_user_id` while the deployed code still queried it; `admin.theanalog.company` 500'd for the ~3-minute window between Studio-apply and PR merge.

**Exception: an INERT drop can ride along with an ADD.** The rule above binds on *live readers*, not on the keyword. A `DROP COLUMN` against a column with **zero readers and zero writers in app code** cannot open a downtime window, because no deployed request path queries it — the only reference is the generated `db/types.ts`, which is compile-time. When such a drop is bundled with an additive change the deployed code SELECTs, the ADD sets the ordering and the whole migration applies in Studio **BEFORE** merge. Rollback is unaffected for the same reason: nothing reads the dropped column on the way back either.

Before invoking this exception, **prove the column is inert** — `grep -rn '<column>' --include='*.ts' --include='*.tsx' . | grep -v node_modules` must return only `db/types.ts`. A grep that finds a single live reader means the normal after-merge rule applies to the whole file, and the two halves should be split into separate migrations rather than argued about.

Source: TAC-313 (2026-08-08). Migration 032 added `messages.generation_id` (SELECTed by deployed code → before-merge) in the same file as dropping `messages.parent_draft_id` (dead since migration 001, zero readers → inert). Bundling was correct; leaving the rule stated only in keyword terms would have made the next person re-derive this from scratch.

### Migration log

- `001_initial_schema.sql` — 13 tables: venues, operators, operator_venues, venue_configs, guests, mechanics, transactions, messages, engagement_events, guest_states, voice_corpus, voice_embeddings, audit_log. Includes the shared `updated_at` trigger function.
- `002_add_message_reactions.sql` — adds `reaction_type` column to `messages`, extends the category check to include `'reaction'`, updates the content constraint to allow rows with only a reaction_type, and adds a consistency check between `category` and `reaction_type`.
- `003_ai_module_refinements.sql` — batches schema changes from AI module design: renames `messages.confidence_score` to `voice_fidelity` for terminology consistency; adds `review_reason` and `pending_until` columns to support routing audit trail and the hold-and-fallback workflow; extends `messages.category` check to include `'acknowledgment'` for fallback messages.
- `004_match_voice_corpus_function.sql` — adds Postgres function `match_voice_corpus(venue_id, query_embedding, match_count, source_type_filter, min_confidence)` for pgvector cosine similarity search over a venue's voice corpus. Used by the RAG module's `retrieveContext` via Supabase RPC.
- `005_post_recognition_refinements.sql` — batches schema changes from RAG/recognition module work: adds retrieval analytics columns to voice_embeddings, authorship column to voice_corpus, extends operator_venues permission_level to include 'analog_admin', adds home_postal_code and distance_to_venue_miles to guests for the recognition distance multiplier, extends engagement_events.event_type to include 'referral_converted'.
- `006_idempotency_and_inbound_message_origin.sql` — adds unique constraint on `messages.provider_message_id` as last-line defense against duplicate orchestrator runs (replaces the prior regular index), and extends `guests.created_via` to include `'inbound_message'` for guests auto-created from unrecognized phone numbers texting in. Inbound→outbound message linking already exists via `messages.reply_to_message_id` from migration 001 — no schema change needed for that. Visit dedupe timezone fix and `venue_info.currentContext` are app-side changes, not migrations. THE-150 later added an optional `expiresAt` field to each `currentContext` entry — runtime filters expired entries via `filterActiveContext` in `lib/schemas/venue-info.ts` before they reach the prompt.
- `007_test_synthetic_guests.sql` — adds `is_test_synthetic` boolean to `guests` (default false, not null) plus a partial index on `(venue_id, is_test_synthetic) WHERE is_test_synthetic = true`. Used by `run-test-scenarios` (THE-181) to mark synthetic guests seeded for Phase 5 testing so analytics can filter them out. Synthetic guests are still per-venue (schema-required); the deterministic phone numbers `+15550001000/100/200/300` are reused across venues but each venue gets its own four guest rows.
- `008_voice_corpus_source_ref.sql` — adds `source_ref text` column to `voice_corpus`, extends the `source_type` check constraint to include `'operator_edit'`, and adds a partial unique index on `(venue_id, source_ref) WHERE source_ref IS NOT NULL`. Enables idempotent upsert-by-source-ref for `ingest-response-review` (THE-178), which keys voice corpus rows on `'08-review:{sample_id}'` so re-runs don't duplicate.
- `009_mechanic_min_state_and_redemption.sql` — adds three columns to `mechanics`: `min_state` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 'new', check-constrained to the four guest states; gates eligibility by relationship band), `redemption_policy` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 'one_time', `'one_time' | 'renewable'`; named "policy" rather than "type" to avoid colliding with the existing `mechanics.redemption.type` jsonb field describing the redemption mechanism), and `redemption_window_days` (nullable integer; required when policy='renewable'). Adds composite CHECK constraint `mechanics_redemption_window_consistency` enforcing `(one_time + null) OR (renewable + non-null)`. Adds index `idx_mechanics_min_state` on `(venue_id, min_state)` for the runtime mechanics-load query. Extends `engagement_events.event_type` to include `'mechanic_redeemed'`. Legacy `'perk_redeemed'` and `'merch_redeemed'` event types stay in the constraint for back-compat but new code emits `'mechanic_redeemed'` only. THE-170.
- `010_command_center_columns.sql` — adds two unrelated columns bundled atomically. `messages.langfuse_trace_id text` (nullable) links each agent-generated outbound message to its Langfuse trace, with partial index `idx_messages_langfuse_trace_id` for trace-ID lookup; populated by THE-200 instrumentation, existing rows stay null. `operators.is_analog_admin boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` gates access to `admin.theanalog.company` (admin routes colocated in this repo, served via a separate Vercel project). Default false so existing operators are unaffected; admin grants are explicit one-off SQL updates per the template under "Common gotchas". THE-199.
- `011_add_inbound_classifier_categories.sql` — drops + recreates `messages_category_check` to include four new classifier categories: `comp_complaint`, `mechanic_request`, `recommendation_request`, `casual_chatter`. Existing values (`welcome`, `follow_up`, `reply`, `new_question`, `opt_out`, `media`, `perk_unlock`, `event_invite`, `manual`, `reaction`, `acknowledgment`) are preserved. CHECK constraint values don't surface in supabase's generated types, so `db/types.ts` doesn't change — runtime category narrowing lives in `lib/ai/types.ts`'s `MessageCategory` union, with `lib/agent/types.ts` aliasing it to prevent drift. Note: `acknowledgment` was added to `messages.category` back in migration 003 but only got its `CLASSIFY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` definition in this PR — the schema enum had it, the model just was never told about it. THE-228.
- `012_add_personal_history_question_category.sql` — drops + recreates `messages_category_check` to include `personal_history_question` (e.g. "what did I get last time", "do you remember me"). All values from migration 011 preserved. Same supabase-doesn't-surface-CHECK-values caveat applies; runtime narrowing lives in `MessageCategory`. THE-233.
- `013_add_knowledge_corpus.sql` — adds `knowledge_corpus` and `knowledge_embeddings` tables (mirror of `voice_corpus` / `voice_embeddings`: same `vector(1024)` dim for voyage-3-large, same HNSW + cosine index, same `set_updated_at` trigger on the corpus table only since embeddings are immutable) plus `match_knowledge_corpus` RPC. RPC mirrors `match_voice_corpus` with two additions: optional `tag_filter text[]` param and `tags` in the result row, since knowledge entries are topic-tagged (sourcing, staff_rayan, ceremony, etc.) in a way voice exemplars aren't. Splits topical content (origin story, sourcing detail, staff personalities, mechanic explanations) into the new tables; `voice_corpus` continues to store style exemplars only. No ticket — schema was applied to the live DB via Studio prior to this file landing.
- `014_message_response_review.sql` — adds `messages.response_review jsonb` (nullable) to capture per-message reviews from the Command Center conversation viewer (THE-235). JSONB shape validated by `MessageReviewSchema` in `lib/schemas/message-review.ts`: `{schemaVersion, reviewedBy, reviewedAt, category?, editedMessage?, comment?, rule?, expectedFailure?}`. No `verdict` field — presence/absence of `editedMessage` IS the edit signal, presence of `rule` IS the rule signal. Mirrors the 08-flow's destinations: `editedMessage` → `voice_corpus` row keyed on `source_ref='cc-review:{message_id}'` (replace-in-place via the partial unique index from migration 008), `rule` → `brand_persona.voiceAntiPatterns` dedupe-append. Forward-only; existing rows stay null. Inbound message reviews are rejected at the API layer (no CHECK constraint).
- `015_voice_critiques.sql` — adds `voice_critiques` table (id, venue_id, message_id, critique_text, kind ∈ `edit_only` \| `edit_and_rule`, embedding vector(1024), promoted_at, dismissed_at, created_by_operator_id, created_at) plus partial index `idx_voice_critiques_venue_unresolved` filtered to `promoted_at IS NULL AND dismissed_at IS NULL` and HNSW + cosine index on embedding. Adds `find_similar_critiques(query_venue_id, query_embedding, exclude_id?, similarity_threshold default 0.85, match_count default 20)` SQL function — cosine search restricted to UNRESOLVED edit_only critiques in a single venue, with `exclude_id` so the just-committed row never surfaces in its own neighborhood query. Embedding stored on the row directly (no separate _embeddings table) since critiques are short and not chunked. Persistence rule: every committed critique inserts here regardless of `kind`; the cluster query filters at read time. THE-238.
- `016_add_inbound_outbound_split_categories.sql` — drops + recreates `messages_category_check` to include three new values: `perk_inquiry`, `event_question`, `unknown`. `perk_inquiry` / `event_question` are inbound counterparts to the outbound `perk_unlock` / `event_invite` triggers (guests asking ABOUT perks/events vs the venue offering them). `unknown` is the inbound catch-all when the classifier can't categorize confidently — replaces the old practice of routing ambiguous inbounds to `manual`. All values from migration 012 preserved. Same supabase-doesn't-surface-CHECK-values caveat applies; runtime narrowing lives in `MessageCategory`. Lib changes that ship in the same PR: classifier Zod enum subsetted to inbound-only (drops `welcome`/`follow_up`/`perk_unlock`/`event_invite`), `CLASSIFY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` rewritten to match, `getCategoryInstructions` switch + `runtimeToProse` switch widened, `acknowledgment` instructions semantically rewritten (guest sign-off, not venue holding-message), em-dash hygiene swept across 7 instruction files. PROMPT_VERSION bumped to v1.10.0. TAC-238.
- `017_knowledge_corpus_tag_split.sql` — adds `knowledge_corpus.primary_tags text[]` (closed-enum routing signal — see `lib/schemas/knowledge-tags.ts`) and `secondary_tags text[]` (free-form descriptive context). Backfills both from the existing `tags` array using the canonical-prefix rule: a tag goes to `primary_tags` if it matches a canonical value exactly OR if its first underscore-prefix matches a canonical value (so `staff_phoebe` stays whole in `primary_tags`); everything else lands in `secondary_tags`. Adds GIN indexes on both new columns. Replaces `match_knowledge_corpus`: drops the unused `tag_filter` parameter, adds `primary_tag_filter text[]` with array-overlap (`&&`, OR) semantics, returns both tag arrays. The legacy `tags` column is **not** dropped this cycle — left as a safety belt; a follow-up migration drops it. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` is re-run post-apply. TAC-242.
- `018_operator_review_state.sql` — adds four columns to `messages` for the mobile operator approval queue (TAC-258): `review_state text` (nullable, CHECK-constrained to `pending | approved | edited | skipped | auto_sent`), `previous_review_state text` (same enum, used by the 3-second undo), `last_operator_action_at timestamptz`, `last_operator_id uuid REFERENCES operators(id)`. `review_state` is a **separate axis from `messages.status`** — status carries delivery lifecycle (received / draft / sent / delivered / ...), review_state carries the human-review verdict applied to outbound drafts. Backfills every existing direction='outbound' row to `'auto_sent'` so the partial queue index isn't polluted. Adds two partial indexes: `idx_messages_review_state_pending` on `(venue_id, created_at) WHERE review_state='pending'` (queue lookup) and `idx_messages_operator_action_recent` on `(last_operator_action_at desc) WHERE last_operator_action_at IS NOT NULL` (undo window). Adds `list_operator_queue(venue_ids uuid[])` RPC with two LATERAL subqueries (latest `guest_states` per draft + jsonb_agg of last-3 recent context excluding the draft row itself, limit 200) — single-round-trip queue read, no N+1. TAC-212 (runtime flag policy) will later override the `auto_sent` default to `'pending'` at the same insert site for drafts that need human review. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` is re-run post-apply. TAC-258.
- `019_mechanics_requires_operator_approval.sql` — adds `mechanics.requires_operator_approval boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`. Consumed by TAC-212's approval-policy gate: when an eligible mechanic carries this flag and `formatMechanicEligibility` renders it for the prompt, the bullet is annotated so Sonnet sets `requiresOperatorApproval=true` on its structured output if the draft commits the guest to that mechanic, which then routes the draft to the operator queue. No index — the agent loads all active mechanics per venue and filters in app code. Per-venue toggles happen by hand in Supabase Studio (template in Common gotchas). db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-212.
- `020_single_pending_draft_per_guest.sql` — adds partial unique index `idx_messages_one_pending_per_guest ON messages (venue_id, guest_id) WHERE review_state='pending'`. Enforces the "at most one pending draft per (venue, guest)" invariant at the storage layer. Race-condition backstop for `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` (lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts) — concurrent rapid inbounds that both miss the existence check get caught here, with the losing INSERT receiving `code='23505'` (unique_violation) and falling through to the UPDATE branch. Composite on `(venue_id, guest_id)` honors the cross-venue isolation invariant (each venue is its own block). Filtered on `review_state='pending'` so the index is sparse and doesn't block any of the other review_state values (approved/edited/skipped/auto_sent), each of which can legitimately appear many times per guest. No backfill required (verified pre-apply: zero `(venue_id, guest_id)` pairs had >1 pending row). No db/types.ts change — indexes don't surface in generated types. Coordinates with migration 018 (TAC-258) `idx_messages_review_state_pending` (additive, no overlap) and migration 019 (TAC-212) (unrelated). TAC-264. **Replaced by migration 041's two per-slot indexes (TAC-394).**
- `021_operator_multi_method_auth.sql` — splits `operators.auth_user_id` into two nullable UUID columns (`auth_user_id_phone`, `auth_user_id_email`) so an operator can sign in via phone OTP OR email magic link and resolve to the same row. Single-transaction migration: adds the new columns, backfills from the existing `auth_user_id` by probing `auth.users.phone IS NOT NULL` (→ phone column) vs `auth.users.email IS NOT NULL` (→ email column), aborts with `raise exception` if any operator ends up with neither column populated, then runs a **generic backfill** over unlinked `auth.users` rows matching on `phone_number = '+' || auth.users.phone` or `email = auth.users.email` — collisions log via `raise notice` and skip rather than picking arbitrarily. Drops the orphan `link_operator_auth(text, text)` Studio-only RPC (zero call sites in app code; would have left a broken function body after column drop) and replaces it with the TS-side `linkOperatorByAuthUser` helper in `lib/auth/link-operator.ts`. Replaces the `operators_select_own` RLS policy (which lived in Studio, NOT in any prior migration — first time the policy text is versioned) — OR clause across both new columns with explicit `IS NOT NULL` guards. Drops old column + index, adds UNIQUE on each new column, adds CHECK `operators_must_have_auth` so every row has at least one method, adds partial `WHERE NOT NULL` indexes per TAC-258's pattern, flips `phone_number` to `NOT NULL` (per ticket: both methods required at provisioning going forward). **db/types.ts hand-patched** in the same commit to reflect the new operators shape and remove the dropped RPC; next `npm run db:types` overwrites with canonical output. TAC-272.
- `022_add_guest_demo_flag.sql` — adds `guests.is_demo boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` + partial index `idx_guests_demo` on `(venue_id, is_demo) WHERE is_demo = true` (mirrors migration 007's `is_test_synthetic` shape). Per-guest demo flag for investor demos / personal smoke-tests of pilot venues: when set, the agent runtime bypasses the TAC-212 approval policy gate IN FULL (incl. the comp regex backstop) and skips the read receipt and typing indicators — see "Demo guest bypass" under "Approval policy gates". (Pre-TAC-421 this also skipped a ~6.5s pre-send sleep; that sleep no longer exists for anyone, so the flag buys no latency now.) Semantically distinct from `is_test_synthetic` (a synthetic-row analytics filter) — `is_demo` marks a real-phone-number guest whose delivery behavior should bypass friction; the two flags are independent (a guest can be either, both, or neither). Additive, but the deployed code SELECTs the new column, so **apply this migration in Studio BEFORE merging the PR** — not after. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-284.
- `023_link_operator_auth_rpc.sql` — adds `public.link_operator_auth(p_phone text, p_email text) returns operators` as `SECURITY DEFINER` with `set search_path = public, pg_temp`. Companion caller is `analog-operator/lib/auth/operator.ts`, which invokes the RPC right after Supabase OTP / magic-link verification; the function stamps `auth.uid()` into the matching `auth_user_id_phone` / `auth_user_id_email` column on the operators row keyed by `phone_number` (E.164 with leading `+`) or `email`, bumps `last_seen_at`, and returns the row. Not found → returns NULL (client maps to a `not_provisioned` toast). Idempotent: repeated calls from the same `auth.uid()` re-set the same column to the same value; UNIQUE constraints on `auth_user_id_phone` / `auth_user_id_email` / `phone_number` / `email` catch cross-row collisions and the RPC lets those bubble as Postgres errors rather than swallow. Grants `EXECUTE` to `authenticated`, revokes from `public, anon`. Re-introduces the RPC name dropped in migration 021 (where it was Studio-only with zero call sites) — the architectural reversal is intentional now that analog-operator has a real caller and the `auth_user_id_*` columns are stable. The TS-side `linkOperatorByAuthUser` helper in `lib/auth/link-operator.ts` remains the canonical path for analog-guest's own auth surfaces (admin cookie session + bearer-token API); this RPC is the operator-app surface only. Additive — no db/types.ts change required (RPCs don't surface in generated types). TAC-285.
- `024_operator_apns_token.sql` — adds `operators.apns_device_token text` and `operators.apns_token_updated_at timestamptz` (both nullable) + partial index `idx_operators_apns_token on operators(id) where apns_device_token is not null` (mirrors migration 022's partial-index shape). Backs the TAC-207 APNs push pipeline: POST `/api/operator/devices` upserts the token from the analog-operator iOS client, `sendDraftFlaggedPush` (`lib/notifications/send.ts`) reads it when the TAC-212 approval-policy gate routes a draft to the queue, and APNs 410 Gone / 400 BadDeviceToken responses null both columns. Single token per operator for pilot; multi-device is deferred — revisiting would mean extracting these columns into a child table without changing the push pipeline. **Backwards-compatible (additive only)** — order doesn't matter per *Database migrations* §"Ordering". db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-207.
- `025_add_guest_context_jsonb.sql` — adds `guests.context jsonb not null default '{}'::jsonb` for the TAC-296 per-guest accumulating context (dietary, home base, life events, observations). Persisted shape validated by `GuestContextSchema` (`lib/schemas/guest-context.ts`), agent-emitted patches validated by `GuestContextPatchSchema` (aggressively permissive — every nested field optional, unknown keys stripped — so a near-miss patch doesn't trip the regen loop). The `lib/guests/context.ts` write helper deep-merges with arrays REPLACED (per the ticket spec — agent emits the full new array; for life_context the prompt instruction explicitly tells the model to include existing entries it still wants to keep), appends the `observation` shortcut to `observations[]` with a runtime-stamped `captured_at`, and syncs `guests.first_name` / `last_name` columns inside the same UPDATE statement (multi-SET, row-atomic, no RPC / no sequenced writes — per the TAC-296 architectural call #5). Read path: `build-runtime-context.ts` SELECTs the column, parses + filters expired `life_context` entries via `filterActiveLifeContext` (mirrors THE-150's `filterActiveContext` pattern), truncates observations to `OBSERVATION_RENDER_LIMIT`=10. Prompt: `formatGuestContext` renders a `## Guest context` block between `## Visit history` and `## Recent conversation` in the user prompt — placement chosen so reading order is "who they are → what they can get → what they've recently bought → what we know about them → what was recently said." Hard char budget ≈ 500 tokens; truncate observations to 5 floor first, then drop oldest life_context entries. **PROMPT_VERSION bumped to v1.15.0.** Last-write-wins concurrency for v1 (documented as a gotcha — see "Common gotchas → guest context write races"). **Backwards-compatible (additive only)** — DEFAULT covers existing rows; new code SELECTs the new column, so apply the migration in Studio BEFORE merging the PR per *Database migrations* §"Ordering for backwards-incompatible migrations" (the SELECT side is the backwards-incompatible half, even though the schema change is additive). db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-296.
- `026_create_guest_commitments.sql` — creates `guest_commitments` table for TAC-297: every promise the agent makes (comp / hold / discount / recommendation), with a `status` state machine (`open → pending_ack → acknowledged | redeemed | expired | cancelled`) enforced via CHECK + app-layer CAS in `lib/guests/commitments.ts`. CAS transitions anchor push idempotency: `transitionToPendingAck` returns `transitioned=true` only on rowcount=1, and the orchestrator (handle-inbound) + cron processor (commitments-due) gate the push call on that flag, so concurrent imminent inbound + cron firing on the same row produces exactly one push (TAC-297 design call #4). Three partial indexes: `idx_guest_commitments_due` on `(expected_arrival) WHERE status='open' AND expected_arrival IS NOT NULL` (cron query), `idx_guest_commitments_active_for_guest` on `(venue_id, guest_id, status) WHERE status IN ('open','pending_ack')` (runtime context lookup), `idx_guest_commitments_pending_ack_venue` on `(venue_id, created_at) WHERE status='pending_ack'` (operator queue lookup). Table added to `supabase_realtime` publication for TAC-298's live card surfacing. NO RLS — matches existing repo convention (no RLS policies in repo today; agent + cron use service role and would bypass RLS regardless). Standard-care migration (not on the high-stakes table list). Additive. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-297.
- `027_messages_pending_commitment.sql` — adds `messages.pending_commitment jsonb` (nullable, no default) as the intent carrier that threads an agent-emitted commitment through the TAC-212 approval queue and materializes a `guest_commitments` row only on successful dispatch (TAC-297 design call #1 — "carry it as a jsonb on the draft row, not an in-memory map"). Validated at the boundary by `PendingCommitmentSchema` (`lib/schemas/guest-commitment.ts`). Write sites: `lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts` `buildOutboundInsert` / `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` (INSERT writes the carrier; regen UPDATE OVERWRITES wholesale so the carrier always reflects the current draft). Read site: `lib/operator/dispatch-operator-outbound.ts` extends its first SELECT to include the column, then after the second UPDATE stamps `status='sent'`, calls `createCommitmentFromPending` to materialize. Rejected / skipped draft → never dispatched → no row (per the "create on send, not at draft" invariant). **TAC-385 PR 1 adds a second read of the same shape**: `messages.rendered_intentions` (migration 045) comes back in the same SELECT, and a step 8 after the commitment materialization fires `recordIntentionPrompts` via `waitUntil` with the DISPATCHED body, so an approved or edited send records the ask the auto-send path already records. Fire-and-forget rather than awaited, unlike step 7: it is a Haiku call on the operator's approve tap, and the message has already gone out, so a recording failure must never turn a successful dispatch into a 502. The ungated rec path (auto-send via `scheduleAndSend`) writes the row inline post-dispatch rather than using the carrier — no queue gap to bridge. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`)** — per *Database migrations* §"Ordering for backwards-incompatible migrations": additive shape but the deployed code SELECTs the new column, so **apply in Studio BEFORE merging the PR**. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-297.
- `028_venue_configs_followup_rules.sql` — adds `venue_configs.followup_rules jsonb` (nullable at the storage layer for migration ordering safety; `parseFollowupRules` in `lib/schemas/followup-rules.ts` fills in defaults on null/missing). Backs the TAC-123 follow-up engine. Backfills a versioned default literal for every existing row in the same migration (kept in sync with `FOLLOWUP_RULES_DEFAULT` — round-tripped in `followup-rules.test.ts` to lock the invariant). Bumps `venue_configs.schema_version` from 1 → 2 (the column has existed since migration 001 — this is the first non-1 value). Pre-existing `venue_configs.messaging_cadence` stays the pure day-schedule (`{day_1, day_3, day_7, day_14}`); the new `followup_rules` carries everything else (gates, weekly cap, quiet hours, cron hour, per-reason kill switches, lapsed-eligible states, dedup windows). **Backwards-incompatible per the deploy-ordering rule** — the deployed code SELECTs the new column on read (`lib/followups/engine.ts`), so **apply in Studio BEFORE merging the PR**. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-123.
- `029_create_followup_log.sql` — creates `followup_log` table for TAC-123's per-reason dispatch audit + claim-before-side-effect idempotency. Columns: `id`, `venue_id`, `guest_id`, `reason` (CHECK-constrained closed enum matching `FOLLOWUP_REASONS` in `lib/schemas/followup-rules.ts`), `dedup_key text` (reason-appropriate: `day_N:<last_visit_at_iso>` / `cold:<last_visit_at_iso>` / `perk:<mechanic_id>` per `dedupKeyForReason`), nullable `message_id` (filled in by `finalizeFollowupLogClaim` post-dispatch — multi-reason runs share one message_id as the run correlator), `created_at`. Three indexes: UNIQUE on `(venue_id, guest_id, dedup_key)` (load-bearing — claim conflicts return `23505`); `(venue_id, guest_id, created_at desc)` (weekly-cap query); `(venue_id, guest_id, reason, created_at desc)` (per-reason last-dispatch lookup). Claim flow per engine run is: INSERT (`claimFollowupLogRows`) → handleFollowup → on `sent`/`queued` UPDATE message_id (`finalizeFollowupLogClaim`) → on `refused`/`failed` DELETE the claim (`releaseFollowupLogClaim`) so dedup isn't burned. Append-only — no `updated_at`. Additive but the deployed code INSERTs on engine tick, so **apply in Studio BEFORE merging the PR**. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit. TAC-123.

- `030_square_integration.sql` — **Backfilled by TAC-323** (previously landed undocumented; flagged for whoever next touched the Square integration). Builds on the mock-first `transactions` table from migration 001 (`guest_id` nullable-until-matched, `source` check already containing `'square'`/`'toast'` from day one) — adds the columns + tables real Square ingestion needs without redesigning the transactions model. Six pieces: (1) `transactions.card_fingerprint` — Square's stable, non-PCI card fingerprint, plus a partial index and two new `match_method` values (`card_fingerprint`, `tap_time_window`) alongside the migration-001 set. (2) `guest_card_fingerprints` — per-venue fingerprint→guest map (matches the `guests UNIQUE(venue_id, phone_number)` isolation decision); first tap writes the mapping, every later transaction with that fingerprint auto-resolves the guest with no tap required. (3) `pos_credentials` — OAuth-ready per-venue-per-provider Square/Toast/Clover credentials, tokens app-layer encrypted (`lib/pos/crypto.ts`), never plaintext. (4) `pos_catalog_items` + (5) `pos_inventory_counts` — mirror of the venue's Square catalog + best-effort inventory, refreshed on backfill and `catalog.version.updated` webhooks. (6) `pos_webhook_events` — dedupes Square webhook deliveries (which can repeat/arrive out of order) on the provider's `event_id`, covering non-transaction events the `transactions` unique constraint doesn't reach. Plus `pos_tap_events` (NFC tap opt-in: a tap opens iMessage with a server-issued `tap_token`; the reconciler matches a pending tap to a Square transaction by location + nearest timestamp) and `pos_devices` (the wifi-connected eink/Arduino display side, bearer-token-authenticated, storing only its sha256 hash). Additive only — standard-care migration, not on the high-stakes table list; the companion Square webhook handler is the payment-adjacent high-stakes surface, flagged separately. No Linear ticket on record for the original migration.
- `031_venue_hold_all_outbound.sql` — adds `venues.hold_all_outbound boolean not null default false`. Per-venue blanket hold: when true, `applyApprovalPolicyStage` fires the `hold_all_outbound` trigger (#6) and queues every guest-facing content message for operator review, EXCEPT `opt_out` confirmations (TCPA/carrier compliance — those must auto-send). Read by `build-runtime-context.ts` into `VenueContext.holdAllOutbound`; ranked last in `PRIMARY_TRIGGER_PRIORITY`. **No UI, no API, no seed default writes this column** — every `from('venues')` call in the repo is a `.select()`. Flip it by hand in Studio (template in Common gotchas). Additive; existing rows covered by the DEFAULT. Shipped in commit `0c1515c` with `TAC-XXX` placeholders and no Linear ticket — the placeholders remain in `lib/agent/stages.ts`, `lib/operator/queue.ts`, and the migration header.

- `032_message_splitting_generation_id.sql` — TAC-313 message splitting. Adds `messages.generation_id uuid` (nullable) + partial index `idx_messages_generation_id WHERE generation_id IS NOT NULL` — the key that groups the rows of one agent response when a reply is dispatched as multiple Sendblue messages. **One row per bubble is forced by evidence:** the Sendblue status webhook looks up rows by `provider_message_id` and each send returns its own handle, so one row for two bubbles leaves the second bubble's delivery status with nowhere to land. Minted fresh per dispatch in `scheduleAndSend` — deliberately NOT `agentRunId`, since the Langfuse link already lives on `langfuse_trace_id` and coupling them would silently merge two responses into one group if a run ever dispatched twice. Plain indexed uuid, NOT a self-FK: a self-FK leaves the first bubble null and forces a null special case at every read site. **Drops `messages.parent_draft_id`** (dead since migration 001 — indexed self-FK, zero readers, zero writers) so it can't sit beside `generation_id` as a second plausible grouping column; not reused either, because a column named `parent_draft_id` holding generation grouping is a naming lie. **NO BACKFILL, deliberately** — every consumer uses the grouping identity `coalesce(generation_id, id)`, so legacy rows, inbound rows, single-bubble replies and queue drafts are each their own group via their own PK. That expression is what makes the three call sites (the two functions below and the TS projection in `build-runtime-context.ts`) agree by construction rather than by discipline. Adds `count_outbound_responses(p_venue_id, p_guest_id)` — `count(distinct coalesce(generation_id, id))` over sent/delivered outbound, consumed by `lib/recognition/load-signals.ts`; without it splitting inflates the `responseRate = replied / sent` **denominator only** (weight 0.10 in `computeRelationshipStrength`) and guests drift downward out of `regular` with no change in behavior. Replaces `list_operator_queue` (migration 018) — only the `ctx` lateral changes: `recent_context` now groups by response and returns the 3 most recent RESPONSES, **merged into ≤3 entries** (id = first bubble's real id, body = bubbles space-joined + `btrim`, createdAt = `min(created_at)`) rather than returning up to 9 bubble rows, because `QueueRecentContextEntry` is a cross-repo shape and widening its cardinality while multi-bubble card rendering is out of scope would be a silent Contract change. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`).** Contains a `DROP COLUMN` but applies in Studio **BEFORE** merge per the inert-drop exception documented above under §"Ordering for backwards-incompatible migrations". db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. TAC-313.

- `034_guest_enrollment_and_self_reported_orders.sql` — TAC-323: guest enrollment via a static QR sign (at pickup, not the register — so there's no transaction to check the guest into) plus self-reported orders (the guest texts back what they got; the venue has no POS record to attach it to). Three changes: (1) `transactions.source` check gains `'guest_reported'`. (2) `transactions.amount_cents` becomes nullable — reserved for the one case where a resolved item has no price in `venue_info`; the WHOLE order's amount is null rather than a partial sum, because a partial sum looks complete and isn't. Every `amount_cents` consumer was audited for this null path in the same PR: `lib/recognition/load-signals.ts` (`totalSpentCents += amount_cents ?? 0` — SUM-ignores-NULL semantics, not a claim the order cost $0), the admin conversations page (`pricedVisitCount90d` excludes null-amount rows from the avg-per-visit denominator so one unpriced order can't drag the average down, while still counting as a visit), and `transaction-row.tsx` (renders a muted "—", never a fabricated "$0.00"). (3) `guests.created_via` gains `'qr_scan'`, same drop-and-recreate pattern as migration 006. Plus one new partial unique index, `idx_transactions_one_guest_reported_per_guest` on `(venue_id, guest_id) where source='guest_reported'` — closes a TOCTOU race between two near-simultaneous inbounds from the same guest each independently naming a menu item, same rationale and shape as migration 020's `idx_messages_one_pending_per_guest` (TAC-264); a `23505` on insert is treated the same as the ordinary "already reported" outcome (log, no throw, no red alert). **No new `venue_configs` column** — the QR sign's expected prefilled-message string lives as a new optional `qrEnrollmentMessage` field on the EXISTING `venue_info` jsonb (`lib/schemas/venue-info.ts`), not a new column; a bare new column was the original draft and was pushed back on during plan review as an unscoped schema change outside the repo's per-venue-config-with-a-Zod-schema convention. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `transactions` and `guests`)** — additive shape (per CLAUDE.md's own §Ordering, "order doesn't matter" for new enum values + a relaxed NOT NULL + a new index), so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merging this PR — a deviation from this ticket's original "deploy code first" call, which only reasoned about the qr_scan half (gated by the sign not being printed yet); the self-reported-orders half has no such gate, so applying after merge would open a real window where an existing guest's self-report hits the pre-migration constraints and is silently, permanently lost. See the migration file's own header for the full reasoning. Constraint names (`transactions_source_check`, `guests_created_via_check`) verified against this migration's own history, not assumed — re-verify against live `pg_constraint` immediately before applying in Studio per the standing caution on this table. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit (`transactions.amount_cents` → `number | null`) until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply.

- `035_guest_intention_prompts.sql` — TAC-324: first-touch intentions. Creates `guest_intention_prompts` (id, venue_id, guest_id, intention_key, message_id nullable + ON DELETE SET NULL, prompted_at, created_at) with a UNIQUE constraint on `(guest_id, intention_key)` — the storage-layer cap: one prompt per intention per guest, ever, enforced by the database rather than application logic. Satisfaction is deliberately NOT stored here (derived live from `transactions` at context-build time — see `lib/agent/intentions/derive.ts`); this table exists only because "Sana asked and the guest changed the subject" leaves no trace anywhere else. Deliberately NOT `engagement_events` — that table feeds `ENGAGEMENT_EVENT_WEIGHTS`, and raising a topic is not a guest action that should move the recognition formula. Additive, new table, not on the standing high-stakes list, but per the `guest_commitments` (026) / `followup_log` (029) precedent, deployed code both SELECTs and INSERTs into it on the very next qr_scan guest's inbound after deploy, so apply in Studio BEFORE merging the PR, same reasoning as those two. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply.

- `036_visit_time_precision.sql` — TAC-377: two nullable `text` columns with CHECK constraints, `transactions.occurred_at_precision` and `guests.last_visit_precision`, both `'pinned' | 'approximate'`. Backs self-reported visits advancing `guests.last_visit_at` — before this, that column was written ONLY by the two Square paths, so at a venue with no POS integration every guest had `last_visit_at = null`, `detectPostVisitReason` and `detectColdLapsedReason` both returned null forever, and the hourly follow-up cron ran, scanned, reported success, and could not fire. **`transactions.occurred_at_precision` has NO reader in app code today** — it is written by `extract-reported-order.ts` and read by nothing; `guests.last_visit_precision` is written independently in the same function rather than derived from it. Stated rather than left to be discovered, per the `approval_policy`-went-102-days-unread lesson: it is per-transaction provenance kept so a future visit-history surface (or a backfill of `last_visit_precision` after a `guest_reported` row is deleted) has something to read. **No backfill, deliberately** — null means "nobody recorded a precision," which is honestly different from either value, and the detector reads null as PERMISSIVE (see the followup-engine section). Both are NEW names on NEW columns, so unlike migration 034 there is no drop-and-recreate and no constraint-name drift to verify — `add constraint` fails loudly if a name somehow already exists. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `transactions`)** — additive shape, but deployed code SELECTs both columns, so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merging the PR, same call 034 made on this table. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply.

- `037_guest_commitments_open_dedup.sql` — TAC-318: partial unique index `guest_commitments_open_dedup` on `(venue_id, guest_id, lower(trim(both from description))) WHERE status = 'open'`. At most one OPEN commitment per guest per description. **Applied in Studio ahead of the migration file, together with a hand-collapse of the existing duplicates** (fleet 9 → 7 rows; Le Mil's left with one Blossom Tonic and one SoFi) — the file is the repo-side record, since `db/migrations` is the single source of DB truth. Three things about the key are decisions, not defaults. **`type` is deliberately NOT in it**: two open promises with the same description are the same promise to the guest whatever the agent labelled them, and a type-scoped key would let one turn's `recommendation: cortado` sit beside a later turn's `comp: cortado` as two live entries in the same prompt block. **`lower(trim(...))` is a FUNCTIONAL index expression**, mirrored in JS by `commitmentDedupKey` (`lib/guests/commitments.ts`) — the two must move together; they are not byte-identical (Postgres `trim(both from)` strips spaces only, JS `.trim()` strips all whitespace) and do not need to be, because both layers are present and each divergence direction degrades safely. **`WHERE status = 'open'` excludes `pending_ack`**, deliberately narrower than the `## Active commitments` block's own open+pending_ack filter: once the guest has signalled arrival the row is mid-flight against a specific visit, so a later promise is genuinely new. Terminal states sit outside the index, so in principle a comp redeemed in March can be promised again in June — **but do not read that as a live escape valve, because two of those states have no writer.** Nothing in this repo ever sets `guest_commitments.status` to `'expired'` or `'redeemed'` (enum values in the schema, zero writers), and `guest_commitments.expires_at` is write-only — set on insert, read by nothing. **SUPERSEDED IN PART BY TAC-341 (migration 038): `expired` now has a writer and `expires_at` now has both a server-side derivation and a reader.** `redeemed` still has none — it ships with its caller in TAC-378. Read the two paragraphs that follow as history: for OBLIGATIONS (comp/hold/discount) a row now does leave `open`, so the dedup on that description is no longer permanent. For `recommendation` every word of them still holds, because TAC-341 deliberately excluded that type — TAC-380 owns it. The only transitions that exist are `open → pending_ack` and `pending_ack → acknowledged | cancelled`, all requiring the guest to signal arrival. **Consequence, which is a product fact rather than a doc nit: for `recommendation` — the whole population this ticket is about, the type that never gates, never carries a code, and that guests rarely signal arrival against — a row enters `open` and effectively never leaves, so the dedup on that description is PERMANENT for the life of the guest's relationship with that venue.** A guest recommended the Blossom Tonic in September can never be recommended it again, and the block renders it as "promised 6 months ago" indefinitely. Right for this ticket's brief, wrong as a steady state; TAC-341 builds the `expired`/`redeemed` writers and sets the horizons, which is what turns the first sentence from principle into fact. Caught in code review, not in the plan. **The app-level check is the PRIMARY enforcement, not this index** — see the runtime-contract entry. Additive (index only), no column, no backfill; indexes don't surface in generated types so `db/types.ts` is unchanged. Standard-care — `guest_commitments` is not on the high-stakes table list. Ordering doesn't matter: deployed code tolerates the index's absence (app check alone) and its presence (the 23505 branch).
- `038_guest_commitments_escalated_at.sql` — TAC-341: one nullable `escalated_at timestamptz` on `guest_commitments`, plus partial index `idx_guest_commitments_expiry` on `(expires_at) WHERE status='open' AND expires_at IS NOT NULL` (sibling of 026's `idx_guest_commitments_due`, different column — that one serves the arrival cron's `expected_arrival` scan, this one the lifecycle cron's). **The status enum is UNCHANGED and that is the point**: migration 026 already permitted all six of `open`/`pending_ack`/`acknowledged`/`redeemed`/`expired`/`cancelled`, and `expired` had simply never been written by anything. The ticket's own AC said "no migration was required" and that was **right about the enum and wrong about escalation** — escalation needs durable state or it is not idempotent, and the hourly cron would re-fire the same Slack alert on every tick for the whole window between escalation and expiry. **The original figure was ~17,000 alerts for one comp, computed against the 2-year horizon this ticket shipped with; the horizon became 60 days the same day, so the live number is ~1,272** (53 open days past the 7-day window, hourly). Recomputed rather than left standing, because a stale justification number is how a decision stops being re-derivable — and the conclusion is unchanged at either figure. That does not make the alert noisy, it makes it worthless, which is the `comp_regex_backstop` illusion in a new costume. **`escalated_at` is an IDEMPOTENCY MARKER, not an audit trail** — it answers exactly one question (has a human been told) and the REASON rides on the `commitment_escalated` PostHog event instead; resisting `escalated_reason`/`escalation_count` columns is what keeps it honest about its one job. **Deliberately NOT a seventh status**: `'escalated'` would have taken the row OUT of `'open'`, which is the filter every other consumer keys on (`findActiveCommitmentsForGuest`, the `## Active commitments` block, 037's open-dedup index) — an escalated commitment is still open and still owed, so escalation is a notification fact about the row, not a lifecycle state of the promise. The index is deliberately NOT filtered on `type`: the scan restricts to obligations in app code, but baking a `recommendation` exclusion into the index would need a migration the day TAC-380 changes what a recommendation is. Additive (one nullable column, no default, no backfill, no constraint change) but deployed code SELECTs `escalated_at`, so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merge per §Ordering — same call 025/026/034/035/036 made. Standard care; `guest_commitments` is not on the high-stakes table list. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. **Companion backfill is SEPARATE SQL, applied by hand** — see the template under Common gotchas.

- `039_operator_queue_review_detail.sql` — TAC-364: two nullable `text[]` columns on `messages` (`review_triggers`, `ungrounded_claims`) plus a recreate of `list_operator_queue` returning both. `review_reason` keeps holding the priority-selected primary; `review_triggers` holds the full set the gate fired, so a comp commitment that was ALSO below the auto-send fidelity floor no longer reaches the operator labelled only "Commitment requires approval" with the second condition unrecoverable. `ungrounded_claims` carries the grounding verifier's verbatim flagged claims — **three-state, deliberately: a non-empty array is what it flagged, `[]` means the check RAN and found nothing, NULL means it DID NOT RUN** (followup, demo guest, model self-reported a gap, or a truncated unreadable verdict). That split is not incidental: TAC-367 was filed because a silently-skipped grounding check was invisible everywhere, and conflating the two would have rebuilt that blind spot in a new column on its first day. `review_triggers` needs no equivalent split — the gate returns `send` when nothing fires, so a queued draft's trigger set is never empty, and there NULL means only "no gate ran for this row". One caveat inherited from TAC-367 rather than introduced here: a TRANSIENT verifier fault fails open and records as `[]`, not NULL. The claims were previously computed, sent to PostHog/Langfuse and then discarded at exactly the point they became useful — TAC-301 part 1.5 stopped blanking the body on a backstop catch, which made a one-swipe bad approval possible for the first time, and the operator had no way to know which sentence was the suspect one. `messages.response_review` was the no-migration alternative and was rejected: that column means "a human reviewed this" and `getReviewedVia` plus the cc-review/mobile-operator consumers read it on that basis. **NO BACKFILL, deliberately** — a null `review_triggers` beside a non-null `review_reason` means "recorded before this shipped" and renders as today; synthesizing a one-element array from `review_reason` would claim the primary was the ONLY trigger, which is the thing the column exists to stop assuming. **The RPC is DROPPED and recreated, not `create or replace`d** — see the gotcha below. The whole file runs in ONE transaction so no reader observes a window where the function is missing. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`)** — additive shape, but deployed code SELECTs both columns through the RPC, so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merging the PR, same call 025/026/034/035/036/038 made. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply.

- `040_intention_state_rows.sql` — TAC-380: `guest_intention_prompts` becomes one STATE row per (guest, intention). `prompted_at` drops NOT NULL (null = eligible, not yet raised) but **KEEPS its `DEFAULT now()`** — the deployed pre-TAC-380 recorder omits the column and relies on the default, so dropping it before deploy would store every window-period prompt as null, which new code reads as unasked and re-asks. Drop it in a follow-up migration after deploy (TAC-392). Adds `eligible_at timestamptz` (the expiry anchor) and `prompt_source text CHECK in ('classified','pessimistic')` (so the brake can exclude closures that asked nothing), plus `venue_configs.intention_rules jsonb` (nullable; `parseIntentionRules` fails open to defaults). Backfill: prompted rows get `eligible_at = prompted_at` and `prompt_source = 'classified'`; `learn_first_order` renames to `understand_order`, carrying an old prompt onto a new eligibility row before deleting so the unique constraint can't abort a post-deploy re-run; `invite_contact_save` is deliberately NOT migrated (ruling 5) — the Command Center renders its orphan as unrecognized. As of 2026-09-14 that orphan is the only row fleet-wide, so the rename matches nothing today.
