Additive, but deployed code SELECTs the new columns, so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merge, then **re-run the backfill block (between its markers) AFTER deploy** to catch rows the old code writes in the window. Not on the high-stakes table list. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. The traps this migration creates are under Common gotchas.

- `041_two_pending_slots_per_guest.sql` — TAC-394, option F: replaces migration 020's single partial unique index with two, so a guest holds at most one pending draft PER SLOT. `idx_messages_one_pending_obligation_per_guest` covers pending rows whose `pending_commitment->>'type'` is `comp`, `hold` or `discount`; `idx_messages_one_pending_conversation_per_guest` covers every other pending row (no carrier, a recommendation, a blank knowledge-gap card). **Why:** on 2026-09-14 a pending comp draft was regenerated in place by the reply to the guest's next question, because 020 allowed one pending row and TAC-264 regenerates that row. Three choices in the file are load-bearing. **Create before drop, in one transaction**, so `messages` is never without a uniqueness constraint on pending rows; both new indexes build while 020 exists, because 020 is strictly stronger. **Not `CONCURRENTLY`**, which cannot run inside a transaction; the one-transaction swap matters more than a write lock lasting milliseconds at pilot size. **`coalesce(pending_commitment->>'type', '')`** in both predicates, because without it a NULL carrier evaluates `not in (...)` to NULL and the row matches NEITHER index. The SQL type list and `OBLIGATION_TYPES` (`lib/guests/commitment-expiry.ts`) move together, and `pending-slots.test.ts` reads this file to enforce that. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`).** Apply in Studio **BEFORE** merge, outside Le Mil's hours (7am to 3pm America/Los_Angeles): run the header's three read-only pre-checks, apply 041 then 042, merge within about two minutes, and run the header's two-card detection query once both production deploys are ready. **Roll back only when BOTH hold:** the new code is NOT live 30 minutes after applying, AND the detection query returns no rows. If it returns rows, fix forward: recreating 020 would need every guest collapsed to one pending card by hand, and the rollback transaction aborts harmlessly on the duplicate anyway. Indexes don't surface in generated types, so this file needs no `db/types.ts` change.
- `042_operator_queue_other_pending.sql` — TAC-394: drops and recreates `list_operator_queue` in one transaction (a new return column is a return-type change; see migration 039 and its gotcha), restating 039's body with two changes. It adds `other_pending_for_guest integer`, the count of OTHER pending drafts for the same venue and guest (0 or 1 under 041), projected as `QueueDraft.otherPendingDraftsForGuest`. And `recent_context` now excludes every pending row, not just the card's own, so a comp card's context never shows the conversation card's unsent draft as history (`id <> m.id` stays, for readability). **HIGH-STAKES (reads `messages`).** Apply in Studio **BEFORE** merge, immediately after 041; it can stay if 041 is rolled back, since old code ignores the extra column. Verify the live function definition before dropping, per the header. `db/types.ts` hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply.
- `043_operator_conversations_reached_guest.sql` — TAC-395: `create or replace` of `list_operator_conversations` (migration 033), same signature and columns, so no `db/types.ts` change. A guest's fields now come only from messages that reached them (inbound, or outbound whose `review_state` is not pending and whose `status` is `sending`, `sent` or `delivered`), so an unsent draft can no longer be the last-message preview. A guest with no such message stays in the list (TAC-395 rulings, 21:44 and 21:55): their fields are computed from all their non-empty messages as before, and `last_message_body` is `''`. Membership, ordering and the 200-row cap are unchanged, and `body <> ''` stays where 033 had it. **HIGH-STAKES (reads `messages`).** Body-only, so it can be applied before or after merge; apply back to back with 044 and check the live definition with `pg_get_functiondef` first. `lib/operator/reached-guest-condition.test.ts` binds its status list to `DELIVERED_OUTBOUND_STATUSES`.
- `044_operator_queue_context_reached_guest.sql` — TAC-395: `create or replace` of `list_operator_queue` (migration 042), restating 042's body. The `recent_context` lateral replaces `review_state is distinct from 'pending'` with the full reached-guest condition, applied per row before grouping and before the 3-response limit, so skipped drafts and replies that never sent leave a card's context too. **No `body <> ''`, deliberately** (22:08 ruling): `recent_context` has always returned empty-body entries, and a photo-only text is context the operator needs; the binding test fails if a body filter is added. `recent_context` is already `jsonb` and no column is added, so the return type is unchanged and no DROP is needed. **HIGH-STAKES (reads `messages`).** Body-only; apply back to back with 043. It narrows what TAC-394's Contract says `recentContext` excludes; the entry shape is unchanged.
- `045_messages_rendered_intentions.sql` — TAC-385 PR 1: one nullable `jsonb` column on `messages`, `rendered_intentions`, holding the intentions RENDERED into the prompt that produced a draft as `{"key", "eligibleAt"}` entries. It is what lets `dispatchOperatorOutbound` record the ask when an operator approves or edits a card — that path recorded nothing, 13 of 34 sent replies at Le Mil's in the 30 days to 2026-09-14, and TAC-380 made it seven intentions per guest rather than two. Shaped like `pending_commitment` (027): a jsonb intent carrier on the draft row, read back at dispatch, camelCase inside the payload. **`eligibleAt` is stored rather than looked up at dispatch, and that is the load-bearing part** — `closeIntentions` guards the two event-armed intentions' stamp on `eligible_at <= anchor` AND `prompted_at IS NULL OR prompted_at < anchor`, and those two re-arm on a newer recommendation or order, so reading the guest's CURRENT anchor after a draft has sat in the queue would stamp a prompt from the OLD arming onto the NEW one. Re-deriving the whole set at dispatch is wrong for the same family of reasons (TAC-380 trap 4). NULL means "nothing rendered, or the row predates this" — both read as "record nothing" — and a blank knowledge-gap card is written NULL deliberately (ruled 2026-09-15): its dispatched text is entirely operator-authored. **NO BACKFILL, NO INDEX** (the only read is by primary key). **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`)** — additive, but deployed code SELECTs it, so apply in Studio **BEFORE** merging, same call 025/026/027/034/035/036/038/039 made. db/types.ts hand-patched in the same commit until `npm run db:types` runs post-apply. **PR 1 does NOT change when an intention closes** — raising still closes; the asked/closed split is PR 2.
- `046_messages_status_drop_dead_default.sql` — TAC-416: drops the default on `messages.status`, which was unreachable. The column has been `not null default 'pending'` since migration 001 while `messages_status_check` has never permitted `'pending'` — both halves sit in the SAME statement in 001, on adjacent lines (318-328) — so any insert relying on the default produced a value the check immediately rejected. **Not a typo: `default 'pending'` is 001's house convention for a status column** (`venues.status` line 35, `venue_configs.onboarding_status` line 147, later `pos_tap_events.status` in 030 — all three list `'pending'` among their permitted values and are correct); `messages` copied the default and then wrote a message-lifecycle value list starting at `'received'`/`'draft'` that never included the generic opening state. **Nothing read it**, necessarily — an insert relying on it would already fail — verified across the three insert sites and the whole source tree, where every other `'pending'` literal belongs to `pos_tap_events.status`, `venues.status`, or `review_state`, each of whose constraints does permit it. **No backfill**, and the durable claim needs no measurement — the check has forbidden `'pending'` since the table was created, so no row can ever have carried it. (A shape-only snapshot at 2026-09-16T00:45Z: 707 rows across `received`/`delivered`/`sent`/`pending_review`. Deliberately not pinned as a fixed total in the verification query — this is a live table that gained six rows in the ten minutes between two readings while the migration was written, so an exact count would be stale before it was run, which is the same false-specification failure the migration exists to remove. The query asserts `count(*) where status='pending' = 0` instead.) `drop default` is catalogue-only: no row rewrite, instant at any size. No constraint change (`messages_status_check` keeps its name and all nine values), and no runtime behaviour change. **But `db/types.ts` IS affected, and a first draft of this entry claimed the opposite** — Supabase marks a column optional in the generated `Insert` type when it has a default, so `messages.status` is `status?: string` in `Insert` TODAY *because of* the dead default, and dropping it makes that field REQUIRED on the next `npm run db:types`. Measured, not predicted: hand-patching the type to `status: string` and running `tsc` yields EXACTLY ONE error repo-wide — `lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts(242,3)`, the return of `buildOutboundInsert`, whose base object omits `status` and relies on `...overrides` (`Partial<MessageInsert>`, every field optional) to supply it. Both callers do supply it at runtime, so it is a type-level gap only. **Applying the migration is safe alone; `npm run db:types` is the step that turns it into a red CI.** **The one-line repair ships in the same PR** — the single deviation from TAC-416's migration-only scope, taken because step 3 of this file's own migration workflow is "Operator runs `npm run db:types`", so the DOCUMENTED next action after applying 046 is the one that reddens CI, and deferring would arm a landmine in a file the follow-up PR never mentions. `buildOutboundInsert`'s `overrides` becomes `Partial<MessageInsert> & Required<Pick<MessageInsert, 'status'>>`, and `db/types.ts:806` is hand-patched to `status: string` per this file's own hand-patch convention. **`Required<Pick<...>>`, not a bare `Pick<...>`: `Pick` PRESERVES optionality**, so a bare Pick is inert until the types flip and a caller could drop `status` meanwhile. Mutation-verified in both schema states — deleting `status: 'sent'` from the auto-send caller fails TS2345 at `schedule-and-send.ts(522,44)` against today's types and against the regenerated ones. Zero runtime change: both callers already pass `status`. **Deliberately dropped rather than corrected**: inbound rows are `received` and drafts are `draft`/`pending_review`, so no single value covers both and picking either would make the wrong one a silent fallback for the other path. After this, an insert omitting `status` fails on NOT NULL instead of on the check — both fail, but the new failure names the actual problem. **Rollback restores a DEAD default** and is only for undoing this migration cleanly. **Ordering doesn't matter** — no deployed read path references a column default, so neither direction opens a window. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `messages`).** The cost being paid here is not a runtime bug: the schema asserted something false and was believed — during TAC-411 the dead default was cited as justification for making `status` optional in a Zod schema, and that reasoning reached a code comment and a CLAUDE.md entry before review caught it. **A schema-wide sweep found this is the only instance** — see the gotcha below.

- `047_ongoing_order_capture.sql` — TAC-325: widens `transactions_source_check` (same drop-and-recreate pattern as migration 034's own change to the identical constraint) to add `guest_reported_ongoing` — a guest's self-reported order AFTER enrollment either already happened or its 7-day window closed. A disjoint `source` value, not a reuse of `guest_reported`, so enrollment's own storage-layer invariant (`idx_transactions_one_guest_reported_per_guest`, migration 034) never sees an ongoing row and a guest can still only ever enroll once. No new index — the same-venue-local-day merge/dedupe is app-level (`lib/agent/extract-reported-order.ts`), since "same calendar day" needs the venue's timezone, which isn't a column on `transactions`. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `transactions`).** Additive per CLAUDE.md's own Ordering rule, but apply in Studio **BEFORE** merging anyway, mirroring migration 034's identical deviation and for the identical reason: the extractor never throws, so a pre-migration CHECK rejection is caught and logged, not crashed — but the guest's report is silently and permanently lost with no retry. No `db/types.ts` change (`source` is a plain `string` there; CHECK values don't surface in generated types).
- `048_instagram_identity_and_message_channel.sql` — TAC-467: the schema an Instagram guest needs, and nothing that writes it (TAC-468 does). Six changes, one transaction. (1) `guests.phone_number` drops NOT NULL; `guests_phone_number_check` and `UNIQUE (venue_id, phone_number)` stay as they are, because a CHECK passes on NULL and NULLs are distinct under a unique constraint. (2) `guests.instagram_scoped_id` (the IGSID), with a non-blank CHECK and **`UNIQUE (venue_id, instagram_scoped_id)` — per venue, not global, deliberately** (the ticket first said global): an IGSID is scoped to one app-and-account pair, so it is not a person identifier. While `venues.instagram_account_id` is globally unique (change 6), an IGSID can only reach the one venue that owns its account, so per venue and global behave the same today. Per venue is chosen because it mirrors `UNIQUE (venue_id, phone_number)` and stays correct if an account is ever allowed to map to more than one venue row. **A first version of this rationale said two venue rows could share one account, which change 6 in the same migration rules out; code review caught it before the migration was applied.** A table constraint rather than a partial index, so a handler can upsert with `onConflict: 'venue_id,instagram_scoped_id'` (PostgREST cannot target a partial index). No format check: samples are 16-digit strings, but Meta documents no format, and a rejected insert on the live webhook path loses the guest's message. (3) `guests_must_have_identity` CHECK: a phone or an IGSID, at least one. (4) `messages.channel text not null default 'text'` with CHECK `in ('text', 'instagram')`. `'text'` is iMessage or SMS through the phone-number provider; not `'sms'` (most are iMessage) and not a vendor name. **The default is a known hazard kept on purpose** — see the gotcha under Common gotchas. (5) `messages.referral_ref` and `messages.referral_source`, nullable, no CHECK: the `ref` and `source` an ig.me link carries, delivered once with the guest's first action and unrecoverable afterwards, stored on the message they arrived with. `type` is not stored (`OPEN_THREAD` is the only value seen, and it follows from a ref being present). (6) `venues.instagram_account_id`, globally unique: maps an inbound event's `entry.id` / `recipient.id` to a venue; without it TAC-468 cannot route. No backfill, no new index beyond the two unique constraints'. **HIGH-STAKES (touches `guests` and `messages`).** It takes every lock up front, `messages` first, under a 5-second `lock_timeout`: altering `guests` first could deadlock with a Sendblue inbound, which holds `messages` and then needs `guests` for its foreign-key check, and the webhook answers 200 on a failed insert, so the guest's text would be lost with no retry. A busy table makes the migration fail cleanly instead; apply outside Le Mil's hours regardless. Order-independent against the deploy (it relaxes one constraint and adds everything else, and no deployed code writes a null phone or reads a new column), **but do not run `npm run db:types` on a `main` without the PR**: regenerated types make the phone nullable and `tsc` fails at the nine sites the PR fixes. `db/types.ts` hand-patched in the same commit. The rollback in the file's footer fails once a phoneless guest exists, by design.

RLS policies will be added in a future migration before any external user gets DB access (THE-110).

---

## Scripts

One-off scripts live in `scripts/` and run via `tsx` with env loading from `.env.local`. To add a new script: drop the file in `scripts/` (CLI orchestrator) and `scripts/onboarding/<name>.ts` (helpers, if it's part of the onboarding pipeline), add a `package.json` entry of the form `"<name>": "tsx --env-file=.env.local scripts/<file>.ts"`, run with `npm run <name> -- <args>`.

### Available scripts

- `npm run send-test -- <phone> [body]` — sends a test message via the messaging module to the given E.164 phone number. Requires `TEST_VENUE_ID` in `.env.local` pointing to a venue row that has `messaging_phone_number` set.
- `npm run extract-venue-spec -- <slug> [--dry-run] [--force] [--interview-date YYYY-MM-DD]` — reads the venue's onboarding transcript + Airtable record + menu CSV from Drive, calls Sonnet to extract a structured venue spec, runs the TAC-346 verification pass (`scripts/onboarding/verify.ts`) against the draft, appends the result as a `## Needs confirmation` section, and writes the combined markdown as `06-{slug}-venue-spec-draft.md` to the venue's Drive folder. **Refuses to run if a `06-` file already exists in the venue folder** (TAC-346, mirrors `seed-venue`'s guard) — pass `--force` to overwrite anyway. `--dry-run` (TAC-331) is exempt from that guard (its purpose is testing against a venue that already has a live 06-file without touching it) and also skips only the Drive write — transcript/menu/Airtable reads still hit real data — saving the draft under `os.tmpdir()` instead and logging the path. `--interview-date` (TAC-346) sets the explicit anchor for resolving relative dates in the transcript ("next month", "this Saturday"); omit it to fall back to inferring a submission date from the Airtable record. **If verification fails after one retry, nothing is written anywhere (Drive or disk) and the process exits 1** — an existing `06-` file is left untouched, and every `06-` file that does land in Drive has passed verification.
- `npm run extract-test-scenarios -- <slug> [--regenerate]` — writes `07-{slug}-test-scenarios.json` to Drive from two independent sources merged into one unified `Scenario` shape (`scripts/onboarding/scenario-schema.ts`, TAC-347 Stage 1): (1) the existing markdown+LLM path (THE-180) — reads the venue spec + the 17-category fixture, calls Sonnet, generates `scenario_source: 'behavior'` rows only (mechanics-derived generation moved out of this path, see next); (2) a new DB-driven path (`scripts/onboarding/generate-db-scenarios.ts`) that reads `knowledge_corpus`, `venue_configs.venue_info`, and `mechanics` directly and generates `knowledge` / `fact` / `menu` / `unanswerable` / `mechanic` / `routing` scenarios with no LLM call — deterministic, content-stable sample ids (`knowledge:{corpus_id}`, `mechanic:{mechanic_id}:{state}`, ...), and an `expected_route` per scenario for the Stage 3 routing grader (not built yet). Each scenario also carries `expected_facts`/`forbidden_claims`/`source_row_ids` for the Stage 3 knowledge grader. `--regenerate` (accepts `--force` as an alias) overwrites an existing 07-file; coverage gaps (DB rows with no covering scenario) print as a warning. Register variety for the DB-driven sources ("terse, typos, emoji-heavy, long") comes from fixed deterministic transforms in `scripts/onboarding/register-variants.ts`, not an LLM call.
- `npm run seed-venue -- <slug> [--messaging-phone <e164>] [--force]` — reads the 06-spec markdown from Drive, parses it via Zod schemas, ingests into the database (venue, venue_configs, mechanics, voice_corpus + embeddings via Voyage). Optional `--messaging-phone` sets `venues.messaging_phone_number` at seed time. First-write-only: hard-refuses when a venue with the slug already exists, across every store it writes, not only menu items. `--force` (TAC-343 Phase 0b, narrowed on plan review) deletes and rewrites CONFIG STORES ONLY — `venue_configs`, `mechanics`, `voice_corpus`+embeddings, `knowledge_corpus`+embeddings — reusing the existing venue's id; it never touches the `venues` row or any guest-relationship table, and refuses outright, even with the flag, if the venue has any `guests` or `messages` rows (re-seeding a venue with real guest history is not a supported workflow — that venue is edited on its config page instead). Logs exactly what it's about to delete first. Nothing in this repo calls `--force`; it exists so a deliberate wipe-and-reseed of a mock venue during development doesn't require a hand-run SQL `DELETE` first. CLI entry point is `scripts/seed-venue.ts`; the heavy lifting lives in `scripts/onboarding/seed-supabase.ts`.
- `npm run run-test-scenarios -- <slug> [--sample-ids id1,id2,...] [--topic t1,t2] [--category c1,c2] [--mode graded|exploratory] [--limit N] [--concurrency N] [--max-cost USD] [--owner-review]` — seeds the four synthetic guests, runs the selected scenarios from the 07-sheet's Scenarios tab through the real agent pipeline decision-only (no Sendblue, no human-feel delay, no fidelity gate), grades each via Stage 3 (deterministic voice + LLM knowledge/voice/routing), and writes the results as a fresh timestamped `Report {date} {time}` / `Run {date} {time}` tab pair on the same 07-sheet — **TAC-347 Stage 4**: replaced a single reused `Report`/`Run` tab per sheet, which meant every run silently destroyed the previous run's result set (a 455-scenario run was lost this way); the 10 most recent tabs of each name are kept, older ones pruned after the new tab writes (`scripts/onboarding/tab-retention.ts` + `pruneTabsByPrefix` in `drive.ts`). The selection flags (`--sample-ids`/`--topic`/`--category`/`--mode`/`--limit`) narrow which scenarios run. `--owner-review` (also TAC-347 Stage 4) is mutually exclusive with those — it selects its own diverse ~30-scenario pool instead (`scripts/onboarding/owner-review-selection.ts`: excludes adversarial/safety-critical/unanswerable content, weights toward greeting/recommendation/menu-question/complaint/perk-request situations spread across guest states, filters to scenarios that passed knowledge+routing grading and produced real deliverable text) and additionally writes a separate `08-{slug}-response-review` gsheet in the exact shape `ingest-response-review`'s `parseReviewSheet` expects (`scripts/onboarding/owner-review-sheet.ts`), so `ingest-response-review` works against it unchanged. Throws after logging all four synthetic-guest tuning outcomes if any state landed in the wrong band. **TAC-367: `grounding_check_failed` can surface as a routing-grade FAIL.** The harness calls the real `verifyGroundingStage`, so a truncated verdict flips that scenario's `route` from `send` to `queue` and `grade-routing.ts` grades it as a routing defect. It isn't one — it is infrastructure, and at `--concurrency` the 429s that cause it are the harness's own doing. Same applies to the Slack relay on `grounding_verifier_unavailable`, which fires once per degraded call across a 455-scenario run. Read a `grounding_check_failed` row as "the check didn't complete", not as a routing bug. **Run it during the venue's open hours (TAC-301).** `## Right now` now carries an open/closed status line, so a run executed after close injects `CLOSED right now. Do not tell the guest to come by now...` into every scenario — including the `--owner-review` pool that becomes the 08-sheet an owner signs off on. Results are not comparable run-to-run by `sample_id` unless both runs fell in the same open/closed state.
- `npm run ingest-response-review -- <slug>` — reads the 08-Sheet, ingests `verdict=edit` rows into `voice_corpus` (Voyage embedding on `edited_message` text only, source_ref upsert key for idempotency), appends `rule:`-prefixed comments to `brand_persona.voiceAntiPatterns`, appends a dated `## Phase 5 review additions` subsection to the 06-spec markdown in Drive (THE-178). Skips rows with `expected_failure:` markers entirely. Skips the markdown append if zero net new ingestions.
- `npm run db:types` — regenerates `db/types.ts` from the live Supabase schema. Run after applying any migration.

## Phase 5 onboarding pipeline

Phase 5 is the voice-quality cycle that completes venue onboarding. Five files in the venue's Drive folder, numbered 04-08:

- `04-{slug}-menu` (gsheet) — menu CSV, owner-editable
- `05-{slug}-transcript` — onboarding interview transcript
- `06-{slug}-venue-spec-draft.md` — structured venue spec extracted from transcript + airtable + menu. **Central voice brain for the venue.** Original extraction at top; a `## Needs confirmation` section is appended by `extract-venue-spec`'s own verification pass (TAC-346, not seeded — see below) immediately after every extraction run, before any human has looked at the draft; Phase 5 review additions are a SEPARATE, LATER append by `ingest-response-review` after the Phase 5 meeting, in dated subsections at the bottom. Both are append-only. Regenerating only happens if the spec genuinely needs to change — Phase 5 voice training does NOT trigger a re-seed.
- `07-{slug}-test-scenarios.json` — venue-tailored test scenarios (~180 rows for le-mils-coffee, ~180 for mock-sextant-coffee-roasters as of TAC-347 Stage 1's database-driven expansion — see the `npm run extract-test-scenarios` entry above for the full source breakdown). Generated by `extract-test-scenarios` from the 06-spec + the categories fixture (behavior-category rows) merged with DB-driven rows read directly from `knowledge_corpus` / `venue_configs.venue_info` / `mechanics`.
- `08-{slug}-response-review` (gsheet) — populated by `run-test-scenarios`. Owner reviews collaboratively during the Phase 5 meeting, marks `verdict` (approve/edit), provides `edited_message` for rejections, adds `rule:`-prefixed comments for anti-patterns. Then `ingest-response-review` reads it back and updates the corpus + persona surgically.

The pipeline is idempotent at every step. Re-running any script is safe. **Exception (TAC-346):** `extract-venue-spec` hard-refuses to re-run against a venue folder that already has a `06-` file, mirroring `seed-venue`'s existing-slug guard — pass `--force` to override, or `--dry-run` (exempt from the guard) to test without touching the real file.

**Extraction hardening: verify every claim, flag gaps, fix where content lands (TAC-346).** The first real non-mock extraction (Le Mil's) needed ~40 hand edits, all tracing to `extract.ts`, not to `parse-venue-spec`/`seed-venue`. Two changes: (1) a second LLM pass, `scripts/onboarding/verify.ts` — `runVerification` reads the draft plus the same raw sources extraction had (never a `parseVenueSpec`-parsed object; the parser's own ≥5-voice-corpus-entry throw would crash the exact thin-corpus case this pass exists to report gracefully) and returns structured flags across five categories (unsupported claims, uncovered transcript answers, missing information, resolved dates, and a **mandatory per-mechanic `requires_operator_approval` review** — every mechanic is listed regardless of whether anything else about it is flagged, with a `⚠` marker when verify's own independent transcript read disagrees with the value extraction wrote). `scripts/extract-venue-spec.ts` runs this immediately after extraction and appends the result as an unnumbered `## Needs confirmation` section (never seeded — `parse-venue-spec.ts`'s `h2s.find` per-numbered-section lookup already ignores any unrecognized H2, proven in production by sections 8/9 today). **Verify failure aborts the whole run**: one retry, then nothing is written to Drive or disk and the process exits 1 — every `06-` file that lands in Drive has passed verification, no partially-verified draft ever ships. (2) `extract.ts`'s prompt rules tightened: `knowledge_corpus` now covers plain operational facts (policies, logistics, ordering, shipping, retail, wholesale, catering, private events, merch — using the existing `policies`/`logistics` primary tags) with no upper cap, not just narrative; `voice_corpus` permits ONLY `source_type='voicenote_transcript'` (no `manual_entry` padding, no floor to hit — a real corpus can come in under 5, with the shortfall reported in Needs confirmation rather than papered over, since `parse-venue-spec.ts`'s seed-time floor of 5 stays unchanged); `currentContext` vs. permanent fields are mutually exclusive, and every `expiresAt` must resolve to a real ISO date (never a relative phrase — `filterActiveContext` silently drops a malformed `expiresAt`, so leaving one unresolved is worse than a best-guess date); `bannedTopics`/`voiceAntiPatterns`/`signaturePhrases` may never carry routing or handoff instructions; mechanics may only be practices the operator already does (brainstormed ideas are omitted from section 5 entirely, never routed there as an instruction); `trigger.type` is constrained to `MECHANIC_TRIGGER_TYPES` (imported from `lib/schemas/mechanic.ts`, not hand-copied — this also fixed a live defect where the fixture's own example listed a non-canonical `date_match` value); `venue_info.staff` is employees only, with collaborators/suppliers routed to `knowledge_corpus` instead. **Extraction prompt rules never name "Needs confirmation" as a destination** — omitted content is just omitted; the verification pass independently looks for the same brainstormed ideas and handoff statements and reports them under uncovered answers, so the two passes can't silently disagree about what got dropped and why. Relative dates resolve against an interview-date anchor: an optional `--interview-date YYYY-MM-DD` CLI flag on `extract-venue-spec`, falling back to the Airtable-inferred "Submission date" already written into section 2 when omitted. The fixture (`venue-spec-example.md`) dropped its self-attested "Pre-seed validation checklist" section entirely — it was model-authored and Le Mil's draft checked "at least 5 voice_corpus entries" against a padded corpus, a green signal nobody had actually confirmed; `## Needs confirmation` (independently generated, not self-attested) replaces it.

**Extraction routes opinionated recommendations to `knowledge_corpus`, never `venue_info` (TAC-331).** Owners describing their business speak in advice ("for a first-timer I'd start them on the latte, don't over-program the first visit") — extraction was recording that faithfully into `venue_info.menu.highlights` / `menu.notes`, where it renders into every prompt turn as if it were a structured fact, indistinguishable from one by the time it reaches the model. `extract.ts`'s system prompt already listed "opinionated recommendations" under `knowledge_corpus` (it's a narrative-knowledge store, gated by retrieval — it only surfaces when the guest's message is actually about what to order), but had no worked example for this exact pattern and the few-shot fixture's own `menu.highlights` placeholder used the leaked vocabulary ("first-timer pick", "perfect-order anchor") as an "e.g." hint, which the model echoed verbatim. Fixed by (1) an explicit routing rule in `extract.ts` with a WRONG/RIGHT worked example matching the live incident, (2) a separate, narrower mood rule for content that legitimately stays in `venue_info` prose (the perfect-order narrative, sourcing description) — must read as description of the venue, never an instruction to the assistant, and (3) a new `knowledge_corpus` fixture example (Entry 5) modeling the correct attributed-indicative form with `primary_tags: ["recommendations"]` — a positive template, not just prose instruction. No schema change, no migration, no serializer change: the `recommendations` primary tag and the `knowledge_corpus` section-7 parser both already existed (see `lib/schemas/knowledge-tags.ts`, `parse-venue-spec.ts`); this was a placement defect in the extraction prompt, not a missing destination. `scripts/onboarding/extract.test.ts` canaries the fixture against reintroducing the leaked vocabulary. Deliberately does not re-extract any live venue — see the ticket for why Mock Sextant's `venue_info` (already hand-fixed in place per TAC-330) and this fix now intentionally disagree in form.

### File-naming convention

The 04-09 numbering is meaningful and reserved. Don't introduce other 04-09 files in venue folders. Each script reads its expected files by prefix (`04-`, `06-`, `07-`, `08-`) — adding ambiguous matches will trip the existence guard.

The `-draft` suffix on the 06-file is misleading post-Phase-5 (the file is canonical at that point, not a draft). Slated for rename in THE-185 — bundle with the next touch of any of the three pipeline scripts that reference the filename.

## Drive integration

Google Drive auth uses Application Default Credentials (ADC) via `gcloud auth application-default login`. The OAuth client lives in the analog Google Cloud project; client JSON is a `client_secret_*.json` file under this repo's `Minimum-Viable-Product` parent directory, not at a fixed path — locate it with a glob (see below). Scopes: `openid`, `userinfo.email`, `cloud-platform`, `drive`.

Workspace org policy blocks the stock gcloud OAuth client. Use the project-owned OAuth client (Desktop app type) instead — Workspace's third-party policy doesn't apply to your own org's apps. Pass the client JSON via `--client-id-file` to `gcloud auth application-default login`.

Drive helpers are in `scripts/onboarding/drive.ts`:

- `getDrive()` — auth + Drive client
- `findVenueFolder(drive, parentFolderId, slug)` — locates the venue's folder by slug
- `listVenueFiles(drive, folderId)` — lists files in a venue folder
- `findByPrefix(files, prefix)` — finds the unique file with a given prefix; throws on ambiguity
- `readDriveFileAsText(drive, fileId)` — reads a Drive file as text. For gsheets, exports as CSV.
- `writeMarkdownFile(drive, folderId, name, body)` — writes/upserts a markdown file by name
- `writeJsonFile(drive, folderId, name, obj)` — writes/upserts a JSON file by name (pretty-printed)
- `writeSheetFile(drive, folderId, name, csvBody)` — writes/upserts a native Google Sheet from CSV body. Uses `mimeType: 'application/vnd.google-apps.spreadsheet'` + `media.mimeType: 'text/csv'` so Drive auto-converts on upload. The update path explicitly pins both mimetypes to preserve sharing.

### ADC token refresh (when Drive scripts fail)

Two distinct failure modes land here, both fixed by the same command:

- `Request had insufficient authentication scopes` — ADC has never been granted Drive scope at all (or was granted it, then re-authed without `--scopes` and lost it).
- `invalid_grant` / `reauth related error (invalid_rapt)` — ADC previously had Drive scope, but the token expired or the reauth policy (RAPT) lapsed. This is time-based, not a one-time setup gap — expect to hit it again after enough elapsed time even with everything configured correctly before.

Either symptom means: `run-test-scenarios`, `extract-venue-spec`, `seed-supabase`, `extract-test-scenarios`, or `ingest-response-review` needs reauth with explicit Drive scope:

```bash
F=$(ls ~/dev/Ideas/the-analog-company/Minimum-Viable-Product/client_secret_*.json) && gcloud auth application-default login \
  --client-id-file="$F" \
  --scopes="openid,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/userinfo.email,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform,https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive"
```

**Canonical client file path:** there isn't a fixed one — `~/.config/analog/oauth-client.json` does not exist on the operator's machine and was never a real path. The file is `client_secret_*.json`, dropped directly under `Minimum-Viable-Product/` (this repo's parent directory) rather than into a dotfile config location. Locate it with a glob, not a hardcoded path: the filename embeds the client ID. The directory has been `~/dev/Ideas/the-analog-company/Minimum-Viable-Product/` since TAC-425 (2026-09-16) moved it off the iCloud-synced Desktop, whose path carried a curly apostrophe (`Desktop’s`) that silently broke hand-typed paths. Client ID `262219665239-3fi6pt39u9m8i0nh9vfcouhqi0t6n9qt`, GCP project `analog-venue-onboarding`. **Never write the client secret itself into CLAUDE.md or any tracked file** — `client_secret_*.json` is in `.gitignore` as a backstop against it landing in the repo by accident.

**Why this is needed:** the default scopes for `gcloud auth application-default login` (with no `--scopes` flag) do NOT include `drive` — **plain `gcloud auth application-default login` with no flags succeeds and reports a healthy login, then every Drive API call still fails.** gcloud's built-in ADC OAuth client cannot request Drive scope at all, regardless of flags — that's what the project-owned client (below) is for. The `--scopes` flag is mandatory for any script that touches Drive; there's no way to add it after the fact short of re-running the full command again.

**Why the `--client-id-file`:** Workspace org policy blocks the stock gcloud OAuth client. The project-owned OAuth client (Desktop app type, `client_secret_*.apps.googleusercontent.com.json`) bypasses that policy because Workspace's third-party policy doesn't apply to your own org's apps.

**This procedure is interactive and human-only.** It opens a browser consent flow — there is no non-interactive or scripted path around it. Claude Code cannot complete this reauth itself; when a session hits `invalid_rapt` or the insufficient-scopes error, it should stop and hand off to the operator rather than attempting a workaround.

## Testing

Vitest is the test runner. Tests are colocated with source files (`module.test.ts` next to `module.ts`). Pure functions get unit tests; DB-touching code generally doesn't (live-DB tests aren't worth the harness yet).

- Run all: `npx vitest run`
- Run single file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`
- Watch mode for development: `npx vitest`


Test count baseline: **4177 tests across 216 files as of 2026-09-18** — most recent (TAC-468, the Instagram inbound handler: **+94 tests / +4 files** over **4083/212** measured on clean `main` 37d75f2 in a worktree outside `.claude/`, exactly matching the line then. Both runs `npx vitest run --exclude '.claude/**'`. Per file: `parse-events.test.ts` 36, `handle-events.test.ts` 36, `agent-gate.test.ts` 9, `app/api/cron/webhook-silence/route.test.ts` 2 (all new; the cron had no tests), `route.test.ts` 33 → 44. **Code review found two gaps the first 84 tests missed, each shown by a mutant that survived them all**: deleting the route's `waitUntil(runInboundAgent(...))` hand-off, since every route test could only assert the agent is NOT called; and dropping a guest MESSAGE's referral, since only the postback path was covered. The route tests now open the real gate through a partial mock of `agent-gate`, and both mutants die. The same review found the webhook-silence alarm would stop watching Sendblue the moment Instagram rows arrived, fixed with a channel filter and its first tests. **Mutation-verified against 29 mutants, all killed, each by a named test with 181 tests collected**: the review's six (hand-off removed, hand-off awaited instead of `waitUntil`, a message's referral dropped, a referral beside `message` ignored, an unknown entry key ignored, the silence alarm counting Instagram); one more the partial mock itself first hid, the route passing `enabled: true` to force the gate open, which survived until the mock honoured an explicit argument and fell back to the real constant; and the first commit's 22: the gate opened; an echo handed to the agent; `channel` dropped from the inbound insert, or from the echo insert (the silent 'text' default migration 048 warns about); an echo creating a guest; the read match or the guest lookup unscoped by venue; the 23505 re-read of a racing guest dropped, or a racing message insert read as a failure; an echo's guest taken from the sender; the read keyed on `messaging_seen`; the postback's `mid` read from beside `postback`; an unhandled event dropped silently; a titleless postback dropped (it still opens TAC-469's window); the referral ref not saved; a failed venue lookup cached; one throw stopping the delivery; the duplicate check removed; the route saving nothing; `created_via` set to `qr_scan`; a phone placeholder on the new guest; and a `mid` added to an outcome log line. The harness refuses an anchor that doesn't match exactly once and reports a file that fails to collect as invalid, not killed. **The shared `node_modules` was missing `@types/js-yaml`**, which TAC-449 added to `package.json` hours earlier, so `tsc` failed on `scripts/lib/linear-prompts.test.ts` in every worktree linked to it; only that package was unpacked into it, from the locked version. Not a defect on `main`: CI installs fresh.) Prior entry (TAC-449, the node allowlist entry narrowed to the Linear helper and python removed: **+4 tests / +0 files** over **4079/212** measured on clean `main` 5b9e818 in a throwaway worktree, exactly matching the line then. Both runs `npx vitest run` from worktrees outside `.claude/`, so no exclude was needed. All of them are in `scripts/lib/linear-prompts.test.ts` (14 → 18): analog-operator's allowlist test copied for each workflow, plus one per workflow that operator's lacks, because operator's test passes under `Bash(node:*)` or `Bash(python3:*)` too and so could not see the change this ticket made. **Mutation-verified against twenty-four mutants, each killed by the test named for it with 18 tests collected, every anchor matching exactly once**: the helper entry dropped from each workflow, the wildcard restored in each, the helper added to the build's deny list, `python3:*` restored in each, and other rules added beside the helper (`node -e`, `node -p`, `node --eval`, a second script, bare `node`, `node*`, `*`, a bare `Bash`, `python3 -c`, `python*`, `python:*`, `python3.12`, `python3.12 -c`, `/usr/bin/python3`, `/usr/bin/node`, `python2`, `nodejs`). Code review found the extra test too narrow three times: it first probed two commands and passed with `Bash(node -p:*)` added, then listed the node rules and passed with `Bash(node*)` or `Bash(*)` added, then listed node and python rules and passed with `Bash(python3.12:*)`, the runners' own python, and five other spellings added. It now lists every rule naming node, nodejs or python, any version or path, probes every rule, and refuses a bare `Bash`; the old pattern still passes `Bash(python3.12:*)` and `Bash(nodejs:*)`, checked. **The first run of the final check was invalid for 17 of the 24**: the replacement put each added rule after the allowlist's closing quote, outside the list the test reads, so they "survived" without being tested. They were re-run with a check that each rule sits inside the quoted list, and all die.) Prior entry (TAC-467, a guest with no phone: **+25 tests / +4 files** over TAC-443's **4054/208**. Measured on the branch before TAC-443 merged, as +25/+4 over **4036/207** on clean `main` 82abce3 in a throwaway worktree, and again on the tree merged with `main` 04aaa05 at **4079/212**; the two deltas agree. All runs `npx vitest run --exclude '.claude/**'`. Per file: `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/guest-name.test.ts` 11 (new; the helper had no tests), `lib/messaging/expressions.test.ts` 4 (new; nor had the typing indicator or read receipt), `lib/messaging/send.test.ts` +2, `lib/operator/queue.test.ts` +1, `lib/operator/conversations.test.ts` +1, `lib/operator/dispatch-operator-outbound.test.ts` +3, the approve and edit routes' `route.test.ts` 1 each (new; neither route had tests), and the Command Center follow-up route +1. **Mutation-verified against twelve mutants, each killed by the test named for it with tests collected**: the `''` fallback removed from each operator projection, the null guard removed from each of the three send functions, the null check removed from `formatGuestPhone`, each of the two name helpers returning a null phone, the dispatch refusal disabled (the flip then runs), the `no_phone_number` case removed from each route (it then falls to a 500), and the follow-up route's refusal disabled. The last four came from the code-review pass, which found both operator-driven paths could still queue or strand a card for a guest with no phone. **The send-guard mutants are killed by the error NAME, not by "never reaches the provider"**: with the guard gone, `E164_RE.test(null)` still fails, so the provider is still never called, and only the `recipient_has_no_phone_number` assertion tells the two apart. The two operator projections are the load-bearing tests: regenerated types call every RPC return column non-null, so `tsc` can never catch a null there. No pre-existing failure this time: the worktree carries no gitignored sandbox script for `pending-slots.test.ts`'s guard to flag.) Full-suite runs are flaky under this sandbox's resource contention — one run timed out an unrelated `build-workflow.test.ts` subprocess-spawning test at 5000ms, which passes in isolation and on a clean re-run; not this ticket's defect. Mutation-verified: removing the added `capture("^(?<head>[^.]*)").head` line from `build-ready.yml` alone (leaving `audit-new-todo.yml` untouched) fails 5 of the 18 tests — the byte-identity drift guard, the content guard, and the three accept-fixtures this ticket exists to stop refusing (TAC-389's and TAC-386's original lines, and TAC-412's conditional line) — while every refuse-fixture still correctly resolves to `defect:multi-repo-line`.) Prior entry (TAC-463, the build pushes with the App token: **+3 tests / +0 files** over **4033/207** measured on clean `main` 7d43ebe in a throwaway worktree. Mutation-verified by pasting a `promptLine` literal into `definitions-list.tsx` — the guard fails and names both the file and the field. **A raw `toContain` was NOT enough, and code review caught why**: `react/no-unescaped-entities` (via `eslint-config-next`) makes a bare apostrophe in JSX text an ERROR and suggests `&apos;`/`&rsquo;` instead, so the repo's own lint rule converts the copy the guard catches into one it would miss — and BOTH current `promptLine` values contain apostrophes, on the exact field the ticket singles out. Both sides are now normalized (quote entities decoded, typographic quotes folded to ASCII, whitespace collapsed so a prettier-wrapped paste can't hide behind a newline), mutation-verified against all three forms: raw literal, entity-escaped JSX text, and entity-escaped plus line-wrapped. **What it still cannot catch is a PARAPHRASE**, stated in the test's own header rather than left to be assumed: prose that describes a prompt line without quoting it passes and can still go stale. That limit is not hypothetical — it is why the gating copy was rewritten mid-build to describe the menu-mention rule instead of quoting the line it suppresses, the first draft having quoted it, which is the same drift the ticket forbids one layer removed. The satisfaction truth table in `definitions.test.ts` is a LITERAL table rather than anything derived from the predicates, because a derived expectation would pass against any predicate at all; `satisfies Record<IntentionKey, ...>` on it is what makes a third intention fail `tsc` there. Honest accounting of the three guards, since they are easy to mistake for one: the required field catches a MISSING label, the truth table catches a CHANGED predicate, the source scan catches a COPIED string — and nothing catches a label that is simply WRONG about a predicate nobody touched. A reviewer is still the only guard there.) Don't let regressions land — every PR should keep tests green.



**Update this line when you add or remove test files, and measure rather than estimate.** A stale baseline is what let an "after" count be reported without an "if the delta doesn't match, a file isn't being collected" check — and an uncollected test file is indistinguishable from a passing one in the summary output. To get a trustworthy before/after on a branch: `git stash push -u -- <the paths you touched>`, run `npx vitest run`, record, `git stash pop`, re-run. `npx vitest list | grep <filename>` proves a specific file is actually collected rather than silently skipped.

THE-164 covers expanding test coverage.

## CI

GitHub Actions runs on every PR and push to `main` (workflow at `.github/workflows/ci.yml`):

1. `tsc --noEmit` — typecheck
2. `npm run lint` — eslint
3. `npx vitest run` — unit tests
4. `npm run build` — Next.js build verification

Steps run sequentially with fail-fast (a tsc failure skips the rest). All steps run with mock env vars set in the workflow YAML — no GitHub Secrets configured. If a future test or module-load path requires real credentials, prefer adding a placeholder to the env-mock block first; reach for GitHub Secrets only if the value can't be public.

When CI fails, the GitHub PR check shows which step failed. Reproduce locally with the same command. Most common failures are tsc (run `npx tsc --noEmit`) and lint (run `npm run lint -- --fix` for auto-fixable issues).

CI does NOT make external service calls. If you add a test that hits Voyage/Anthropic/Supabase/Sendblue, CI will either fail (no real API key) or slow down significantly. Mock at the test boundary instead.

Branch protection requires the `CI / ci` status check to pass before merging to `main` (configured in repo settings; see README for the operator checklist).

---

## Observability and alerting

Three layers, each filling a different gap. Don't conflate them.

- **PostHog events.** Product/event analytics. Captures inbound/outbound events, fidelity scores, classification outputs, etc. Configured to fire to Slack on certain event filters. Today only fires on errors; THE-187 expands coverage to silent failures (low fidelity, empty corpus, classification confidence drops, latency spikes, regeneration cycles, webhook silence). PostHog event emission is wrapped in try/catch — failures must not crash the agent path.
- **Slack alerts** (THE-159). Diagnostic-rich alert content for fired alerts. Narrow scope: improves what existing alerts say, doesn't change what fires.
- **Trace-level observability.** Per-run visibility into the agent's reasoning (corpus retrieval scores, prompt content, fidelity self-rating, etc.). Wired up via Langfuse Cloud (THE-200). `lib/observability/langfuse.ts` is a thin wrapper exposing `startAgentTrace` returning an `AgentTrace` (`span()`, `update()`, `flushAsync()`); spans expose `span()` / `generation()` / `update()` / `end()`. Wrapper invariants:
  - Never throws. SDK errors are caught at the wrapper boundary and logged via `console.warn` — observability is diagnostic, not load-bearing.
  - No-op fallback when `NODE_ENV=test`, `LANGFUSE_ENABLED=false`, any of the three env vars (`LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY`, `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`, host) are missing, or SDK init throws. The host can come from `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` (preferred — matches the SDK's `baseUrl` constructor field) or `LANGFUSE_HOST` (legacy alias accepted for compatibility with Langfuse's own docs / Vercel integration; slated for removal post-pilot). `BASE_URL` wins when both are set. In no-op mode `trace.id === ''`, all methods are silent no-ops, and agent code runs unchanged.
  - Trace ID is available synchronously the moment `startAgentTrace` returns. `schedule-and-send.ts` writes `trace.id || null` to `messages.langfuse_trace_id` at insert time so traces are queryable from the row.
  - Span tree per agent run: `agent.inbound` (or `agent.followup`) → `context_build` → `classify` (inbound only) → `retrieve` → `retrieve_knowledge` (conditional, gated by `shouldRetrieveKnowledge` in `lib/agent/stages.ts`: always for inbound, only for followup `event` / `manual` triggers) → `generate` → `verify_grounding` (TAC-350; **widened by TAC-376** — `verifyGroundingStage` now runs on every proactive generation too (followups, the knowledge-gap holding message), not just inbound, same verifier/triggers/failure posture; the named `verify_grounding` Langfuse SPAN itself stays inbound-only, since only `handle-inbound.ts` wraps the call in `trace.span('verify_grounding', ...)` — `handle-followup.ts` and `handle-holding-message.ts` call the stage without a dedicated span, a pre-existing asymmetry this ticket didn't touch. Only makes a real call when `generation.knowledgeGap === false` and the guest isn't a demo guest — see `verifyGroundingStage`) → `send`. The `generate` span has post-hoc `generate.attempt_N` sub-spans synthesized from `attemptScores` — these have no real per-attempt timing; threading the trace into `lib/ai`'s regen loop for true timing is tracked in THE-215. Knowledge retrieval degrades gracefully on Voyage / DB error (logs + returns `[]`, span closes normally with `matchCount=0`); voice retrieval continues to fail closed because voice failure breaks voice fidelity itself.
  - Spans carry **metadata always** (counts, scores, IDs in `output` / span input) and **content gated** by `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT` (full inbound body, full retrieved corpus chunks, full system + user prompts, per-attempt generations, full sent body — folded into `output.content` when on, dropped entirely when off; THE-216). Default-on; set `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT=false` to revert to THE-200's metadata-only shape (e.g. for a venue with strict data-handling requirements). The flag is read once at module init — flipping it requires a redeploy. `lib/agent/trace-content.ts` centralizes the per-stage content shape (`buildRecognitionContent`, `buildCorpusContent`, `buildGenerateContent`, `buildGenerateAttemptContent`); `trace.captureContent` is exposed so handlers can skip building heavy payloads when no one will look at them. Coverage gap doc lives in the THE-216 PR description.
  - `flushAsync` is awaited in the `finally` block of each handler. Both handlers run inside a `waitUntil` keep-alive window from their callers (webhook route, cron) so the flush completes before the function returns.
  - `/admin/health` reports four states via `app/admin/(authed)/health/check-langfuse.ts`: **Active** (all keys + known host, green), **Disabled** (`LANGFUSE_ENABLED=false` set explicitly, neutral), **Misconfigured** (some keys present but not all, or unrecognized host — red, with the specific reason in the detail line), **Not configured** (no `LANGFUSE_*` vars set, neutral — local-dev / no-op is appropriate). The check does not actively probe Langfuse — the SDK has no synchronous ping, and probing on every health-page load would pollute the trace stream.

## AI agent runtime contract

These constraints are real and have caused bugs:

- **Crisis-safety short circuit (TAC-348):** self-harm/suicidal-ideation or immediate-medical-emergency signals on an inbound get a FIXED, hardcoded reply — never generated, never persona-styled. `ClassifyMessageResult.crisisSafety` (`lib/ai/classify-message.ts`) is an independent boolean alongside `category`, decided by the SAME classifier call (no second LLM round trip). `handle-inbound.ts` checks it immediately after `classifyStage` succeeds — before `retrieveCorpusStage`, `retrieveKnowledgeStage`, `generateStage`, or `applyApprovalPolicyStage` ever run — and on true, dispatches `buildCrisisSafetyResult()`'s fixed body (`lib/agent/crisis-safety.ts`) straight through `scheduleAndSend` (`skipHumanFeelDelay: true`, `reviewReason: 'crisis_safety_reply'`), bypassing the approval gate entirely: no operator flag, by design, because the gate never runs (not because a trigger is suppressed) — same treatment `hold_all_outbound` gives opt-out confirmations. **Why the short circuit sits before retrieval, not just before generation:** `retrieveCorpusStage` fails CLOSED on the inbound path (throws below `MIN_STRONG_MATCHES`), and a crisis message has no reason to resemble venue voice-corpus exemplars — waiting until inside `generateStage` to intervene would let a thin-corpus venue abort the whole run with silence on exactly the turn where silence is worst. **Why fixed text, not a prompt rule:** three prior rounds in `system-template.ts`'s own history (v1.27.0-v1.31.0, the R12 message-splitting saga) show a rule positioned ahead of `# Voice imperative` can still lose to venue persona content rendered later, because that section tells the model the venue's voice wins when the two conflict — unacceptable for a safety reply. `scripts/onboarding/run-test-scenarios.ts` (the TAC-347 harness) carries the identical short circuit in the identical position (right after `classifyStage`, before retrieval), so `adversarial_safety_critical` scenarios grade the actual shipped mechanism, not a full-pipeline generation the code never runs on this turn. `captureCrisisSafetyReplySent` (`lib/analytics/posthog.ts`) is PostHog-only, no Slack relay (owner decision — the reply is fixed and unconditional, so this is audit/count visibility, not an alert). **The classifier's `MAX_CLASSIFIER_INPUT_CHARS` (1000) category-classification cap does NOT apply to crisis detection.** Code review caught that a guest genuinely in crisis may write a long, escalating message where the actual statement lands past that cutoff — silently defeating the whole mechanism with no error and no alert. `classifyMessage` (`lib/ai/classify-message.ts`) appends a SECOND block to the user prompt, bounded by its own more generous `MAX_CRISIS_CHECK_INPUT_CHARS` (4000), carrying the fuller text explicitly scoped to the `crisisSafety` determination only, whenever the body exceeds the category cap — the category-facing block stays truncated and unchanged. Adds nothing to the prompt for the common case (body under 1000 chars).

**Known gap, deliberately out of scope for this ticket:** the guest's NEXT inbound after a crisis reply goes through the normal pipeline unchanged and could produce an upbeat or promotional-sounding reply with no awareness a crisis turn just happened.

**`lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.ts` mirrors this short circuit too** (its own file header already commits to mirroring `stages.ts` gating changes) — see that file's own TAC-348 note. Without this, an operator who regenerates a crisis-safety auto-sent reply from the Voices playground (indistinguishable in the thread view from an ordinary AI reply — `review_reason` isn't rendered there) would trigger a full persona-driven `generateMessage` call against the original crisis inbound, with zero safety framing, and could commit that output to the voice corpus as a style exemplar. `regenerateWithCritique` now checks `crisisSafety` immediately after its own `classifyMessage` call and returns a distinct `crisis_safety_ineligible` outcome instead of proceeding to generation.
- **Voice fidelity floor (3-tier, TAC-212):** the agent self-rates voice fidelity. Below `SEND_FIDELITY_FLOOR` (0.4) → refused with red alert (existing behavior — `generateStage` returns `'refused'`, no row is persisted, no send). At or above 0.4 but below `AUTO_SEND_FIDELITY_FLOOR` (0.6) → queued for operator review (`applyApprovalPolicyStage` fires the `fidelity_below_auto_send_floor` trigger). At or above 0.6 → continues to the resource-commitment + sticky-pending gates inside `applyApprovalPolicyStage`. Both floors live in `lib/agent/stages.ts`. The intermediate `VOICE_FIDELITY_LOW_THRESHOLD` (0.5) still fires the `voice_fidelity_low` PostHog observation event for the 0.4–0.5 band (now always queued).
- **Corpus retrieval floor:** retrieval is fail-closed *on the inbound path*. Need at least `MIN_STRONG_MATCHES` (1) chunk scoring at or above `STRONG_MATCH_SIMILARITY` (0.3); below that, the agent bails to a fallback acknowledgment. Followup path skips the gate entirely (THE-231) — operator-initiated sends shouldn't be blocked by sparse corpus retrieval. Defined in `lib/agent/stages.ts`.
- **Classifier confidence routing (TAC-240, v1.11.0):** 3-tier handling in `classifyStage`. Above `CLASSIFICATION_CONFIDENCE_LOW_THRESHOLD` (0.7) → keep the classifier's pick, no event. Between `CLASSIFICATION_CONFIDENCE_REROUTE_THRESHOLD` (0.3) and 0.7 → keep the pick + fire `classification_low_confidence` (operator should check). Below 0.3 → rewrite the returned `Classification.category` to `'unknown'` so the agent ships a holding response; original category and confidence are preserved on the PostHog event payload (`autoRoutedToUnknown=true`) and on the returned `Classification` for observability. Regen path (`lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.ts`) does not apply the reroute — operator iterates on the output. Classifier inputs were also widened to receive `recentMessages` + `guestState` so one-word inbounds ("yes", "iced") can be classified in conversational + relationship context; classifier `temperature` dropped from the SDK default 1.0 to 0.2 (analytical task); inbound is truncated to `MAX_CLASSIFIER_INPUT_CHARS` (1000) before classification while generation still gets the full body.
- **Per-message emoji cadence (TAC-362, v1.48.0):** whether a given message carries an emoji is decided in CODE by a weighted coin, not by the model reading a frequency word. `resolveEmojiDirective(policy, rng)` (`lib/ai/emoji-cadence.ts`) is flipped once per generation inside `buildAiRuntime` — the only place holding both the venue's `emojiPolicy` and the rng, same reasoning as TAC-301's open/closed join — and `runtimeToProse` renders the result as `## Emoji for this message`, the last BLOCK of the user prompt — ahead of the runtime-facts tail, so it is the last INSTRUCTION the model reads rather than literally the last text. `composePrompt` runs once before `generateMessage`'s regen loop, so all attempts of one message share one directive.
  **The measured basis, because the numbers are the whole argument.** Counting every generated outbound per RESPONSE (`coalesce(generation_id, id)` — per-ROW counting reads a split response's first bubble as a clean miss and understates this): `never` 0/52, `sparingly` **0/188**, `frequent` **10/11**. Two conclusions. (1) The model obeys a per-message emoji instruction near-perfectly in both directions, which is what makes moving the decision into `Math.random()` work where a reworded "occasionally" would not — the sentence "an emoji now and then, not in every text" sat in Le Mil's prompt on every one of those turns and still produced 10/11, because a frequency word has no referent inside a single generation. Same failure TAC-319 paid for on splitting, same fix. **Do not read that sentence as evidence of what the venue wanted** — the transcript (1:15:22) has the owner asking for the opposite ("always use emojis... hit them with emojis until they're like why"), so `emojiPolicy: frequent` was faithful and 10/11 was the system obeying. The `tone` sentence matches nothing in the transcript; extraction fabricated it. It is mechanism evidence only. (2) **The enum has three values and two behaviours**: `sparingly` is empirically identical to `never`, because its guidance ends "Default to none" and the model takes that literally. That second finding is RECORDED, NOT FIXED — `EMOJI_PROBABILITY.sparingly` is deliberately `null` (no flip, no block, persona statement unchanged) so Mock Sextant stays at 0%; giving it a probability is its own ticket with its own evidence. `never` is `null` for a different reason: it already works and its persona prohibition always renders, which is what makes "a venue set to no emoji gets none" hold by construction.
  `EMOJI_PROBABILITY.frequent = 0.75` is **a deliberate softening of a stated preference, not a calibration** — the owner asked for MORE emoji and the pre-change ~91% was closer to that request; the product judgment (recorded on TAC-362) is that literal compliance reads as a template rather than warmth. Labelled that way in its own docstring because a guess that reads as a measurement is how `KNOWLEDGE_RELEVANCE_FLOOR = 0.5` survived until TAC-358. **Known consequence, recorded not fixed: there is now no setting meaning "an emoji in nearly every message"** — `frequent` is the top of the enum and caps at 0.75, so a venue that wants what Le Mil's actually asked for cannot express it. It is a CEILING: `'allowed'` is permission the model can decline, never a mandate (a mandate would restore the determinism at a lower rate), so the realised rate sits below it. Verified live at Le Mil's across **48 generations on two different inbounds: 35 `allowed` (72.9%), 32 with emoji (66.7%), and 0 violations across all 13 `none` turns.** Baseline was 10/11 = 90.9% with no variation at all.
  **No strip backstop, deliberately.** `emojiDirectiveViolated` recomputes on the final body and `generateStage` fires `emoji_directive_violated` (PostHog only, no Slack relay — a voice-quality observation, not an alert); the body SHIPS. A regex-strip would be this repo's first post-generation body mutation, against a 0-in-272 measured violation rate, and carries a real empty-body edge (a single-emoji reply strips to nothing, which TAC-309 made load-bearing at the send guard). If that rate moves, the strip lands with evidence behind it.
  **Category-gated, and the gate is the compliance half.** `shouldRenderEmojiDirective` (`serializers.ts`) suppresses the block for `opt_out` and `comp_complaint`, mirroring `shouldRenderOpenIntentions`'s placement and TAC-328's reasoning. Caught in code review, not in the plan: the block renders LAST in the user prompt while a category instruction sits in the SYSTEM prompt, so on proximity the block wins — a trailing "an emoji is welcome here" would have outranked `OPT_OUT_INSTRUCTIONS`' "respectful and final" on ~75% of opt-outs at a `frequent` venue. Both exclusions are strictly NARROWER than what shipped before (the old standing licence applied on every turn including these two), so the gate can only reduce emoji. Scope is otherwise unaudited — not a claim these are the only two categories that should suppress it.
  **The grader's `frequent` emoji limit changed from `Infinity` to 1** (`scripts/onboarding/grade-voice-deterministic.ts`), because the per-message block says "at most one" and an unlimited grader would let a four-emoji reply violate the prompt and pass the grade. The count distinction between `sparingly` and `frequent` is no longer per-message; it is the per-message PROBABILITY, which a single-message grader cannot see. The existing "frequent policy never fails on emoji count" test was UPDATED to assert the reversal rather than deleted. **The shared detector also carries four behavioural deltas, enumerated in the grader's own comment** because a first version of that comment undercounted them: text-default pictographic symbols (`®` `™` `©` `‼` `ℹ` `▶`) and plain arrows no longer count (the detector's base is `Emoji_Presentation`, or `Extended_Pictographic` plus an explicit U+FE0F — `Extended_Pictographic` alone would grade "Analog®" as an emoji at a `never` venue); a ZWJ-joined family counts as ONE rather than as each component, which is the delta that flips a `sparingly` verdict; and flag sequences now count at all, which the replaced regex named as a known gap in its own comment.
  **Not fixed here, and it is worse than a misrouting:** `extract.ts` wrote a `brand_persona.tone` sentence stating the OPPOSITE of what the interviewee said — a fabrication in the persona, not a style description filed in the wrong field — and every venue onboarded from here inherits that risk. Filed as TAC-371 (TAC-331 precedent).
- **Em-dash regen loop (THE-225) — CORRECTED BY TAC-362, the prior wording described behaviour this repo does not have.** R3 in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` says no em dashes, but Sonnet still emits them occasionally even at high voice fidelity, so `DASH_REGEX` forces a regen inside `generateMessage`'s per-attempt loop (`lib/ai/generate-message.ts`). **What happens when the attempts exhaust is the part that was documented wrong:** the dash is NOT hard-blocked, NOT rewritten, and the body ships unmodified — `dashViolationPersisted` is a recompute of the BOOLEAN on the final body, and `stages.ts` turns it into a PostHog/Slack observation. `voiceFidelity` is likewise the model's own unmodified number; nothing recomputes it. **There is therefore NO post-generation body mutation anywhere on the generation path**, which matters because the old wording reads as a precedent for adding one: TAC-362 nearly built an emoji strip-backstop on it and only caught the error by reading the code. Third instance in this file of a written claim that was never true (see `approval_policy`'s 102 unread days and the `future-add safety` test that asserted its own opposite) — the lesson generalizes past this line.
- **Visit history block (TAC-234, v1.13.0):** `RuntimeContext.recentVisits` carries up to `MAX_VISIT_HISTORY_TRANSACTIONS` (20) transactions within the past `MAX_VISIT_HISTORY_DAYS` (90) for the guest, most-recent-first. Rendered as `## Visit history` in the user prompt with bracketed time-delta bullets matching the `## Recent conversation` style. Replaces the v1.12 single-transaction `## Last visit` block. The intro line tells Sonnet to use this for pattern recognition (recommendations, "what haven't I tried") — not to recite history back at the guest. Skipped at the block level for `welcome` and `opt_out`. Empty `recentVisits` omits the block entirely (parallels `## Recent conversation`'s skip-on-empty; distinct from `## Venue knowledge`'s render-explicit-no-match — that pattern exists because the model might invent venue facts, R9 is the safety net there). Projection lives in `lib/agent/extract-recent-visits.ts`.
- **Guest relationship surfacing (TAC-234, v1.13.0):** `recognition.state` is now rendered as `Guest relationship: <state>` directly after the inbound framing line, symmetric with the classifier's TAC-240 surfacing. Closes the tone-calibration gap between regulars and new guests. Single source of truth: the recognition snapshot — surfacing only, no recomputation.
- **runtimeToProse field-presence rendering (TAC-234, v1.13.0):** the per-category switch in `lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts` was replaced with three independent conditional blocks: inbound framing (one consistent `The guest just sent: "..."` line for any inbound), `perk_unlock` data, `event_invite` data. Carve-outs ("just asked", "(opt-out request)") collapsed into the unified inbound line — the category-specific instructions in the system prompt already convey question vs statement intent. The mutual exclusion between inbound and outbound runtime contexts is enforced by the orchestrator entry points (`handleInbound` populates `inboundMessage`; `handleFollowup` populates `perkBeingUnlocked` or `eventBeingInvited`); type-system enforcement is tracked separately as TAC-243 (backlog).
- **Recent conversation block:** the last 14 days of messages between this guest and this venue are loaded into RuntimeContext. THE-173 added this; future changes to the window should consider impact on synthetic-guest seeding (`run-test-scenarios` seeds messages >30 days old to keep this block empty during testing).
  **Every history line says whether the guest received it (TAC-394, v1.50.0).** Until this, the history query had no filter on `status` or `review_state` and `RecentMessage` carried no delivery field, so a pending draft rendered exactly like a sent message. On 2026-09-14 a model regenerating a pending comp ("the next one's on us") read the comp as already offered, replied only to the guest's next question, and TAC-264's regen-in-place replaced the comp with that reply. **The draft stays IN history and is MARKED, never excluded** (ruling): a pending comp the model cannot see is one it offers a second time, which is TAC-398 arrived at from the other side. `deriveDelivery` (`lib/agent/group-responses.ts`) is the one rule, and `RecentMessage.delivery` is required so no construction site can default a line to delivered:
  - inbound → `delivered`
  - outbound with `review_state = 'pending'` → `awaiting_review` (checked BEFORE status, so no status value can make a pending row read as sent)
  - outbound at `sending` / `sent` / `delivered` → `delivered`
  - outbound with `review_state = 'skipped'` and no delivered status → `skipped_by_operator`
  - any other outbound (failed, rejected, draft, the approved-but-never-dispatched gap, and any status nobody has mapped yet) → `never_sent`
  **`sending` counts as delivered here and NOT in `count_outbound_responses`, on purpose.** The Sendblue webhook maps QUEUED to `sending` and its callbacks arrive out of order, so a message the guest read can sit at `sending` indefinitely. Leaving it out of a reply-rate count under-counts; marking it NEVER SENT in a prompt invites the model to say it again. The Command Center viewer's `TRAVELED_STATUSES` makes the same call. A split response is delivered if ANY bubble was. A delivered status beats `skipped`: an operator can only skip a pending draft, so a skipped row never went out, and if one ever carried a delivered status the guest read it.
  Rendering (`formatRecentConversation`, and `formatClassifierRecentConversation` for the markers and note): each unsent line gets a marker inside its bracket saying WHY it never arrived: `NOT SENT: waiting for the venue to approve it`, `NOT SENT: the venue decided not to send it`, or `NEVER SENT: it failed to send`. One note follows the block when any line is marked. **There is deliberately no instruction about what to do with an unsent line.** v1.50.0 was first built with one (the reply takes the pending draft's place, so offer a pending comp, hold or discount again), scoped by a `pendingDraftReplacedByReply` flag, with pending lines exempt from truncation and knowledge-gap cards given their own delivery value so they never got it. The 2026-09-14 ruling removed the instruction before release, and the flag, the exemption and the gap-card value went with it, since nothing else read them. **A skip gets its own marker** because a bare "never sent" beside "they have not read them" can nudge the model to raise content an operator decided not to send, which is TAC-398 arriving from a third direction. **A history with nothing unsent renders byte-for-byte as v1.49.0**; the note appends only when a marker is present, and in both renderers (the generator's and the classifier's) a test pins the whole block exactly, so anything appended after the note fails it however it is worded.
  **The removed instruction, and why (measured 2026-09-14).** On a turn the gate won't guarantee review (`willBeReviewed` false, e.g. `new_question`), `## What this guest can access` says "Do not offer comps, remakes, replacements, or discounts" (no eligible perks) or "Do not offer items that are not on this list" (some), so an instruction to offer a pending comp again contradicted it. **Measured with the bar set before running (n=10 per arm, synthetic venue, incident history):** a structured comp was re-emitted 0/10 on the old prompt, **6/10 with the instruction and the denial present** (missing the ≥7 bar), and 10/10 with the perks block removed. Under the conflict the model wrote long two-topic replies: **7/10 kept an em dash**, and **3/10 self-rated voice fidelity 0.00**, which `generateStage` refuses, leaving the guest with no reply. An informational re-run after rewording gave 9/10 re-emitted and 2/10 at 0.00. Em dashes appeared in 6/10, and in 5/10 with the perks block removed, so the dashes came with the two-topic reply, not with the conflict. **The ruling dropped the instruction rather than touching the denial**: post-incident safety wording is not edited to move a metric. Scoping it ("do not originate a comp" rather than "do not repeat one the venue drafted") needs its own ruling and ticket. **Markers-only re-run (prediction posted before generating, n=20 per arm):**
  - Refusals and dashes: neither baseline nor markers-only had a generation below the 0.4 floor or any em dash (0/20 each), and all 40 replies answered the hours question.
  - Fidelity: markers-only ran 0.72–0.85 (median 0.82), baseline 0.75–0.85.
  - Comp: neither arm re-emitted a structured comp or offered one in prose.

  Observed but not pre-registered: **17/20 markers-only replies acknowledged the cortado complaint**, where baseline never did. **6/20 said some form of "we'll make it right"**, which v1.23.0 counts as a resource commitment. **Measured afterwards whether the gate holds them** (n=60; pattern and reading posted before generating; real grounding check and gate; pending-draft detection off):
  - **Held, 20/20 matching replies; none would auto-send.** The hold came from the grounding verifier (`knowledge_gap_backstop`, 20/20), which read the promise as contradicting the no-comps denial in the runtime context.
  - **The control v1.23.0 relies on barely fired:** the model's self-flag caught 3/20, and the comp regex 0/20.
  - **So the hold is fragile.** It rests on a check that fails open on transient faults, never runs on followups (`verifyGroundingStage` skips without an inbound), and cited a denial that renders only for a guest with no eligible perks.
  - **Under option B's decision 1 as planned** (send beside a protected obligation card when nothing else holds the reply, otherwise drop), all 20 of those turns would be DROPPED, and 29 of the 60 overall. The guest asking the hours would get no reply about half the time on this turn shape. Option F, which shipped instead, does not drop them: a held reply with no structured obligation takes the conversation slot beside the comp card (see *Two pending slots per guest* under the single-pending-draft invariant).
  - **The grounding verifier also reads the markers**, because it is handed the generator's own prompt. It flagged "sorry again" because the earlier apology was never sent.
  **Known residue, not fixed:** a regenerated row keeps its original `created_at` (TAC-264 kept queue position deliberately), so a draft rewritten and then approved still sorts ABOVE the question it answers, and the next turn's history reads the answer before the question. The operator thread endpoints (`lib/operator/thread.ts`) run their own history query; TAC-395 filters them to messages that reached the guest rather than marking delivery (see *Full-thread fetch*).
  **Also not fixed:** a pending draft more than 14 days old, or behind 30 later responses, falls out of history entirely and is still regenerated over, unmarked. And three delivery edge cases read wrong. (1) An operator dispatch whose Sendblue send succeeded but whose status write failed stays `pending_review`/`approved` and reads `never_sent` though the guest received it. (2) An ERROR callback arriving after DELIVERED sets `failed`, so that row reads `never_sent` too. (3) An operator dispatch in flight: `dispatchOperatorOutbound` flips `review_state` from pending to approved BEFORE calling Sendblue and writes `status='sent'` only after, so a guest inbound in those seconds reads the line as `NEVER SENT: it failed to send`. With the row no longer pending, `previous_pending_held` no longer holds the reply either, so the reply can repeat what the guest is receiving.
- **Today's date in context:** `RuntimeContext.todayInVenueTimezone` is set from `new Date()` at runtime, formatted in the venue's timezone. THE-174 added this so the agent answers "are you open today?" correctly.
