- **Venue capability is a venue fact, and `hold` is conditional on it (TAC-301 part 2, v1.46.0):** the prompt carried THREE statements about holding that could not all be true — `# Hard rules` ("no promises about staff or stock"), `# Resource commitment self-flag` (holding is permitted but gated), and `# Commitments` (`hold` as a first-class type with a worked example) — and R34, four commits earlier, carved holding OUT of its own prohibition and reaffirmed it. At a counter-only walk-in venue the prompt was **instructing** the behavior the ticket was filed about. **`# Commitments` is now the single source of truth**: `hold` is available ONLY when the venue-services block says so, and the rule closes the workaround ("anything that amounts to the same thing") rather than just the word. `# Hard rules` no longer re-legislates, but note it still **scopes** reservations and stock rather than dropping them — a first version deleted both outright and code review caught that nothing else covered reservations at any venue. R34's carve-out points at `# Commitments` (wording narrowed, id untouched, `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` in lockstep). **`# Resource commitment self-flag` is deliberately UNCHANGED** — it governs whether a commitment needs approval, never whether the service exists; don't sweep it up in a future tidy. **Worked examples were the highest-leverage half and the easiest to half-fix**: the `# Commitments` heads-up example taught holding by demonstration, and an IDENTICAL readiness demonstration survived 31 lines later in `# Arrival capture` past a test keyed on the deleted noun ("almond croissant") rather than the behavior. Both are gone and the guard now asserts the shapes. `comp-complaint.ts`'s "have it ready for them" remedy is capability-scoped for the same reason — it renders LAST and would otherwise win. Verified behaviourally against Le Mil's live config: `holds:false` declines, `holds:true` permits, unconfigured declines.
- **Open/closed status line (TAC-301, v1.45.0):** the `## Right now` block carries a fourth line stating whether the venue is OPEN (with its closing time) or CLOSED (with the next opening), resolved in code by `resolveOpenState` (`lib/schemas/venue-hours.ts`) and threaded onto `today.openState` by `buildAiRuntime`. **Why in code rather than left to the model:** both inputs were already in the prompt — the weekly hours table via `venueInfoToProse` (system prompt) and the venue-local clock via `formatRightNow` (user prompt) — and at ~7:57pm, five hours after close, the agent still answered "omw can you have my usual ready?" with "Got it, see you soon." Nothing in the twelve-trigger `APPROVAL_TRIGGERS` set keys on time, so that reply auto-sent with no operator in the loop. The model was being asked to join a free-text en-dashed range against a 24h clock across two prompt sections, unprompted, mid-commitment. **`buildAiRuntime` is the only place that holds both halves** (`ctx.venue.venueInfo.hours` + the validated timezone) — `venueInfoToProse` sees the hours but not the clock, `runtimeToProse` sees the clock but not the hours — which is why the join lives there and not in a serializer. The CLOSED line carries its own instruction ("do not tell the guest to come by, and do not confirm anything for right now") rather than stating the fact alone: a bare fact is something the venue persona can talk past, and the failure being fixed is specifically a confirmation. `unknown` renders NOTHING — see the governing rule under `lib/schemas/`, including the three cases a code-review pass had to correct (ambiguous shorthand, absence-as-closure, substituted timezone), each of which would have produced a confident WRONG verdict rather than silence. One `now` is taken per `buildAiRuntime` call so the rendered clock and the verdict can't straddle a minute boundary. The regen path inherits this free (`regenerate-with-critique.ts` imports `buildAiRuntime` rather than mirroring it). **Deliberately no "closing soon" threshold** — the block states the closing time and the current time, and a threshold constant would need its own calibration.
- **Resource commitments are VALUE TRANSFER, not money (v1.23.0):** the `# Resource commitment self-flag` block and the `# Commitments` comp type both test "does the guest end up with product, service, or money they did not pay for" rather than enumerating monetary instruments. A remake, replacement, redo, "another one," or a vague "I'll make it right" is a resource commitment exactly as much as a comp or refund. **This is written from a production incident**: on 2026-08-07 the agent auto-sent "Come by and I'll have another made for you" in direct response to a refund request, `review_reason` NULL, no operator in the loop. The prior wording enumerated "comp, discount, refund, or any monetary credit," and the model's trace reasoning followed it exactly: *"a redo/remake … is standard recovery, not a perk … isn't a 'comp' in the structured sense … I'll leave commitment empty and not flag operator approval since I'm not promising a monetary credit."* All three gates (self-flag, commitment type, comp regex) shared the monetary blind spot. The self-flag carries an explicit carve-out so information promises ("let me find out") don't over-fire — that over-fire direction is a worse bug than the one being fixed, because it stalls complaint threads on turns that commit nothing. `COMP_COMPLAINT_INSTRUCTIONS` states its prohibition flatly and first: the previous "do not promise X **unless** Y" was inverted by the model into standing permission, which it then self-authorized against an empty mechanics list.
- **Warm-but-gated complaint handling (v1.24.0; qualified by TAC-307):** a guest reporting a bad experience gets ONE clarifying question that auto-sends, then a warm, apologetic, comp-forward draft that QUEUES for operator approval and pushes. **The auto-sending clarifying question is conditional as of TAC-307** — it survives only when the `comp_complaint` hold came from the fleet-wide code default. At a venue that explicitly holds `comp_complaint` (or holds everything) from Command Center, the clarifying question queues too. See trigger 8 under *Approval policy gates*. The agent proposes generosity; the human authorizes it. Three parts: (1) `venue_configs.approval_policy` routes `comp_complaint` to `operator_approval` — a column seeded fleet-wide on 2026-04-27 that had **zero readers until v1.24.0**; the code-level `APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT` is the base and stored `perCategory` entries merge ON TOP, so the empty `{}` every venue carries can't erase the route and no data migration was needed. (2) `GeneratedMessageSchema.complaintIntent` (`clarifying | resolving | none`, REQUIRED so it costs zero against the TAC-300 optional budget) drives the only exemption, gated behind four checks in `lib/agent/complaint-routing.ts` that ALL fail toward queue. (3) `AiRuntimeContext.willBeReviewed` conditions the `## What this guest can access` block: when a human is guaranteed to see the draft it invites a proposal, otherwise the v1.23.0 denial stands **verbatim**. **The safety property: warmth is unlocked by the gate, never by loosening the brake.** `willBeReviewed` is FALSE for demo guests even on a routed category, because TAC-284's bypass returns `action:'send'` unconditionally and would ship a comp-forward draft to a real phone. Written after v1.23.0 fixed an unauthorized comp and, forty minutes later in UAT, produced "Sour matcha's usually a sign something was off with the prep. Noted." on a guest complaining about a bad drink — warmth on complaints had **never** been instructed in any prompt version, so this is authored behavior, not a restoration.
- **Universal voice rules R1-R34:** live in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` (the count grows — check the file, don't trust a number quoted elsewhere, including this one; a prior version of this line said R1-R15 while the file held 16). R11 landed with TAC-305; R12 (message splitting, TAC-313) is **RETIRED as of TAC-319 v1.31.0** — splitting moved out of the prompt into deterministic dispatch code (see "Message splitting" below) and the id is never reused; R13-R16 are the undisplayed guidance bullets (greeting / operator-instruction / Last-Visit / Unanswered-question — the last added by TAC-308), R17-R20 landed with TAC-314 (price scoping, nearby places, register mirroring, ## Length authority), and R21 landed with TAC-334 (don't volunteer advice the guest didn't ask for). **Numbering is positional, APPEND-ONLY (TAC-314), and retired ids are never reused (TAC-319)** — never insert mid-list; renumbering or recycling live rule IDs stales every external reference (this file, tickets, tests, anti-pattern prose). Consequence: `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` curates a deliberately NON-contiguous set (R1-R11 + R17-R18 + R21) and its lockstep test asserts the exact id sequence, not contiguity. Apply to every agent on every venue. Don't reference actions the guest didn't take, give today's specific answer, no em dashes, don't reference physical artifacts the agent doesn't have, don't redirect to alt channels for things the venue can answer, yes/no questions get yes/no answers, don't restate context already in the conversation. **TAC-324 (v1.32.0) edits two rules' WORDING without touching numbering or R11/R12.** R1 gains a narrow exception: when the runtime context confirms a `qr_scan` guest's first-ever inbound inside a freshness window, the model may greet them as present (not narrate the scan itself) — see "First-touch intentions" below. R15 (Last-Visit, undisplayed) is scoped rather than reworded: its one-item cap was always about backward references to past visits; a forward recommendation is now explicitly carved out as a separate act that doesn't count against it. `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY`'s R1 entry moves in lockstep (its `summary` text); R15 isn't displayed, so no other registry entry changes. **TAC-329 (v1.34.0) rewords R1's carve-out RATIONALE, not its gate.** The TAC-324 physical-presence justification ("greet them as present... the way you'd greet a person standing in front of you") licensed present-tense location assumptions and was observed producing "Password's on the board when you get here" sent to a guest who'd left the venue twenty minutes earlier. Replaced with channel-context framing: the guest knows which number they just texted and why, regardless of where they physically are. `firstTouchAfterQrScan` (the condition that fires the exception) is unchanged; only the prose teaching the model what the exception means moved. `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY`'s R1 summary moves in lockstep again. **R21 (v1.37.0, TAC-334) closes a gap R11 does not cover.** R11 governs how a delivered recommendation, description, or fact ENDS; it never fires on a turn that isn't delivering one in the first place. Found in UAT: a guest reported an order already placed ("I got a cortado") and Sana evaluated the choice and proposed a different one for next time, unprompted — the assistant reflex of holding knowledge and therefore deploying it. R21's trigger is scoped to a guest statement containing no question, so a recommendation request ("what should I get") or opinion request ("is the cortado good") never reaches the prohibition at all — the boundary lives in the trigger clause, not in an exception bolted onto an absolute ban, deliberately avoiding the TAC-330 v1.35.0 self-contradicting-qualifier failure mode. `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` gains R21, displayed alongside its closest siblings R10/R11 (both also govern when a recommendation or opinion is permitted). Known adjacent tension, not resolved here: R15 permits volunteering a forward recommendation off the Last-Visit block "when the moment calls for it," independent of whether the current turn asked anything — R21's trigger is about the guest's current-turn statement only, so the two don't literally conflict, but a `welcome`-category turn combining both could still read as volunteered advice. Flagged during TAC-334 UAT (see the ticket for the fifth, reported-not-resolved UAT case); adjacent to TAC-318 (ledger hygiene) and TAC-331 (extraction mood), neither of which this ticket touches. **R22 (v1.38.0, TAC-314 second round) is undisplayed, appended after R21.** Promoted from the jurisdictional carve-out sentence TAC-330 (case 2) added locally to `acknowledgment.ts` — a category's register guidance is never authority over whether the model acts on an open goal from the intentions block. That principle is general, not `acknowledgment`-specific, so it moved to the universal layer where it protects every category from the same silent-veto failure class instead of requiring the same audit-and-patch cycle per category. The local sentence was deleted from `acknowledgment.ts` as a paired move in the same PR — same treatment R17 got. See "Category instruction layer carries NO form authority (TAC-314)" below for the full second-round accounting, including the sibling candidate that was evaluated and deliberately NOT promoted. **R23-R28 (v1.42.0, TAC-348) promote six cross-venue rules mined from Mock Sextant's manual venue-rule history, displayed alongside R17/R18/R21.** R23: no visit-count or tracking language, scoped explicitly against R15 so the two don't collide (R15 permits WHAT the guest had; R23 bans HOW OFTEN); promoted out of `follow-up.ts`, where the equivalent line was removed in the same PR. R24 + R25 + R26 are a deliberately coherent set on menu-item description: don't over-explain familiar drinks, no bare comma-list of ingredients, cap recommendations at two with varied phrasing — R25's content used to live inside the now-retired R12 and was lost when TAC-319 deleted that bullet wholesale. R27: never refer to yourself in the third person when speaking as a named person — TAC-338 explicitly deferred this exact prohibition pending "UAT evidence [that] a symptom survives," which it did (`owner` persona: "lemme check with Himanshu"); scoped to SELF-reference only, since TAC-338 already rejected a broader "them" ban for misfiring on ordinary correct staff speech. R28: never blame or criticize a staff member to a guest. Two candidates from the original nine-item audit were dropped in plan review rather than promoted: a universal "don't push a return visit" ban would contradict `comp-complaint.ts`'s own approved remedy design (a return-visit invitation IS the default comp), and a universal "don't ask curiosity questions" ban would silently outrank the first-touch intentions block's conditional restraint with no carve-out — the same silent-veto class TAC-327/TAC-330 already closed once. **R8 and R11 were reworded, not renumbered, in the same PR** — both gaps are narrow extensions of an existing rule's own stated purpose. R8 now also bans claiming to have personally witnessed the guest ("I saw you earlier") even when the guest's own message confirms the underlying fact (distinct from R1, which is about unconfirmed actions — here the action WAS confirmed, only the sensory-witness claim was invented). R11 now covers the whole description, not just the closing sentence — the original UAT gap was a closer, but the same flourish moved earlier in the sentence wasn't covered by wording that only named "a closing sentence." **R29-R31 (v1.43.0, TAC-356) promote three more rules mined the same way, displayed alongside R17/R18/R21/R23-R28.** R29 permits a sentence fragment when it reads naturally ("Open until 3" beats "We are open until 3pm today"), written as permission rather than a mandate and self-contained (it states its own deference to a full-sentence venue voice rather than relying solely on `# Voice imperative` three sections later). R30 says to ask what a guest means rather than guess when their message is unclear, with an explicit carve-out against the `unknown` category's own classifier-driven holding response (TAC-240's low-confidence routing is a system-decided outcome, a different kind of "unclear" than content ambiguity within an otherwise-confidently-categorized message) — the same scoping technique R23 used against R15. R31 bans naming a specific product in reply to a greeting or any content-less message, the fix for a real production incident at Le Mil's (a guest's "hey" got "come try Indian coffee sometime" back); the category a bare guest greeting actually classifies to is `casual_chatter`, whose only instruction was "stay in voice" with nothing stopping this. All three were audited against the first-touch intentions opener (TAC-324/329) and found compatible: the opener only ever asks a question, never names a product, so R31 doesn't conflict, and R30's trigger is a structural flag unrelated to message clarity. No conflicts required dropping a candidate this round. **R32-R34 (v1.44.0, TAC-359) promote a third round of three more rules mined the same way, from a second Le Mil's owner-review run, displayed alongside R17/R18/R21/R23-R31.** R32: never tell the guest to send a message, reach out, or get in touch as if that's a separate, future action, since they're already texting. Checked against `invite_contact_save` (`lib/agent/intentions/definitions.ts`), which legitimately invites a guest to save the number and text again later — the rule scopes to same-turn redundancy and carries an explicit carve-out for future contact, plus a boundary note against R5 (alt-channels is a different axis: routing elsewhere vs. treating the current channel as a future action) so the two don't sit unacknowledged near each other. R33: when retrieved venue knowledge narrates a first-visit order as a multi-step sequence, recommend only the first step. This is the first universal rule of this promotion pattern that DIRECTS rather than prohibits — a straight ban on sequences would be outranked by the venue's own knowledge_corpus content, which renders later in the prompt than this section (`compose-prompt.ts`: template → persona → venue_info → voice corpus → knowledge corpus → category instructions), the same most-proximate-wins failure class as TAC-314/329/330/338. venue_info itself was audited and found clean (not a repeat of TAC-330's "Maiden Voyage" defect); the contributor was an accurate, correctly-attributed knowledge_corpus chunk. Carries a boundary against R26 (item count vs. framing). R34: the agent cannot place, confirm, or take an order — acknowledge what the guest wants and defer to how that specific venue actually takes orders, never asserting a channel (an early draft said "at the counter," which would misinstruct any venue without counter service). Two carve-outs: `# Commitments` holds are scoped to items that already exist AND (TAC-301 part 2) to venues whose services block says they hold at all (a made-to-order drink is not held, it is made, so prep instructions stay on the order-taking side), and a guest reporting an order already placed points at R21 rather than restating it, so TAC-323's `extract-reported-order.ts` (past-tense reports) isn't touched. A redundancy pass against all 30 pre-existing bullets found none fully subsumed by R32-R34; the two boundary notes above (R32→R5, R34→R21) were the actionable output instead of a removal. An early R34 draft also leaked a venue-specific drink name and a venue-specific ingredient verbatim from the motivating case; the first no-venue-names pass had only checked for em/en dashes, not actual names, so a lowercase, uncapitalized brand name survived a "verified clean" claim. Fixed by genericizing the example and adding a real name-blocklist check before landing.
- **Identity framing (Core principles, TAC-338, v1.39.0):** `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`'s opening line and the fourth `# Core principles` bullet establish that the agent works at the venue — never phrased as representing it "on the venue's behalf." That phrasing is now a banned framing repo-wide for this identity fact (grep before reintroducing it in prompt text describing who is speaking); it read as third-party, agent-for-hire framing and was the root cause of a voice defect where the agent referred to venue staff as "them" and to itself in the third person ("tell them Sana said..."). Two legitimate `speakerFraming: 'named_person'` deployments exist — a real staff member replying as themselves, and a persona standing in for a real role that isn't a specific individual (a maître d', for instance) — and neither `BrandPersonaSchema` nor the prompt distinguishes them with a dedicated field; the identity text is written to hold for either. `voiceName` (`BrandPersonaSchema`, Command-Center display label) is confirmed NEVER rendered into the prompt — the name the model actually knows itself by is `speakerName`, rendered in `speakerFramingProse`'s `named_person` branch (`lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`). Deliberately no symptom-level ban (no "never refer to yourself in third person," no "them" prohibition) — plan review rejected that as the same technique that hit its ceiling with R21 (TAC-334: naming "good pick" / "the right call" didn't stop "good call"), and a "them" ban would misfire on ordinary correct staff speech ("Kinani will have it ready, ask him at the counter"). Fix the misstatement, verify via UAT, add a prohibition later only with evidence a symptom survives. **TAC-348 (v1.42.0) removes the same branch's signing instruction.** `named_person` previously told the model "Sign messages as {speakerName}." — real iMessage/SMS threads don't carry signatures, and at least one venue needed a manual anti-pattern rule to undo it. Removed outright, not reworded; the sentence had no other job. The rest of the branch (staff-identity framing, the "not an outside service" line) is unchanged.
- **Mechanic eligibility (THE-170):** mechanics with `min_state` set are filtered out of `RuntimeContext.mechanics` when the guest's recognition state is below the gate. Mechanics with active redemptions are also filtered (`one_time` policy blocks forever after any `mechanic_redeemed` event; `renewable` blocks within `redemption_window_days`). Helpers: `isStateAtLeast` and `filterEligibleMechanics` in `lib/recognition/`. The filter runs at load time in `build-runtime-context.ts` after `recognition.state` is computed, sharing `computedAt` so the filter timestamp matches the recognition snapshot. Empty list is meaningful — the serializer renders an explicit "do not offer perks of any kind" instruction when `mechanics: []`. Empty-list framing is load-bearing for closing the eligibility-leak failure case where Sonnet would otherwise improvise mechanic offers from voice corpus matches.
- **Voice vs knowledge retrieval:** the agent retrieves from two corpora. `voice_corpus` (always-on, fail-closed) supplies HOW the venue speaks — style exemplars rendered as `## Examples of how the venue actually communicates`. `knowledge_corpus` (gated by `shouldRetrieveKnowledge`, fail-graceful) supplies WHAT IS TRUE about the venue — narrative content (sourcing stories, staff personalities, mechanic explanations, philosophy) rendered as `## Venue knowledge` with `[primary: ...]` and `[secondary: ...]` lines per chunk. Knowledge fires for every inbound and for followup triggers `event` / `manual`; skips `day_*` cron triggers. Failure asymmetry is intentional — voice failure breaks voice fidelity (the whole point of the message), knowledge failure just means a less specific reply, still in the venue's voice. Knowledge retrieval uses `match_knowledge_corpus` (migrations 013 + 017), top-K=4, with a `KNOWLEDGE_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR_DEFAULT` of 0.7 (matches the classifier low-confidence threshold for symmetry).
- **Knowledge corpus confidence floor (TAC-242):** `KNOWLEDGE_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR_DEFAULT = 0.7` is applied as the default `min_confidence` on every `match_knowledge_corpus` call. Pre-TAC-242 retrieval passed no floor (admitted all chunks); the new floor retroactively excludes any `confidence_score < 0.7` row from the prompt. Production seed defaults are `0.85` (direct quotes) and `0.85` (synthesized) so this is a no-op for normally-seeded venues, but hand-loaded low-confidence rows will silently disappear from retrieval. Operators can override per-call via `RetrieveKnowledgeContextInput.minConfidence` (passing `0` is honored).
- **Knowledge corpus relevance floor (TAC-350; RECALIBRATED to 0.30 by TAC-358 — read this first):** the floor is a **sanity bound, not a relevance filter**. TAC-350 introduced it at 0.5 as a proxy for topicality; TAC-358 measured that proxy against Le Mil's live corpus and **the distributions invert** — "where is the bathroom" (genuinely unanswerable) scores 0.5439, above EVERY answerable terse recommendation question (max 0.4971, the Blossom Tonic chunk ranked #1 and deleted three thousandths under the floor). No threshold fixes it: an absolute floor is boxed in (any guard above 0.3250 kills a real answer, any below 0.3991 admits a weather question), a relative margin admits the top match for every query by construction, and peak shape separates no better. A chunk written to contain the question verbatim still scored 0.4871. **Cosine here tracks query length and specificity, not answerability**, so the floor stopped judging relevance and the semantic call moved to `verify-grounding`. 0.30 sits below every measured on-topic query and excludes essentially nothing. Queue volume was measured BEFORE the change, not assumed: 5/36 queued at 0.5 vs 3/36 at 0.30 across two runs of 18 queries through the real generate + verify path, with off-topic questions still declining cleanly. **The `knowledgeChunksToProse` header reframing ships with it and the two cannot be separated** — those declines were measured under the old "facts you can ground replies in" wording, which is exactly what invites building on weak chunks. **At 0.30 it coincides with `lib/rag`'s `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` and therefore filters NOTHING today** — `retrieveKnowledgeContext` already drops anything below 0.3 on the same field with the same `>=`. Stated plainly because an inert constant with a long justification is how `approval_policy` went 102 days unread. **The CHANGE was not inert**: at 0.5 this filter was deleting the 0.3–0.5 band `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` had already admitted, and the headline query went from 0 chunks to 4. The constant survives as a separate lever because `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` serves retrieval generally (voice corpus included) while this is the agent's knowledge gate specifically — the coincidence is not a guarantee, and someone tuning `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` for another caller must not silently move what the agent grounds a guest-facing reply in. **`knowledgeChunksToProse` is shared with `verify-grounding`**, so the reframed header renders in the verifier's source material too and its prompt version bumped alongside (v1.2.0). **Exposure recorded, not fixed (at the time):** `verifyGroundingStage` used to return `skipped` when `currentMessage === null`, so NO followup outbound had a grounding backstop — and `shouldRetrieveKnowledge` is true for `manual`/`event` triggers, where the retrieval query is the synthetic string `Followup {reason} for {name}`. **RESOLVED by TAC-367: none of these paths retrieve any more.** The holding-message generator and BOTH followup paths (`manual`, `event`) now set `ctx.knowledgeCorpus = []` unconditionally, so the 4-arbitrary-chunk exposure this paragraph described is closed. The followup change is an INTERIM narrowing, not the designed answer — TAC-367 PR 3 decides what followups should query on (the operator's own instruction for `manual`, the event being invited for `event`, both real text rather than a template string). Accepted loss, recorded because it is not pure narrowing: the `event` query did pull event-tagged chunks, since the word "event" is in the template string, so some were topically adjacent — about SOME event at the venue, not necessarily the one being invited to. Every measured query was an inbound, where the backstop existed at the time — since TAC-376, `verifyGroundingStage` runs on followups and the holding message too, so the once-uncovered population this sentence flagged now has the same check. **THE PARAGRAPH BELOW IS HISTORY** — it describes the original 0.5 calibration and its reasoning no longer applies. `KNOWLEDGE_RELEVANCE_FLOOR` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`), applied inside `retrieveKnowledgeStage` — a DIFFERENT axis from the confidence floor above (that one filters on the seed-time trust score `confidence_score`; this one filters on the per-query cosine `similarity`, i.e. is this chunk actually about what the guest asked). `lib/rag/retrieve.ts`'s own `SIMILARITY_FLOOR` (0.3, shared with voice retrieval) is too loose to do this job on its own: a TAC-350 audit found every one of 12 representative queries against a real venue's live corpus returned a full `KNOWLEDGE_RETRIEVE_LIMIT` (4) chunks above 0.3, including for a genuinely unanswerable question (wifi password) whose "best matches" were all topically unrelated — meaning `knowledgeChunksToProse`'s "no specific venue knowledge matched" framing (TAC-242) almost never fires once a corpus has more than a handful of rows, and the model is instead handed padding framed as "facts... you can ground replies in." 0.5 is calibrated against that same audit (min top-match similarity 0.5170 across 7 genuinely on-topic queries vs. max 0.4900 across 5 genuinely off-topic ones) and applied per-chunk, not as an all-or-nothing gate, so a strong match isn't diluted by weaker padding riding alongside it. Calibrated against one venue's corpus and embedding distribution; revisit if a differently-shaped corpus shows the gap doesn't hold. The zero-result-triggers-fallback rule (TAC-242's tag-preference retry) now triggers on zero chunks surviving this floor, not just zero rows returned.
- **Guest context block (TAC-296, v1.15.0):** `RuntimeContext.guestContext` carries the parsed per-guest accumulating context (dietary, home base, life events, observations) loaded from `guests.context` JSONB. Rendered as `## Guest context` in the user prompt by `formatGuestContext` (`lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`), inserted **between Visit history and Recent conversation** so reading order is "who they are → what they can get → what they've recently bought → what we know about them → what was recently said." No category gate — useful for every category including `welcome` (e.g., NFC-tap from a known phone whose context says "vegan"). Empty context omits the block entirely (zero tokens). Hard char budget ≈ 500 tokens; serializer truncates observations to 5 floor first, then drops oldest life_context entries if still over. Parser is permissive: `filterActiveLifeContext` drops expired + malformed `expires_at` entries per-entry (mirrors THE-150's `filterActiveContext`), `toParsedGuestContext` truncates observations to `OBSERVATION_RENDER_LIMIT`=10 at read time. The write path lives on `GeneratedMessageSchema.contextUpdate` (required object, inner `structured` + `observation` both optional — the no-op shape is `{}`); dispatched by `handle-inbound.ts` + `handle-followup.ts` **between `generateStage` success and `applyApprovalPolicyStage`** so context capture reflects what the agent UNDERSTOOD from the inbound regardless of whether the draft ships, queues, or refuses. Arrays in `structured` are REPLACED, not appended (per ticket spec — the prompt instruction tells the model to include any existing entries it wants to keep when emitting a life_context patch). `observation` shortcut appends to `observations[]` with runtime-stamped `captured_at`. Identity-column sync (`first_name` / `last_name`) happens inside the same UPDATE statement (multi-SET, row-atomic). Last-write-wins concurrency — two near-simultaneous inbounds from the same guest can clobber each other's deep-merge; documented in "Common gotchas." Hallucination guardrail is the prompt rule + a Langfuse `context_write` span tag — no refusal regex, no fidelity self-check.
- **Knowledge corpus tag-aware retrieval (TAC-242, v1.12.0):** knowledge_corpus rows carry a two-bucket tag model. `primary_tags` is a closed enum (`sourcing`, `staff`, `mechanic`, `menu`, `philosophy`, `recommendations`, `events`, `history`, `space`, `policies`, `logistics`, `other` — namespacing allowed: `staff_phoebe`, `mechanic_<slug>`) used for retrieval routing; `secondary_tags` is free-form descriptive context that surfaces in the prompt but doesn't affect routing. Validated at the parse boundary via `isCanonicalPrimaryTag` in `lib/schemas/knowledge-tags.ts` — fail-loud on non-canonical primary tags so extraction drift surfaces immediately. The category → primary tag preference map (`lib/agent/knowledge-tag-mapping.ts`) sets routing for four categories: `mechanic_request` and `perk_inquiry` → `mechanic`; `recommendation_request` → `recommendations` / `menu` / `sourcing`; `event_question` → `events`. Other categories use cosine-only retrieval. When a category's preference yields zero matches (sparse corpus for that topic), `retrieveKnowledgeStage` falls back to a no-filter query so recall doesn't collapse. The same pattern is mirrored in `lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.ts` for the regen path. The `## Venue knowledge` block now ALWAYS renders when retrieval ran (even on empty results — explicit "no specific venue knowledge matched" framing makes R9 fire reliably); the block is omitted only when `knowledgeChunks` is `undefined` (retrieval was gated off). The legacy `tags` column on `knowledge_corpus` stays for one cycle and is dropped in a follow-up migration. Adding a category to the routing map requires an explicit code change to `CATEGORY_TO_PRIMARY_TAG_PREFERENCE`.

- **Follow-up context block (TAC-244, v1.20.0; multi-reason intake + perk_unlock TAC-123, v1.21.0):** the AI-facing `RuntimeContext.followup` (`lib/ai/types.ts`) carries an outbound-only render payload — `{ reasons: FollowupReason[]; daysSinceLastVisit: number; anchorVisit?: FollowupAnchorVisit }`. Rendered as `## Follow-up context` in the user prompt by `formatFollowupContext` (`lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`), inserted **immediately BEFORE `## Visit history`** (intent-then-evidence — this block states *why* we're reaching out; visit history is the supporting detail it draws on). `FollowupReason` is a closed enum: `post_visit_day_1 | post_visit_day_3 | post_visit_day_7 | post_visit_day_14 | cold_lapsed | perk_unlock` — DISTINCT from the orchestrator-side `FollowupTrigger.reason` union which also carries `event` / `manual` (those have dedicated context surfaces: `eventBeingInvited` and `operatorInstruction`). **Derived, not stored.** `buildAiRuntime` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) is the single mapping seam; `deriveFollowupContext(trigger, recentVisits, lastVisitAt, now)` returns `undefined` on the inbound path (trigger=null) AND on `event` / `manual` (no renderable reason). Anchor source: post_visit_* reasons default from `recentVisits[0]` (carries `items` so the prompt can name what the guest had); `cold_lapsed` and `perk_unlock`-only use `guests.last_visit_at` (date-only minimal anchor, items omitted) because a deep-lapsed guest's last visit can fall outside the recentVisits window (90d / 20 txn). Mixed runs (post_visit_* + perk_unlock, etc.) take the post_visit anchor — freshest visit wins for items context. `lastVisitAt` is loaded onto `GuestContext.lastVisitAt` (`lib/agent/types.ts`) by `build-runtime-context.ts`. **Multi-reason rendering (TAC-123, v1.21.0):** `deriveFollowupContext` consumes `[primary, ...trigger.additionalReasons]` — primary maps from `FollowupTrigger.reason`, additionalReasons rides as a typed-channel additive widening on the trigger (legacy single-reason callers omit it and get the length-one identity path). De-duplication is order-preserving with primary at the head. When `reasons.length > 1`, a positively-framed weaving rider ("Multiple reasons apply. Write the single text a thoughtful owner would actually send — touch what's genuinely worth mentioning, lead with one and fold in the other, drop one if it doesn't fit.") tells the model to weave via medium. The hard guardrail against leaking internal taxonomy (`post-visit day 7`, `cold lapsed`, `perk just unlocked`) to the guest lives in the `follow_up` category instruction (`lib/ai/prompts/categories/follow-up.ts`), not the block — the block uses positive medium-framing, the instruction carries the never-enumerate rule. **Inbound-XOR-outbound invariant** enforced by `throw`-on-violation entry-point assertions in `handleInbound` + `handleFollowup` (loud, dev-time-catching; top-level catch routes to `fireRedAlert` with stage='context_build') — same posture as TAC-234, no dependency on the TAC-243 discriminated-union refactor. Inbound output is unchanged by this block; the entry-point assertion guarantees `followupTrigger===null` there, so `deriveFollowupContext` returns `undefined` and the block is absent by construction. **Engine sends persist as `category='follow_up'`** regardless of which reasons fire — including perk-bearing runs (TAC-123 plan-review call). `triggerToCategory('perk_unlock')='follow_up'`; the existing `perk_unlock` MessageCategory remains for the inbound / standalone perk moment, different tuning. The perk detail itself rides as `perkBeingUnlocked` + a `'perk_unlock'` entry in `reasons[]`. Coexists with `## Visit history`, `## Perk being unlocked`, `## Event being invited` — when followup + perk/event both apply (a freshly unlocked perk on a day-7 touch), both blocks render and generation produces one woven message. No category gate (the follow_up-category instruction's pointer covers the natural caller; other categories never see this field set in practice).
- **Guest commitments block (TAC-297, v1.16.0; commitment-id render TAC-302, v1.18.0; emission discipline TAC-302 follow-up, v1.19.0):** `RuntimeContext.activeCommitments` carries the open + pending_ack rows for this guest at this venue (projected through `toActiveCommitment` in `lib/schemas/guest-commitment.ts`). Rendered as `## Active commitments` in the user prompt by `formatActiveCommitments` (`lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`), inserted **between Guest context and Recent conversation** so reading order is "...what we know about them as a person → what we've promised → what was recently said." Per-commitment line shape: `- [type] description (id: <uuid>, code: XXXX, status: ...) — promised <delta>` (code omitted for `recommendation`; id always rendered). Tail copy frames the block as PERMISSION to ask about arrival timing if it fits, NOT a standing directive (TAC-297 design call #5 — interrogation-risk mitigation). Empty list omits the block entirely (zero tokens). No category gate. The write path lives on `GeneratedMessageSchema.commitment` and `GeneratedMessageSchema.arrivalCapture` (both required objects with all-optional inner fields — the no-op shapes are `{}`, mirrors TAC-296 `contextUpdate`); helpers `isEmptyCommitmentEmission` + `isEmptyArrivalCapture` short-circuit before any DB hit. **The commitment path is post-send (not pre-gate)** — intent rides the queue via `messages.pending_commitment` jsonb and materializes only on successful dispatch (TAC-297 design call #1). **The arrival-capture path IS pre-gate** — fires regardless of whether the draft ships/queues/refuses, mirroring the TAC-296 `contextUpdate` precedent (what the agent UNDERSTOOD is independent of what we SAID back). `dispatchArrivalCapture` in `lib/agent/dispatch-arrival-capture.ts` is the shared helper called from both `handle-inbound.ts` (live push) and `handle-followup.ts` (defensive no-op). **TAC-302 (v1.18.0):** the `id:` segment is the verbatim value the model copies into `arrivalCapture.referencesCommitmentId`. Pre-TAC-302 the system prompt told the model to populate that field "from the ## Active commitments block" but the block didn't render the id — every arrival signal either no-op'd (id omitted) or hallucinated against the 4-char `code:` (CAS rowcount=0). Result: no commitment ever reached `pending_ack`, the imminent push never fired, the morning-of cron had nothing due. The system-template `# Arrival capture` block AND the serializer intro both carry the "id is system-internal, never read aloud, never include in your reply to the guest" framing — that's the primary line of defense against UUID leakage into conversational text (R1 / R7 don't directly cover structured-emission discipline). **TAC-302 follow-up (v1.19.0):** UAT after v1.18.0 confirmed the id-render fix landed (model's `content.reasoning` explicitly names the commitment by code), but surfaced the next layer — the model narrates the correct arrival action inside reasoning prose, then emits `arrivalCapture: {}` (no-op shape) because it talks itself out of the field with reasons like "the heads-up was already asked last turn, no need to repeat." The fix reframes the `# Arrival capture` block from action-based ("when to emit") to detection-based ("populate whenever ACTIVE COMMITMENTS non-empty AND inbound contains arrival intent — including confirmations, closers, previously-discussed times"), explicitly decouples the structured emission from the conversational heads-up ask ("detection, not communication"), enumerates four specific anti-patterns Sonnet uses to suppress emission and tags each `IRRELEVANT` / `A CONFIRMATION IS A SIGNAL` / `END-OF-CONVERSATION IS WHEN ARRIVAL DETECTION MATTERS MOST` / `DOESN'T MATTER`, and adds a worked example matching the prod failure trace (prior-turn heads-up ask → current-turn confirmation "ok i'll come in tomorrow around 8" → MUST emit `signal: "scheduled"`). No schema change — the schema is structurally permissive (no-op shape is valid by design per Anthropic structured-output reliability) and the lever is the prompt instruction. v1.17.0 (TAC-300) was a `GuestContextPatchSchema` slim reshape with no runtime-contract block change; documented under "Common gotchas → `GeneratedMessageSchema` optional-field budget."

- **At most one OPEN commitment per (venue, guest, description) — and the APP CHECK is the enforcement, not the index (TAC-318):** production carried a clean 2× duplication rate — two Le Mil's guests, two distinct recommendations, each minted twice within ~100 seconds, descriptions byte-identical. **The audit's finding, which determines where the fix belongs: it is NOT a double-invoke.** `createCommitmentFromPending` is the only INSERT into `guest_commitments` in the repo, it fires once per DISPATCHED response (TAC-313 anchored it outside the per-bubble loop), and `generateStage`'s retry happens before any send. The duplicate pairs had two distinct `source_message_id`s — one `auto_sent`, one `approved`, which are two DIFFERENT mint call sites reachable only from two separate agent runs. So N rows means N generations means N independent emissions: **the agent re-emitted `commitment` on a later turn.** It does so because `# Commitments` triggers per turn on "what your reply *is promising*", nothing in that block or the `## Active commitments` intro tells the model an open row already exists, and the adjacent `# Arrival capture` block was deliberately written (v1.19.0) to teach the *opposite* discipline in the strongest terms in the prompt ("Emit on **every turn**… **DOESN'T MATTER**"). Two structured fields, adjacent sections, opposite emission disciplines, and only one of them says which it is. **The prompt half is NOT fixed — deliberately out of scope for TAC-318, and it is the half that stops the agent re-raising the same promise with the guest.** This layer only makes the ledger correct regardless of how often that happens; the pestering the ticket describes is still live. Two enforcement layers, and the ordering is the point: **(1) an app-level check-then-insert inside `createCommitmentFromPending`** reads the guest's open rows and resolves a match to the existing row — this is what stops the write, and `commitmentDedupKey` (exported, `trim().toLowerCase()`) is literally the decision, mirroring migration 037's SQL expression; **(2) the partial unique index** is the backstop for the check-then-insert TOCTOU window and for Postgres/JS normalization divergence, caught as `23505` and resolved to the existing row rather than surfaced as an error. **Fail-OPEN on a dedup READ failure** — proceed to insert, because losing a real commitment to a hiccuped SELECT is far worse than a duplicate the index will reject anyway. The description match runs in JS, not SQL, on purpose: matching `lower(trim(description))` through PostgREST needs `ilike` with pattern-escaping for `%`/`_` in free-form model prose, and it would put the decision somewhere a unit test can only observe through a mock's opinion of a query string. **A repeat bumps `updated_at` and never `created_at`** — `created_at` renders as "promised N ago" and TAC-341 will key the recommendation horizon off it, so bumping it would misreport the age and make a stale commitment immortal; a failed touch still returns the deduped row rather than converting a correct dedup into an error. **Dedup covers EVERY type, not just `recommendation`, because the index does** — an app check narrower than its own index would silently stop running for the types it excluded, leaving only the 23505 path. **Excluding `type` from the key creates one path where a plain reuse LOSES a real promise, and code review caught it before merge:** an open `recommendation: "croissant"` absorbing a later operator-approved `comp: "croissant"` returned `ok` while recording no comp — no verification code, arrival push announcing "recommendation", the operator's authorisation leaving no trace. The sequence is realistic (recommend it, guest says it was stale, comp it), and narrowing the app check to same-type does NOT fix it because the index excludes type too, so the insert would take a 23505 and land in the same place. `shouldUpgrade` is the fix and it is ONE-DIRECTIONAL: `recommendation → comp/hold/discount` upgrades the row in place. **The FIELD SET is the half that actually closes the defect, and it is pinned with `toEqual`, never `toMatchObject`** — a partial match passes while `code` silently goes missing, which is the original bug's exact shape (a comp on the ledger with no verification code). THREE columns move: `type`, `code`, `source_message_id`, plus `updated_at`. That set is decided by where the arrival push reads: BOTH call sites (`handle-inbound.ts`, `commitments-due.ts`) take `type` and `code` OFF THE ROW, so the row is what determines whether the push says "comp … Q4X9" or "recommendation" with nothing. Mutation-verified in both directions — dropping any one of the three fails a test, and ADDING any of `created_at` / `status` / `created_by` / `description` / `expires_at` fails one too. **`expires_at` is the field worth reading the reasoning on, because BOTH obvious behaviours are wrong and it was written the wrong way first.** Today it is inert — the prompt never mentions `expiresAt` (zero hits in `lib/ai/prompts/`), so the model never emits one, `pendingFromEmission` yields null, creation writes null, and nothing reads the column. Once TAC-341 lands: writing the emission's value (what this code did until the ruling) OVERWRITES TAC-341's derived expiry with null, and a null expiry never elapses, so the expiry job can never move the row to `expired` and an upgraded comp stays open forever; while keeping the recommendation's own horizon gives an upgraded comp 30 days where a new comp gets two years, so a venue's obligation dies quietly after a month. **TAC-341 owns every expiry derivation, including the upgrade case** (operator ruling, 2026-09-13) — deriving it here would put a second derivation site in a file that does not own the horizons, which is how the two drift. A dedicated test with a NON-NULL `expiresAt` in the fixture pins the omission, so it reads as deliberate rather than as null-in-null-out, and a local recompute — option A by stealth — fails three tests. **Three more fields deliberately do not move and the reasons differ**: `created_at` (renders as "promised N ago", TAC-341 keys expiry off it), `created_by` (hardcoded `'agent'` at the only insert site and defaulted `'agent'` in migration 026, so it is invariant on this path — there is nothing to carry), and `description` (equal under `commitmentDedupKey` by construction; only case and whitespace can differ). `expected_arrival` / `arrival_signal` also stay: if the guest already signalled against the recommendation, that signal is about the same visit and survives the type change. **There is no gating field to carry, and that is recorded as a test so the question isn't re-asked** — `guest_commitments` has no gating column; the gate is `isCommitmentTypeGated(generation)`, which reads the EMISSION pre-dispatch, so by the time an upgrade runs the operator has already approved; **anything → `recommendation` keeps what's there**, because downgrading would destroy a code the guest has already been texted; gated → a different gated type keeps what's there and emits, since picking a winner is a product call nobody has made. Both dedup paths route through one `resolveToExisting` helper so the app-check path and the 23505 path cannot drift — the race path is the worse of the two to forget an upgrade in, since it is reached exactly when the app check already missed. Comp lifecycle is untouched in the sense that matters: codes, `pending_ack` and the approval gate are unchanged, a comp is never downgraded, and a repeat of the SAME comp keeps the guest on the one code already texted. **Observability is not optional here and was also a review finding:** the ticket's premise was a MEASURED duplication rate, and with only `console` output the fix's effect would be visible solely in ephemeral Vercel logs — CLAUDE.md's own "distrust any gate whose true-positive history you cannot produce on demand" applies verbatim. `captureCommitmentDeduped` fires on every hit (carrying `via: 'app_check' | '23505'` and both types, Slack-relaying only on a type change, since a same-type dedup is the expected steady state and would be pure noise) and `captureCommitmentDedupCheckFailed` fires on the fail-open read failure, which is the condition under which the primary enforcement is inert while the table still looks correct. The dedup read also logs rather than silently skipping an unparseable row: `GuestCommitmentRowSchema` closes enums on `type`/`status`/`arrival_signal`/`created_by`, so a future migration widening any of them without updating the schema would switch dedup off fleet-wide with nothing to show for it. **Known limit, documented rather than solved:** exact-match leaves near-duplicates alone — Mock Sextant's "cortado or Frosty Gandhi" beside a bare "cortado" stays two rows. The proposed next step is whole-word containment (keep the shorter), which collapses that pair without embeddings or a similarity threshold, but it can over-collapse where exact match only ever under-collapses, and under-collapsing is the safe direction. Not built without its own evidence; there is a test pinning the current non-collapsing behaviour so a future change to it is deliberate.

- **Every obligation reaches a terminal state, and escalation precedes it (TAC-341):** migration 026 documented `open → expired → redeemed` and **only `open` was ever built** — `expired` and `redeemed` were legal enum values with zero writers, and `expires_at` was write-only (set on insert from an agent emission the prompt never asked for, so null on all nine production rows, read by nothing). TAC-341 builds the expiry half. **Scope is OBLIGATIONS ONLY — `comp`, `hold`, `discount`.** A `recommendation` promises nothing and owes nothing, so a horizon for one was always arbitrary; it is an intention and TAC-380 owns it. That cut is expressed once, as `OBLIGATION_TYPES` in `lib/guests/commitment-expiry.ts`, and enforced as an **ALLOWLIST at the SQL scan** (`.in('type', [...])`) rather than a `!== 'recommendation'` exclusion — a fifth type added later defaults to being left alone instead of silently inheriting the comp horizon from a negation nobody revisited. There is a source-level test for this because an exclusion would pass every behavioural test in the file.
  **`expires_at` is SERVER-DERIVED, never agent-set.** The emission's own `expiresAt` is now ignored outright (it has always arrived null — zero mentions of the field in `lib/ai/prompts/`), and no prompt instruction was added for it: the agent has no reliable sense of time and an agent-populated expiry is one more claim needing verification. Horizons: comp and discount get **60 days** from creation; a `hold` expires at **venue close on the day it was set aside**, because a hold is a physical item and is day-of by nature. **The comp horizon was 2 years for exactly one merge and was revised to 60 days on 2026-09-14 — read that as an inverted argument, not a tightened number.** At 2 years expiry was DECORATIVE: escalation at 7 days was the entire live mechanism and the horizon existed only so the row had a terminal state to reach eventually. At 60 days expiry actually fires, which is a deliberate change to the promise (defensible for a café — an unclaimed free drink two months on is not going to be claimed) and makes the 7-day escalation proportionate, an eighth of the obligation's life rather than noise against 730 days. The superseded docstring argued that LONG was the safe direction because too-short refuses a guest at the counter for a drink the venue promised; that risk is now accepted rather than dismissed. **The ordering between the two constants is the thing to preserve, and it now has its own test** — a horizon shorter than `COMP_ESCALATION_DAYS` would make every comp reach expiry before its escalation window arrived, converting the `|| isElapsed` catch-up branch from a last resort into the primary path for the whole fleet. It would still work; it would just stop meaning what its comment says.
  **The hold horizon is derived exactly where venue hours are absent, which is why the load lives in the helper.** Gated types always queue (`commitment_type_gated`), so an obligation is materialized from `dispatchOperatorOutbound`, which holds a message row and never loads `venue_configs`; the one call site that does have `ctx.venue` in scope (`scheduleAndSend`) only carries obligations for demo guests. So caller-passes-it would mean the path that needs the clock cannot supply it. `createCommitmentFromPending` loads `venues.timezone` + `venue_configs.venue_info.hours` **lazily, only for `hold`** — a comp pays for no extra round trip. That load parses the **hours SUB-SCHEMA**, never the whole `VenueInfoSchema`, which requires `address`: parsing the whole thing means an unrelated missing field silently costs a hold its horizon. Caught by a test whose fixture carried only the field the function actually reads.
  **When hours are unreadable the horizon falls back to 23:59 venue-local AND escalates** (ruled 2026-09-13). Both obvious alternatives are wrong for a physical item: failing open means the hold never expires, failing closed means it expires at the moment of creation and the guest is refused an item on the shelf. A **stated `closed` on the creation day is folded in with `unknown`** (ruled 2026-09-14) — someone set an item aside at a venue the config says was shut, so the config is what is wrong. **This is deliberately the opposite call from TAC-377**, where an unreadable clock makes the precision guard go inert: there the cost is a slightly wrong timestamp, here it is a pastry nobody claims. An overnight range (`5:00 PM – 2:00 AM`) closes on the NEXT local day, or the hold expires ~22 hours early.
  **The fallback stamps `escalated_at` AT CREATION, and that is the only moment it can be** — a 23:59 guess is indistinguishable later from a venue that genuinely closes at midnight, so the cron reading the row tomorrow could not tell.
  **The upgrade case (TAC-318 §3 item 6) is closed here.** An open `recommendation` upgraded to a `comp` arrives with a null `expires_at` because TAC-318 deliberately declined to derive one; the upgrade now takes the comp horizon **keyed off the row's own `created_at`, never `now`** — the promise is as old as it always was, and keying off `now` would extend the horizon every time the guest mentioned it. TAC-318's `toEqual` field-set test was REVERSED rather than deleted, and `expires_at` left its must-NOT-move list, which is exactly what that ticket set up.
  **Escalation is DATA + TELEMETRY ONLY — do not touch `listHeadsUpQueue`.** It feeds `app/api/operator/queue/route.ts`, cross-repo Contract surface consumed by the iOS client, and widening its `pending_ack` filter would make this a cross-repo ticket needing a `## Contract`, server-first sequencing and curl verification. TAC-381 renders escalated rows in Command Center; TAC-378 follows for the operator app. **Escalation never changes `status`** — the row stays `open` because the promise is still owed.
  **`serializers.ts` needed NO change, and the reason is worth knowing before anyone "wires up" expiry rendering:** `findActiveCommitmentsForGuest` filters `.in('status', ['open','pending_ack'])` at the DB and `toActiveCommitment` filters again in TS, so writing `expired` removes the row from `## Active commitments` by construction. No serializer edit, no `PROMPT_VERSION` bump. The agent-runtime blast radius of this whole ticket is exactly one thing: the status writer.
  **"A comp is never silently expired without having surfaced first" is enforced by `|| isElapsed`**, not by the ordinary window check — if the cron were down for the whole escalation window a row arrives already elapsed and never surfaced, and would expire in silence. That branch is **unreachable through TAC-341's own derivation** (a comp's 7-day window always precedes its 2-year horizon; a hold's lead is defined relative to its own expiry), so it takes a horizon this code did not derive — which is precisely what the hand-applied Studio backfill is. **The first test written for it did not reach it and the mutant survived all twelve tests**; the replacement uses a hand-set short horizon and is mutation-verified. **`COMP_ESCALATION_DAYS = 7` and `HOLD_ESCALATION_LEAD_MINUTES = 120` are PLACEHOLDERS, not measurements** — labelled as such at the constants, because there is no comp volume to derive them from and a bare `7` reads as calibrated to the next person, which is how `KNOWLEDGE_RELEVANCE_FLOOR = 0.5` survived until TAC-358.
  **Redemption is NOT in this ticket.** Building a `redeemed` writer here would leave zero-reader code — this repo's recurring dead-layer pattern — and the real caller is the operator at the counter, not an Analog admin, so it ships with TAC-378.
- **Knowledge-gap routing (TAC-308, v1.25.0; independent backstop TAC-350):** when the agent answers a guest question it can't ground in venue knowledge, the reply is NOT sent. `GeneratedMessageSchema.knowledgeGap` (required boolean, `complaintIntent` precedent — costs zero against the TAC-300 optional budget) fires the `knowledge_gap` approval trigger, which queues the draft as a card AND stamps `messages.pending_until` — the first write to a column that had existed unwritten since migration 003. The body is the model's best attempt at the ANSWER, because that text becomes the prefilled draft an operator corrects; "let me find out" as a body would give them nothing to work with. **The prompt half is the load-bearing half.** THREE separate sites in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` taught "let me find out and get back to you" as correct behavior, on every venue and every turn — the universal rule endorsed it by name, the never-invent rule closed on it, and the physical-artifact rule offered it as the substitute. All thirteen unkept promises in Mock Sextant's history are downstream of instructions the prompt was giving. The ticket described this as a voice-corpus problem; the corpus rows were reinforcing the prompt, not causing it. A `# Knowledge gaps` block now defines the emission, with the distinction that stops it over-firing: a question nobody at the venue could answer either (weather, traffic) is NOT a gap — say you don't know and stop. Companion user-prompt block `## Unanswered question` renders immediately before `## Recent conversation` (next to the history the model would otherwise mine for an earlier promise to imitate) with a three-mode discriminator — `outstanding` / `acknowledged` / `writing_holding` — so the block never tells the model both to answer and not to answer the same question. (A fourth mode, `answering_after_holding`, existed briefly for the post-holding card regen; TAC-309 deleted that regen and the mode with it.) **`knowledgeGap` is pure self-report, and TAC-350 found it insufficient on its own:** an audit of a full TAC-347 harness run found 8/8 observed fabrications (an invented wifi password, four invented menu-item variation names, ingredients attributed to the wrong drink, etc.) had `knowledgeGap=false` — the prompt-level "never invent" instruction and the `# Knowledge gaps` self-assessment rule didn't catch a single one under real traffic. See "Approval policy gates" trigger 9b (`knowledge_gap_backstop`) below for the independent second check this added, and the "Knowledge corpus relevance floor" bullet above for a related contributing cause (irrelevant retrieved chunks presented as if they were grounding material).

- **A `knowledge_gap_backstop` card KEEPS its body (TAC-301 part 1.5) — this REVERSES TAC-350's "identical card treatment".** TAC-309's argument for blanking is specific to the self-report path: there the model ADMITTED it could not ground the answer, so the body is an acknowledged guess and one-swipe approval is the hazard. On the backstop path the model admitted nothing — the body is its confident answer and the flag is a second opinion that **can be wrong**. On 2026-09-13 it was wrong twice within two minutes of deploy, on replies that were entirely correct ("we're closed for the night, back at 7 tomorrow"); blanking destroyed a correct message and handed the operator an empty card with nothing to approve, so the guest got silence. Keeping the body makes every false positive of this class recoverable in one swipe instead of destructive, and costs nothing on a true positive (the operator sees the fabrication and edits or skips). `pending_commitment` now rides along too — TAC-309 stripped it because it was INVISIBLE on a blank card, and the body is visible again. `pendingUntil` still arms on both triggers: a guest owed an answer is owed one either way. Everything else about the two triggers stays identical. Two accepted costs, recorded rather than left to be discovered: `ungroundedClaims` does NOT reach the operator card (PostHog/Slack and Langfuse only), so the operator sees a fluent draft plus a generic label and has to spot the bad part themselves — and on a co-firing turn `PRIMARY_TRIGGER_PRIORITY` hands the label to another trigger, so the card says nothing about grounding at all; and `pending_commitment` now rides along with the body rather than being nulled. Surfacing the claim list on the card is the real fix and is deliberately not done here.
- **Knowledge-gap drafts are BLANK (TAC-309, v1.26.0).** TAC-308 shipped these cards prefilled with the model's best attempt; the first live one read *"Not sure on the specific matcha we source. I can find out if that matters for your order."* — the exact promise phrasing TAC-308 had just deleted from the corpus, sitting in a field an operator can approve with one swipe. The prompt rewrite stopped the agent SENDING that; it did not stop her WRITING it. **Reverses TAC-308 decision #3.** The model still writes a body (`body: z.string().min(1)` is unchanged, the dash regex needs real text, and `voiceFidelity` is self-assessed against it) — the discard happens at the persist boundary via `PersistQueuedDraftOptions.blankBody`, so the guess never reaches the database, is never stashed, and is never shown as a hint. `voice_fidelity` is nulled alongside, because a blank card carrying 0.85 claims a score for text that doesn't exist. **No migration was needed, and the reason is a trap worth remembering:** `messages_has_content` reads `CHECK (body <> '' OR array_length(media_urls,1) > 0 OR reaction_type IS NOT NULL)`, and `array_length('{}'::text[], 1)` is **NULL**, not 0. The whole expression evaluates to NULL for a blank draft, and a CHECK constraint passes on NULL — it is violated only by FALSE. Empty bodies were always legal. TAC-309's Phase 0 audit called this a blocker by reading the constraint text instead of evaluating it, and a migration on `messages` was approved on the strength of that before a rolled-back probe disproved it. **There is therefore NO database-level backstop on the blank-body path — the app-level guards are the only guards:** the trimmed check in `lib/messaging/send.ts` and the pre-flip refusal in `dispatchOperatorOutbound`. Three further consequences to know: the send-layer guard in `lib/messaging/send.ts` is now load-bearing and trimmed (`" "` used to ship); `dispatchOperatorOutbound` gained a pre-flip `empty_body` refusal (the send guard fires AFTER the optimistic `review_state` flip has already pulled the card out of the queue, which would strand the row); and `regenerateCardForElapsedWait` was DELETED — it existed for WYSIWYG on a prefilled body and had nothing left to keep in sync.
- **A knowledge-gap turn is exempt from `SEND_FIDELITY_FLOOR` (TAC-309).** `generateStage` skips the refusal when `knowledgeGap === true`. The floor stops a badly-voiced message reaching a guest; on this path nothing reaches the guest and the scored body is discarded, so refusing would gate CARD CREATION on the quality of text that never exists — and the refused branch returns without a card, producing the same silence the ticket exists to remove. The holding message stays fidelity-gated: it does reach the guest, and on this path it is the only text that does.
- **A generation crash produces a card, not silence (TAC-309).** `handle-inbound` retries `generateStage` once (the internal `MAX_ATTEMPTS` loop only covers fidelity/dash — a parse throw exits it immediately), then writes a blank card via `persistGenerationFailureCard` (`review_reason='generation_failed'` since TAC-364; `knowledge_gap` before that). It can't route through `applyApprovalPolicyStage` because the gate takes a `GenerateMessageResult` and a crash didn't produce one, so it reads both slots itself and calls `decideSlotAction` with the `regen_gap_card_only` policy (TAC-394: a blank crash card takes the conversation slot, so a comp card in the obligation slot is neither in its way nor at risk), and re-applies the gate's clock rule — a crash can't reset a deadline already running, and arms none while a knowledge-gap card sits in either slot. The timer, holding message and gap-card protection work unchanged because none of them key on the label; crashes stay separately visible because the red alert Slack-relays. `opted_out_at` suppresses the card; **`hold_all_outbound` does NOT** — a queue card IS the hold outcome, and suppressing it would restore the exact silence being fixed at the venues that asked for the most oversight. Inbound-only: a followup crash has no waiting guest.
- **`MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` is a schema-growth hazard (TAC-309).** Raised 500 → 1500. The emission serializes `body` / `voiceFidelity` / `reasoning` first and `knowledgeGap` / `contextUpdate` / `commitment` / `arrivalCapture` last, so exhausting the budget truncates mid-JSON and the whole object fails to parse as a generic "could not parse the response." `reasoning` is the only unbounded field and sits third; the system prompt now caps it at two sentences. Measured on live traffic: successful single attempts run 4.1–5.7s emitting ~121–299 tokens, while the 2026-08-08 crash ran 13.7s on a single call — ~450 tokens at the observed throughput, i.e. the old cap. **The correlation is the nasty part: the model reasons longest on questions it can't answer cleanly, so truncation preferentially killed the knowledge-gap path.** 500 predated TAC-296 / TAC-297 / TAC-308, each of which appended a required field to the tail while the cap stood still — the same failure class as TAC-300's optional-parameter budget, without the guardrail. `captureGenerationTruncated` now Slack-relays on `finishReason === 'length'` so the next one announces itself instead of costing a UAT session.

- **Message splitting (TAC-313 dispatch shape; TAC-319 v1.31.0 decision-maker):** a reply MAY arrive as separate messages, and as of TAC-319 the decision is **deterministic code, not model judgment**. Two prompt-side rounds (v1.30.0's two-job beat taxonomy, a canceled late-position re-surfacing) failed identically — splitting was a decision the model was allowed to not make, and in a ~50k-char prompt it reliably didn't (traces `700c4fe4`, `4c3c52c8`). Now `scheduleAndSend` calls `resolveDispatchBubbles(body, rng)` (`lib/agent/sentence-split.ts`, pure): strip stray `[[BREAK]]` markers as noise, sentence-split conservatively (boundary = `.?!` + whitespace + capital/digit/emoji opener; guards: ellipsis, abbreviation blocklist `St. Ave. Dr. a.m. p.m. etc. vs.`; prices/ratios/times safe by construction — no whitespace after their internal punctuation), then: 1 sentence → as-is; 2-3 sentences → ONE fair coin flip (`SPLIT_PROBABILITY` = 0.5, the only tuning knob) — split into per-sentence bubbles (terminal `.` stripped per piece, `?`/`!` kept, internal punctuation untouched) or send as one untouched block; 4+ sentences → NO flip, one block (the cap would force partial grouping, violating all-or-nothing). The rng is injectable via `scheduleAndSend` options (defaults to `Math.random` at the boundary; the pure module requires it as a parameter) so tests pin both branches. **The capital gate is deliberate (TAC-319 ruling #2):** production sends capitalize sentence starts; lowercase-after-boundary means no split, the safe failure direction. **Operator dispatch does NOT flip (ruling #3):** `dispatchOperatorOutbound` stays single-block — when a human wrote or approved exact text, sending it verbatim is the least surprising behavior; putting operator sends in the flip needs its own ticket and gate. The mechanics below are TAC-313's and unchanged: caps at `MAX_BUBBLES_PER_RESPONSE` (3) in the sender, loops — `markAsRead` once, then per bubble: typing indicator, `INTER_BUBBLE_GAP_MS` (1500ms, a fixed constant never derived from the opening send sequence — which is why TAC-421 could delete that sequence without touching this), send, persist. **One `messages` row per bubble**, sharing a `generation_id` (migration 032). That row-per-bubble shape is forced by evidence: the Sendblue status webhook resolves rows by `provider_message_id` and every send returns its own handle, so one row for two bubbles loses delivery tracking on all but the first. Splitting is universal across outbound categories — message structure, not a category rule. **Pure helpers live in `lib/agent/sentence-split.ts`** (`splitIntoSentences`, `stripTerminalPeriod`, `resolveDispatchBubbles`, `SPLIT_PROBABILITY`), `lib/agent/split-message.ts` (`collapseToSingleMessage` + the near-miss-tolerant matcher; `splitIntoBubbles` retired), and `lib/agent/group-responses.ts` (`groupIntoResponses`, the read-side inverse). There is no over-cap merge anymore — a body past the flippable sentence range simply doesn't split.
  - **The invariant is "no delimiter ever reaches the database" — and post-TAC-319 the delimiter is legacy noise, not signal.** The prompt no longer teaches `[[BREAK]]` at all; `resolveDispatchBubbles` strips any stray marker (via `collapseToSingleMessage` and its near-miss-tolerant matcher) BEFORE sentence detection, and the queue path still strips inside `buildOutboundInsert` + the regen UPDATE payload. `dispatchOperatorOutbound` dispatches `messages.body` unchanged. Accepted cost, reaffirmed by the TAC-319 ruling: an approved draft does not split where an auto-sent one would.
  - **Failure asymmetry, and the reason for it.** The boundary is "have we committed anything to the guest yet" (`persistedIds.length`), deliberately not a bubble index. Nothing committed → red alert + throw, exactly as before TAC-313. Something already committed → alert with `bubbleIndex` + **return successfully, truncated**. Throwing maps to `AgentResult.failed`, which for the follow-up engine calls `releaseFollowupLogClaim` and lets the next tick re-dispatch — sending the guest the first bubble a second time. A truncated reply is worse-than-intended; a duplicated one reads as broken.
  - **Counting responses, not rows.** Five surfaces were row-grained and are now response-grained, all via the same identity `coalesce(generation_id, id)`: `build-runtime-context` (fetches `MAX_HISTORY_MESSAGES * MAX_BUBBLES_PER_RESPONSE` rows then groups, so the cap is on responses), both prompt serializers (fixed at the projection — see the gotcha below), `lib/recognition/load-signals.ts` (via the `count_outbound_responses` RPC), and `list_operator_queue.recent_context`. The recognition one is not cosmetic: splitting inflates the `responseRate = replied / sent` **denominator only**, so a row count would walk guests downward out of `regular` with no change in their behavior.
  - **Untouched, verified:** the Command Center thread (already collapses same-direction bubbles within `SEQUENCE_GAP_MS`), trace prefetch (bubbles share a generation's trace), the reaction/media paths (`lib/messaging/expressions.ts` inserts directly and never reaches `scheduleAndSend`), follow-up Gate 1, APNs badge counts, `findExistingReply`, the pending-draft read (`loadPendingRowsBySlot` since TAC-394) / `findPendingQuestion`, webhook-silence. **The opening-sequence sleeps were explicitly out of scope for TAC-313 and were removed whole by TAC-421** — `lib/agent/timing.ts` is gone; see that gotcha.

- **Category instruction layer carries NO form authority (TAC-314, v1.29.0).** Governing principle, enforced by the forbidden-pattern test in `lib/ai/prompts/categories/index.test.ts`: a category instruction block governs what the turn is ABOUT — topic, intent, relevant content. It may not prescribe message structure, length, sentence count, splitting, hedging policy, or disclosure policy; those belong to the universal layer in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`. **Why this is load-bearing:** category blocks render LAST in the system prompt and `# Voice imperative` reads to the model as most-proximate-wins, so the layer that is conceptually the bottom of the hierarchy binds at the top. Three defects survived two full fix rounds (#110, #111) because fixes went into general layers while `"Keep it short, one or two short sentences total"` in the recommendation block made a two-beat split structurally impossible, price scoping lived only in `new_question` while the leak happened on `reply`, and the nearby-places carve-out lived only in venue data that renders earlier and loses. TAC-314 stripped 19 length directives, 4 structure directives, and 5 hedging/disclosure rules across 16 category files (`follow-up.ts` needed nothing) and promoted the two stranded rules to R17/R18. **Two deliberate keeps carry `TAC-314 KEEP` comments — do not strip them in a future sweep:** `personal_history_question`'s no-record handling (routing to `# Knowledge gaps` would open an operator card for "have I been here before?", which the record answers better than any operator) and `comp_complaint`'s ask-one-question shape (load-bearing for `complaintIntent` → the approval gate; stripping it changes gate behavior, not prose). A third near-keep, documented here rather than in code per the TAC-314 review: `unknown.ts`'s "will be followed up on" is NOT TAC-308 survivor phrasing — it is the category's real contract (the message routes to operator attention and the follow-up genuinely happens); the TAC-308 defect was promising a follow-up that never occurred. New category blocks must be born compliant — the reference shape is `new-question.ts`.

  **Second round (v1.38.0), 2026-09-08 — the FORM sweep re-audited fresh, plus one PURSUIT-adjacent promotion.** A same-day comment claimed 15 of 17 blocks were still untouched; that claim was wrong and was corrected on the ticket — the first round's FORM sweep genuinely covered all 17 files, and a from-source re-audit on this date found zero new FORM violations anywhere, including the five zero-traffic blocks. What the comment was right about, on a different axis: `acknowledgment`'s TAC-330 (case 2) jurisdictional carve-out sentence — "a category's register guidance is never authority over whether you act on a goal you're already carrying" — was itself category-scoped policy about which prompt LAYER decides pursuit, the exact kind of authority this ticket's governing principle exists to strip from category blocks, just on the PURSUIT axis (TAC-327) rather than FORM. **Promoted to universal R22** (see "Universal voice rules" above) rather than left local, because the failure it closes is structural: any future category block that bans a pivot or scopes a close could reintroduce the same silent veto TAC-327/TAC-330 found, and a universal rule closes the whole class at once. The local sentence was deleted from `acknowledgment.ts` in the same PR as a paired move (not a deletion) — the comment archaeology that had accumulated there across three tickets (TAC-327's original KEEP, its correction, TAC-330's carve-out) was collapsed into a short pointer back to this entry.

  **A second, structurally identical candidate was evaluated and deliberately NOT promoted.** The "don't push a return visit" restraint is duplicated across `acknowledgment.ts` ("do not push for a return visit"), `event-question.ts` ("Do not pivot to suggesting they come anyway"), and `follow-up.ts` ("Do not push a return visit explicitly") — same shape as the R22 candidate: pursuit policy, appearing in multiple files, tempting to generalize by the same "policy generalizes" logic. Not promoted because `comp-complaint.ts`'s entire approved remedy design IS a return-visit invitation ("find a way to make it up to them... asking them to come back and have another one on us... you want to see them again") — a universal ban would directly contradict the venue's own product-thesis-level default remedy on the one category where the model must invite a return visit. Carving a comp-complaint exception into the "universal" rule would recreate exactly the pattern this ticket exists to eliminate. Stays local in all three files, unchanged. Revisit only if a future intention models generic return-visit nudging (`plant_next_visit` was cut from TAC-324's original scope, so nothing currently does).

- **Category instruction layer carries NO pursuit authority (TAC-327, v1.33.0).** Sibling boundary to the FORM rule above, same mechanism, different axis. A category instruction governs REGISTER — how to sound, how long, how literal, what shape the reply takes for this kind of turn. It may not prescribe PURSUIT — what goals are open, or the restraint around raising them — because that's the first-touch intentions block's job (`lib/agent/intentions/`, see "First-touch intentions" below), and intentions states its restraint CONDITIONALLY ("only raise one if the conversation opens a natural door," "never steer back to them"). An ABSOLUTE ban living in a category block outranks that conditional restraint by construction — category blocks render LAST in the system prompt, same load-bearing reason TAC-314 cites for FORM. **The concrete incident:** `casual_chatter` read "don't pivot to perks, events, or a service offer. Don't try to read a service intent into a friendly remark." — both PURSUIT rules in a REGISTER slot, duplicating (more strictly than) what intentions already conditionally states.
