Live evidence: guest `980ccc7d-eded-4b79-af0c-3a0610ad3753`'s first-ever message, "Hi Sana!", classified `casual_chatter` with BOTH `learn_first_order` and `invite_contact_save` correctly open in the rendered runtime context (TAC-326's boundary fix holds) — Sana raised neither, on the single most natural door a first-touch conversation offers, because the category's absolute ban silently overrode the feature's own correctly-conditional restraint. Fixed by DELETING the two lines from `CASUAL_CHATTER_INSTRUCTIONS`, not migrating them — intentions already states the restraint, and states it correctly. Enforced by a `FORBIDDEN_PURSUIT` regex run with `it.each(ROUND_TRIP_TABLE)` in `lib/ai/prompts/categories/index.test.ts` — a LITERAL-REVERT CANARY, not a semantic guard: it catches the exact deleted phrasing (whitespace/comma-tolerant) coming back verbatim, deliberately narrow rather than a generic "no pursuit-shaped language anywhere" rule, because a broader pattern would immediately false-positive on the three legitimate keeps below. A differently-worded reintroduction of the same restraint would pass this sweep silently; catching that needs human review at the next audit, same as TAC-314's sibling `FORBIDDEN_FORM` sweep.
  **Three-way audit classification used for the sweep:** REGISTER (keep — governs sound, not pursuit), PURSUIT-DUPLICATE (delete — restrains a goal intentions ALREADY conditionally covers), PURSUIT-UNCOVERED (keep, flagged — pursuit-shaped language restraining a goal NO current intention models, so deleting it would open a real gap, not remove a duplicate). Audited across all 17 category files (the TAC-314 entry above counts 16 files it EDITED, since `follow-up.ts` needed nothing that round — this is an audited-file count, one more); `casual_chatter`'s two lines were the only PURSUIT-DUPLICATE found.
  **Three deliberate keeps at the time of the sweep; one has since been fixed, not kept:**
  - `event_question`'s "Do not pivot to suggesting they come anyway" and `follow_up`'s "Do not push a return visit explicitly" (the tone half, "check in like a person would," stays REGISTER) are PURSUIT-UNCOVERED, not duplicates — both restrain generic return-visit nudging, a goal no current intention models (`plant_next_visit` was cut from TAC-324's original four-intention scope). Grouped together deliberately: the same restraint appears in two files, and a future `plant_next_visit`-style intention would need to re-examine both together, not find one in a table and lose the other in an unrelated REGISTER bucket.
  - `acknowledgment`'s "Do not pivot to a new topic, do not push for a return visit, do not start a new thread" was kept as REGISTER at the time of this sweep — it would exist even with zero intentions (its job is "keep a close a close") and it banned everything, not specifically the two modeled goals. Flagged then as **safe by COINCIDENCE with the current two intentions, not by design**, with an explicit note to re-examine the KEEP if a future intention modeled something a sign-off could legitimately open a door for. **TAC-330 (v1.36.0, case 2 of that ticket's live UAT) is that re-examination, and the coincidence had already broken** — not because a new intention arrived, but because a bare one-word reply ("first") classified as `acknowledgment` instead of `reply`, and the absolute ban silently vetoed `learn_first_order` on exactly the turn it was meant to be free to raise. Fixed by narrowing, not deleting: the return-visit half is untouched (nothing conflicts with it); the new-topic half is now unqualified prose ("Do not pivot to a new topic... do not turn the closer into a fresh exchange") followed by an explicit jurisdictional carve-out ("not authority over whether you act on a goal you're already carrying: that call belongs elsewhere, and this line has no say in it") rather than a same-sentence qualifier — a first draft read "do not pivot... on your own initiative" and was caught in review as self-contradicting, since raising a held goal is exactly as much her own initiative as inventing a topic from nothing. The carve-out is deliberately scoped to goal state, not to "whatever the rest of this prompt tells you" — the broad version would have quietly re-authorized `venue_info`, the exact content that won in case 2 (see the TAC-330 entry below). Full history lives in `system-template.ts`'s v1.36.0 changelog comment and the "Second round (v1.38.0)" entry under "Category instruction layer carries NO form authority (TAC-314)" below — the detailed version of this history that used to live inline in `acknowledgment.ts` was collapsed to a pointer when the carve-out sentence itself was promoted to universal R22. Not live-UAT-reverified as of v1.36.0 — sequenced behind TAC-332 (a separate, unrelated intention-recording defect that makes a passing UAT result on this seam uninterpretable until it also lands).

**TAC-328 closes the compliance-adjacent gap flagged above: the intentions block could render on an `opt_out` turn.** The fix is NOT in `build-runtime-context.ts` / `derive.ts`, despite the ticket's original framing suggesting a derive-layer gate — investigation found that's structurally impossible without a pipeline reorder: `openIntentions` is derived during the **context_build** stage, which runs *before* `classifyStage` in the inbound pipeline (`context_build → classify → retrieve → generate → send`), so the classified category doesn't exist yet at derivation time. The gate instead lives at render time: `shouldRenderOpenIntentions(category)` in `lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`, guarding the call to `formatOpenIntentions` inside `runtimeToProse`, mirroring `shouldRenderVisitHistory`'s existing placement in the same function for the same category. This suppresses the whole block — both the baseline two-line shape and the more directive TAC-329 opener — on any `opt_out` turn, with zero pipeline changes. `buildAiRuntime` remains classification-blind by design (confirmed: it maps `ctx.openIntentions` through unconditionally); the suppression is a serializer-level render decision, not a data-availability change, so `runtime.openIntentions` still carries both lines even on a suppressed turn — only the rendered prompt omits them. Verified against the real pipeline at Mock Sextant, not just unit tests: a mid-conversation qr_scan guest with both intentions open sent a realistic opt-out message, the real classifier (not a hardcoded category) returned `opt_out` at 0.99 confidence, and the assembled prompt went directly from `## What this guest can access` to `## Recent conversation` with no intentions block anywhere in system or user prompt. Two things on the record from this fix, deliberately not acted on: (1) the fix mirrors `shouldRenderVisitHistory`'s single-category (`opt_out`-only) check without auditing whether any other category poses the same risk — this is an unexamined scope boundary, not a finding that `opt_out` is the only category that should ever suppress this block. (2) `record.ts` (the post-send intention-prompt writer) needed no change: since the block never renders on a suppressed turn, the sent body never discusses either intention topic, so the post-send classifier naturally finds nothing raised and no `guest_intention_prompts` row gets written — intentions simply stay open across an opt-out exchange. The one unhandled edge, noted rather than scoped in: a guest who opts out and later re-engages (on a still-active number, a policy question this repo hasn't addressed) would still see open intentions on that later turn, since nothing here writes an opt-out-shaped closure into `guest_intention_prompts`.
  - `welcome`'s "Do not pitch anything, do not list benefits, and do not ask the guest to do anything in return" is REGISTER and structurally inert today, verified not assumed: `welcome` is outbound-only (dropped from the classifier's inbound enum since TAC-238/migration 016) and intentions is gated to `created_via='qr_scan'` only, so the two literally cannot co-occur on any current origin flow. **Inert by GATE COINCIDENCE, not by permanent design** — TAC-324 explicitly frames intentions as a primitive expected to grow, and a future intention with an origin gate other than qr_scan-only would break the non-co-occurrence this KEEP currently relies on. Re-verify the gate logic before assuming this stays inert.
  **A fourth finding was filed as its own ticket, not folded in here:** [TAC-328](https://linear.app/the-analog-company/issue/TAC-328) — the intentions block's RENDERING gate (in `build-runtime-context.ts` / `formatOpenIntentions`) doesn't exclude the `opt_out` classification, so a fresh qr_scan guest's first message opting out could render `## What you're hoping to get to` (including "you haven't told them to save your number") alongside the `opt_out` category's own "do not try to retain them" instruction. This is a compliance-adjacent exposure, not a voice-quality one — a prose restraint the model could rarely talk itself past is the wrong strength of guarantee on the one turn where continuing to solicit contact matters most. Deliberately a separate ticket: it's a gap in the intentions-rendering GATE, not a leak in category-instruction PROSE, a different mechanism than everything else this entry covers. **Fixed at TAC-328 — see the dedicated paragraph under "First-touch intentions" below for the mechanism.**
  `comp_complaint`'s comp-offering language ("come back and have another one on us") is NOT on this axis at all — it belongs to the resource-commitment/approval-gate mechanism (`# Resource commitment self-flag` + `commitment_type_gated`), a directive to pursue rather than a restraint, on an entirely different system.

- **First-touch intentions (TAC-324, v1.32.0):** a small, permanent primitive — a goal Sana carries into a conversation she doesn't control — with its first instance shipped alongside it. v1 ships exactly two, both gated to `guests.created_via = 'qr_scan'`: `learn_first_order` (satisfied when any `transactions` row exists for the guest, any source — expires 3 days after guest creation, its OWN independent constant, `LEARN_FIRST_ORDER_WINDOW_DAYS` in `lib/agent/intentions/definitions.ts`, deliberately NOT imported from `extract-reported-order.ts`'s `REPORTED_ORDER_WINDOW_DAYS` = 7 despite both being about the same first-touch order — one is how long Sana still ASKS, the other is how long the extractor still LISTENS, and listening longer than asking is free while asking as long as listening produces the "system catching up on a backlog" failure this ticket exists to prevent; only relationship asserted is `LEARN_FIRST_ORDER_WINDOW_DAYS <= REPORTED_ORDER_WINDOW_DAYS`, locked by a test) and `invite_contact_save` (never independently observable — prompted-once IS closure, expires in 14 days). An earlier draft had four; `share_item_context` and `plant_next_visit` were cut because neither is a goal Sana carries into a conversation — they're what a good reply to an answered order question looks like, persona/voice territory, not independently pursuable or capped. Rendered as `## What you're hoping to get to` (`formatOpenIntentions`, `lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`) between `## What this guest can access` and `## Follow-up context` / `## Visit history`, only when non-empty. Phrasing is deliberately a STATE Sana is in ("you haven't heard what this guest ordered yet"), never an instruction to execute ("ask what they ordered") — the non-steering paragraph that follows ("not a checklist to work through... never steer back to them") is load-bearing the same way the empty-mechanics framing is and is asserted verbatim in tests. Current-turn suppression (`applyCurrentTurnSuppression`, `lib/agent/intentions/derive.ts`) drops `learn_first_order` from the rendered (and later recording-eligible) set for one turn when `bodyMentionsMenuItem` hits the CURRENT inbound — otherwise the exact reply answering "what'd you get" would render next to "you haven't heard yet," the contradiction most likely to produce a re-ask. The `guest_intention_prompts` read (migration 035) fails CLOSED, not open: a broken read is modeled as "everything already prompted" (the full `INTENTION_DEFINITIONS` key set passed as `promptedKeys`), so a DB hiccup costs a missed soft nudge rather than risking the one re-ask this ticket exists to prevent — the inverse posture from `activeCommitments` / `pendingQuestion` in the same function, which fail open, because a missed nudge is cheap and a re-ask is exactly the failure being guarded against. **Gated to inbound runs only** — `build-runtime-context.ts` sets `openIntentions: []` unconditionally when there's no `currentMessage`, because `record.ts` (the post-send writer) only ever fires from `handle-inbound.ts`; rendering on a followup tick (day_1/day_3, which fire for exactly this qr_scan non-responder population) would raise an intention with no row ever capping it, silently breaking the one-prompt-per-intention-ever invariant on the modal path, not an edge case. R1's carve-out (see "Universal voice rules" above) reuses `REPORTED_ORDER_WINDOW_DAYS` for its OWN freshness check (`firstTouchAfterQrScan`, computed inline in `buildAiRuntime`) — a deliberate departure from the intention-expiry independence above, because "is this still a fresh first-touch moment" is the same concept whether Sana is greeting or asking, unlike the extractor's listen-window. `firstTouchAfterQrScan` is true only when `createdVia === 'qr_scan'`, `recentMessages.length === 0` (no other messages in the 14-day lookback, current inbound excluded — verified against the Sendblue webhook route that a qr_scan guest's row and their triggering message are created in the same request, so there's no gap on the true first turn), and the guest is within the window; without the age check, a guest who scans, goes silent, then texts three weeks later would ALSO show `recentMessages.length === 0` and incorrectly re-trigger "just scanned" framing. **TAC-329 (v1.34.0) fixes the turn this mechanism couldn't open on.** Four UAT runs on fresh `qr_scan` guests sending a bare "Hi Sana!" produced the identical reply "Hey, what's up?" — not a bug in derivation, ordering, or category leak (all three checked and eliminated by TAC-324/326/327 UAT), but a structural gap: the non-steering paragraph correctly restrains Sana from raising `learn_first_order` / `invite_contact_save` on a turn that opens no natural door, and a bare greeting opens no door by any plain reading, so she waited, as instructed. Fix reuses `firstTouchAfterQrScan` (the same flag driving R1's carve-out — no second definition of first touch) to LEAD `formatOpenIntentions`'s output with an opener paragraph on a guest's true first message, in the model's own words every time. **The paragraph is split, not unconditional** — code review on the first draft caught that `firstTouchAfterQrScan` is content-blind (it fires on ANY qr_scan guest's true first message, not just a bare greeting), so an unconditional "ask if it's their first time" would fight a real first question ("are you open right now?") for the turn. Saying hello and identifying yourself never conflicts with answering a question, so that half stays unconditional; the first-time question explicitly defers to whatever the guest's own message actually asks — answered if they asked something, asked if they didn't. Explicitly carries the never-texted-vs-never-visited distinction (`created_via: 'qr_scan'` means never-texted, not never-visited) so the question reads as genuinely open rather than hollow against a `Guest relationship: new` line that only reflects absence of signals, not absence of history. Leads rather than appends: appending directly after "Never steer back to them" made "them" ambiguous and framed the opener as an exception to a prohibition rather than this turn's actual job. **Tradeoff, not fixed here:** the opener lives behind the same `lines.length === 0` guard as the rest of the block, so a `guest_intention_prompts` read failure (fails CLOSED per above) empties `openIntentions` and silently removes the opener along with it — on exactly the turn this ticket exists to fix. Accepted for pilot scale. **Compounds TAC-328** (filed separately, out of scope here): the intentions block's render gate doesn't exclude `opt_out`, and this ticket adds a more directive paragraph to that same ungated block — raises TAC-328's real-world severity without fixing it. **TAC-330 (v1.35.0) fixes the turn TAC-329's own UAT list named as the next beat: reply "first time!" to the opener, and instead of following up on the order, Sana recommended a drink.** Two independent causes. First, the non-steering paragraph is symmetric — it had no way to distinguish the guest raising their own topic from the guest answering something Sana herself just asked, so the turn-two reply drew the same restraint as an unprompted pivot. `formatOpenIntentions` appends a bounded exception (the original four sentences stay byte-identical): when Sana's own last message asked the guest something about themselves (new vs. regular) and the reply answers it, following up is not a pivot. Deliberately narrower than "Sana's last message was any question" — that condition would have licensed a pivot after almost any exchange (plan-review's own counter-example: a parking Q&A closing with "you heading in soon?" would have qualified), which is close to the exact pivot the paragraph exists to prevent; residual risk accepted rather than solved is that the model actually generalizes from the worked exemplar ("new or a regular"), not a crisp category boundary — see the comment above `formatOpenIntentions` in `serializers.ts`. Second, the recommendation itself came from `venue_configs.venue_info` — `menu.notes` and `menu.highlights` phrased the owner's first-timer pick as an imperative addressed to the persona ("don't over-program the first visit, let them meet the Maiden Voyage") rather than an attributed fact, and an imperative in the prompt beats a goal that invites judgement. Rewritten to indicative, attributed form at Mock Sextant (a live data UPDATE, not a migration — `venue_info`'s shape is unchanged); three more instances of the identical mood problem found auditing the rest of the column (`hours.notes`, two `menu.items[].description` fields) fixed the same conservative way, preserving the informational content rather than deleting it. The extractor that produces this imperative mood at every new venue onboarded is out of scope here — TAC-331 owns it. **TAC-330's own live UAT surfaced a second, independent cause of the same turn failing (case 2): `acknowledgment` category instructions silently vetoing the block above whenever a terse reply misclassified into that category.** Fixed at v1.36.0 — see "Category instruction layer carries NO pursuit authority (TAC-327)" above for the fix itself. Not live-UAT-reverified as of v1.36.0, sequenced behind TAC-332 (a separate, unrelated intention-recording defect). **TAC-423 (v1.52.0) changes the opener's fallback question from "ask whether it's their first time" to "ask what they got."** Both `understand_order` (the top-priority intention, already arming on this exact turn since TAC-436's `visit_confirmed`) and the opener's own scripted first-time question were rendering in the same block with nothing reconciling them, and the model followed the nearer, more specific script — live at Le Mil's, a guest scanning the QR sign at the drink pickup counter got asked whether it was their first time, then got a volunteered recommendation, while standing there holding the drink they'd already ordered. The QR's placement is assumed to be the pickup counter (ruled 2026-09-18; not made venue-configurable — no second venue yet, so a placement field would carry exactly one possible value). The opener's "thank them for coming in" half is unchanged; only the fallback question moves, so it now asks the same thing `understand_order` already wants asked instead of racing it. A guest who answers is captured through TAC-323's original one-scan enrollment path (its gates 2 and 3 both hold on a guest's first or second message), not TAC-325's ongoing path.

- **Intentions, redefined (TAC-380, v1.49.0).** Supersedes the specifics of the TAC-324 bullet above; its render-gate history (TAC-328/329/330/332) still holds.
  - **Seven intentions**, defined in `lib/agent/intentions/definitions.ts` as a `satisfies Record<IntentionKey, IntentionDefinition>` map — TAC-324 shipped a plain array, which is not exhaustiveness-checked (the `TERMINAL_STATUSES` trap) — and sorted by `priority`: `understand_order` (10, renamed from `learn_first_order`), `got_the_recommendation` (20), `did_they_like_it` (30), `learn_name` (40), `are_they_local` (50), `their_rhythm` (60), `why_theyre_here` (70). **Event-armed intentions rank right after `understand_order` because they perish** (ruling 3): "did you get the Pink Panther?" is worthless in a fortnight. `invite_contact_save` is retired (a guest saving a contact is unobservable, so it was a permanent open loop). Cut before building: capture-only intentions (volunteered information already lands in `contextUpdate`) and `they_said_theyd_come` (its closing move is an outbound, which makes it a followup).
  - **Closure is prompted-once for all seven.** Closing on the guest's actual answer is TAC-385. `isSatisfied` is a second closure where a proxy exists: `understand_order` ← any transaction, `learn_name` ← `guests.first_name`, `are_they_local` ← context `home_base`. The rest have none.
  - **Arming** (`armsOn`): `qr_scan_enrollment` for `understand_order` (the scan confirms a visit, which is what keeps asking about an order R1-safe), `open_recommendation` (the NEWEST open recommendation still inside its window — ruling 1; arming off the first one ever blocked any guest with an old recommendation for good, because recommendations never leave `open`), `recorded_order` for `did_they_like_it` (the NEWEST recorded order, for the same reason, read from the PARSED visit history the context build already loads newest first, which is what `## Visit history` renders, so the model can see the order it is asked about; it replaced a dedicated earliest-transaction query, so the reply path runs one query fewer), and `first_contact` for everyone. **While the newest recommendation or order is still inside its conversation, the intention that asks about it neither arms nor renders**, even off an older event and even as an already-open row: the rendered line doesn't say which event it means and `## Active commitments` lists every open recommendation, so arming off an older one let the model ask about the one it had just suggested. An earlier version fell back to the older event; code review caught it. If the open recommendations can't be read that turn, `got_the_recommendation` is held outright: that read fails closed, as the read at `build-runtime-context.ts:203` does, where an empty list would silently lift the hold. Also caught in review. The TAC-324 `qr_scan`-only origin gate is gone (ruling 6); it was an artifact of these being first-touch intentions, and it excluded the guest this ticket came from.
  - **Gate** (`conversational`): `recognition.signals.responseRate >= intention_rules.response_rate_floor` AND lifetime `repliedMessageCount >= min_replies` (defaults 3/3/3/5/8/11 in priority order, overridable per key). **One ratio floor, staggered on the RAW reply count**, because `normalizeResponseRate` reads 0 until three responses have been sent and ~100 for an engaged guest after that — tiers on the ratio all open on the same turn. `repliedMessageCount` is threaded out of `computeRelationshipStrength` for exactly this. `understand_order` is ungated: asking what someone got needs no permission. Every threshold is a PLACEHOLDER.
  - **State rows** (migration 040). Eligibility is recorded (`prompted_at` null, `eligible_at` set) on the first inbound turn an intention arms and passes its gate, by `recordIntentionEligibility`, fired via `waitUntil` after classification — **never on an `opt_out` turn or a crisis-safety turn**, because the row starts an expiry window and neither turn should record intent to pursue the guest. Skipping loses nothing: eligibility re-derives from live facts on the next inbound. **Expiry runs from `eligible_at`, not guest creation** (ruling 5): from creation, one-per-turn raising leaves the lower-priority intentions expiring unraised, which makes the priority list decorative. First-contact intentions anchor to the turn their gate was first seen open; event-armed ones anchor to the moment their event became part of a DIFFERENT conversation — once it is older than `followup_rules.recent_conversation_hours` (ruling 2: the same number followups and the brake use, no second one) — so "did you try it?" is never asked in the exchange where it was suggested, the whole window stays askable at any configured conversation length (anchored at the event, a 72-hour setting would leave nothing), and they still perish on schedule however late the gate opened. Windows: `understand_order` 3 days, event-armed 3, first-contact 14. **Eligibility is sticky for first-contact intentions** — an existing row decides and the gate is never re-checked; disengagement is the brake's job. Event-armed intentions re-arm instead (next bullet).
  - **Re-arming** (acceptance criteria corrected 2026-09-14). First-contact intentions never re-arm: re-asking is nagging. `got_the_recommendation` and `did_they_like_it` re-arm on a strictly newer recommendation or order (`rearmsOnNewerEvent`, an exhaustive switch in `definitions.ts`), whether the existing row was prompted, ran out unraised, or is still open. Without it, one row per guest per intention meant a guest whose window ran out was never asked about any later event, which is ruling 1's trap one level down. **"Strictly newer" means the event happened after the row's stored `eligible_at`**, i.e. after the earlier event had already become askable. That is ruling 2's one definition of a different visit (a second recommendation from the same conversation is the same thing to ask about), and comparing against the STORED anchor, never the old event recomputed through today's window, means lengthening `recent_conversation_hours` can't make an event look newer than itself. A re-arm is a fresh arming (gate, satisfaction and expiry are checked again) and rides the eligibility write as `rearm: true`. **It moves `eligible_at` and nothing else**: the row keeps its last prompt, the brake still counts it, and the intention reads as open again because that prompt predates the new anchor (`isReopened`, event-armed keys only). **A re-arm waits while the intention's own last prompt went unanswered**, by the brake's own rule, and lifts once that prompt ages out of the visible history. The first version cleared the prompt instead, and code review showed that erased the evidence the brake runs on: a guest who ignored every question but kept ordering was asked after every order, because a single re-arming intention's row only holds one prompt and can never build a streak of two. How the re-arm write and the prompt stamp stay safe in either order is under Common gotchas.
  - **The brake.** When the last `unanswered_streak` (2) prompts — grouped by `message_id`, excluding `prompt_source = 'pessimistic'` — each saw no inbound within `followup_rules.recent_conversation_hours`, nothing renders. **The ticket's first definition ("saw no subsequent inbound") was unreachable**: prompts only record on replies to inbounds, so every earlier prompt is followed by the inbound the next one answered, and the brake would read correctly and never fire — the `comp_regex_backstop` shape again. The window REUSES the followup setting (ruling 1: one definition of "the same conversation"); at the 48h default it rarely fires, which is the intended direction, since a prompt closes on being asked anyway. **Prompts older than the visible inbound history are left out, never counted as unanswered** — see Common gotchas.
  - **Rendering.** Every open line renders, in priority order, plus one sentence: "take the one listed first" (ruling 4). **`renderableIntentions(open, category, hasPendingQuestion)` is the ONE predicate both `buildAiRuntime` and handle-inbound's recording gate read** (trap 4): nothing renders on `opt_out` or while a knowledge-gap question is pending. A suppressed turn is burned, not the intention — nothing records, so it stays open.
  - **Recording.** The post-send classifier is retried once (`CLASSIFIER_ATTEMPTS = 2`); if both attempts fail, every RENDERED intention is closed with `prompt_source = 'pessimistic'`. The read already failed closed; a write that failed open was the asymmetry. `closed_pessimistically` and `write_failed` both fire `captureIntentionPromptRecordingFailed` (PostHog + Slack) **on every send path**, auto-send and operator dispatch alike (TAC-385 PR 1 added the dispatch half; before that it only `console.warn`ed, on the path where a pessimistic closure is MOST likely to be wrong). `agentRunId` is nullable for that reason — a queued draft's run ended when it was queued — and a required `via: 'auto_send' | 'operator_approve' | 'operator_edit'` says which path instead, so a fourth send path has to decide rather than inherit. Before this ticket a recording failure was a bare `console.warn`, and this ticket multiplies the intentions it applies to. **Every send path records, since TAC-385 PR 1.** An intention is ASKED when the message carrying it reaches the guest — auto-sent, operator-approved and operator-edited-then-sent alike (ruled 2026-09-15). `handle-inbound` records the auto-sent path; `dispatchOperatorOutbound` records the other two off `messages.rendered_intentions` (migration 045), the carrier the queue persist writes. Until then those sends recorded nothing: 13 of 34 sent replies at Le Mil's in the 30 days to 2026-09-14. **`renderableIntentions(...)` is called ONCE in `handle-inbound`, hoisted above the queue/send fork**, so what a card STORES and what an auto-send RECORDS are the same value by construction; the TAC-332 opener guard is applied there too, so a queued opener draft stores nothing and the dispatch path inherits the guard without re-deriving it. The dispatch recorder is handed the **DISPATCHED body**, never `row.body` — that is the whole mechanism behind "read the ask from what was sent, not what was drafted": a question the operator edited OUT is simply not returned by the classifier. **Known limit, accepted:** a question the operator writes IN that the model never rendered cannot be returned either, because the key set is a per-call `z.enum` over the offered set; it stays open and may be asked again. **PR 1 changes nothing about WHEN an intention closes** — raising still closes, exactly as before; the asked/closed split is PR 2. The Command Center raised-prompts lists mark `prompt_source = 'pessimistic'` rows "closed without a verdict".
  - **Prompt (v1.49.0).** R31 gains a carve-out for asking how an item went — **only an item already in this guest's `## Visit history` or an open recommendation to them in `## Active commitments`, never any item the venue offers**; the broader version reopens exactly what TAC-356 closed. R32 lost its "the first-touch intentions block already covers it" clause, which became false when `invite_contact_save` retired. `their_rhythm` asks TIME OF DAY, never frequency: the R23 trip is the turn AFTER the question ("since you're in most mornings"), and no rewording rescues a goal that is about frequency. `definitions.test.ts` guards the wording.
  - Config lives on `venue_configs.intention_rules`; defaults are on `/admin/tunables`. `/admin/intentions` and the venue page's intentions section were reworked for state rows.

- **Intentions were never asked, and why (TAC-436, v1.51.0).** Between TAC-380 shipping and this, **zero intentions were ever raised in production**. They rendered and the model declined, and on 15 of 16 traced turns the block's own rules told it to. Two independent causes, both closed here.
  - **The prompt licensed the ask only in a situation that could not arise.** `formatOpenIntentions`' restraint paragraph carried six restraints and ONE concretely-worked permission — "when your own last message asked them something about themselves… and this reply answers it" — which presupposes an ask nothing else licensed. The deadlock in practice was one sentence, *"If the guest asks about something else, answer that and let these wait"*, which fired on all nine question turns of the sixteen. **That sentence is deleted** (ruling 1b: a reply may answer and then ask one small thing), and **four ordinary openings are now named positively** (ruling 1c) in place of the negation-only "a natural door". Every other restraint survives, one is new (an apology, bad news or an upset guest is never an opening), and `serializers.test.ts` carries a CANARY on the deleted sentence, because restoring it for brevity reverts the whole ticket silently. **Two deliberate omissions in the copy, both approved:** no em dash anywhere (the replaced text had two; R3 bans them in output and the prompt should not model them), and no emoji in the worked example (this block renders immediately before the per-message emoji call, so an example carrying one would argue with a `none` directive on ~25% of turns at a `frequent` venue).
  - **`comp_complaint` joins `opt_out` in BOTH `shouldRenderOpenIntentions` AND `renderableIntentions`** — the STRUCTURAL half of that new restraint, and it is what actually holds. **The two must move together and a test now enforces it across every category.** Suppressing on one side only does not leak a question into the reply, which is why it survived three commits of unit tests and was caught by the gate run: the block genuinely does not render, and the post-send classifier is still offered intentions the prompt never showed. The block renders LAST in the user prompt while `COMP_COMPLAINT_INSTRUCTIONS` lives in the SYSTEM prompt, so on proximity the block wins; the prose line is a second line of defence. Same pair `shouldRenderEmojiDirective` already excludes, the same TAC-314/329/330/338 failure class. Still not a claim these are the only two categories that should suppress it — no other has been audited.
  - **The bootstrap gap was arithmetic, not judgement.** `gateOpen` required `responseRate >= response_rate_floor`, and `normalizeResponseRate` returns 0 until three outbound responses exist against a floor defaulting to 50, so every conversational-gated intention was closed on a guest's first turns BY CONSTRUCTION, and the four first-contact intentions became eligible strictly after the only licence to raise them had expired. A third gate kind, **`replies_only`**, drops the floor and keeps the count. `gateOpen` is now an exhaustive switch, so a fourth kind fails `tsc`. **The waiver is declared PER INTENTION on its own gate, never inferred from `armsOn`** — arming and gating are orthogonal here on purpose, and nothing branches on `armsOn.kind` to decide a gate.
  - **`learn_name` alone is free on a guest's first-ever message** (`firstMessageMinReplies: 0`; ruled 2026-09-17, "explicit per intention, not a blanket zero"). The other three repeat their ongoing count there, which waives nothing, and say so explicitly so a new `replies_only` intention has to choose. NOT venue-overridable: `min_replies` tunes the ongoing stagger, not the opening exchange. "First-ever message" is read off `repliedMessageCount <= 1`, the same number the gate compares against, rather than a separately-plumbed flag that could drift from it — and deliberately NOT `recentMessages.length === 0`, which is a 14-day window and is also true for a guest returning after a long gap.
  - **`understand_order` arms on a CONFIRMED VISIT, not a QR scan** (`visit_confirmed` replaces `qr_scan_enrollment`). Two sources today, resolved by `build-runtime-context` and handed to the pure derivation as one date: `created_via = 'qr_scan'` anchored to enrollment, and the earliest `guest_commitments.acknowledged_at`. **The EARLIEST of them wins**, because the window runs from the anchor and this intention never re-arms, so a later visit must not renew an ask about the first order nobody heard. **`guests.last_visit_at` is DELIBERATELY NOT a source, and it is the one anyone reaches for first**: all three of its writers run downstream of a transaction row, and a transaction SATISFIES this intention, so arming on it would close the intention in the same breath it opened it. There is a source-level guard, matching both the snake_case column and the camelCase field — the first version matched only one and a mutant walked past it. A guest merely SAYING they came in, naming nothing they ordered, is recorded nowhere; that third source is TAC-386's and lands without touching `derive.ts` (ruled 2026-09-17). `DeriveOpenIntentionsInput.guest` was REMOVED in the same change rather than left dead, so a future `qr_scan` branch has to be added back deliberately instead of finding the field in scope.
  - **`rendered_intentions` is written on the auto-send path too** (ruling 4), on the FIRST row of a split turn only — one prompt, one rendered set, so a count of non-null rows counts responses rather than bubbles. **AUDIT, NOT MECHANISM**: that path records inline and nothing downstream reads the column there. It exists so a query can prove what rendered, which is precisely what TAC-385's raising-half audit could not do — every auto-sent row carried NULL and its claim about those turns was inference. `handle-inbound` passes the SAME hoisted value to both the queue and send branches, so a card and an auto-send cannot drift.
  - **A successful raise now emits `intention_prompt_raised`** (ruling 5) to PostHog and Slack, from all three send paths, carrying `via`, the raised keys, the OFFERED keys and the sent body. Until this it was a bare `console.log`, which is why "nothing has ever been raised" was invisible for the feature's whole life. `offeredKeys` is the RENDERED set, never `ctx.openIntentions`, so the event can never claim a door was open that the turn suppressed; without it a 1-of-4 raise is indistinguishable from a 1-of-1. Slack-relayed deliberately at pilot volume: the question it exists to answer is "has an intention ever been raised", and at this rate the relay IS the answer.
  - **Known and NOT fixed here:** the first-touch opener is still QR-only (`computeFirstTouchAfterQrScan`), so a guest who texts the number without scanning gets no introduction — most guests on file. Ruling 3 widens ARMING, not the licence; ruling 1's rewrite is what makes the line askable on an ordinary turn, so the two only work together. Widening the opener is its own ticket (ruled 2026-09-17, audit question 3).

### Approval policy gates (TAC-212, v1.14.0)

`applyApprovalPolicyStage` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) runs immediately after a successful `generateStage` on both the inbound and followup orchestrators. It returns `{ action: 'send' }` or `{ action: 'queue', triggers, primaryTrigger, compMatchedPattern, existingPendingDraftId }`. On `queue`, the orchestrator calls `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` (`lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts`) — same row shape as `scheduleAndSend` via the shared `buildOutboundInsert` helper, but with `status='pending_review'`, `review_state='pending'` (TAC-258 migration 018 predicate), `review_reason=<primaryTrigger>`, `sent_at=null`, `provider_message_id=null`. No Sendblue dispatch. The `AgentResult.queued` discriminated-union member carries `outboundMessageId`, `triggers`, and `primaryTrigger`. **TAC-350 adds a third, optional parameter** — `applyApprovalPolicyStage(ctx, generation, groundingBackstop?)` — carrying the result of `verifyGroundingStage`, which the orchestrator must run (it needs an async AI call the gate itself can't make mid-evaluation) and pass in; omitted or `null` behaves exactly as before TAC-350. **TAC-355 adds a fourth, optional parameter** — `applyApprovalPolicyStage(ctx, generation, groundingBackstop?, mechanicOfferBackstop?)` — carrying the result of `verifyMechanicOfferStage`, run by the orchestrator alongside `verifyGroundingStage` via `Promise.all` (both are independent Haiku calls with no dependency on each other). Omitted defaults to `{ status: 'skipped' }`, behaving exactly as before TAC-355.

**Thirteen triggers compose; any one queues.** Enumeration order (the order the gate evaluates and the order they appear in `triggers[]`):
1. `fidelity_below_auto_send_floor` — `voiceFidelity` in `[0.4, 0.6)`. (`< 0.4` is already refused upstream.)
2. `model_flagged` — `GeneratedMessageSchema.requiresOperatorApproval=true`. Reason is in `GenerateMessageResult.approvalReason`. Two prompt triggers teach the model when to flag: the universal comp rule in `# Resource commitment self-flag` (`SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`), and per-mechanic instructions appended to flagged-mechanic bullets in `formatMechanicEligibility`.
3. `comp_regex_backstop` — `matchComp` (`lib/agent/comp-backstop.ts`) hits a high-precision operator-action regex. Runs **independently** of `model_flagged` — when the model misses a self-flag on a comp, the regex still queues. False positives are acceptable; false negatives are the failure mode this exists to prevent.
4. `previous_pending_held` — `loadPendingRowsBySlot` finds a pending card in the SAME slot this draft would take (TAC-394: a card in the other slot never fires it, and a manual followup is refused rather than held, see below). **Fails OPEN** on DB error: lookup throws or returns an error → returns `null` → gate proceeds to send. Migration 041's two partial unique indexes (which replaced TAC-264's migration 020) are the structural backstop against concurrent rapid-inbound races that slip past this fail-open read.
5. `commitment_type_gated` (TAC-297) — `GeneratedMessageSchema.commitment.type ∈ {comp, hold, discount}` AND `description` non-empty. Fires regardless of `requiresOperatorApproval` self-flag — the structured emission IS the backstop (TAC-297 design call #2). Recommendation type does NOT fire this trigger. **The comp regex stays as belt-and-suspenders** for prose-comp-without-emission cases the model might produce.
6. `hold_all_outbound` (commit `0c1515c`, migration 031) — `ctx.venue.holdAllOutbound === true` AND `ctx.classification?.category !== 'opt_out'`. Per-venue blanket hold: every guest-facing content message is queued for review. The opt_out carve-out is a TCPA/carrier requirement — compliance confirmations must auto-send — and it bypasses ONLY this trigger; an opt_out reply still runs triggers 1–5. Composes with (does not short-circuit) the others, so `existingPendingDraftId` still routes regen-in-place correctly. **No UI or API writes this flag** — `venues.hold_all_outbound` is Studio-SQL-only, like `guests.is_demo` and `operators.is_analog_admin`; SQL template in Common gotchas.

7. `complaint_commitment_floor` (v1.23.0) — `ctx.classification.category` is in `FLOOR_CATEGORIES` (today: `comp_complaint` only) AND `matchForwardCommitment(body)` hits first-person forward-commitment grammar (`lib/agent/complaint-floor.ts`). **Deterministic: does not read `requiresOperatorApproval` or `commitment` at all.** That independence is the entire point — on 2026-08-07 the model reported both empty and the reply still gave away free product on a refund request. Gates on promise GRAMMAR, not remedy vocabulary, because the set of ways to say "I commit to a future action" is small and closed while the set of ways to describe what's being given is unbounded. Measured against the complete production history of `comp_complaint` outbounds: 8/12 queue, 6 true positives, 2 accepted false positives (prompt-endorsed information stalls), all pure-question turns pass through. Scope is `comp_complaint` ONLY — `mechanic_request` is deliberately excluded so ordinary perk refusals ("can i get a free drink") can never be held.

9. `knowledge_gap` (TAC-308) — `GeneratedMessageSchema.knowledgeGap === true` AND `ctx.currentMessage !== null`. The model answered a guest question it could not ground in venue knowledge. **The only trigger that starts a clock**: the queue persist stamps `messages.pending_until = now + KNOWLEDGE_GAP_WINDOW_MS` (5 min), and if no operator answers before it elapses the timer cron sends the guest a model-generated holding message. Inbound-only — a followup has no guest question outstanding, and letting a cron-triggered proactive message arm a clock would produce a holding note for something nobody asked.

9b. `knowledge_gap_backstop` (TAC-350) — independent grounding backstop, fired from `groundingBackstop.status === 'flagged'` (TAC-367 shape; was `!== null` before that widening — see the new third parameter above). Exists because trigger 9 is pure self-report and an audit of a real TAC-347 harness run found 8/8 observed fabrications had `knowledgeGap=false` — the model confidently invented a wifi password, four menu-item variation names, and other specifics with no structural check ever comparing the claim against what was actually retrieved. `verifyGroundingStage` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) closes that gap with a second, independent Haiku call (`lib/ai/verify-grounding.ts`) that inspects the drafted body against the SAME `venueInfo` + `knowledgeChunks` the generator saw and reports any claim not supported by them — same relationship `comp_regex_backstop` has to `model_flagged`. **Only ever invoked when trigger 9 is already false** (no point re-checking a self-reported gap), so the two are mutually exclusive on any single turn by construction. Shares trigger 9's clock arming and protected-card carve-out via the `isGapTurn = knowledgeGapFired || backstopFired` union inside the gate, and `isKnowledgeGapCard` recognizes both `review_reason` values — but **NOT body blanking**, which TAC-301 part 1.5 made the one asymmetry between them (self-report blanks, backstop keeps its body; see below). **TAC-301 part 1.5 fixed three defects in this backstop that together made it fire on CORRECT replies.** (1) **The verifier was missing the entire runtime context.** `buildSourceMaterial` was `venueInfo` + `knowledgeChunks` only, so every fact the generator drew from a runtime block read as unsupported — measured against Le Mil's live config, SIX of six fact-bearing blocks produced a false flag (`## Right now`, `## What this guest can access`, `## Active commitments`, `## Visit history`, `## Guest context`, `## Recent conversation`). It now receives `generation.userPrompt` **verbatim** — the literal string the generating model got, available on `GenerateMessageResult` since THE-216. **Never rebuild or curate that string**: a hand-maintained subset falls behind the next runtime block, which is exactly how all six arrived (TAC-296/297/308/324 each appended one). (2) **`reasoning` was declared LAST in the output schema**, and structured-output fields generate in declaration order, so the model emitted its verdict before doing the analysis; a reply naming the guest's last order produced reasoning that reversed itself and ended "This IS supported... The claim is grounded" while the boolean stayed `true`. `reasoning` is now FIRST and **the order is load-bearing**. (3) **The verifier exceeded its remit** three ways, each flagging a correct reply: re-litigating whether a guest QUALIFIES for a perk (eligibility is already decided by `filterEligibleMechanics` before the block renders), flagging hedged phrasing about a fact that IS in the source material, and judging whether saying something was wise. The system prompt now scopes it to factual support only. **`blankBody` no longer fires on this trigger** — see the reversal note below. Verified behaviourally, not just by unit test: all six divergences plus four controls (fabricated password, perk not on the list, eligibility-is-not-a-reservation, and the agent's own prior fabrication) pass against live calls, 7 of 8 full runs clean with one unidentified single-case flake. Fails OPEN on a TRANSIENT AI-call error (degrades to pre-TAC-350 behavior, never worse) — **but NOT on truncation as of TAC-367, see trigger 12.** PostHog: `captureUngroundedClaimCaught` (`lib/analytics/posthog.ts`), Slack-relayed, fired from inside `verifyGroundingStage` — distinct from the generic `draft_queued` event so "how often is the model caught fabricating, per venue" is directly queryable. **`verifyGroundingStage` no longer returns `GroundingBackstopFinding | null`** — TAC-367 widened it to a four-state `GroundingBackstopResult` (`skipped | clean | flagged | truncated`) mirroring TAC-355's `MechanicOfferBackstopResult`, because a two-state shape had nowhere to put "we could not read the verdict" that was distinguishable from "there was nothing to report." `null` is still accepted at the gate and normalized to `skipped`.

8. `category_requires_approval` (v1.24.0; **made absolute by TAC-307**) — `resolvePolicyDecision(venue.approvalPolicy, classification.category)` returns `'operator_approval'`. **The only trigger decided by the CLASSIFICATION rather than the draft**, which is what makes it knowable before generation and lets the prompt be comp-forward (see `willBeReviewed` below).

   **The clarifying-question carve-out is now SUBORDINATE to the policy, not parallel to it.** It used to run alongside the policy check (`policy === 'operator_approval' && !canAutoSendComplaintTurn(...)`), which meant a turn the model labelled `clarifying` could auto-send THROUGH a hold an operator had deliberately switched on. The line is drawn on **who chose the hold**, via `PolicyDecision.source`:
   - source `stored` (an explicit `perCategory` entry on this venue) or `policy_default` (this venue's own `default`) → **ABSOLUTE**. `canAutoSendComplaintTurn` is not consulted at all.
   - source `code_default` (`APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT.perCategory`, which ships fleet-wide and nobody chose per venue) → the carve-out still applies, because stalling a complaint's opening "what went wrong?" behind an operator is worse service than the cold reply v1.24.0 shipped to fix.

   Every venue in production stores `perCategory: {}`, so this is a **no-op against current behaviour** — and the moment a category is ticked in Command Center it becomes strictly stronger than the default it replaced. A venue-level `default: 'operator_approval'` is checked BEFORE the code-level `perCategory` map on purpose: without that ordering the master switch would not be absolute for `comp_complaint`, the one category that has a code default of its own — the hole hiding in exactly the category this ticket's incident was about.

   **`willBeReviewed` is scoped to complaint categories (TAC-307).** `buildAiRuntime`'s flag used to be "policy holds this category", full stop, which was harmless while a venue-wide `default: 'operator_approval'` needed hand-written SQL and no venue had one. This ticket ships a master switch that sets exactly that, and an unscoped flag would turn `formatMechanicEligibility`'s comp-forward branch on for EVERY turn at a holding venue — the agent proposing to make it up to a guest who asked whether the cafe is open. It now additionally requires `isFloorCategory(category)`. Guaranteed review is a necessary condition for inviting generosity, never a sufficient one.

   **`POLICY_EXEMPT_CATEGORIES` (TAC-307) beats everything above.** Today: `opt_out` only. TCPA/carrier compliance — an opt-out confirmation must reach the guest unconditionally, so it can never be routed to approval whatever a venue stores, an admin clicks, or someone hand-writes in Studio. **Enforced in the resolver, not the UI**, because hand-editing `venue_configs` is a normal workflow here and an exclusion guarded only by a rendering decision is not guarded. The Command Center surface reads the same constant to omit the control, and the write route refuses it independently. This mirrors the carve-out migration 031 already has on the `hold_all_outbound` axis (trigger 6, `category !== 'opt_out'`) — the two are now symmetric.

10. `self_talk_detected` (TAC-355) — `GenerateMessageResult.selfTalkViolationPersisted === true`. Deterministic, unconditional — fires whenever the agent's own self-correction or reference to its instructions/rules/AI-nature survived every regen attempt inside `generateMessage`'s loop (`lib/ai/self-talk-detector.ts`, wired alongside `DASH_REGEX`, sharing its attempt budget). **Never a send, unlike the dash case** — THE-225 ships a persistent dash anyway (punctuation isn't worth refusing over); a guest reading agent self-talk learns they're texting a bot, which is categorical harm regardless of rate, so this trigger always queues instead. No body-blanking, no clock — the operator card already renders the full draft body, so the broken text is directly visible for them to edit.

11. `mechanic_offer_backstop` (TAC-355) — independent verification backstop for the mechanic-approval gate, fired from `mechanicOfferBackstop.status === 'flagged' || 'check_failed'` (the new fourth parameter — see below). Exists because trigger 2 (`model_flagged`) and trigger 5 (`commitment_type_gated`) both missed a real approval-gated mechanic grant (Referral Surprise, `le-mils-coffee`): audited and confirmed structural — nothing in the prompt links a mechanic grant from `## What this guest can access` to `commitment.type`, and a mechanic's reward can be genuinely non-nameable (`"exact item at staff discretion"`) or not a product at all (an event-invite-type mechanic), so the vocabulary has no bucket for it regardless of how faithfully the model tries. **This is the PRIMARY mechanism for that failure mode, not a secondary layer** — unlike `comp_regex_backstop`'s relationship to `model_flagged`, a regex on mechanic name/reward text was evaluated and rejected (mechanic identifying text is per-venue free prose, not a closed vocabulary; see the TAC-355 audit trail on the ticket). `verifyMechanicOfferStage` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) runs a second, independent Haiku call (`lib/ai/verify-mechanic-offer.ts`) against the SAME eligible-`requires_operator_approval`-mechanics bullets the generator saw. Skips (no model call) when nothing gated is eligible this turn (the common case), when trigger 2 or 5 already fired (`isModelFlagged`/`isCommitmentTypeGated`, extracted so this skip check can't drift from the trigger's own push logic), or for a demo guest. **FAILS CLOSED** — a `check_failed` result (errored/timed out/unparseable) queues exactly like a confirmed `flagged` result, the deliberate opposite of `knowledge_gap_backstop`'s fail-open posture: an unauthorized perk grant costs the owner money and control, a failed check costs one unnecessary review. Runs on BOTH inbound and followup paths — a mechanic can be offered on a proactive outbound exactly as easily as in reply — and, since TAC-376, grounding runs on the same set of paths too (plus the holding message), so the two backstops no longer differ on REACH. What still differs is failure posture: this one fails CLOSED on any check failure (`check_failed` queues exactly like `flagged`), where grounding stays fail-OPEN on a transient fault and fails closed only on truncation. PostHog: `captureMechanicOfferBackstopCaught`, Slack-relayed, fired only on a confirmed `flagged` result (not on `check_failed`).

12. `grounding_check_failed` (TAC-367) — the grounding backstop produced a verdict that could not be READ: `finishReason: 'length'`, the output cap cut the JSON off mid-object. **Fails CLOSED**, a deliberate narrowing of trigger 9b's otherwise fail-open posture scoped to this one cause. Measured on Le Mil's live config: 12 paced calls on a production-size prompt (13,299 chars / 5,673 input tokens) produced 11 successes emitting **373–496 output tokens against a 500 cap** — the best success cleared it by four tokens — and one truncation. Every truncation failed open with no PostHog and no Slack, so the only fabrication check that fires under real traffic was silently not running on a slice of inbound turns. Same `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` schema-growth hazard CLAUDE.md documents for TAC-309, arriving by a different route: TAC-301 part 1.5 moved unbounded `reasoning` ahead of the verdict, which is load-bearing for CORRECTNESS and must stay, but put the long field first against a cap that never moved. `VERIFY_GROUNDING_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` is now 2000 — headroom, not a bound, since `reasoning` is still unbounded; the fail-closed handling is what actually closes the hole. **Transient faults (network, provider 5xx, timeout) still fail OPEN** and that line is load-bearing in the other direction: grounding runs on every inbound, so queuing every provider hiccup closed would be a fleet-wide queue flood, a worse failure than the one being fixed. **A DISTINCT trigger rather than reusing `knowledge_gap_backstop`**, even though `mechanic_offer_backstop` sets the opposite precedent by covering both `flagged` and `check_failed` under one code — reusing it would tell the operator "ungrounded claim caught" for a turn where nothing was caught, a third instance of the wrong-reason-copy problem TAC-364 exists for. **Deliberately NOT part of `isGapTurn`**: a truncated check is an absence of information about the reply, not a finding against it, and everything keyed on `isGapTurn` has a guest-facing consequence that would be wrong here — arming `pending_until` puts a holding message in front of a guest whose reply was probably fine, and the protected-card carve-out silently DROPS the guest's next turn if that turn queues for any other reason. It queues the draft for a human to glance at; that is the whole intended consequence. PostHog: `captureGroundingVerifierUnavailable`, ONE event with an `outcome: 'truncated' | 'degraded'` discriminator and an explicit `failedClosed` boolean, **Slack-relaying BOTH** — the degraded case relays because fail-open means a guest-facing message shipped with a safety check skipped, and the precise property that let the truncation bug survive was that nothing was emitted at all. Known cost: a sustained provider outage relays once per inbound; the lever if that proves unworkable is a PostHog-side filter on `outcome`, not deleting the emit.

`primaryTrigger` is **not** `triggers[0]`. It's picked via `PRIMARY_TRIGGER_PRIORITY` (severity-of-signal order): `commitment_type_gated` > `mechanic_offer_backstop` > `knowledge_gap_backstop` > `knowledge_gap` > `comp_regex_backstop` > `model_flagged` > `self_talk_detected` > `complaint_commitment_floor` > `previous_pending_held` > `fidelity_below_auto_send_floor` > `grounding_check_failed` > `category_requires_approval` > `hold_all_outbound`. **`grounding_check_failed` ranks below every trigger that names something about this draft** — it is the only trigger reporting an ABSENCE of signal, so any concrete co-firing finding is the more useful operator label — and above the two venue-wide policy signals, because it is at least specific to this message. **`knowledge_gap_backstop` ranks just above `knowledge_gap`**, mirroring `comp_regex_backstop`'s rank above `model_flagged` — a caught claim is a more specific operator label than an honest self-report. Since the two never co-fire on the same turn, this ordering is documentation today rather than a live tie-break. **`category_requires_approval` ranks second-to-last** because it is a policy signal, not a claim about this draft — every trigger above names a concrete risk in the specific message and carries a more useful operator label. It sits BELOW `previous_pending_held` so a regenerated complaint turn doesn't re-push at an operator already holding the card. `primaryTrigger` lands on `messages.review_reason` (the field the operator queue UI surfaces first), so the most-severe signal is what gets shown. **Commitment_type_gated tops the priority** because it carries explicit type information (the operator queue UI sees "hold" / "comp" / "discount" directly rather than just "regex matched some comp prose"). **`mechanic_offer_backstop` ranks directly below it, at parity rather than beneath the other backstops** — TAC-355's audit found it's the PRIMARY defense for the mechanic-grant failure mode, not a secondary layer, so an unauthorized-perk signal carries the same operator-facing priority as an unauthorized comp/hold/discount. **`hold_all_outbound` is ranked last** because it's a venue-wide policy rather than a per-message signal — any concrete co-firing trigger carries more operator-actionable information. **`knowledge_gap` ranks below the two structural/backstop triggers above it, deliberately not first.** TAC-308 originally asked for top, but that request was reasoning about the timer — and the timer anchors on `pending_until`, not on `review_reason`, so rank decides only the operator-facing label. It sits below `commitment_type_gated` and `mechanic_offer_backstop` because either losing its label is the worse failure (this repo has bled from an unlabelled comp signal twice) and above everything else because a knowledge-gap card is the only card with a running clock and a guest sitting in silence. **`self_talk_detected` ranks below `model_flagged`, above the softer signals** — a hard, deterministic catch once it fires (the text is confirmed broken), but a voice-quality/product-premise failure rather than a money-or-perk exposure, so it sits below every resource-commitment-class trigger. Confirmed via the operator card (`analog-operator/components/queue/queue-card.tsx`) already rendering the full draft body regardless of which trigger wins the primary label, so this ranking only affects the displayed reason string, never visibility of the actual self-talk text.

**Every trigger except `previous_pending_held` fires an APNs push.** The fire/skip decision is a TOTAL map in `lib/notifications/push-policy.ts` (`satisfies Record<ApprovalTrigger, PushDecision>`) — adding a trigger above without a decision there fails `tsc`. See "Common gotchas → sets keyed on approval triggers must be TOTAL maps" for why this is load-bearing.

**Manual followups never regenerate over a pending card (TAC-307; enforced end to end by TAC-394).** `previous_pending_held` never fires when `ctx.followupTrigger?.reason === 'manual'`. Since TAC-394 a manual followup that would queue into an occupied slot is REFUSED (`slot_occupied`, the `never_regen` policy), including when only a unique violation reveals the card; before that, the race path regenerated the card anyway. The old bypass was doing two unrelated jobs and only one was meant to go: left in, a Follow Up click on a guest who already has a pending card would fire `previous_pending_held`, route to `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft`'s UPDATE-in-place branch, and overwrite the draft the operator was about to approve — body, `review_reason` and `pending_commitment` replaced, `created_at` preserved so the card looks untouched in the queue. That is data loss on a human's work and a different axis from "does approval policy apply". Caught in code review.

**Follow Up button bypass — REMOVED (TAC-307).** `handle-followup.ts` used to skip `applyApprovalPolicyStage` entirely when `input.trigger.reason === 'manual'`, on the reasoning that the operator had already approved by clicking. **It now runs the gate on every followup path.** Clicking Follow Up authorises THE ACT OF REACHING OUT, not the text — the body is model-generated and nobody reads it before it ships — so the old bypass let a button click auto-send past a hold a venue had deliberately switched on. Consequence to know before debugging it: at a venue with an approval hold configured, the Follow Up button now produces a QUEUED CARD rather than a sent message. That is the intended meaning of the switch. Manual followups still bypass Gate 1 (`canSendFollowup` lives in the engine, not the orchestrator) and still don't write `followup_log` rows or count toward `weekly_cap`. `skipHumanFeelDelay` still keys on `reason === 'manual'` — that heuristic is untouched.

**Generation schema fields:** `GeneratedMessageSchema` (`lib/ai/generate-message.ts`) rigidly requires `requiresOperatorApproval: boolean` and `approvalReason: string` — no `.optional()` because the Anthropic structured-output validator is more reliable with explicit presence. `approvalReason` defaults to `""` when `requiresOperatorApproval=false`. Both are threaded onto `GenerateMessageAttempt` and `GenerateMessageResult` for trace observability.

**Per-mechanic flag (`mechanics.requires_operator_approval`):** added in migration 019, defaults `false`. The eligible-mechanics serializer (`formatMechanicEligibility` in `lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts`) annotates flagged mechanic bullets with `[operator approval required: …]` so the model knows committing this mechanic should set `requiresOperatorApproval=true`. Loaded by `build-runtime-context.ts` through `EligibilityCandidate` → `EligibleMechanic`. SQL UPDATE template for toggling per-venue is in Common gotchas.

**PostHog events:** `captureDraftQueued` (`lib/analytics/posthog.ts`) fires whenever the gate queues a NEW pending draft (INSERT path), with `triggers` + `primaryTrigger` + `voiceFidelity` + `modelRequiresApproval` + `modelApprovalReason` + `compRegexMatchedPattern` + `hasPreviousPending` + `kind` ('inbound' | 'followup') + `category` + bodies. `captureDraftRegenerated` (TAC-264) fires when the gate queues a REGENERATION of an existing pending row (UPDATE path), with the same shape plus `originalDraftId` (the `messages.id` that was updated in place) and `priorReviewReason` (the row's `review_reason` BEFORE overwrite — captured pre-UPDATE so analytics can surface trigger evolution across regens). Both Slack relay on (pilot ops needs visibility into both paths). Mirrors the `voice_fidelity_low` / `dash_violation_persisted` shape.

**Demo guest bypass (TAC-284).** When `ctx.guest.isDemo === true` (the `guests.is_demo` flag, migration 022), `applyApprovalPolicyStage` still evaluates every trigger (thirteen, see above) — so the would-have-queued set is accurate, except that `knowledge_gap_backstop`, `grounding_check_failed` and `mechanic_offer_backstop` can never appear in it, because `verifyGroundingStage` and `verifyMechanicOfferStage` return `skipped` for a demo guest — then **short-circuits to `{ action: 'send', reason: 'demo_bypass' }` regardless of trigger outcome.** The bypass is total: every trigger, **including the comp regex backstop**, is overridden. The comp backstop is therefore **not a guarantee for demo guests** — a demo guest who is committed a comp gets the comp auto-sent with no operator review. This is intentional (demo guests are teammates' own phones, not real customers); the visibility guarantee is the `demo_bypassed_approval_gate` PostHog event, which fires whenever the would-have-queued trigger set is non-empty and **Slack-relays only when `comp_regex_backstop` is among those triggers** (the irreversible-financial-commitment case). The bypass is **fail-closed**: only the literal boolean `true` bypasses — `undefined` / `null` / a missing column flow through the normal policy. Both orchestrators also pass `skipHumanFeelDelay: ctx.guest.isDemo` to `scheduleAndSend`, and the demo send is stamped `messages.review_reason='demo_bypass'` (the auto-send path otherwise leaves `review_reason` null) so the row is self-describing without a PostHog cross-reference. Manual followups (`reason='manual'`) have run the gate like every other followup since TAC-307, so this short-circuit applies to them too: a demo guest's manual followup sends, and `handle-followup.ts` passes `approval.reason` into `scheduleAndSend`, so that send is stamped `review_reason='demo_bypass'` like any other. Flip a guest's flag via the SQL template in Common gotchas; the flag is read fresh every agent run (no runtime caching).

### Single pending draft per guest invariant (TAC-264, v1.14.0)

**The invariant:** at most one row with `review_state='pending'` per `(venue_id, guest_id)` **per slot** since TAC-394 (see *Two pending slots per guest* below). Enforced at the storage layer by migration 041's two partial unique indexes, which replaced migration 020's single `idx_messages_one_pending_per_guest`; enforced at the application layer by `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` (`lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts`) which branches on `existingPendingDraftId` to UPDATE in place vs INSERT.

**Two persist modes via `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft(ctx, generation, primaryTrigger, existingPendingDraftId, options)`** — returns `{ outboundMessageId, action: 'inserted' | 'updated', priorReviewReason }`. Two outcomes write nothing and carry a null `outboundMessageId`: `action: 'dropped'` (TAC-394: race recovery found a card this draft must not overwrite; it carries the drop `reason`, the protected card and both commitments) and `action: 'skipped'` (TAC-308: an `updateOnly` caller whose row is already gone):

1. **INSERT** — `existingPendingDraftId === null` AND the partial unique index doesn't fire. Status quo INSERT via `buildOutboundInsert` with the queue-path overrides. `priorReviewReason` is always `null` on this path.
2. **UPDATE in place (regenerate)** — `existingPendingDraftId !== null`. Conditional UPDATE gated on `id = existingPendingDraftId AND review_state = 'pending'` so a TOCTOU race vs. `dispatchOperatorOutbound` produces rowcount=0 rather than corrupting the operator's just-dispatched body. Captures `priorReviewReason` via a SELECT before the UPDATE so analytics can observe trigger evolution.

**Columns UPDATED on regen** (the "current state of the draft" subset): `body`, `voice_fidelity`, `prompt_version`, `category`, `reply_to_message_id`, `langfuse_trace_id`, `review_reason`. `updated_at` is auto-bumped by `trg_messages_updated_at`.

**Columns PRESERVED on regen** (operator-visible history + queue ordering): `status='pending_review'`, `review_state='pending'` (unchanged — **no demotion to send across regen**), `created_at` (FIFO queue position — operator's "this guest has been waiting since X" mental model), `last_operator_action_at`, `last_operator_id`, `previous_review_state`, `id`, `venue_id`, `guest_id`, `direction`, `generated_by`, `response_review`.

**Race recovery (bounded loop, `RACE_RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`)**:
- INSERT path: a concurrent inbound for the same (venue, guest) wins the unique-index race; the losing INSERT receives `code='23505'`. Recovery re-reads both slots (`loadPendingRowsBySlot`, TAC-394) and runs `decideSlotAction` with the caller's policy: regenerate the card in this draft's own slot on the next loop tick, or return `action: 'dropped'` when that card must not be overwritten. If the racing row vanished between violation and read (operator immediately dispatched), the loop retries INSERT.
- UPDATE path: a TOCTOU race vs. `dispatchOperatorOutbound` clears the pending slot between our slot read and our UPDATE. Either the prior-reason SELECT returns no row OR the conditional UPDATE returns rowcount=0 — either way we drop `existingPendingDraftId` and recurse into a fresh INSERT. A `23505` on the UPDATE itself (moving a row into an occupied slot, which only old code can do, during migration 041's deploy window) is a distinct red alert (`uniqueViolation: true`) and a throw.
- Sustained ping-pong (3 ticks without convergence) fires a red alert (`stage='persist'`) and throws. Caller maps to `AgentResult.failed`.

**No-demotion invariant**: structurally enforced by the `previous_pending_held` trigger inside `applyApprovalPolicyStage`. When the slot read finds a card in this draft's own slot, the trigger fires unconditionally; `triggers.length > 0`; the gate returns `action='queue'`. A high-fidelity untriggered regenerated draft on a guest with an existing pending row still queues — never sends out from under the operator. The fail-open path (`loadPendingRowsBySlot` errors → returns null → trigger doesn't fire → action might be 'send') is the only loophole; migration 041's partial unique indexes close it at the storage layer.

**TAC-308 narrows the no-demotion invariant, and adds `AgentResult.dropped`.** A pending row that is a *knowledge-gap card* is treated differently from every other pending draft, in exactly three ways. (1) If the new turn is independently sendable (no trigger fires at all), it SENDS — `previous_pending_held` no longer forces queue. What TAC-264 actually guards is a regenerated version of THE SAME draft going out from under an operator; a reply to a different question is not that, and the old behavior both silenced the guest AND overwrote the card. The coexistence is permitted because the send writes `review_state='auto_sent'`, outside the pending indexes (migration 020's, and since TAC-394 migration 041's two). (2) If the new turn also gaps, it UPDATEs in place and PRESERVES the original `pending_until`, so a chatty guest can't push the deadline out. (3) If the new turn would queue for any OTHER reason, the gate returns `action:'drop'` and the draft is discarded — not sent, not persisted. The guest is silent on that turn; that is the accepted cost of not losing the operator's question, and `captureDraftDropped` Slack-relays it precisely because it's the one path where a guest says something and receives nothing. **Every other pending row keeps pre-TAC-308 behavior exactly.**

**TAC-394: the regeneration's precondition was never built.** TAC-264's AC is "Regenerated pending draft reflects full conversation context, not just the latest message", and the regen path depends on it: a replacement that answers only the newest message silently drops whatever the pending draft said. Nothing ever told the model the pending draft was unsent, because the history projection rendered it like a sent message. v1.50.0 marks it (see *Recent conversation block* in the runtime contract) and deliberately does not tell the model to carry the draft forward. **Marking it does not stop the overwrite; PR 2's two slots do.** With v1.50.0 alone, a reply to the guest's next question still queues behind `previous_pending_held` and regenerates over a pending comp. Option B (refuse the overwrite, then send beside the card or drop the reply) was measured and would have dropped 29 of 60 replies on the incident's turn shape (see *Recent conversation block*); option F shipped instead, as the two pending slots below. A queued reply is always one unit: the gate judges the whole generated body, and only an auto-sent reply is split into bubbles, after the gate.

**Two pending slots per guest (TAC-394 PR 2, option F, migrations 041 and 042).** The invariant above is now ONE PENDING ROW PER SLOT. The obligation slot holds a draft whose structured carrier (`pending_commitment.type`) is `comp`, `hold` or `discount`; the conversation slot holds everything else, including a blank knowledge-gap card (TAC-309 nulls its carrier). A comp promised in prose without a carrier sits in the conversation slot (TAC-401). "Same commitment" means the same type AND the same `commitmentDedupKey` (trim plus lowercase). `decideSlotAction` (`lib/agent/pending-slots.ts`) is the ONE decision table, used by the gate, by `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft`'s 23505 recovery, by the crash card and by the decline, in this order:
  1. The draft's slot is empty: insert.
  2. The obligation slot holds a DIFFERENT commitment: drop, `obligation_slot_taken`, whatever the caller. No path overwrites one obligation with another.
  3. `never_regen` (manual followups): drop, `slot_occupied`. The Command Center route returns 409 with a plain-words detail.
  4. `regen_always` (operator decline): regenerate the card in the draft's own slot.
  5. `regen_gap_card_only` (crash card): regenerate a knowledge-gap card; drop `slot_occupied` over anything else.
  6. `regen` (inbound and engine followups): TAC-308's protected gap card still wins over a non-gap turn (`knowledge_gap_card_protected`); otherwise regenerate.

`previous_pending_held` fires only for a card in the SAME slot, so a reply beside a pending comp card sends when nothing else holds it, or queues as a second card when something does. **The knowledge-gap clock is decided per guest, not per slot:** a gap turn arms `pending_until` only when NEITHER slot already holds a knowledge-gap card (`anyKnowledgeGapCard`), at the gate, on the crash card and in race recovery, because the timeout scan fires once per card and a second clock would send the guest a second holding message. The realistic pair is a self-reported gap card in the conversation slot and a backstop-flagged comp in the obligation slot; code review caught it before merge. **What that guarantees, and what it does not.** A guest gets one holding message per wait whenever each gap turn can read the card the other wrote. Inside race recovery, each write decides the clock from the last successful slot read, less any card since found gone, so a card an operator handles mid-write takes with it the reason recovery had to withhold the clock. That restores only a clock race recovery withheld. When the GATE withheld it (its own read saw a gap card in the draft's own slot) and that card is handled before the regenerate lands, the card written in its place has no clock: it never sends a holding message, and its label keeps it protected. The crash card has the same gap. Both predate TAC-394 and are recorded under TAC-404. **Two gap turns seconds apart can still send two holding messages:** both reads find no gap card, one draft lands in each slot, the inserts never collide, and each card arms a clock. That needs a grounding-flagged comp and a knowledge gap for one guest within seconds. Migration 020 prevented it only by overwriting one of the two cards, the defect TAC-394 fixed, so it is accepted; a per-guest check in the timeout processor is TAC-404, which also covers the reverse case (an operator handles the card holding the clock before it fires, and a gap card created meanwhile has no clock of its own). **A residual, recorded and not fixed:** a comp the grounding backstop flags, created beside an existing gap card, arms no clock (the other card holds the guest's), and its `review_reason` is `commitment_type_gated`, which outranks the backstop label. `isKnowledgeGapCard` therefore never recognizes it: a same-commitment non-gap turn regenerates it without TAC-308's protection, and it never counts toward the per-guest clock. That is the gap-card predicate's accepted residual (see the `isKnowledgeGapCard` gotcha), reached at creation rather than after the clock fires. The gate reads both slots with `loadPendingRowsBySlot`, which fails OPEN to two empty slots; a card it missed surfaces as a 23505, and race recovery decides with the caller's own policy. A drop found there returns `action: 'dropped'` and is reported exactly like a gate-time drop. `captureDraftDropped` names both commitments and the guest (first name, last four digits of the phone), because the one production instance of two overlapping obligations (2026-08-07) happened mid-incident; the full number never leaves `lib/analytics/posthog.ts`. `draft_queued` gains `slot` and `otherSlotOccupied`. The operator queue gains `QueueDraft.otherPendingDraftsForGuest`, and each card's `recentContext` excludes every pending row (migration 042); the client half is TAC-402, blocked on TAC-395.
