Until TAC-403 ships in the operator app, a draft push tap opens the guest's oldest pending card rather than the one the push is about (the client matches drafts by `guestId` and ignores the payload's `draftId`); it is a normal build, independent of TAC-395, and does not gate migration 041. `lib/agent/two-pending-slots.test.ts` runs the real gate and persist layer against `lib/agent/testing/pending-rows-fake.ts`, an in-memory `messages` table that returns rows in insertion order (a read that forgot to order or to name its slot is handed the wrong card) and enforces migration 020 or 041.

**Manual followup interaction**: TAC-307 removed the gate bypass, so a manual followup now runs `applyApprovalPolicyStage` like any other and CAN queue. When it does, it takes its slot but never overwrites a card there: `previous_pending_held` does not fire for it, and when its slot is already occupied the gate, and persist race recovery through the `never_regen` policy, refuse it with `slot_occupied` instead of regenerating, and the Command Center route answers 409 with a plain-words detail (TAC-394). When the gate returns `send` — the common case at a venue with no hold configured — behaviour is unchanged from before: `review_state='auto_sent'`, no partial-unique-index conflict, and it can coexist with pending cards on the same guest.

### Follow-up engine (TAC-123, v1.21.0)

Daily per-venue scan that detects which enrolled guests are due for an unprompted touch (post-visit day_N, cold_lapsed re-engagement, perk_unlock), runs Gate 1 (`canSendFollowup`) + the claim-before-dispatch idempotency machinery, and hands off to the existing `handleFollowup` → `applyApprovalPolicyStage` (Gate 2) → dispatch tail. **No fork** of the outbound path. Concrete sibling to TAC-297's commitments processor; both live in their own folders (`lib/followups/` / `lib/guests/commitments-due.ts`) and the shared seam extraction is deferred per design call #3.

**Cron + trigger.** Hourly GH Actions tick (`.github/workflows/followups-due-cron.yml`) hits `/api/cron/followups-due`. The route delegates to `processDueFollowups(now)` in `lib/followups/engine.ts`. Per-venue local-hour filter (against `followup_rules.cron_hour_local`, default 10) lives in the processor; the cron fires hourly UTC and the processor matches per venue (Vercel Hobby blocks sub-daily Vercel cron — same posture as commitments-due).

**Detectors → reasons[] aggregation.** Three pure detectors in `lib/followups/detectors.ts`:
- `detectPostVisitReason(lastVisitAt, messagingCadence, now)` — highest ENABLED day_N tier the guest has crossed since their last visit. "Highest only" — skipped tiers don't backfill (sending day_1 + day_3 + day_7 at once reads as spam).
- `detectColdLapsedReason(lastVisitAt, currentState, rules, now)` — last visit older than `absence_window_days` AND current state in `lapsed_eligible_states` (default `['regular','raving_fan']` — we don't try to re-engage guests who never engaged).
- `detectPerkUnlockReason({currentState, eligibleMechanics, announcedMechanicIds, rules})` — first eligible mechanic the guest can now unlock that hasn't been announced yet (`announcedMechanicIds` parsed from `followup_log.dedup_key` rows with `perk:` prefix).
The engine aggregates hits into a `FollowupReason[]`, picks the primary via `PRIMARY_REASON_PRIORITY` (`perk_unlock > cold_lapsed > post_visit_day_14 > … > post_visit_day_1`), maps the primary to `FollowupTrigger.reason` (renaming `post_visit_day_N` → `day_N`; `cold_lapsed` / `perk_unlock` pass through), and puts the rest in `trigger.additionalReasons`. For perk_unlock, the chosen mechanic also rides as `trigger.perkMechanic` — `buildAiRuntime` reads it and populates `perkBeingUnlocked` on the AI runtime (first production caller of that AI-runtime field).

**A self-reported visit sets `last_visit_at`, gated by time precision (TAC-377).** Until this ticket `guests.last_visit_at` was written in exactly two places, both Square (`lib/pos/reconcile.ts`, `lib/pos/reconcile-tap.ts`). Le Mil's transactions are `manual` and `guest_reported`, so all six of its guests had `last_visit_at = null` — and since `detectPostVisitReason` and `detectColdLapsedReason` both key on that field, **the engine could only ever fire `perk_unlock` there.** The cron ran hourly, the scan reported success, and no `follow_up` message had ever been sent at that venue. The decision is that a self-report counts: `load-signals.ts` already consumes `guest_reported` transactions for recognition scoring, so excluding them from visit recency alone was an inconsistency, not a policy. TAC-323's write path (`lib/agent/extract-reported-order.ts`) now advances `last_visit_at` after its INSERT succeeds, guarded on the value being newer and mirroring `reconcile.ts`'s log-and-continue on failure.

**Precision is a three-state field and `null` is PERMISSIVE — do not "tidy" that into a stricter default.** `post_visit_day_*` requires the visit to be pinned to a real moment, because a "how was it?" scheduled a day after a visit nobody can place reads as the system inventing the visit. But only an explicit `'approximate'` blocks it:
- `pinned` — a present-tense report (`ReportTiming` from the Haiku extractor) sent while the venue was open. The message IS the receipt.
- `approximate` — any past-tense report, or a present-tense one sent after close. Written to `last_visit_at` all the same, so the guest profile stays honest; it just schedules no post-visit touch.
- `null` — nobody ever recorded a precision. Every row predating migration 036, and every Square write until that path opts in, where the timestamp is a receipt and is as pinned as it gets. Blocking on null would silently switch those off. (Forward-looking rather than a live regression: as of 2026-09-13 only four guests fleet-wide have a non-null `last_visit_at`, all at mock venues.)

`detectColdLapsedReason` deliberately takes NO precision parameter — it fires at 21+ days of silence, where a few hours of drift is noise. The precision value rides on `guests.last_visit_precision`, denormalized alongside `last_visit_at` and written in the SAME UPDATE so the two can never describe different visits; the engine reads it directly in its per-venue guest scan. **That invariant binds ALL THREE writers of `last_visit_at`, and the two Square ones were nearly missed** — `lib/pos/reconcile.ts` and `lib/pos/reconcile-tap.ts` both stamp `'pinned'` (a POS receipt is the most pinned evidence there is). A first version of TAC-377 changed only the `guest_reported` writer while asserting the invariant in three places, which would have left a guest who self-reports once ('approximate') and then becomes a POS regular permanently blocked from `post_visit_*`: the Square path would advance the timestamp past a stale precision, and `extractReportedOrder` disarms after one row so nothing would ever rewrite it. **Any future writer of `last_visit_at` must write the precision in the same statement.** An engine-side join back to `transactions` was rejected — a `cold_lapsed` candidate's anchoring row is 21+ days old, so there is no usable `occurred_at` cutoff to bound the scan.

**Two consequences worth knowing before reading follow-up volume as a bug.** (1) **The ladder fires once per guest, ever.** `extractReportedOrder`'s gate 2 permanently disarms after the first `guest_reported` row for a guest, so `last_visit_at` is written once on this path and then frozen — `post_visit_day_N` runs its course once, and `cold_lapsed` evaluates a date that never advances, so a weekly regular who reported once eventually reads as lapsed. TAC-325 is the fix. (2) **`manual` transactions still write nothing** — they are the bulk of the fleet's rows, and only `guest_reported` got this fix.

**Gate 1 — `canSendFollowup` (`lib/agent/followup-rules.ts`).** Pure function. Suppression order: opt-out → quiet hours (venue-local with midnight-crossing handled; default 21:00–08:00) → recent conversation (`recent_conversation_hours`, default 48) → weekly cap (rolling 7d, default 1; counts engine-initiated only — manual sends from TAC-249 don't count) → time-bound per-reason dedup (`cold_lapsed` only — `cold_dedup_days`, default 30). Intent-bound dedup (don't re-fire post_visit_day_7 for the same `last_visit_at`; don't re-fire perk_unlock for the same mechanic) is NOT here — that's the claim layer's job via the `followup_log.dedup_key` UNIQUE constraint.

**Gate 2 — `applyApprovalPolicyStage` (existing, TAC-212).** Engine sends are subject to all the same triggers — voice fidelity floor, model self-flag, comp regex backstop, sticky pending, commitment-type gate. A perk-bearing followup that requires operator approval queues normally; the operator queue UI surfaces it with whatever `primaryTrigger` the gate picked.

**Idempotency — claim-before-side-effect (TAC-123 plan-review).** Beats double-send under overlapping cron runs. Per guest, per run:
1. Engine builds `FollowupClaimRow[]` from detected reasons + their dedup_keys (`day_N:<last_visit_at_iso>` / `cold:<last_visit_at_iso>` / `perk:<mechanic_id>` per `dedupKeyForReason`).
2. `claimFollowupLogRows` atomically INSERTs all rows. UNIQUE conflict on `(venue_id, guest_id, dedup_key)` → entire INSERT rolls back (atomic), helper returns `{conflict: true}`, engine skips this guest.
3. `handleFollowup` dispatches. AgentResult `sent` / `queued` → `finalizeFollowupLogClaim` stamps `message_id`. `refused` / `failed` → `releaseFollowupLogClaim` DELETEs the claim so dedup isn't burned (next morning tick can re-attempt).

**Manual followups bypass the ENGINE entirely, but no longer the GATE.** TAC-249's Command Center button calls `handleFollowup` with `trigger.reason='manual'`; that path skips Gate 1 (`canSendFollowup` lives in the engine, not the orchestrator), doesn't write `followup_log` rows, and doesn't count toward `weekly_cap`. **It DOES run Gate 2 (`applyApprovalPolicyStage`) as of TAC-307** — see "Follow Up button bypass — REMOVED" under *Approval policy gates*.

**`messages.category` on engine sends.** All engine sends persist as `category='follow_up'` regardless of which reasons fire (TAC-123 plan-review call). The perk-weaving clause lives in `lib/ai/prompts/categories/follow-up.ts` and only fires when category is `follow_up`; routing perk-bearing engine runs to `category='perk_unlock'` would mean the clause never applied. The existing `perk_unlock` MessageCategory remains for the inbound / standalone perk moment (different tuning, untouched). `triggerToCategory` in `lib/agent/handle-followup.ts` maps both day_* and `perk_unlock` and `cold_lapsed` to `'follow_up'`.

**Per-venue config — `venue_configs.followup_rules` (migration 028).** Zod schema in `lib/schemas/followup-rules.ts`; default in `FOLLOWUP_RULES_DEFAULT`. Fields: per-reason `enabled` toggles, `absence_window_days`, `lapsed_eligible_states`, `cold_dedup_days`, `weekly_cap`, `recent_conversation_hours`, `quiet_hours_start_local` / `quiet_hours_end_local`, `cron_hour_local`. `parseFollowupRules` is fail-OPEN — null/missing/malformed jsonb returns defaults with a `console.warn` (same posture as `filterActiveContext`). The pre-existing `messaging_cadence` column stays the pure day-schedule (`{day_1, day_3, day_7, day_14}`); the engine reads both.

**Observability.** Two PostHog events in `lib/analytics/posthog.ts`: `followup_suppressed` (per-guest, fires from canSendFollowup blocks; PostHog + Slack relay so pilot ops can chart unexpected spikes) and `followup_scan_complete` (once per processor run, PostHog only; aggregated summary + per-venue breakdown). Both events are leaf — failures swallowed via `capturePostHogEvent`.

**Tunables** are surfaced read-only at `/admin/tunables` via `lib/tunables/manifest.ts` — five `agent_runtime`-category entries (`followup_absence_window_days`, `followup_cold_dedup_days`, `followup_lapsed_eligible_states`, `followup_weekly_cap`, `followup_recent_conversation_hours`) and three `timing`-category entries (`followup_quiet_hours_start_local`, `followup_quiet_hours_end_local`, `followup_cron_hour_local`). All sourced from `FOLLOWUP_RULES_DEFAULT` — runtime reads `venue_configs.followup_rules` per venue and the viewer shows the canonical default.

## Auth boundary (bearer-token path for analog-operator)

The venue-operator path is bearer-token authenticated via `verifyOperatorRequest` (or its `getCurrentOperator` wrapper, or the `withOperatorAuth(handler)` HOF for route handlers). The helper resolves the Supabase auth JWT to our internal `operatorId` plus an `allowedVenueIds` array. Route handlers do venue-mismatch checks themselves (`allowedVenueIds.includes(targetVenueId)`) and choose their response code — by convention `app/api/operator/*` returns 404 (not 403) for out-of-allowlist messages so existence isn't leaked; the helper itself only ever produces 401. RLS is the longer-term defense; the helpers are the v1 enforcement point.

**Status today:** the bearer path is live as of TAC-258 — five route handlers under `app/api/operator/*` (queue / approve / edit / skip / undo) consume `withOperatorAuth`. The `verifyAnalogAdminRequest` variant (bearer + `is_analog_admin = true`) remains forward-scaffolding for the future `analog-operator` admin-only surface. Active admin auth for `app/admin/(authed)/*` uses the cookie-session path via `verifyAnalogAdminAccess(authUserId)`, documented in "Admin scaffold" below. Don't conflate the two — the cookie path is for Analog staff in the Command Center; the bearer path is for venue operators in the mobile app + future dashboard.

### Multi-method auth + linking (TAC-272)

**Schema:** `operators` carries two nullable UUID columns — `auth_user_id_phone` and `auth_user_id_email` — each UNIQUE and FK-by-convention to `auth.users.id`. A CHECK enforces at least one is populated. `phone_number` is NOT NULL going forward. Both the bearer-token path (`verifyOperatorRequest`) and the cookie-session path (`verifyAnalogAdminAccess`) resolve the operator with the same OR-match: `auth_user_id_phone.eq.X OR auth_user_id_email.eq.X` where `X` is the just-verified `auth.uid()`. The RLS policy `operators_select_own` mirrors the OR clause with explicit `IS NOT NULL` guards on each side.

**Linking:** `linkOperatorByAuthUser(authUserId)` in `lib/auth/link-operator.ts` is the single point where an `auth.users` row gets bound to an `operators` row. Flow: `supabase.auth.admin.getUserById` → probe phone-vs-email identity → fast-path OR lookup → match against `operators` by `phone_number = authUserPhoneToE164(auth.users.phone)` OR `email = auth.users.email` → idempotent UPDATE of the appropriate column. Errors-as-values per the `RAGResult` / `AIResult` convention — six failure modes (`auth_user_not_found`, `auth_user_has_no_identity`, `no_matching_operator`, `multiple_matching_operators`, `already_claimed_by_different_user`, `db_error`). Never throws.

**Three call sites for linking:**
- `app/admin/auth/callback/route.ts` (eager) — right after `exchangeCodeForSession`, before the redirect to `/admin`. `no_matching_operator` → redirect with `?error=no_access`; other link failures → `?error=link_failed`.
- `lib/auth/verify-jwt.ts` (lazy) — when the OR-lookup misses, call `linkOperatorByAuthUser` and retry. Self-heals any auth user that authenticated before the callback was wired (and any future "auth-but-no-link" case).
- `lib/auth/verify-analog-admin.ts` (lazy, cookie-session) — same pattern, plus a follow-up `select is_analog_admin where id = operatorId` because the link helper doesn't return the admin flag.

**Phone normalization:** Supabase stores `auth.users.phone` WITHOUT the leading `+` (e.g. `"18777804236"`); `operators.phone_number` stores E.164 WITH `+` (e.g. `"+18777804236"`). `authUserPhoneToE164` in `lib/auth/normalize-phone.ts` bridges them at match time — Supabase manages `auth.users`, so we don't normalize at storage. Idempotent (an input already with `+` returns unchanged), null-safe, whitespace-trimming.

**Studio-only RPC drop (021):** A `link_operator_auth(text, text)` RPC existed in Studio (created during TAC-206) but had zero call sites in app code — orphaned linking infrastructure that never landed a caller. Migration 021 drops it. The TS-side `linkOperatorByAuthUser` is the canonical replacement. **Lesson worth remembering:** when CLAUDE.md flags "some policies may have been added directly in Studio, not via migrations" for RLS, the same risk applies to SQL functions. Audit Studio for both before any migration that touches `operators` (or any other table that's been long-lived enough to accumulate Studio-only objects). **Update (023, TAC-285):** the RPC is re-introduced under the same signature because `analog-operator/lib/auth/operator.ts` calls it post-OTP / magic-link to link the auth user to the operators row. The TS-side helper remains canonical for analog-guest's own surfaces; the RPC is the operator-app surface only. Now versioned via migration 023, not Studio-only.

## Admin scaffold

The Command Center lives at `app/admin/*` inside this repo and is served via a separate Vercel project (`analog-admin`) on `admin.theanalog.company`. Production middleware at `middleware.ts` (root) host-gates: admin host serves only `/admin/*`; guest host 404s `/admin/*`. Local dev (`localhost`/`127.0.0.1`) and `*.vercel.app` previews allow everything for QA.

**Admin API routes live under `/admin/{surface}/api/{thing}`, not `/api/admin/...`.** The host gate above 404s anything not starting with `/admin` on the admin apex. Routes placed at `/api/admin/...` work locally and on `*.vercel.app` previews (which bypass the gate for QA) but 404 in production. Existing pattern to match: `/admin/conversations/api/trace/[traceId]`. Failure signature: route works locally, deploys cleanly, prod fetches return 404 with the correct request URL — first thing to check is whether the path starts with `/admin/`. (THE-203 hotfix #29.)

Auth is magic-link via Supabase (`signInWithOtp`). The protected tree lives under `app/admin/(authed)/` — its layout reads the cookie session via `createServerClient` and calls `verifyAnalogAdminAccess(session.user.id)`. Sign-in (`/admin/sign-in`) and the OAuth callback (`/admin/auth/callback`) are siblings outside the route group so they bypass the gate without redirect loops.

Three states at the gate:
- No session → redirect to `/admin/sign-in`.
- Session, not analog admin → render `<NotAuthorized>` in place (don't redirect; the user has identity, just not authorization).
- Session + analog admin → render `<AdminShell>` (sidebar + topbar + content).

To grant admin access: SQL template under "Common gotchas" (the THE-199 entry).

Brand language is canonical at `docs/brand/style-guide-v01.html`. Fonts load via `next/font/google` in `app/layout.tsx` (Fraunces with `opsz/SOFT/WONK` axes + Inter Tight). Tokens live in `app/globals.css` under `@theme inline` — Tailwind v4, no separate config file. Copy register: **Direct** per the style guide. No emoji, no checkmarks; status indicators use `<StatusDot>`.

**Per-surface palette overrides (TAC-237).** Admin renders on white, not the canonical cream — implemented as a `[data-surface="admin"]` block in `app/globals.css` that re-binds `--paper`, `--parchment`, `--highlight`, and `--stone-light` to a neutral grayscale, scoped via `app/admin/layout.tsx` setting `data-surface="admin"` on its root div. The cascade lets every admin component keep its existing `bg-paper` / `bg-parchment` / `bg-highlight` Tailwind classes unchanged. **Don't mutate `:root` tokens for surface-specific tweaks** — scope them via `[data-surface="..."]` so canonical brand stays canonical and other surfaces (operator dashboard, future repos) can opt into their own palette without drift.

**shadcn/ui component layer (TAC-306).** CC's shared component set is shadcn/ui (Radix + Tailwind, copy-in source we own under `components/ui/*`), themed to the brand — NOT a second palette. The bridge lives in `app/globals.css`:
- **Token bridge.** shadcn's semantic tokens (`--color-background`, `--color-primary`, `--color-muted-foreground`, `--color-border`, `--color-ring`, the `--color-sidebar-*` set, …) are registered in the `@theme inline` block mapped **directly onto the brand source vars** (`var(--paper)`, `var(--clay)`, `var(--ink)`, `var(--stone-light)`, …). Because `@theme inline` inlines the source var into each utility, and the brand vars are re-bound under `[data-surface="admin"]` (TAC-237 above), the shadcn tokens inherit the admin grayscale automatically at every use site in CC. **Keep the mapping aliased to brand vars, not literals** — a literal would freeze the token to cream and break the admin override. The only non-brand literals are `--destructive` (`#DC2626`, matched to `StatusDot` `bad`) and `--radius`, both on `:root`.
- **Named radius scale is intentionally NOT remapped.** Standard shadcn derives `--radius-sm/md/lg/xl` from `--radius` in `@theme`; we omit that so existing surfaces' `rounded-md`/`rounded-lg` don't shift. Vendored components render with Tailwind's default radius scale (acceptable for an internal surface); a future surface PR can revisit if spec-accurate radii are wanted.
- **Admin-scoped border default.** A `@layer base` block pins `border-color: var(--stone-light)` for `[data-surface="admin"] *` because Tailwind v4's bare `border` utility defaults to `currentColor`; shadcn components rely on a `--border` default. Scoped to admin so guest surfaces keep Tailwind's default; every existing CC border already sets an explicit color, so only the new shadcn components are affected.
- **Card.** Exactly one Card component (`@/components/ui/card`). The old `lib/ui/card.tsx` `default` / `trace` variants are reproduced at call sites via className overrides (`block gap-0 rounded-[2px] border-stone-light/60 bg-paper py-0 shadow-none` for default; `rounded-md border-stone-light/60 bg-parchment shadow-none` prefix for trace).
- **Token-resolution guard.** `lib/ui/token-bridge.test.ts` statically parses `globals.css` and fails if any required shadcn token is unbridged or points at an undefined var. Adding a component that references a new token means adding the bridge entry AND the token name to `REQUIRED_SHADCN_TOKENS`.
- **Shell (TAC-306 PR #2).** The CC shell (`app/admin/_components/*`) runs on the shadcn `Sidebar` family (`SidebarProvider` + `Sidebar` `collapsible="icon"` + `SidebarInset`, wired in `admin-shell.tsx`). `app/admin/_components/nav-items.ts` is the **single source of nav truth** (pure data + `isNavItemActive`) consumed by BOTH the sidebar and the ⌘K command palette so they can't drift — add a surface there, not inline. Nav icons live in `sidebar.tsx`'s `NAV_ICONS` (keyed by href) to keep `nav-items.ts` pure/unit-testable (`nav-items.test.ts`). The ⌘K palette is `command-palette.tsx`: a client `CommandPaletteProvider` (owns open state + the global `⌘K`/`Ctrl+K` listener + renders `CommandDialog` once) with a `useCommandPalette()` hook and a `CommandPaletteTrigger` for the top bar; `SidebarProvider` owns the separate `⌘B` collapse. The chrome wraps but never touches the auth gate in `(authed)/layout.tsx`.
- Per the ticket, the foundation (init + token bridge + Card reconcile) ships first; per-surface reskins (shell + ⌘K ✓, conversations, health, tunables, venue-config viewer, voices) are follow-up PRs that wire the installed components.

Magic-link callback URL is constructed from `NEXT_PUBLIC_ADMIN_URL` (per environment) plus a fallback to `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_URL` so preview deploys self-resolve. Supabase project's "Site URL" + "Redirect URLs" allowlist must include all three: localhost, `*.vercel.app`, and `admin.theanalog.company`. Configure in Supabase Studio.

### Conversations viewer (THE-201)

`/admin/conversations` is the headline debugging surface — single-conversation focus, iMessage-style bubble thread, inline Langfuse trace render. Filters live in the URL (`?venue=&guest=`); reload preserves view, links are shareable.

- **Routing:** server-component page (`page.tsx`) does all initial fetches in one render path. Client component (`conversations-client.tsx`) owns selection, trace cache, Realtime. Pre-filter / venue-only paths render `<EmptyState>` with a 5-row recent-activity list scoped to the operator's `allowedVenueIds`.
- **Bubble palette:** real iMessage colors (#007AFF outbound / #E5E5EA inbound) on a paper-toned background. Inter Tight inside bubbles. Brand discipline yields to fitness-for-purpose on internal surfaces — same logic as health-page status colors.
- **Trace fetch:** server prefetches the last 5 outbound messages' traces in parallel via `lib/observability/fetchTrace`; on-demand fetch for older messages via `app/admin/(authed)/conversations/api/trace/[traceId]/route.ts` (cookie-session auth, never exposes the Langfuse secret key client-side). `Promise.allSettled` so a Langfuse outage doesn't 500 the page.
- **Trace panel shape:** linear stage stack matching the agent pipeline order (`context_build` → `classify` → `retrieve` → `generate` → `send`, with `generate.attempt_N` nested). `lib/select-trace-stages.ts` is the pure projection; unknown observations bucket into "Other" so future stages don't silently disappear before this code is updated.
- **Realtime:** subscribes to `messages` filtered by `venue_id` (single-column server filter), refines by `guest_id` client-side. Uses `lib/db/browser.ts` (browser-side anon Supabase client). Subscribe on mount; tear down on unmount or filter change (filter changes navigate via `router.replace`, which remounts the client component cleanly).
- **Recognition state:** read latest `guest_states` row directly. **Don't** call `computeGuestState` from the page — it's expensive and writes audit rows on transitions.
- **StatusDot mapping for guest state:** `neutral` for `new`, `good` for `returning|regular|raving_fan`. `bad` is reserved for actually-wrong states (failed sends, errors), not "this guest is new."
- **Message window (TAC-316):** newest-200 rows per conversation load — `ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 200`, reversed via the projection. Was ASC until TAC-316, which kept the OLDEST 200 and silently hid everything newer once Mock Sextant crossed the cap (the ticket's headline truncation). Older history reachable via TAC-202 (guest detail page) once that ships.
- **Show-everything contract (TAC-316):** the conversation query has NO filter on `status`, `review_state`, or body content — never-sent superseded drafts, pending cards, failed sends, blank knowledge-gap cards, reaction rows, and media rows all render. `app/admin/(authed)/conversations/lib/project-thread.ts` is the pure projection: groups raw rows into RESPONSES by `generation_id ?? id` (keyed-not-adjacent, migration 032 identity — sibling of `lib/agent/group-responses.ts` but viewer-shaped, and unlike the sibling it sorts internally so input order can't silently break it), resolves blank-body placeholders per kind, and derives delivery-state labels via `deriveResponseState` — known combos get friendly labels ("never sent — superseded", "pending review", "failed to send"), **unknown enum values render as literal text** (`status=X · review_state=Y`), never an exhaustive switch. Split responses render as separate chained bubbles sharing ONE selection/trace/review identity keyed on the first bubble's row id. Response-rate stats count dispatched RESPONSES only (`wasDispatched` — consistent with `count_outbound_responses`); never-sent/pending/failed/unknown rows are visible but excluded from stats. Client state is raw rows; the thread derives via `useMemo(projectThread)` so Realtime handlers stay row-grained.
- **Full-width frame (TAC-316):** the admin shell's content frame is `ContentFrame` (`app/admin/_components/content-frame.tsx`) — left-pinned `max-w-5xl` by default, with an explicit `FULL_WIDTH_ROUTES` list (currently `/admin/conversations` only) that drops the cap so the detail panel and context cards flex into the full viewport. Deliberately NOT centered (TAC-316 layout ruling); the thread column stays 400px. New full-width surfaces opt in by adding their route prefix there.
- **Routes:**
  - `GET /admin/conversations/api/trace/[traceId]` — on-demand Langfuse trace fetch (cookie-session auth, server-side secret).
  - `POST /admin/conversations/api/follow-up` — operator-initiated manual outbound. Calls `handleFollowup` with `reason='manual'`; the operator's note becomes a top-level block in the system prompt (THE-232).
  - `PUT /admin/conversations/api/review/[messageId]` — captures per-message review (THE-235); writes `messages.response_review` and mirrors the 08-flow destinations (`voice_corpus` row keyed on `cc-review:{message_id}`, anti-pattern dedupe-append).
  - `DELETE /admin/conversations/api/transactions/[transactionId]` (TAC-323) — the recovery path for a false-positive self-reported order: deletes a `transactions` row, restricted server-side to `source='guest_reported'` (400 for any other source; no editing of items or amounts, delete only). Auth/venue-allowlist pattern mirrors the `review/[messageId]` route above exactly. Deletion needs no separate re-arm flag — `lib/agent/extract-reported-order.ts`'s gate reads the transactions table directly, so the row's absence IS the re-arm.

---

### Voices command center (THE-237 + THE-238)

`/admin/voices` (list) and `/admin/voices/[slug]` (per-voice workbench) are the surface for refining a venue's agent voice — direct edits to rules, corpus, and persona; threads list + bubble thread + live regen playground; pattern-detection banner in the Rules tab. The flow is: click flagged outbound → type why it's bad → regen → pick the best attempt → commit. Pattern detection surfaces a banner when ≥3 similar edit_only critiques accumulate without promotion.

**Routes** (under `/admin/voices/api/...` per the host-gating convention):
- `PATCH /admin/voices/api/persona/[venueId]` — single writer for all `BrandPersona` fields (voiceName, tone, formality, lengthGuide, emojiPolicy, signaturePhrases, bannedTopics, voiceTouchstones, speakerFraming, speakerName). Anti-patterns are excluded; they have their own endpoints.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/venues/[venueId]/corpus` — ad-hoc corpus add (uses `addCorpusEntry`).
- `PATCH /admin/voices/api/corpus/[entryId]` — content/tags update; re-embeds only on content change.
- `DELETE /admin/voices/api/corpus/[entryId]` — delete + FK-cascade voice_embeddings.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/venues/[venueId]/rules` — anti-pattern add via `dedupeAndAppendAntiPatterns({source:'manual', authorOperatorId})`. Outer whitespace trimmed at the boundary.
- `DELETE /admin/voices/api/venues/[venueId]/rules` — exact-text remove via `removeAntiPattern`.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/regenerate` — runs `regenerateWithCritique` and returns one new attempt. Multi-attempt state lives client-side; this endpoint is stateless. No PostHog, no Langfuse, no alerts.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/classify-critique` — fires when the commit modal opens; advisory only, operator overrides before commit.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/commit` — orchestrates corpus row (replace-mode upsert keyed on `voices-commit:{messageId}`) + anti-pattern (when `kind=edit_and_rule`, `source='manual'`) + critique row + cluster check (when `kind=edit_only`). Fires `voice_critique_committed` PostHog event with `clusterFormed` boolean for empirical cosine-threshold tuning.
- `GET /admin/voices/api/patterns/[venueId]` — re-derives confirmed clusters across the venue's unresolved edit_only pool. Runs cosine search per row + verification per candidate cluster. **TODO** to persist `cluster_signature + proposed_rule + last_verified_at` if verification frequency becomes annoying — until then, every rail load pays for one Sonnet call per cluster.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/patterns/[venueId]/promote` — appends the synthesized rule with `source='auto'` (the pill that distinguishes promoted-cluster rules from manually-typed ones) and marks member critiques `promoted_at = now()`.
- `POST /admin/voices/api/patterns/[venueId]/dismiss` — marks member critiques `dismissed_at = now()` so the cluster stops surfacing. No rule write.

**Why two parameter shapes:** `/corpus/[entryId]` and `/venues/[venueId]/corpus` live at different path depths because Next.js's dynamic-route disambiguator can't distinguish two `[param]` siblings at the same depth. Venue-scoped collection routes nest under `venues/[venueId]/...` to keep entry-scoped routes clean. Same pattern applied to rules. Persona stays at `/persona/[venueId]` since there's no entry-scoped sibling.

**`router.refresh()` propagation pattern.** All mutation handlers in the rail components do `await fetch(...)` → on success call `router.refresh()`. The refresh re-runs the `(authed)` layout (which re-fetches the sidebar's voice list) and the per-voice page (which re-fetches persona, corpus, threads, tab counts, and last-refined). Optimistic local form state can ride on top — server is source of truth, refresh is what reconciles. Specifically: voiceName changes are visible in the topbar + sidebar + voice list page after a single refresh. No client-side store, no duplicated fetch, no stale-while-revalidate concerns.

**Universal rules display vs SYSTEM_TEMPLATE.** The locked panel in the Rules tab (currently R1-R11 + R17-R18 + R21 + R23-R34, per the "Universal voice rules R1-R34" entry above — don't trust this number either, check `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` itself; R22 is undisplayed, so it does not appear in the rail) reads from `app/admin/(authed)/voices/[slug]/_lib/universal-rules.ts` — a hardcoded `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` constant. The corresponding rules live in `lib/ai/prompts/system-template.ts` under "# Universal voice rules". **The two are dual sources of truth and must move in lockstep.** When a universal rule is added, removed, renumbered, or substantially reworded in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`, update `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY` in the same commit. Tracking follow-up to extract a structured rules registry so this coupling goes away (THE-237 follow-up: structured rules registry).

**Display-completeness guard, and the label fix (TAC-348).** The original 14-shown-vs-21-in-the-prompt bug (the motivating defect for TAC-348) wasn't a lockstep failure exactly — it was that nothing forced anyone to decide whether a *new* rule belonged on the rail at all. `universal-rules.ts` now also exports `UNIVERSAL_RULES_UNDISPLAYED` (every mechanical/rendering-timing bullet id, by name), and a test in `system-template.test.ts` ("universal rule classification completeness") counts the actual `- ` bullet lines in `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`'s `# Universal voice rules` section and asserts the displayed + undisplayed sets together account for every one of them, with no id double-classified. This deliberately does NOT assert `UNIVERSAL_RULES_DISPLAY.length === template bullet count` — that would force every mechanical bullet onto the operator rail and revert the TAC-314 decision that a deliberately non-contiguous displayed set is correct. The rail's label in `rail-rules.tsx` also changed from `Universal · {N} (locked)` to `Universal · {N} shown of {N + undisplayed} (locked)` so it no longer reads as if the displayed count is the complete rule count.

**Realtime on the per-voice page.** The threads list + thread pane subscribe to `messages` rows for the venue using the same `createBrowserClient` + `postgres_changes` pattern as the conversations viewer ([conversations-client.tsx:153-214](app/admin/(authed)/conversations/conversations-client.tsx#L153-L214)). Duplicated inline this round; extract a shared `useVenueMessagesSubscription(venueId)` hook in a follow-up if drift becomes real.

**Regen helper coupling with `lib/agent/stages.ts` (THE-238, refined in TAC-183).** `lib/voices/regenerate-with-critique.ts` deliberately mirrors the `classifyStage → retrieveCorpusStage → retrieveKnowledgeStage → generateStage` wiring in `lib/agent/stages.ts`, calling the raw `lib/ai` + `lib/rag` primitives directly because the agent stages emit PostHog/Langfuse events that would flood telemetry on the regen path (5–20 regens per session). **Analytics isolation means: don't invoke each other's telemetry paths. Sharing values via imports is fine and preferred.** TAC-183 dedupes the four retrieval thresholds (`STRONG_MATCH_SIMILARITY`, `MIN_STRONG_MATCHES`, `CORPUS_RETRIEVE_LIMIT`, `KNOWLEDGE_RETRIEVE_LIMIT`) by importing them from `lib/agent/stages.ts` rather than redeclaring them — silent drift between the two paths is now structurally impossible. **TAC-366 extends that from values to the first shared HELPER**: `filterByRelevance` is exported from `stages.ts` and imported here, so a change to `KNOWLEDGE_RELEVANCE_FLOOR` reaches both paths without anyone remembering to mirror it. **Mirror-by-discipline has failed once on this seam, with one near-miss of the same class.** The failure: TAC-350 added the relevance floor to `stages.ts` and mirrored only its grounding backstop here, leaving regen on pre-TAC-350 retrieval semantics — Voices surfaced up to four chunks where production surfaced zero, drift running in the direction that HID the TAC-358 bug from anyone reproducing it in the playground. The near-miss: TAC-301 part 1.5's grounding-verifier defect. **Mirroring WORKED there** — commit `396d73f` updated this file in the same PR with an explicit "mirrored from verifyGroundingStage per this file's standing obligation" comment — and the defect lived in the shared `lib/ai/verify-grounding.ts` `buildSourceMaterial`, hitting both paths identically. Same failure class (a maintained subset falling behind), different mechanism. Prefer importing a shared helper over restating the logic, every time. **The subtle half of the TAC-366 drift is worth knowing before the next fix here**: regen was missing the filter AND keying its zero-result fallback off zero RETURNED rows where production keys off zero RELEVANT rows (TAC-350 changed that deliberately). Applying the filter only at the end of the block fixes the first and silently leaves the second — there is a mutation-verified regression test for exactly that mutant. The two paths still share the same `buildAiRuntime` mapper (exported from stages.ts so regen can reuse it) and the same knowledge-degrades-gracefully posture. **If gating logic (e.g. `shouldRetrieveKnowledge`) or post-generation behavior changes in `stages.ts`, mirror it in `regenerate-with-critique.ts`.** Regen does NOT enforce `SEND_FIDELITY_FLOOR` — operator decides what's good enough by reading the result. **TAC-350 mirrors the grounding backstop here too, but ADVISORY rather than gating** — `RegenerateWithCritiqueResult` now carries `knowledgeGap` (the model's self-report, computed by `generateMessage` all along but not previously surfaced on this path), plus `hasUngroundedClaim` / `ungroundedClaims` from the same `verifyGrounding` call `verifyGroundingStage` makes, run under the identical skip condition (only when `knowledgeGap` is false). There is no send/queue decision to gate on the regen path — the operator reviews the raw attempt directly — so these ride through the `/admin/voices/api/regenerate` response as signals for the Voices UI to surface, not as a trigger. No PostHog event (analytics isolation, same as everywhere else on this path).

**Pattern detection persistence rule.** `voice_critiques` stores every committed critique regardless of `kind`; the cluster query (`find_similar_critiques` SQL function) filters to `kind = 'edit_only' AND promoted_at IS NULL AND dismissed_at IS NULL` at read time. Converting an edit_only critique into a rule (kind change) would require row reshape; in practice that path doesn't exist yet, but the rule is documented to keep schema-future-readers from gating insert by kind. The 0.85 cosine threshold is the verification-call gate; logged via `voice_critique_committed` PostHog events with `topSimilarity` and `clusterFormed` so the threshold can be empirically tuned during the first 30 days of usage.

### Venues surface (TAC-343 — Stage A read-only, Stage B knowledge_corpus writes, Stage C venue_info + mechanics writes, Stage D currentContext queue + readiness polish)

`/admin/venues` lists venues; `/admin/venues/[slug]` is the per-venue page — the ongoing source of truth for everything variable about a venue once onboarding's initial load is done (§0 of TAC-343: "onboarding is the initial load, this page is the ongoing source of truth"). All four planned stages have shipped: Stage A (read-only: every section renders, nothing writes), Stage B (knowledge_corpus add/edit/delete/split/merge), Stage C (venue_info + mechanics editing), and Stage D (the `currentContext` expiry queue's Add/Drop/Promote actions, plus readiness panel polish). The surface sits behind the analog-admin auth gate and writes to stores (`venue_configs`, `knowledge_corpus`, `mechanics`) that change live agent behavior.

**Files.** `app/admin/(authed)/venues/page.tsx` (list) + `app/admin/(authed)/venues/[slug]/page.tsx` (detail, orchestrates every section below) + `app/admin/(authed)/venues/[slug]/_components/*` (one component per §2 section, sharing `<KnowledgeEntryList>` for every knowledge_corpus-tag-backed section and `<SectionShell>`/`<EmptySectionNote>` for chrome). Loaders live at `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/load-venues.ts` (list, mirrors `load-voices.ts`'s shape) and `load-venue-detail.ts` (detail — mirrors `voices/[slug]/_lib/load-voice-page.ts`'s degrade-gracefully posture: venue-not-found → `null` → `notFound()`; a malformed `venue_info`/`brand_persona` jsonb → a fallback value plus a parse-error string rendered as a banner, not a 500). Pure logic — readiness thresholds, knowledge-tag section grouping, the currentContext active/expired/malformed partition, mechanic-row field helpers, and the unclaimed-field computation — lives at `app/admin/(authed)/venues/_lib/*.ts`, each with a colocated `.test.ts`.

**Two deliberate divergences from what you'd copy by default, both load-bearing:**
- **The mechanics SELECT is wide and does NOT reuse `build-runtime-context.ts`'s narrower runtime SELECT.** The runtime query omits `trigger`, `expiration_rule`, and `redemption` because the agent never reads them (see the TAC-343 investigation entries under "Approval policy gates" / "Mechanics editing" above) — exactly the columns this admin surface needs to render and, in later stages, edit. Copying the runtime SELECT here would silently reintroduce the same runtime/investigation-visibility gap TAC-343 exists to close.
- **"Render from the data, not from a hand-built list of sections."** `unclaimed-fields.ts` declares an explicit claimed-keys allowlist for both `venue_info` top-level keys and `VenueDetailMechanicRow` fields; anything not on the allowlist renders in a visible "Unclaimed" catch-all instead of being silently dropped, and anything whose value is empty (per `isEmptyUnclaimedValue`) is additionally suppressed from that catch-all — an empty `{}`/`[]`/`''` unclaimed field is noise, not signal. `menu.highlights` and `venue_info.qrEnrollmentMessage` surfaced through this mechanism first, then got PLACED deliberately rather than left in the catch-all: `menu.highlights` → "The menu" section, alongside the roster — it's the exact field TAC-331's always-on-advice bug lived in (an always-on, every-turn-rendered field that carried "first-timer pick" opinion instead of a fact about the menu, which is what made the agent recommend a latte to a guest already holding a drink), so `MenuRosterSection` renders it with an explicit caption carrying that ruling forward: facts about the menu, not advice about what to order. `qrEnrollmentMessage` → Venue facts (guest-facing operational copy, not knowledge, not menu). `mechanics.metadata` was checked directly against the live table before any placement decision (confirmed `{}` on all 6 live mechanics, no exceptions) rather than assumed — since it's empty everywhere, it's excluded from the render entirely by the empty-value suppression above, not placed anywhere; a future mechanic that actually populates `metadata` will surface it in the catch-all automatically. The mechanics allowlist is keyed on the LOADER'S camelCase `VenueDetailMechanicRow` shape (`isActive`, `rewardDescription`, …), not raw snake_case DB column names — an earlier version kept the allowlist snake_case while the only call site passed the camelCase row through an `as unknown as Record<...>` cast, so every mapped field silently read as "unclaimed" and the catch-all became noise instead of signal on every mechanic. Caught in code review before merge, not after. The allowlist is typed `satisfies ReadonlyArray<keyof VenueDetailMechanicRow>` now specifically so a field rename fails `tsc` instead of quietly reintroducing the same class of bug.

**Knowledge-tag section grouping is a closed map, not a scan of every canonical tag.** `section-grouping.ts`'s `TAG_TO_SECTION` covers 11 of the 12 `KNOWLEDGE_PRIMARY_TAGS` values; `mechanic` is deliberately unmapped, because §2's "Mechanics" section names the `mechanics` DB table, not `knowledge_corpus` rows tagged `mechanic`/`mechanic_*` — those fall into the structural unclaimed bucket, not into the `mechanic`-adjacent-sounding "Mechanics" section or the `other` tag's own named section.

**Approval policy controls (TAC-307).** `<ApprovalPolicySection>` (`_components/approval-policy-section.tsx`, client) is the write side of `venue_configs.approval_policy` — the column had a runtime reader since v1.24.0 but no way to set it short of hand-written SQL. One checkbox per category plus a master "Hold everything" switch, writing via `PATCH /admin/venues/api/venues/[venueId]/approval-policy`. Four things about it that are decisions, not incidentals:
- **Categories come from `MESSAGE_CATEGORIES`**, never a list maintained in the component — see the gotcha on that constant. `opt_out` is filtered out via `POLICY_EXEMPT_CATEGORIES` and refused independently by the route.
- **The master switch writes `{default:'operator_approval', perCategory:{}}`** and disables every per-category control. It holds everything with no per-category escape hatch, because it is what gets reached for when something is already wrong.
- **Saving writes ONLY the categories the admin actually touched**, layered on the venue's existing stored map. It still closes the merge trap (unchecking a code-defaulted category is a touch, so it persists an explicit `auto_send` and the box stays unchecked on reload) while avoiding the reverse hazard: writing every shown category would turn `comp_complaint`'s pre-checked, nobody-chose-it code default into a `stored` entry, silently stripping its clarifying-question carve-out because an admin ticked something unrelated — and would break the fleet-default mechanism itself, since a future category added to `APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT.perCategory` for no-migration rollout would silently skip every venue anyone had ever saved here. **This was caught in code review, not in the original plan, which specified write-everything.**
- **The route is STRICTER than the runtime reader, deliberately** — it rejects unknown category keys and unknown dispositions, where `parseApprovalPolicy` degrades gracefully. Admin write boundary vs. live read boundary; the same strict-offline / permissive-live split documented under *Common gotchas*. It replaces the whole `approval_policy` object rather than read-modify-writing, which is safe here (and only here) because that column holds exactly `{default, perCategory}` and the client sends both — unlike `venue_info`, which has sibling keys a partial write would drop.

The Readiness panel still renders the effective policy read-only, now labelled as such and pointing at this section, so the two can't be read as contradicting each other.

**Readiness thresholds** (`READINESS_THRESHOLDS` in `readiness.ts`): `MIN_VOICE_CORPUS = 8`, `MIN_KNOWLEDGE_CHUNKS = 15`, `MIN_CURRENT_CONTEXT_DATED = 2`, sourced from the onboarding appendix's minimum-viable-transcript numbers. `computeReadiness` is pure (caller-loaded data in, a `ReadinessReport` out) and enforces two exclusion rules worth remembering if you touch it: an `is_processed=false` knowledge entry has no `knowledge_embeddings` and is therefore invisible to retrieval despite looking like ordinary content, so it's excluded from the count-vs-15 threshold and separately flagged as a non-zero "not retrievable" count; every mechanics check (missing-params, `manual_invite`-without-`requires_operator_approval`) is scoped to `is_active=true` mechanics only, since a deactivated mechanic's gaps aren't reachable by any guest.

**Resolved in Stage C, deliberately not done in Stage A:** `readiness.ts` used to reimplement the `{ ...APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT.perCategory, ...policy.perCategory }` merge inline for display, duplicating the same expression inside `resolveCategoryPolicy` (`lib/schemas/approval-policy.ts`) — flagged in Stage A's code review, deliberately deferred rather than touching live agent-runtime code (that file's own header says "THE MERGE IS LOAD-BEARING") inside a PR whose whole point was a read-only surface. Stage C extracted the merge into `getEffectivePerCategoryPolicy(policy)`, exported from `approval-policy.ts`; `resolveCategoryPolicy` now calls it internally (pure extract-method, hand-verified byte-identical, `applyApprovalPolicyStage`'s existing tests pass unchanged) and `readiness.ts` calls the same function, so the two can no longer independently drift.

**`lib/schemas/venue-info.ts` gained `classifyContextEntry`** (active/expired/malformed classification for one `currentContext` entry) as part of this ticket — `filterActiveContext` is now built on top of it, behavior-unchanged (same warning text, same strictly-future comparison), so the admin page's expiry queue (`app/admin/(authed)/venues/_lib/expiry-queue.ts`) and the agent's own prompt-filtering can't independently drift on what "expired" means.

**Stage B — knowledge_corpus add/edit/delete/split/merge.** `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/knowledge-corpus.ts` mirrors `lib/voice-training/{add,edit,remove}-corpus-entry.ts` 1:1 for the single-row operations (insert/update → `ingestKnowledgeCorpusEntry` → cleanup-on-embed-failure), admin-route-scoped rather than a new `lib/knowledge-training/` directory since nothing outside this surface needs single-row knowledge_corpus CRUD yet. Auth: `requireKnowledgeEntryAdmin` (mirrors `requireCorpusEntryAdmin`) and `requireKnowledgeEntriesAdmin` (new shape for merge — resolves the target venue from the entries themselves, refusing if fewer than 2 ids, any id doesn't resolve, or the ids span more than one venue; never trusts a client-supplied `venueId`). Routes under `app/admin/(authed)/venues/api/**`: `POST venues/[venueId]/knowledge` (add), `PATCH|DELETE knowledge/[entryId]` (edit/remove), `POST knowledge/[entryId]/split`, `POST knowledge/merge` (flat, not `[entryId]`-scoped, since it takes N ids in the body).

**Split/merge atomicity, without a transaction.** `splitKnowledgeEntry`/`mergeKnowledgeEntries` insert all new rows first, embed each via `ingestKnowledgeCorpusEntry` in sequence, and only delete the source row(s) after every embed succeeds. A failure partway through deletes whatever new rows were already inserted (`.delete().in('id', [...])`, cascade clears their embeddings) and leaves the source(s) untouched — there is no point where content is lost. The one accepted residual, stated in the code and here: if the final delete of the source(s) itself fails after every embed succeeded, the result is a duplicate (source and new rows both live), not data loss — logged loudly via `console.error`, still returned `ok:true`, since split/merge's actual deliverable (the new, retrievable rows) succeeded. `chunkText()` is NOT used to decide split boundaries — it's what `ingestKnowledgeCorpusEntry` already uses internally to turn ONE row's content into 1+ embedding chunks, a different problem (character-count chunking) from "which claim goes in which new row" (an operator judgment call). Split's own naive starting suggestion (`naiveSplitSuggestion` in `knowledge-entry-list.tsx`) is UI-only — a paragraph-boundary guess that seeds the split textareas, never sent to the server; the write path embeds whatever final strings the operator actually submits.

**The `is_processed=false` fix path is edit, not a separate retry action.** `editKnowledgeEntry` re-embeds whenever `content` is present in the request (optimistically flips `is_processed` to `false` first, same "stale while re-embedding" signal `editCorpusEntry` uses for voice_corpus; `ingestKnowledgeCorpusEntry` flips it back to `true` on success). The edit UI always sends the (possibly unchanged) content string on Save, so hitting Save on an already-unprocessed entry IS the retry — there's no dedicated "retry embed" button. Confirmed: a section going to zero knowledge entries after a delete renders the same `<EmptySectionNote>` a genuinely-empty section already rendered in Stage A — no separate empty-state handling was needed, since `KnowledgeEntryList`'s `entries.length === 0` branch was never section-specific to begin with.

**UI: `KnowledgeEntryList` (`app/admin/(authed)/venues/[slug]/_components/knowledge-entry-list.tsx`) is a client component**, mirroring `rail-corpus.tsx`'s inline add/edit/delete pattern (local `busy`/`error` state, `router.refresh()` on success — no separate store), extended with per-entry split and multi-select merge. `allowAdd` defaults to `true`; `CatchAllSection` passes `false` since intentionally adding a new entry into the unclaimed bucket doesn't make sense. Every canonical primary tag is selectable from any section's Add/Edit/Split/Merge form (`PrimaryTagSchema` in `lib/schemas/knowledge-tags.ts` validates server-side); a section's `defaultPrimaryTag` prop is only the select's initial value, not an enforcement — an entry tagged into a different section's territory will render there on the next refresh, which is expected, not a bug. Merge selection has two layers, both load-bearing. `validSelectedIds` is derived from `selectedIds` filtered against the current `entries` prop on every render, not stored directly — a checked entry can be deleted or merged away by a *different* selection without this component's own `useState` resetting (`router.refresh()` gives fresh props, not fresh state), and an unfiltered stale id riding into a submitted merge would either 404 at `requireKnowledgeEntriesAdmin` or, worse, silently merge against a since-deleted row. Separately, once the merge review panel is open, `mergeIds` freezes the exact id set at the moment "Merge N selected" was clicked, and checkboxes are `disabled` for the duration — **caught in code review as a real, reproducible data-loss bug**: the panel's `mergeContent` textarea is a one-time snapshot of the selected entries' text, but if selection could still change live, checking one more row after the panel opened would ride that row's id into the submitted `originalIds` (and the server would delete it) without its content ever having been folded into the merged text — a silent loss, not a duplicate. `submitMerge` reads `mergeIds`, never live `validSelectedIds`, specifically to close this.

**Stage C — venue_info editing (facts, menu roster + highlights, staff roster) and mechanics editing (add/edit/deactivate).** Both write paths use the same read-modify-write-validate-the-WHOLE-object pattern, mirroring the pre-existing `/admin/voices/api/persona/[venueId]` route exactly — for `venue_info` this is load-bearing in a way it isn't for `brand_persona`: `venue_info` renders into EVERY prompt turn, so a partial write that silently drops a sibling key isn't a display bug, it's the agent losing a fact it's supposed to know with no error anywhere to point at.

- **`PATCH /admin/venues/api/venues/[venueId]/venue-info`** — single writer for everything editable in `venue_info` (address/contact/hours/amenities/qrEnrollmentMessage/menu/staff). `currentContext` is deliberately NOT accepted here — the expiry queue's Drop/Promote actions own that field's lifecycle. `lib/schemas/venue-info.ts`'s sub-schemas (`VenueAddressSchema`, `VenueContactSchema`, `VenueHoursSchema`, `VenueAmenitiesSchema`, `VenueMenuSchema`) were extracted and exported specifically so this route validates against the exact same shapes `VenueInfoSchema` composes from, not a hand-duplicated copy — a pure extraction, confirmed behavior-identical (existing `venue-info.test.ts` passes unchanged).
- **Mechanics: `POST venues/[venueId]/mechanics` (add), `PATCH|DELETE mechanics/[mechanicId]` (edit/deactivate).** `lib/schemas/mechanic.ts` is the admin edit-boundary schema — deliberately separate from `parse-venue-spec.ts`'s onboarding-time `MechanicSchema` (which stays untouched). `MechanicFullSchema` adds a cross-field `.refine()` pairing `redemptionPolicy` with `redemptionWindowDays` (renewable requires a window, one_time forbids one), mirroring migration 009's DB-level CHECK constraint in application code so a violation 400s before it ever reaches Postgres. `MechanicCreateSchema` is `MechanicFullSchema` itself, not a separately-hand-typed copy — **an earlier draft defined it as the bare per-field schema without the pairing refine, and a route test caught the gap immediately** (a pairing-violating POST threw a runtime TypeError instead of 400ing), fixed by aliasing. `MECHANIC_TYPES`/`MECHANIC_REDEMPTION_POLICIES` are derived `as const satisfies readonly MechanicType[]` / `readonly RedemptionPolicy[]` against the canonical types already exported from `lib/recognition/eligibility.ts` — an earlier draft redeclared both unions from scratch as a second copy, caught in code review as the exact class of drift `APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT`'s own `satisfies` annotation exists to prevent. `deactivateMechanic` (the DELETE verb) is a soft update (`is_active=false, deactivated_at=now()`), never a hard DELETE — `engagement_events.mechanic_id` FK-references mechanic rows, so a real delete would orphan or cascade-destroy redemption history.
- **`editMechanic`'s trigger-jsonb merge.** `trigger` is read-modify-write at the single-field level: the helper merges only the new `triggerType` onto whatever the raw `trigger` jsonb already holds (`{...currentRawTrigger, type: newType}`), never replacing the object wholesale, in case a future or hand-edited mechanic ever carries other keys there (every live mechanic today is confirmed `{type: '...'}`-shaped, but the merge doesn't assume that going forward). Fails safe if `trigger` is ever not a plain object (null, an array, a primitive): falls back to a clean `{type: newType}` rather than merging into corrupted data, covered by a dedicated test.
- **Menu roster stays whole-array-replace** (§2 plan decision, unchanged from the original plan): `MenuItemSchema` has no stable id, so every add/edit/delete submits the full `items` array in one PATCH rather than addressing a single item server-side. The `menu.highlights` caution caption from Stage A's read view ("facts about the menu, not advice about what to order") renders in the editor too, byte-identical component (`HighlightsCaption`), not just the read path — carrying the TAC-331 ruling into the surface that can actually reintroduce the mistake.
- **Client-side numeric inputs** (menu item price, mechanic `redemptionWindowDays`) guard against `NaN`: a non-numeric keystroke is rejected at the `onChange` handler rather than being allowed to reach `JSON.stringify` (which would silently serialize `NaN` as `null`) and rely on the server's Zod parse to catch it. `qrEnrollmentMessage` is sent on every venue-facts save, including empty string — omitting it when empty (an earlier draft's behavior) meant an operator could never actually clear the field, since the route's merge treats an omitted key as "leave alone."

**Stage D — the `currentContext` expiry queue (Add/Drop/Promote) and readiness panel polish.** `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/current-context.ts` holds the three write helpers (`addCurrentContextEntry`, `dropCurrentContextEntry`, `promoteCurrentContextEntry`), each read-modify-write-validate-the-whole-`venue_info`-object like Stage C. The venue_info select+parse step (`loadVenueInfo`) is now a **shared helper at `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/venue-info.ts`**, extracted out of both this file and the Stage C `PATCH .../venue-info` route in the same PR — the two had drifted into an inline duplicate with matching error-message text before the extraction (caught in code review), so a future change to the lookup/parse error wording only has one place to land. Routes: `POST venues/[venueId]/current-context` (add), `DELETE .../current-context/[entryId]` (drop), `POST .../current-context/[entryId]/promote` (promote). Add/Drop are venue-scoped (no per-entry auth lookup — `currentContext` entries are array elements, not rows); Promote takes `{primaryTag, secondaryTags}` in the body, `primaryTag` validated against `KNOWLEDGE_PRIMARY_TAGS` server-side (the operator picks it at promote time — not free text, not inferred).

**Promote's ordering matches split/merge exactly:** insert the new `knowledge_corpus` row and embed it via `ingestKnowledgeCorpusEntry` FIRST; only once that succeeds is the `currentContext` entry removed. A failed embed cleans up the just-inserted row (`.delete().eq('id', ...)`) and leaves the `currentContext` entry untouched in the queue — never destroyed with nothing to show for it. **The final `venue_info` write re-reads fresh, not the snapshot taken before the embed call** — caught in code review as a TOCTOU race: the Voyage embed is a slow network round trip, and writing back the pre-embed snapshot after it returns would silently clobber any edit a different admin made to that venue's `venue_info` during the window. `promoteCurrentContextEntry` calls `loadVenueInfo` a second time immediately before the final merge+write; filtering the promoted entry out of a fresh read is also safe (a no-op) if another admin already dropped or promoted the same entry concurrently. If that fresh re-read is already invalid or the write itself fails, the knowledge row is already live and retrievable — the function returns `db_error` and logs loudly, but never deletes the already-succeeded knowledge row on this path (only an embed failure triggers cleanup). All three result types (`AddCurrentContextResult`, `DropCurrentContextResult`, `PromoteCurrentContextResult`) carry the same `invalid_after_merge` error code alongside `db_error`/`not_found`/`embed_failed` for consistency, even though for Drop/Promote it's defensive (filtering an already-valid array can't actually invalidate the schema the way Add's append can).

**UI (`right-now-section.tsx`, client component):** one flat, untagged list — `currentContext` isn't sectioned like `knowledge_corpus`. Active entries get Drop; the expiry queue (expired + malformed-date entries, via `partitionCurrentContext` from Stage A) gets inline Promote (tag select + secondary-tags text input) and Drop. `promotingId` state tracks which single entry's promote panel is open, and its toggle-open button is `disabled={busy}` — without that guard, opening a different entry's promote panel while another entry's request is in flight lets that in-flight request's success handler force-close the newly-opened panel via an unconditional `setPromotingId(null)` (a real, if low-severity, UI race caught by QA; never causes a wrong write, just discards in-progress UI state).

**Readiness panel polish** was mostly already correct going into Stage D — `readiness-panel.tsx` already implemented the per-tag knowledge breakdown, named+listed missing mechanic params, read-only `approval_policy` display, the `manual_invite`-without-approval warning, the `brand_persona` field map, and the `is_processed`/"not retrievable" count from Stage A. No changes were needed there.

### Tunables viewer (TAC-183)

`/admin/tunables` lists every operational tunable (agent runtime thresholds, recognition signal weights, retrieval floors, timing windows, alert thresholds) with name, current value, type, category, and source location. Read-only — values come from `lib/tunables/manifest.ts`, which imports the live constants from their source files so they can never drift. URL-synced single-select category pill + name-substring search; click a row to expand an inline detail panel (object-typed entries render as `<pre>` JSON). No mutation API exists — read-only is enforced structurally, not just by the UI. Editable overrides are Phase 2 (separate ticket).

**Adding a tunable** = (a) `export const` the source file's constant if not already exported, (b) add a metadata entry to `TUNABLES` in `lib/tunables/manifest.ts`, (c) bump the count assertion in `lib/tunables/manifest.test.ts`. Categories are fixed: `agent_runtime`, `classification`, `timing`, `recognition`, `retrieval`, `mechanics`. Adding a new category requires updating `TunableCategory` plus the `CATEGORIES` pill list in the table component.

### Intentions viewer (TAC-379)

`/admin/intentions` is the read-only catalogue of first-touch intentions (TAC-324). Three sections: the definitions read straight from `INTENTION_DEFINITIONS`, the four gates that decide whether an intention reaches the prompt at all, and the recorded prompts from `guest_intention_prompts`. Read-only structurally, not just by convention — no route handlers, no mutation API, nothing on this surface writes.

**Authoring was scoped out on the strength of the audit, and the two reasons are worth keeping** because they are the reasons it stays out: `isSatisfied` is a JS predicate and cannot be stored in a row, so a DB-backed authoring surface needs a stored satisfaction format designed first — a schema problem, not a CRUD form; and `promptLine` renders verbatim into the one prompt slot universal rule R22 and `system-template.test.ts` name as the SOLE authority on whether the model pursues an open goal, the TAC-327/328/330/338 silent-veto class. Making that editable at runtime with no `PROMPT_VERSION` bump and no test is not worth saving a pull request. **Intentions stay in code; adding one is a reviewed PR.**

**Definitions render from the constant, never from a copy.** `app/admin/(authed)/intentions/no-copied-strings.test.ts` reads the surface's own source and fails if any definition's `promptLine` / `classifierDescription` / `satisfactionLabel` appears as a literal — mutation-verified, and it names the offending file and field. Same source-level technique as `lib/ui/token-bridge.test.ts` and TAC-366's `filterByRelevance` import assertion, and for the same reason: a copied literal is behaviourally invisible until the constant moves. **It does not catch a paraphrase** — which is why the gating copy describes the menu-mention rule rather than quoting the prompt line it suppresses. The first draft quoted it.

**The gates are static text, deliberately (four under TAC-379; six since TAC-380, which removed the `qr_scan` origin gate and added arming, the conversational gate, the brake, and pending-question suppression).** Two of them (`createdVia === 'qr_scan'` in `derive.ts`, the `opt_out` check in `serializers.ts`) are bare literals in their source files with no exported constant to read, so "deriving" the list would mean inventing constants in agent-runtime files to serve an admin page. The gates are on the page at all because without them the view implies every definition applies to every guest, which is false several times over.

`loadIntentionPrompts` (`app/admin/(authed)/_lib/load-intention-prompts.ts`) mirrors `load-venues.ts` — `cache()`-wrapped, allowlist-scoped where an EMPTY `allowedVenueIds` means analog-admin scope and sees everything, warn-and-return-`[]` on error so a DB failure costs the recorded-prompts half and leaves the definitions half (a static import) rendering. Guest and venue ride PostgREST embedded relations in one round trip, same as `listHeadsUpQueue`; both FKs are NOT NULL per migration 035 so `!inner` cannot silently drop a row. Bounded at `RECORDED_PROMPTS_LIMIT` (200), and **the cap is STATED on the page rather than applied silently**, per TAC-316's truncation lesson. **The query fetches `LIMIT + 1` and drops the extra row**, returning `{rows, hasMore}` — comparing `rows.length` to the cap cannot tell exactly-200 from more-than-200, and the page states "older prompts exist" as fact, so the cheaper comparison would have made the surface misreport on a total of exactly 200. Caught in code review; both the `>` boundary and the `+ 1` are mutation-verified, each killed by exactly one test. **A row whose `intention_key` matches no live definition renders anyway, marked unrecognized** — that column is bare `text` with no FK, so a renamed or removed definition leaves orphan history behind, and filtering it out would hide the row that still enforces the one-prompt-per-guest-ever cap.

No migration. Not on the agent's hot path — the query runs on one admin page render.

**TAC-381 extended `no-copied-strings.test.ts` from one root to a ROOT LIST.** It scanned `resolve(__dirname)` only, so when the venue page grew its own intentions section rendering the same `promptLine` values, a pasted literal there would have passed — a guard rooted at one directory that misses the second place the string renders is not a guard. Both roots are now scanned and the "guards the guard" test asserts a non-empty file list **per root**, because a single combined count stays green when one root resolves to nothing, which is the exact failure the second root exists to prevent. Mutation-verified against a raw paste AND an `&rsquo;`-escaped JSX paste into the venue component; a mis-rooted second root throws `ENOENT` at collection rather than passing vacuously. A third surface rendering these strings adds its root there.

### Venue-level commitments and intentions (TAC-381)

Two read-only sections on `/admin/venues/[slug]`, rendered after `<ReadinessPanel>` — grouped with it because both are derived live rather than stored. They answer "what does this venue owe, and what is the agent pursuing here", the questions that previously needed hand-run SQL against production.

**`loadVenueCommitments` (`app/admin/(authed)/_lib/load-venue-commitments.ts`)** — `guest_commitments` for one venue, guest name via the `guest:guests!inner(...)` embed already proven on this table in `heads-up-queue.ts`. **Two queries, not one filtered in JS**: the open set (`open` + `pending_ack`) is UNCAPPED because an unclaimed obligation is the entire point, while the closed set is history and capped at `CLOSED_COMMITMENTS_LIMIT` (20, `limit + 1` probe → `hasMore`, stated on the page per TAC-316). One query would force a single cap across both and a busy month of expiries could push a live obligation off the page. The halves degrade independently — a failed history read must never blank the list representing money owed. `NON_TERMINAL_STATUSES` / `TERMINAL_STATUSES` are an explicit complement with a test asserting they partition all six statuses, so a seventh status cannot become invisible on the page. Indexed by migration 026's `idx_guest_commitments_active_for_guest` on its leading `venue_id` — **no new index, no migration.**

**`loadVenueOpenIntentions` (`app/admin/(authed)/_lib/load-venue-intentions.ts`)** — the OPEN set, derived live, distinct from `loadIntentionPrompts` above. **The two answer opposite questions and the page renders both**: a `guest_intention_prompts` row means the intention was already raised and is closed for that guest (unless a newer recommendation or order re-armed an event-armed one), so the recorded-prompts read is the inverse of "currently pursuing". Loads facts and hands them to the shared derivation rather than restating the open/closed rule (`deriveOpenIntentions` until TAC-380, `deriveIntentionState` since — see the TAC-380 note at the end of this paragraph). Three things are decisions: the cohort cutoff is **computed from `INTENTION_DEFINITIONS`' longest `expiresAfterMs`**, never a literal; the SQL deliberately does **NOT** prefilter on `created_via = 'qr_scan'` even though that would shrink the cohort, because that gate is `deriveOpenIntentions`' own first line and encoding it twice means a future intention that drops it renders nothing here with no error; and both supporting reads **fail CLOSED** like `build-runtime-context.ts`, so a hiccup under-reports rather than claiming an intention is open when the row saying otherwise merely failed to load — with a `degraded` flag the page surfaces, because an unlabelled empty list reads as "nothing open", the invisibility this ticket exists to remove. `applyCurrentTurnSuppression` is NOT applied: it is scoped to one inbound body and there is no current turn. **Reworked by TAC-380:** the scan now starts from unprompted ELIGIBILITY rows (`prompted_at IS NULL`, `eligible_at` inside the longest window), plus re-armed event-armed rows whose last prompt predates their `eligible_at`, and calls the shared `deriveIntentionState`, not `deriveOpenIntentions`, so the `created_via` point above no longer applies — there is no guest-cohort query left to prefilter. An intention whose gate opened since the guest last texted is not open there until their next inbound records its eligibility, and the brake and render-time suppressions are not reproduced; the page copy says both.

**`commitment-display.ts` (`venues/_lib/`)** is the pure display half, split out because this repo has no React component harness. `classifyKind` delegates to `isObligationType` rather than re-listing comp/hold/discount — that set is TAC-341's deliberate ALLOWLIST, and a local copy would silently opt this page out of it (guarded source-level, since a copy is behaviourally identical until a fifth type exists). `formatExpiry` uses **`Math.floor`, not `ceil`**: ceil rounds a horizon six hours out up to "expires in 1 day", overstating time left on an obligation about to lapse. A null horizon renders as a statement, never a blank — a recommendation never has one by design (TAC-380 owns that), an obligation without one is a pre-TAC-341 row awaiting the hand-applied backfill, and a blank cell conflates the two with missing data.

## Operator API (TAC-258 + TAC-207 + TAC-277 + TAC-297 + TAC-299)

The mobile-operator approval queue is served by nine route handlers under `app/api/operator/*` (SINGULAR — applies to every operator endpoint without exception), on the **guest host** (the middleware host-gate only restricts `/admin/*` — `/api/operator/*` flows through). When a dedicated operator host comes online, the routes can move under a host-rewrite without touching the handlers. Five TAC-258 queue routes (`queue`, `messages/[id]/{approve,edit,skip,undo}`), the TAC-207 `POST /api/operator/devices` (APNs token registration), the TAC-277 `GET /api/operator/messages/[id]/thread` (full conversation history behind the edit screen), the TAC-297 `POST /api/operator/commitments/[id]/acknowledge` (heads-up card swipe-right), and the TAC-299 `POST /api/operator/commitments/[id]/draft-decline` (heads-up card swipe-left → agent-drafted apology persisted as pending, commitment cancelled).

**Path note (TAC-207 surfacing case).** The TAC-207 ticket draft text referenced the plural `/api/operators/devices` and the iOS client (TAC-288) was initially built against that. Server shipped at singular per convention; a hotfix briefly moved the server to plural to match the client during UAT; then both sides realigned on singular as part of TAC-207 cleanup. Lesson worth remembering for future cross-repo work: ticket draft text isn't a contract — confirm the path against the established convention before the client encodes it.

**Auth.** All five TAC-258 routes plus the TAC-207 devices route wrap their handler in `withOperatorAuth` (from `lib/auth/operator-auth.ts`). The HOF runs `verifyOperatorRequest` (bearer-token JWT → `operators.id` + `operator_venues` allowlist), shapes `AuthError` as 401/403 JSON, and threads `{operator, params}` into the inner handler. Operator-venues with **zero rows** means an operator has no venue access — the queue returns `{drafts: []}`, the action endpoints return 404 (don't leak existence).

**Auth-failure body shape — Contract-bound routes bypass the HOF.** `withOperatorAuth` forwards `AuthError.message` verbatim into the 401 body (e.g. `'missing Authorization header'`, `'invalid or expired token: JWT expired'`). That's fine for the six legacy operator routes that predate cross-repo Contracts. **Routes bound to a cross-repo `## Contract` MUST shape the 401 body to match the Contract literal** (e.g. `{ error: 'unauthorized' }`) instead of leaking `err.message`. Pattern: call `verifyOperatorRequest` inline with a `try { ... } catch (err) { if (err instanceof AuthError) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'unauthorized' }, { status: 401 }); throw err }` block. TAC-277's `GET /api/operator/messages/[id]/thread` is the first route to do this — apply the same posture to any future Contract-bound operator endpoint (404 body becomes `'not_found'`, 500 body becomes `'internal_error'`, etc., per the Contract). Don't back-port to the legacy six unless their sibling clients adopt a Contract — touching the HOF in place would change wire shape for all six at once.

**State machine.** `messages.review_state` is a **separate axis from `messages.status`** (migration 018). Five values: `pending` (draft awaiting review — TAC-212 will set this), `approved` (operator approved, Sendblue dispatched), `edited` (operator edited body, Sendblue dispatched with the edit), `skipped` (operator chose nothing), `auto_sent` (never queued — set by the autonomous-agent path via `lib/agent/schedule-and-send.ts`). NULL for inbound rows and pre-TAC-258 outbounds (the migration backfilled the latter to `'auto_sent'`).

**Idempotency.** Approve + edit + skip use optimistic conditional UPDATEs that gate on `review_state='pending'`. Rowcount=0 means another caller already acted (or the row was never pending) — surface as 200 `{status: 'already_acted', reviewState: ...}`. The migration 006 `provider_message_id` unique constraint is the second backstop against double-send. There's no explicit transaction wrapper — the conditional UPDATE is itself atomic, and the small TOCTOU window before the Sendblue call is acceptable v1.

**Undo.** 3-second server-side window enforced via `now() - last_operator_action_at < 3000ms`. Only skip→pending is truly revertible; approve and edit can't be unsent so undo on those fires a PostHog event with `undoneAfterDispatch=true` and leaves state alone. Operator-mismatch (different operator's action) returns 403. Outside the window returns 409 Conflict with `code: 'UNDO_WINDOW_EXPIRED'`. v1 supports single-level undo only.

**Edit path persistence.** Mobile-operator edit overrides `messages.body` (canonical sent text invariant — see plan v3 §7). The operator's text is also written to `response_review.editedMessage` (the "human edited this" signal), and the AI's pre-edit draft is captured in `response_review.originalAiBody` (closes the Langfuse-retention gap so single-row diff queries work). Voice corpus ingestion uses `upsertCorpusEdit` with `source_ref = 'operator-approve:{messageId}'` (registered in `lib/voice-training/channels.ts`) in `'replace'` mode. **Anti-corpus-poisoning rule still applies** — only the operator's final text is embedded; the AI draft never is. The route mirrors cc-review's circuit-breaker chain: corpus → response_review stamp (last). On already-acted, do NOT re-stamp `response_review` (the first operator's stamp wins).

**v1 failure-recovery gap.** If Sendblue throws after the optimistic state flip, the row is left in `review_state='approved'` (or `'edited'`) with `provider_message_id=NULL`.
