The queue no longer surfaces it; the operator can't retry via the queue. Recovery is manual SQL or a future `failed_dispatch` reconciliation cron. Mitigation: dispatch errors return 502 from the route — the mobile client surfaces a generic "send failed" toast. **Follow-up ticket needed** for `failed_dispatch` state + reconciliation cron; track post-merge.

**PostHog events.** `operator_message_approved`, `operator_message_edited`, `operator_message_skipped`, `operator_message_action_undone` — all in `lib/analytics/posthog.ts`. IDs-only payload shape per the operator-side precedent (`voice_critique_committed`); bodies live on the row and Langfuse. `operator_message_edited` carries length-delta scalars (`bodyLengthBefore`, `bodyLengthAfter`, `bodyLengthDeltaPct`), not the bodies themselves. None of these post to Slack — they're product analytics, not operational alerts.

**Full-thread fetch (TAC-277).** `GET /api/operator/messages/[id]/thread` returns the full conversation history (guest-at-venue) for the operator-app edit screen. Path param `id` is the messageId the operator just tapped on (any message in the thread works as an entry point, not just the pending draft). Auth uses the Contract-bound inline pattern documented above — 401 body is `{ error: 'unauthorized' }` (not `err.message`); 404 body is `{ error: 'not_found' }` for invalid-UUID, message-not-found, AND out-of-allowlist (uniform per Contract — see CLAUDE.md → Auth boundary on existence-leak); 500 body is `{ error: 'internal_error' }`. Response is `{ messages: ThreadMessage[] }` ordered oldest→newest, capped at the most-recent 200 (the helper does `ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 200` server-side and reverses in JS). Delegates to `loadGuestThread` in `lib/operator/thread.ts`. Empty-body messages (reactions, status pings) filtered server-side. **TAC-395: the thread holds only messages that reached the guest**: inbound, or outbound whose `review_state` is not pending and whose `status` is in `DELIVERED_OUTBOUND_STATUSES` (`sending`, `sent`, `delivered`). Pending drafts, skipped drafts and approved replies that never sent are left out, in the query and so before the 200-row cap. The messageId lookup stays unfiltered, so a pending draft's own id still opens its thread. `GET /api/operator/guests/:guestId/thread` shares the query through `fetchThreadMessagesForGuest`. The Realtime channel is not filtered by the server; the operator app applies the same rule to live rows (TAC-411). Contract: TAC-395. No PostHog event — read-only fetch, no operator action. Sibling client: TAC-290.

**APNs push (TAC-207).** `POST /api/operator/devices` accepts `{ token: string, platform?: string }` (`token` Zod-validated: hex-only, 32-256 chars, opaque-length per Apple's guidance; `platform` is sent by the iOS client today as `"ios"` but **not persisted** — iOS-only pilot, no column. Zod's default object behavior strips unknown keys, so `platform` is silently accepted + ignored. If multi-platform support arrives, add a column AND validate here at the same time). Upserts onto `operators.apns_device_token` + `apns_token_updated_at` (migration 024). Re-registration is idempotent. The token is consumed by `sendDraftFlaggedPush` in `lib/notifications/send.ts`, fired fire-and-forget from `handle-inbound.ts` + `handle-followup.ts` (via `waitUntil`) immediately after `persistOrRegenQueuedDraft` succeeds in the queue branch. Trigger filter is a **DENY-list** owned by `lib/notifications/push-policy.ts`: every `ApprovalTrigger` pushes except an explicit `'skip'` — today only `previous_pending_held` (regen of an already-pushed draft; the persist layer UPDATEs that row in place, in the draft's own slot since TAC-394, and TAC-298's Realtime subscription refreshes the card). The map is `satisfies Record<ApprovalTrigger, PushDecision>`, so a new trigger fails `tsc` until someone decides; `shouldSendDraftFlaggedPush` additionally fails OPEN on an unrecognized string. It was an ALLOW-list until 2026-08, which silently dropped `commitment_type_gated` and `hold_all_outbound` for two months — see "Common gotchas → sets keyed on approval triggers must be TOTAL maps". Context labels live separately in `send.ts` as a `Partial` map; a trigger with no label still pushes, just without the context dash. Every APNs response now emits ONE unconditional `[apns] apns response` log line carrying `status` + `reason` + `apnsId` (Apple's per-notification UUID, captured from the `apns-id` response header) — success included, so a UAT run can distinguish "APNs accepted it" from "we never got there" without waiting on PostHog. `lib/notifications/send-commitment-push.ts` mirrors the same line as `[apns] commitment apns response`. Two PostHog events: `push.sent` (per send attempt, `ok:boolean` carries success vs. failure — no Slack relay; analyst-facing) and `push.token_invalid` (APNs 410 Gone OR 400 BadDeviceToken — nulls the token columns + Slack-relays because the operator's push pipeline is non-functional until they reinstall the app). Privacy invariant: payload carries `{ aps: { alert: {title, body}, badge, sound }, draftId, guestId, operatorId }` only — no message contents, ever (asserted in `send.test.ts`). Body is `"Reply to {firstName} — {context}"` where context is a categorical trigger label.

**Heads-up commitments (TAC-297 + TAC-299).** The TAC-258 queue endpoint's response gained an additive `commitments: HeadsUpCommitment[]` field (sibling to the existing `drafts: QueueDraft[]`); shape locked at the cross-repo Contract. TAC-297 shipped the base shape `{ id, type, guest: {name}, description, code, expected_arrival, created_at }`. **TAC-364 added two more**: `venueId` and `guestId`, both camelCase on the wire. `venueId` is not a convenience — TAC-382 made the operator app filter every venue-scoped view through `filterByVenue<T extends { venueId: string }>`, which is what makes "every view respects the selected venue" true by construction rather than by vigilance; a heads-up card without it cannot pass through that filter, so the client would have to either bypass it (showing one venue's commitments to an operator looking at another, on the surface TAC-382 just fixed) or drop the card. `guestId` backs the push-tap venue switch. `guest_id` was already in the SELECT and merely dropped from the projection; `venue_id` is the one new column. **TAC-299 added two additive fields** consumed by the operator-app heads-up card: `recognitionState: GuestState | null` (drives the recognition pill — typed via the canonical `GuestState` union from `lib/recognition/types.ts` rather than a re-typed local; same value set as `QueueDraft.recognitionState`) and `sourceMessageId: string | null` (the messageId the operator-app routes into `/api/operator/messages/[id]/thread` to render the inbound conversation thread). Powered by `listHeadsUpQueue` in `lib/operator/heads-up-queue.ts` — main query joins `guests.first_name` via PostgREST embedded relations; **TAC-299 added a second batched query** against `guest_states` keyed on the collected `guest_ids` (deduped) filtered to `exited_at IS NULL` (the partial-index `idx_guest_states_current` hot path) AND the `venue_id IN allowedVenueIds` scope. Two queries because PostgREST nested ordering+limit on embedded relations is unreliable for "latest row per parent"; this is O(2) round trips deterministically. Recognition-state lookup degrades gracefully on error (logs + leaves the map empty → all commitments project `recognitionState=null` rather than failing the whole list — the heads-up card still renders, just without the pill). **`POST /api/operator/commitments/[id]/acknowledge`** uses the Contract-bound inline-auth pattern (matches TAC-277): 401 `{error: 'unauthorized'}`, 404 `{error: 'not_found'}` for invalid-UUID + commitment-not-found + out-of-allowlist (uniform per existence-leak rule), 409 `{error: 'already_acknowledged'}` (CAS lost with row visible — already acknowledged / cancelled / never reached pending_ack), 500 `{error: 'internal_error'}`. Delegates to `markAcknowledged` (`lib/guests/commitments.ts`), a CAS-gated UPDATE on `(status='pending_ack' AND venue_id IN allowedVenueIds)`. PostHog event: `operator_commitment_acknowledged` (`venueId`, `guestId`, `commitmentId`, `operatorId`, `timeToActionMs`, `type`) — IDs-only payload shape mirroring TAC-258's draft events. No Slack relay. **Companion arrival-push (`sendCommitmentArrivalPush` in `lib/notifications/send-commitment-push.ts`):** TAC-207-style fanout fired from `handle-inbound.ts` (imminent CAS win → live push, agentRunId threaded) and the hourly cron route `/api/cron/commitments-due` (scheduled fires, agentRunId=null). Payload `{ aps: {alert: {title, body}, badge, sound}, commitmentId, guestId, operatorId }` — content-free (no commitment description, no inbound text). Body is `"{firstName} arriving {context} — {typeLabel}{ code}"`; `code` omitted for `recommendation` type, included for comp/hold/discount. Badge sums pending drafts + pending_ack commitments per operator's allowed venues (single source of truth across both push surfaces). PostHog discriminator: `surface: 'commitment_arrival'` on `push.sent` / `push.token_invalid` events. **Known duplication:** `loadRecipients` + `nullOperatorToken` + badge-count are duplicated in `send-commitment-push.ts` vs `send.ts`. Extraction to a shared `lib/notifications/recipients.ts` is a follow-up — duplicating now to keep TAC-297 scoped.

**Operator-initiated decline (TAC-299).** `POST /api/operator/commitments/[id]/draft-decline` is the swipe-left counterpart to `/acknowledge`. Contract-bound inline-auth: 401 `{error:'unauthorized'}`, 404 `{error:'not_found'}` for invalid-UUID + commitment-not-found + out-of-allowlist (uniform per existence-leak rule), 409 `{error:'invalid_state'}` (commitment exists but is not `pending_ack` — distinct from 404 because the row was found, just in the wrong state — different operator-app toast), 422 `{error:'refused'}` (voice fidelity below `SEND_FIDELITY_FLOOR`), 500/502 `{error:'internal_error'}` (DB load failure / pipeline failure inside `handleOperatorDecline`), 200 `{messageId, body}`. **TAC-364 added `body`** — the persisted draft text, read back off the row rather than threaded out of the generation, because the persist layer strips bubble delimiters before writing and it is the STORED string the operator sends. It exists so the operator app's `/queue/edit` renders the apology on first paint: that screen resolves the draft out of its client-side `queue.drafts` cache, which cannot contain a row created server-side milliseconds earlier, so before this it rendered "That draft is no longer pending" on every decline — not a race, the guaranteed first frame. TAC-298 worked around it with a blocking full-queue refetch, which TAC-304 then filed as a 5-10s lag and was cancelled unbuilt. Degrades to `''` rather than 5xx if the read fails: the draft is already persisted and the commitment is about to be cancelled, so a 5xx would strand the operator on a draft they cannot reach and a retry would hit 409 on the now-cancelled commitment. The route loads the commitment row (allowlist-scoped), invokes `handleOperatorDecline` (`lib/agent/handle-operator-decline.ts` — see the `lib/agent/` paragraph above for the persist-not-send structural invariant + skipped-gate posture), then calls `markCancelled` AFTER the pipeline succeeds (cancellation is **trigger-time**, not send-time — per TAC-299 design decision #1: the operator's swipe-left intent is load-bearing even if they abandon the edit screen; `editAndSend` stays commitment-agnostic). `markCancelled` CAS lost (concurrent ack from another operator) or DB-errored is **logged + accepted**: the decline draft is already persisted; the route returns 200 with `{messageId, body}` and the PostHog event records `commitmentCancellationRaceLost=true` for observability. PostHog event: `operator_draft_decline_initiated` (`venueId`, `guestId`, `commitmentId`, `messageId`, `operatorId`, `type`, `timeToActionMs`, `commitmentCancellationRaceLost`). No Slack relay. Pending-draft interaction (TAC-299 design decision #2, scoped to slots by TAC-394): when a card is pending in the decline draft's own slot, the decline **clobbers** it in place (operator's "we can't fulfill" supersedes the auto-generated reply; `decideSlotAction` with `regen_always`), and `captureDraftRegenerated` observes the trigger transition. The card in the other slot is never touched. A decline draft carrying a DIFFERENT obligation than a pending obligation card is dropped before any write (`obligation_slot_taken`, alerted through `captureDraftDropped`); the route maps that to its existing 502 `{error:'internal_error'}` and does NOT cancel the commitment, because no decline draft exists for the operator to send. `operator_decline_initiated` joins the set of `messages.review_reason` values surfaced by the queue UI but is NOT in `APPROVAL_TRIGGERS` (the policy gate doesn't fire it; the route does) — `lib/operator/queue.ts` carries a separate `ExtraReviewReason` union for this exact reason so the label-map type stays exhaustive at compile time. Sibling client: TAC-298 (operator-app swipe-left wiring).

**Commitments cron (TAC-297, morning-of model).** `/api/cron/commitments-due` is hit hourly to fire each venue's day-prep push at its local morning hour, NOT at each commitment's stated `expected_arrival` time. One push per pending_ack commitment per venue at opening — deliberate day-prep accountability (the imminent path covers just-in-time off the inbound).

**Trigger source is GitHub Actions, NOT Vercel cron** — the project stays on Vercel Hobby, whose cron tier caps granularity at daily/weekly. The workflow `.github/workflows/commitments-due-cron.yml` runs on `cron: '0 * * * *'` (UTC) + `workflow_dispatch` (manual smoke-test trigger) and one curl step authenticated by the same `CRON_SECRET` Bearer the route validates. **Two repo settings required:** `PROD_BASE_URL` as a repo variable (the guest-host origin, e.g. `https://webhooks.theanalog.company`) and `CRON_SECRET` as a repo secret (same value as Vercel's `CRON_SECRET` env var). Without both set, the workflow exits 1 loudly rather than 200-and-silent. **Reliability caveat:** GH Actions scheduled runs can lag the target minute under platform load — the morning-of model tolerates ±1h precision; revisit (Vercel Pro native cron, or QStash) if drift bites or when the project moves to a commercial plan. The route handler is auth-equivalent to a Vercel cron call (dev-skip pattern matches webhook-silence). **Vercel `vercel.json` currently lists only the daily `webhook-silence` cron** (Hobby-compatible); anything sub-daily must live on GH Actions until the project upgrades.

**Eligibility per row in `processDueCommitments(now)`** (`lib/guests/commitments-due.ts`):
1. `findScheduledOpenCommitments()` returns every `status='open' AND arrival_signal='scheduled'` row (no time filter — the date filter is timezone-bound and lives below). `imminent`-signal rows are excluded at the SQL boundary; they fire off the inbound, never here.
2. Look up the row's venue timezone. Skip if missing (counts as `invalid`).
3. **Morning-hour gate.** Compute the hour-of-day in the venue's timezone via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`. Skip unless it equals `MORNING_HOUR_LOCAL` (currently `7`, exported constant). Mismatches count as `notMorningHour` — they'll be picked up on a future hourly tick when their venue's local time rolls around to 7am.
4. **Date gate.** Compute `expected_arrival_date_in_venue_tz` and `today_in_venue_tz` (YYYY-MM-DD via `en-CA` locale — lexicographically comparable). Skip if `expected_date > today_date` (`future`). Process if `<=` — **lean catch-up** means a past-due scheduled commitment whose morning run was missed (created after that day's tick, or GH Actions outage) fires on the next morning tick.
5. CAS-transition via `transitionToPendingAck` (gated on `status='open'`); push fires via `waitUntil` only when CAS won. A scheduled commitment that ALSO got an imminent inbound before the morning tick is already `pending_ack` → CAS rowcount=0 → no double-push.

`MORNING_HOUR_LOCAL = 7` is a module constant for v1; per-venue override via `venue_configs` is a follow-up. Summary shape: `{ scanned, transitioned, skipped, notMorningHour, future, invalid, errored, pushed }` — split out so an operational dashboard can distinguish "didn't fire because not morning" from "didn't fire because data integrity" from "didn't fire because CAS lost to a racing imminent inbound."

**Concrete, not generic** (design call #3) — when the follow-up engine lands, it writes its own sibling `processDueFollowups(now)` and we extract the shared "find-eligible → CAS-transition → side-effect" seam THEN.

**Knowledge-gap timeout cron (TAC-308; trigger moved to cron-job.org 2026-08-09).** `/api/cron/pending-timeout` → `processDueKnowledgeGaps(now)` in `lib/agent/knowledge-gap-timeout.ts`. Triggered by an **external HTTP cron on cron-job.org, every minute** — NOT `vercel.json` (Hobby caps at daily) and NOT GitHub Actions anymore. The route originally shipped on a GH Actions `*/5` schedule, but GH queues scheduled workflows at low priority: measured over 37 runs the gap was **min 22m / median 36m / max 117m — never once 5 minutes** — and the two guests who received holding messages waited 37–38 minutes. Do not put a minutes-precision timer on GH Actions scheduled workflows; the hourly crons (commitments-due, followups-due) tolerate the jitter only because their windows are hour-scale, and the followups local-hour match is exposed to the same drift (tracked separately). Auth is a **dedicated `EXTERNAL_CRON_SECRET`** bearer (Vercel env var, Production + Preview), deliberately not the shared `CRON_SECRET` — the caller is a third-party service, and a dedicated secret bounds the blast radius of a leak and rotates independently. Manual UAT firing is `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $EXTERNAL_CRON_SECRET" <prod>/api/cron/pending-timeout`. **The 5-minute window is a FLOOR, not an SLA.** `pending_until` is the earliest a holding message may fire; with the every-minute trigger it lands within ~6 minutes of the floor. The asymmetry is deliberate — firing late costs a guest a few more minutes inside a silence they're already in, while firing early would talk over an operator about to answer. **Never shorten the window to compensate for jitter.** Scan predicate is `review_state='pending' AND pending_until IS NOT NULL AND pending_until <= now()`, deliberately NOT `review_reason='knowledge_gap'`: review_reason holds the priority-selected primaryTrigger, so a draft that both gapped and committed a comp would silently lose its timer. It also means the eight legacy `pending_review` rows with no expiry are excluded by construction rather than by a carve-out. Idempotency is claim-before-side-effect: the claim is a CAS `UPDATE … SET pending_until = null WHERE id = ? AND review_state='pending' AND pending_until IS NOT NULL`, and rowcount=1 is the exclusive right to send. Two overlapping runs cannot both win, so the holding message fires exactly once per card. The claim runs BEFORE generation, trading one possible lost holding message (process dies mid-flight) against the possibility of two — for a message whose entire content is "still on it," a duplicate is worse than a miss. Summary shape `{ scanned, claimed, casLost, sent, fallbackSent, suppressed, errored, invalid }` (TAC-309 removed `regenerated` along with the post-send card regen).

---

## Cross-repo contracts

When a ticket spans this repo and `analog-operator` (or any other sibling repo) — TAC-207 ↔ TAC-288 is the canonical example — the ticket description MUST contain a `## Contract` section that locks the boundary character-exact:

- Endpoint paths (full path string, HTTP method)
- Request body shape and a concrete example
- Response shapes per status code (including the 401/404/409 negatives)
- Required env vars with format notes (multi-line PEM vs single-line secret, etc.)

The Contract section is the single source of truth. Both repos implement against it; at integration time the Contract resolves any disagreement.

**Cross-repo work is two tickets, one per repo, linked.** A single ticket carrying both repo labels is never built: the build posts `[BUILD-SKIPPED]` asking for a split (`.claude/process.md`, "Which repo works a ticket").

**Writing a Contract** (full text in `.claude/process.md`, "Contracts"). Every Contract carries a `### What this doesn't settle` subsection next to `### What it may not assume`: silence in a spec reads as permission, and three times on TAC-395 the spec was applied to a case it said nothing about, producing code that was exactly correct and exactly wrong. Any line saying "apply X to all of Y" states what Y surprisingly contains — empty bodies, NULL columns, rows in states nobody pictures; all three TAC-395 cases came from there. And a `## Contract` makes a ticket cross-repo only when the other side is not built yet: TAC-298 has a Contract against already-shipped tickets and is operator-only.

**Deviation rule.** If implementation needs to diverge from the Contract — for example, the codebase convention is `/api/operator/*` (singular) but the Contract drafted `/api/operators/*` (plural) — the ticket description gets updated FIRST during the plan-review phase. The implementation never silently diverges from a still-stale Contract. The 2026-05-27 TAC-207 incident — server shipped singular without updating TAC-288's plural spec, the iOS client hit 404 on registration, recovery cost a hotfix plus a re-alignment cycle — is the documented case for why this rule exists.

**Sequencing rule.** Cross-repo work ships sequentially, **server-first**. Before the sibling client ticket starts, the server endpoint must be deployed AND verified via `curl` against the Contract's exact request shape, returning the expected status code. The bar is "401 on missing auth" (proves the route exists, parses, and reaches the auth check) — NOT "404 on missing route," NOT "400 on bad body." A 404 means the route wasn't deployed where the Contract said it would be; a 400 on a valid Contract-shaped body means the body schema diverged.

**UAT gate.** Manual end-to-end UAT is a precondition for marking the server ticket Done — never deferred to the sibling client ticket. Unit tests passing on both sides does not prove the contract is consistent. TAC-207 surfaced this: server tests green, client tests green, integration broken on the first push attempt 9 hours after deploy.

**Test-assertion rule.** Tests that touch a cross-repo boundary assert against the Contract's literal payload, never against the code's own serialization. A test written by reading the implementation is worthless for cross-repo agreement — it can only confirm that the code does what the code does. When writing or editing an assertion on a request body, response shape, endpoint path, or status-code body that crosses the repo boundary, transcribe the expected value from the ticket's `## Contract` block. Not from the handler you just wrote, not from a passing run's actual output, not from memory. If the Contract isn't reachable, that's a blocker to raise — never a licence to assert what the code happens to emit.

This has now cost us twice, and the second time was worse. TAC-207 was two internally-consistent readings of an *unwritten* contract, caught at integration. TAC-310 was a **written** contract with a client test that contradicted it: `analog-operator`'s `__tests__/lib/api-queue.test.ts` asserted `toEqual({ body: 'my version' })` while `POST /api/operator/messages/[id]/edit` has always parsed `BodySchema = z.object({ editedBody: z.string().min(1).max(4000) })`. The test didn't miss the defect — it **certified** it, and kept certifying it on every green CI run while five operator sends failed in production against a live guest thread. Note which side that test lived on: this repo was correct throughout and the feature still shipped broken. Server-side correctness does not protect you from a client test that only ever agreed with itself.

Corollary for this repo: when a Contract pins an error-body literal (`{ error: 'empty_body' }`, `{ error: 'unauthorized' }`, `{ error: 'not_found' }`, …), the route test asserts that exact string, not a status-code-only check and not `expect.objectContaining`. The literal is the contract surface — a bare 422 doesn't distinguish `empty_body` from `opted_out`, and the client branches on the difference. Same for request-side constraints the client must mirror: a `.min(1)` / `.max(4000)` on a body field is part of the Contract, and the sibling ticket needs to say so or the client will happily let an operator type past the cap and eat an unexplainable 400. (TAC-310.)

## Synthetic guests

Four synthetic guests are seeded per venue when `run-test-scenarios` runs against that venue for the first time. Deterministic phone numbers, reused across venues:

- `+15550001000` — `new`
- `+15550001100` — `returning`
- `+15550001200` — `regular`
- `+15550001300` — `raving_fan`

Each is marked `is_test_synthetic = true` (migration 007). Per-venue history rows (transactions, messages, engagement_events) are seeded with `created_at` ≥30 days old so the recent-conversation block stays empty during testing. The seeded values are tuned so `computeGuestState` lands in the right band. If recognition formula changes, re-tune `seedSignalsForState` in `scripts/onboarding/run-test-scenarios.ts`.

If a state lands in the wrong band, the script logs all four outcomes and then throws (so the operator sees the full tuning picture, not just the first miss).

THE-184 tracks the alternative of fixture-based synthetic guests (skip the DB entirely) — picked up only if the DB-seeded approach becomes annoying to maintain.

## Environment variables

One line per var, grouped by purpose. This section is the index — defaults and behavior live in the relevant sections (Observability, Drive integration, Admin scaffold) and are not duplicated here.

- **LLM:** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
- **Embeddings:** `VOYAGE_API_KEY`.
- **Database (Supabase):** `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` (service role, `lib/db/admin.ts`); `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (browser client `lib/db/browser.ts`).
- **Messaging (Sendblue):** `SENDBLUE_API_KEY_ID`, `SENDBLUE_API_SECRET_KEY` (outbound), `SENDBLUE_SIGNING_SECRET` (inbound webhook verification).
- **Messaging (Instagram/Meta, TAC-445, TAC-458):** `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` (the GET handshake's shared string; unset means every handshake 403s and the Meta dashboard refuses to save the callback URL), `INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET` (keys the `x-hub-signature-256` HMAC, ENFORCED since TAC-458: unset or empty refuses EVERY delivery with 403, genuine ones included), and `INSTAGRAM_APP_ID`, which **has zero readers in this repo** and is set for later Graph API work. Read at FIRST CALL inside the handlers, never at module load, per the carve-out below — CI defines none of these. **`INSTAGRAM_LOG_RAW_INBOUND` was REMOVED by TAC-458**: it logged full DM bodies and IGSIDs, and removal beat gating it off in production because only production receives Meta deliveries, so a production-off gate would never see real traffic. Delete it from Vercel if it is still set there; a variable nothing reads looks like live config. **There is deliberately no `/admin/health` row for these (ruled 2026-09-17), and a TAC-445 line here promising one was wrong.** It said to add the row when enforcement landed "because that is when a rotated secret starts failing quietly", but a shape check can see a missing or malformed secret, never a rotated one, and a health row that can't see the failure it claims to catch is worse than none. So the failure enforcement creates has no detector today: every genuine delivery 403s until Meta disables the subscription. An unset or empty secret logs `instagram_signature_misconfigured` at error level; a rotated one shows only as ordinary `instagram_signature_rejected` warnings with `reason: 'mismatch'`, which a probe produces too (the logged, capped `userAgent` is a hint to which is which, not proof: Meta's user-agent is public and a probe can send it). Revisit when there is an alert channel. **This is still a deliberate exception to the APNs three-part shape**: `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` fails at Meta-dashboard-save time, the loudest possible signal, delivered to the person doing the setup.
- **Drive:** `GOOGLE_DRIVE_VENUES_FOLDER_ID` (parent folder for venue artifact files 04–09).
- **Onboarding (Airtable):** `AIRTABLE_API_KEY`, `AIRTABLE_BASE_ID`, `AIRTABLE_TABLE_ID` — read by `extract-venue-spec`.
- **Observability (Langfuse):** `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY`, `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`; host as `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` (preferred) or `LANGFUSE_HOST` (legacy alias); `LANGFUSE_ENABLED` (explicit kill-switch); `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT` (THE-216, default on).
- **Analytics (PostHog):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST`.
- **Admin auth callback:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_ADMIN_URL` (per environment); `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_URL` (preview-deploy fallback).
- **Cron:** `CRON_SECRET` (bearer auth for cron handlers — shared by the Vercel `webhook-silence` cron and the four GH Actions crons: commitments-due, followups-due, pos-tap-reconcile, commitment-lifecycle); `EXTERNAL_CRON_SECRET` (dedicated bearer for `/api/cron/pending-timeout`, whose caller is the external cron-job.org service — separate from `CRON_SECRET` so a third-party leak can't expose the internal cron routes and the secret rotates independently).
- **APNs push (TAC-207):** `APNS_AUTH_KEY` (PEM contents of the `.p8` private key, multi-line value **including both `-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----` and `-----END PRIVATE KEY-----` armor lines** — pasted from 1Password); `APNS_KEY_ID` (10-char Apple key identifier, `S4PR9KNPKA` for analog); `APNS_TEAM_ID` (10-char Apple team identifier, `W4J9A9K9YX`); `APNS_BUNDLE_ID` (`company.theanalog.operator`); `APNS_ENV` (`production` or `sandbox` — selects the APNs host; **a TestFlight or App Store build requires `production`**, an Xcode dev build requires `sandbox`, and a mismatch returns HTTP 400 `BadDeviceToken` and delivers nothing). Shape-validated by `checkApnsEnv` (`lib/notifications/apns/env.ts`) — see the note on first-call validation below.
- **Scripts / dev:** `TEST_VENUE_ID` (used by `npm run send-test`); `NODE_ENV` (switches Langfuse + other dev/test branches).

**Boot-time validation.** Every required env var must have a validator that fails loudly with a useful error message if missing or malformed, and it ships in the same PR as the var. For credential env vars (PEM keys, JWT secrets, signed-payload secrets), validate the SHAPE — don't wait for a downstream parse failure to surface a truncated paste. The 2026-05-27 `APNS_AUTH_KEY` bug — pasted without the `-----END PRIVATE KEY-----` footer, signed nothing, failed silently at the first push-send attempt 9 hours after deploy — is the documented example.

**Module-load vs first-call — read this before writing a validator.** The original guidance here said to parse at module init. **Do not do that in this repo.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` sets mock values for a subset of env vars only (it has no `APNS_*` at all) and its env block explicitly notes the codebase "reads env vars lazily inside functions (not at module load)". A module-init throw crashes `tsc`, `vitest`, and `next build` on the next PR, and satisfying it would mean committing real credential material to a public workflow file. The shipped pattern instead is:

1. **A pure shape validator** — `checkApnsEnv(env, vars)` in `lib/notifications/apns/env.ts`. Reads the passed object at call time, returns `{ok} | {ok:false, problems: string[]}`, never throws, and **never returns key material** (it reports "missing END footer", not the value). The `vars` parameter scopes it to one layer's own variables so the transport layer isn't coupled to signing credentials it never reads.
2. **First-call enforcement** at each layer boundary — `sendApnsRequest` validates the transport vars, `getApnsJwt` validates the signing vars. Failures land in the `[apns]` Vercel logs and the PostHog `push.sent` `errorDetail` with the specific defect named.
3. **Ambient visibility** — `/admin/health` renders the same check via `app/admin/(authed)/health/check-apns.ts`, so a misconfiguration is greppable without waiting for a guest to text in. This is the closest safe equivalent to "loud at boot" for a fire-and-forget path.

Mirror this three-part shape for any future credential env var. A validator that only runs on the unhappy path of a fire-and-forget code path is not loud enough on its own — pair it with a health row.

---

## Common gotchas

- **`@/*` aliases in tests.** Resolved via `vitest.config.ts` (THE-231); previously a recurring cause of test-side import failures. Module-split pattern is still required when a transitively-imported module runs heavy SDK init at module load (see "Module split for testability").
- **Zod `.min()`/`.max()` on LLM output number fields.** Anthropic's structured output rejects these. Use `.refine()` or post-LLM validation. (THE-157.) The same rejection applies to `.max()` on `z.array()` fields (`maxItems` unsupported) — `.min()` on an array is fine, only `.max()` breaks. (TAC-347.)
- **Permissive schema + filter-time validation pattern.** When the same field is validated at two boundaries (input parse + runtime), prefer strict at the offline boundary (parser/seed) and permissive-with-defensive-filter at the live boundary (runtime). Crashes during seed are catchable; crashes during agent runs hurt guests. Three instances: THE-157 (`.min()/.max()` rejected by Anthropic structured output, validate post-LLM), THE-150 (`expiresAt` stored as plain `z.string().optional()`; `filterActiveContext` parses + drops malformed entries with `console.warn` instead of failing the whole `venue_info` JSONB parse), THE-170 (mechanic `min_state` is strict `z.enum(GUEST_STATES)` at the parser boundary inside `parse-venue-spec.ts`, but `isStateAtLeast` at runtime treats unknown values defensively — drop the gated mechanic, log, don't crash).
- **`filterActiveContext` (THE-150).** Pure helper at `lib/schemas/venue-info.ts`. Drops `currentContext` entries whose `expiresAt` is past or equal to `now`; entries with no `expiresAt` are permanent; malformed `expiresAt` logs and drops. Wired in `build-runtime-context.ts` via the same `computedAt` reused for the recognition snapshot (single "now" per agent run).
- **Re-seeding via `npm run seed-venue` for voice training.** Don't. Phase 5 voice training uses surgical updates via `ingest-response-review`. Re-seed only when the venue spec markdown itself changed structurally.
- **Drive ADC re-auth.** Tokens expire. If you see `invalid_grant` / `invalid_rapt`, re-run `gcloud auth application-default login --client-id-file=... --scopes=...`.
- **gcloud Python version.** Workspace install can break against newer Python. If `gcloud` errors with Python version mismatch, set `CLOUDSDK_PYTHON` to a specific Python 3.10-3.14 binary.
- **Migration application order.** Always apply the migration in Supabase Studio BEFORE running scripts that depend on it locally. Hand-patches to `db/types.ts` are temporary; `npm run db:types` is the resolution.
- **Postgres does NOT validate a column default against that column's own check constraint (TAC-416).** `create table (status text not null default 'pending' check (status in ('received','draft',...)))` is accepted without complaint, and the contradiction is only felt by an insert that actually relies on the default. `messages.status` carried exactly that since migration 001 and went unnoticed for months, because every insert sets `status` explicitly — necessarily, since one that did not would already fail. **The damage from a dead default is not a runtime bug, it is a false statement in the artefact people check against when they doubt a claim**: during TAC-411 it was cited as the reason to make `status` optional in a Zod schema, and that reasoning reached a code comment and a CLAUDE.md entry before review caught it, along with three test payloads modelling a state no row can hold. Same family as this file's other "a statement that reads as specification and is not" entries — worse than most, because a schema is the appeal of last resort. **As of 2026-09-15 `messages.status` was the ONLY instance in the public schema**, established by evaluating each default against its constraint predicate rather than matching text — re-run this after adding a defaulted column with a check:

  ```sql
  with defaulted as (
    select c.oid as reloid, c.relname, a.attname, a.attnum,
           format_type(a.atttypid, a.atttypmod) as coltype,
           pg_get_expr(ad.adbin, ad.adrelid) as coldefault
    from pg_attribute a
    join pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
    join pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
    join pg_attrdef ad on ad.adrelid = a.attrelid and ad.adnum = a.attnum
    where n.nspname = 'public' and c.relkind in ('r', 'p')
      and a.attnum > 0 and not a.attisdropped
      and pg_get_expr(ad.adbin, ad.adrelid) not ilike '%nextval%'
  ),
  single_col_checks as (
    select con.conrelid, con.conname, con.conkey[1] as attnum,
           pg_get_expr(con.conbin, con.conrelid) as expr
    from pg_constraint con
    join pg_class rel on rel.oid = con.conrelid
    join pg_namespace nsp on nsp.oid = rel.relnamespace
    where nsp.nspname = 'public' and con.contype = 'c'
      and array_length(con.conkey, 1) = 1
  )
  select d.relname, d.attname, d.coldefault, s.conname,
    (xpath('/row/ok/text()', query_to_xml(
      format('select (%s) as ok from (select (%s)::%s as %I) t',
             s.expr, d.coldefault, d.coltype, d.attname),
      false, true, '')))[1]::text as default_satisfies_check
  from defaulted d
  join single_col_checks s on s.conrelid = d.reloid and s.attnum = d.attnum
  order by default_satisfies_check nulls first, d.relname, d.attname;
  ```

  **Read it with four caveats.** (1) `query_to_xml` EXECUTES the inner `select`, which evaluates the **default expression** — not just the predicate. So a volatile default runs for real: the `nextval` filter exists because a sequence advance SURVIVES A ROLLBACK and so cannot be undone. It is defensive, not something that has fired — this schema has zero sequence defaults (no `serial`, no `nextval` in any migration; every PK is `gen_random_uuid()`). Filtering one function by name does not make the sweep side-effect-free in general, so wrap it in `begin; ... rollback;` **as well as** keeping the name filter: the two cover different failure modes. (2) It covers SINGLE-column constraints only. Multi-column ones need whole-row context and are swept separately (swap the `= 1` for `> 1` and substitute each referenced column's default, or NULL where it has none); as of the same date, none of the four in this schema has every referenced column defaulted, so none can be violated by defaults alone. (3) **A `false` there is not automatically this defect.** `operators_must_have_auth` evaluates false on a defaults-only row because NEITHER column has a default — that is the constraint correctly demanding the caller supply an auth method, not a dead default. And a NULL result is not a pass in the ordinary sense: `messages_has_content` returns NULL on a defaults-only row, which a CHECK accepts, and that is the deliberate `array_length('{}', 1) IS NULL` behaviour documented under TAC-309 below. (4) Two shapes are invisible to it and would read as a clean sweep: a **partitioned** table's columns (hence `relkind in ('r','p')` above — the first version said `= 'r'`), and a **domain-level** CHECK (`contype = 'c'` with `conrelid = 0`), so a `create domain status_t as text check (value in (...))` behind a defaulted column is never examined. Neither exists in this schema today.
- **Preview-env parity for server-side env vars.** Every `process.env.*` reference that runs server-side must be set on **both** the Production AND Preview Vercel environments. Preview parity has bitten twice on the operator-API surface (`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, then `VOYAGE_API_KEY` — both initially set on Production only). Failure mode is HTTP 500 with **empty body** from the route, because `lib/db/admin.ts` (and similar helpers) throw on missing env at call time and the throw escapes the route handler before any JSON response is shaped — debugging from the client side looks like a code crash. Mitigation, run as part of post-merge hygiene: grep the changed PR diff for `process.env.` references, cross-check each name against the Preview env var list in Vercel project settings, fix any gaps before opening the preview-protected route to QA.
- **APNs env vars fail SILENTLY (TAC-207).** Unlike the Supabase/Voyage case above, missing `APNS_*` vars on Preview don't surface as a 500 — the agent path continues normally (push is fire-and-forget via `waitUntil`, errors-as-values, never throws), and `sendDraftFlaggedPush` returns early with `ok=false / error='env_missing'` PostHog events. **Symptom is "push didn't arrive,"** not "API call broke." Diagnostic chain: (1) PostHog `push.sent` events with `error='env_missing'` / `error='jwt_failed'` — the env var name is in `errorDetail`; (2) check Vercel project settings for `APNS_AUTH_KEY` / `APNS_KEY_ID` / `APNS_TEAM_ID` / `APNS_BUNDLE_ID` / `APNS_ENV` on the failing environment; (3) for `APNS_AUTH_KEY` specifically, the value is the PEM contents (multi-line, includes `-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----` lines) pasted directly into the Vercel value field — a path or a single-line transformation won't parse.
- **A guest can have no phone number, and four things follow (TAC-467, migration 048).** A guest who reached the venue on Instagram has `guests.phone_number` NULL and `guests.instagram_scoped_id` set.
  1. **`messages.channel` defaults to `'text'`, so an Instagram message inserted without an explicit channel is silently recorded as `'text'`**, and stays invisible until someone queries by channel. The default exists only because no deployed insert site names a channel yet. Every Instagram insert must set `channel: 'instagram'` explicitly and test it. Dropping the default is a tracked dependency of the Instagram outbound ticket, done once every insert site names its channel; the same backwards-incompatible ordering rule applies then (code that sets it first, the migration after).
  2. **The operator app cannot take a null phone.** `analog-operator` parses the queue's `drafts` and the conversations list all-or-nothing, with `guestPhoneFallback` / `phoneFallback` as `z.string()`, so one null fails the whole list, and every operator who can see that venue gets an error instead of it. `lib/operator/queue.ts` and `conversations.ts` send `''` instead. **`tsc` cannot guard this**: regenerated types call every RPC return column non-null, so the tests in those two files are the only guard. Showing an Instagram guest properly is a cross-repo Contract change.
  3. **Sending to a guest with no phone fails closed, and the two operator-driven paths refuse before doing anything.** `sendMessage`, `markAsRead` and `sendTypingIndicator` refuse null with `recipient_has_no_phone_number` before the venue lookup; on the auto-send path that is a red alert and `AgentResult.failed`. `dispatchOperatorOutbound` refuses as `no_phone_number` BEFORE the optimistic flip, like `empty_body`, so an approved card stays queued instead of stranding; the approve and edit routes map it to the same 502 body as `sendblue_failed`, so the operator API Contract is unchanged. The Command Center Follow Up route refuses a phoneless guest with a 400 before generating: it was the one existing path that could queue a card for such a guest, and that card would have been recorded as channel `'text'`. The Instagram handler (TAC-468) is not to run the agent for Instagram guests until outbound routes by channel.
  4. **The followup engine already skips them**: `lib/followups/engine.ts` scans `phone_number is not null`. Right until outbound exists; revisit it then.
  Command Center shows `NO_PHONE_LABEL` ("Instagram guest", `app/admin/(authed)/_lib/guest-name.ts`) where a phone would go. That label rests on `guests_must_have_identity`: no phone means an IGSID, which stops being true the day a third channel exists.
- **A webhook whose secret rides in the QUERY STRING cannot log `request.url` (TAC-445).** The Sendblue and Square routes both log `{ url: request.url }` on their failure paths, and both are safe doing it because those providers put their secrets in HEADERS. Meta's verification handshake puts `hub.verify_token` in the query string, so the same line writes `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` into Vercel logs on every SUCCESSFUL handshake — and the instinct that produces it is the good one, copying the sibling route for parity. `app/api/webhooks/instagram/route.ts` logs booleans only (`modeIsSubscribe`, `tokenMatches`, `hasChallenge`), carries a comment saying not to add `url` back, and is guarded by a mutation-verified test (`never writes a verify token into a log line`) that fails the moment anyone does. **Generalization for the next provider: before copying a log line between routes, check where that provider puts its secret.** **Scope, stated so it does not harden into a stronger claim than it is: this keeps the token out of OUR log lines. It cannot keep it out of Vercel's own runtime request log, which records the request path independently of the handler** — if that retains the query string, `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` lands in platform logs on every successful handshake whatever this route does. Unverified as of TAC-445; check one real handshake in Vercel Runtime Logs. If it does retain it, the mitigation is treating `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` as low-value and rotatable, not trying to hide it. The sibling trap, same ticket: comparing a secret against a request value by coalescing both sides to `''` makes `'' === ''` true, so an UNSET `META_VERIFY_TOKEN` would verify an attacker's handshake. `verifyMetaChallengeToken` rejects an empty expected value explicitly, and the route refuses before comparing at all.
- **Never log a webhook signature digest, and an empty key is a valid HMAC key (TAC-458).** Two traps. The first was already a hole in TAC-445's log-only scaffold, just not yet exploitable; enforcement is what made it one. The second is harmless until the route trusts the signature. (1) HMAC is keyed by OUR secret over THEIR body, so our digest of any body is a valid signature for exactly that body for as long as the secret is unchanged: a log line carrying it lets anyone who can read the logs, or a log drain, replay that body as a signed delivery. **So a digest must never be logged at all, not merely once enforcement is on.** TAC-445's scaffold logged `computed` on every delivery (and `received`, beside the raw body whenever capture was on), which looked harmless because nothing trusted it; enforcement made every one of those lines valid at once, and only rotating the secret invalidates them. `verifyInstagramSignature` returns a reason and never a digest, so the route cannot log one by accident, and a test fails if any outcome carries one. Related, and not fixed by any of this: Meta's signature carries no timestamp or nonce, so a verified delivery can be a REPLAY of a genuine one. Today a replay produces only a shape log line; the handler that persists or acts on deliveries must dedupe on the message `mid`, which Meta's retries require anyway. (2) `createHmac('sha256', '')` does not throw: it produces a deterministic digest anyone can compute. A verifier handed an empty secret therefore verifies a forger who signs with `''` (verified against TAC-445's helper, which returned `matched: true`). Refuse an empty secret in the VERIFIER, not only in the route, and test each layer separately: with both guards in place, removing either still refuses an ordinary request, so a plain 403 assertion cannot tell whether the other is there. The route's guard is told apart by what it logs, and by refusing before the body is read: without it, a body read that throws falls into the catch and returns 200. **Generalization for the next provider:** never log a digest, even from a log-only scaffold, because enforcing later makes every earlier line a valid signature. A verifier's result type is where you stop it leaking, and its first line is where you stop the empty key.
- **`vercel env pull` overwrites unconditionally AND returns empty strings for `Encrypted` variables — it is a destructive command that cannot restore what it destroys.** Both halves matter, and together they make it strictly worse than useless as a recovery tool on this project. (1) **It never merges, never prompts, and never backs up.** The target file is replaced wholesale. `--yes` only suppresses the confirmation on an *existing-file* prompt; there is no flag that makes the write additive. A key that exists locally but not in the Vercel project is simply gone. (2) **Variables stored as `Encrypted` in Vercel pull as `KEY=""`.** The CLI's diff output still prints a `+ KEY` line for each one, so the run *looks* like a successful repopulation while writing nothing but empty values — reading the summary rather than the file's bytes is how a pull got reported as having restored credentials when it had restored none. Verify with a line-length or content check, never with the CLI's own diff. **On this project every single variable in the Vercel project is stored `Encrypted`** (verified against `vercel env ls`, 2026-09-12 — all 30 rows), so the empty-string failure is not an edge case here, it is the whole result: a pull returns a file of empty values, 100% of the time. Compounding it, `.env.local` carries five keys that are **not in Vercel at all** — `TEST_VENUE_ID`, `GOOGLE_DRIVE_VENUES_FOLDER_ID`, `AIRTABLE_API_KEY`, `AIRTABLE_BASE_ID`, `AIRTABLE_TABLE_ID` — so even a hypothetical pull that returned every stored value correctly would still be a net loss. See the standing rule under "Workflow rules for Claude Code → Never write `.env.local`" — the conclusion is not "pull carefully," it is "do not pull." `vercel env ls` is read-only, writes nothing, and is the right command when the question is "which variables does the project have." (A sixth local-only key, `LINEAR_BOT_TOKEN`, was a dead OAuth token that Linear rejected with 401; Jaipal removed it by hand on 2026-09-16.)
- **Adding a column to an RPC's `returns table(...)` requires `DROP FUNCTION`, not `CREATE OR REPLACE` (TAC-364).** Postgres refuses a return-type change outright — `ERROR: cannot change return type of existing function` — and adding a column IS one. Migration 032 replaced `list_operator_queue` with `create or replace` and got away with it because it changed only the BODY. Migration 039 changes the signature and has to drop first. **Wrap the drop and the create in one transaction**: Postgres DDL is transactional, so a concurrent caller blocks on the lock and then sees the new definition, whereas two bare statements leave a window where a queue read fails with "function does not exist" — a 500 on the operator's queue, which is the downtime window §Ordering exists to prevent. The recreate also has to restate the entire body, so diff it against the previous migration rather than retyping it. **`DROP FUNCTION` also discards every `grant`/`revoke` on the function** — inert for `list_operator_queue` (service-role client only; the only grant statements in `db/migrations` are migration 023's), but the obvious next candidate is `link_operator_auth`, which carries an explicit `grant execute ... to authenticated` plus `revoke ... from public, anon`; recreating that one from its migration body without re-issuing both would silently 403 the operator app. **And verify what is LIVE before dropping, not what is in the repo** — this codebase has already been bitten by a Studio-only SQL object (migration 021's orphan `link_operator_auth`), so `select pg_get_functiondef(...)` and a `pg_proc` overload check belong in the migration header, the way migration 039 carries them.
- **`messages.review_reason` has THREE values outside `APPROVAL_TRIGGERS`, and two predicates key on the set (TAC-364).** `operator_decline_initiated` (TAC-299), `crisis_safety_reply` (TAC-348) and `generation_failed` (TAC-364) are all stamped directly by the path that owns them, never by `applyApprovalPolicyStage` — a crash or a compliance reply never produces the `GenerateMessageResult` the gate takes. `lib/operator/queue.ts`'s `ExtraReviewReason` union exists for exactly this, and its label map is total over `ApprovalTrigger | ExtraReviewReason` so a new one fails `tsc` there. **What `tsc` does NOT catch is the two runtime predicates**: `isKnowledgeGapCard` (`lib/agent/stages.ts`) and `findPendingQuestion` (`lib/agent/pending-question.ts`) both decide whether a pending row is a card the guest is owed an answer to, and both used to hand-list the qualifying `review_reason` values. They drifted — the PostgREST filter carried one value where the predicate carried two, so a `knowledge_gap_backstop` card whose clock had already fired was recognized by one and invisible to the other, and the `## Unanswered question` block silently vanished for that guest while the card still sat in the queue. The comment at the query claimed the two mirrored each other the whole time. Both now read `KNOWLEDGE_GAP_CARD_REVIEW_REASONS`, exported from `stages.ts` — **do not inline those values back into either site.** The failure mode is asymmetric and that is why it survived: the query re-checks rows it DID return, so a row the filter never returns is indistinguishable from no row at all.
- **A crash card is `generation_failed`, not `knowledge_gap` (TAC-364).** TAC-309 stamped `persistGenerationFailureCard`'s output with `knowledge_gap` to inherit the timer, the holding message and the eviction protection for free. All of that still works — none of it keys on `review_reason` — but the operator-facing copy read "a guest asked something I don't have an answer for" on a turn where the guest may have asked something perfectly answerable and the generator simply fell over. The split needed BOTH predicates above widened in the same change, or the card would have lost its protection the moment the holding timer cleared `pending_until` and the next queuing turn would have overwritten the guest's outstanding question. `generation_failed` is deliberately NOT in `APPROVAL_TRIGGERS` (the gate can't fire it) and therefore not in `PUSH_POLICY`'s total map — it reaches the fail-open default and pushes, which is asserted in `push-policy.test.ts` rather than left inferred.
- **`name 2.ts` / `name 3.ts` copies under `.next/types/` break local `tsc` AND the pre-commit hook.** Until TAC-425 (2026-09-16) this repo lived in an iCloud-synced Desktop folder, and iCloud's conflict copies of generated files appeared with a space-and-digit suffix. Off iCloud they should not recur — a new one means something else is syncing or copying the tree. `tsconfig` includes `.next/types/**/*.ts`, so a stray `cache-life.d 3.ts` / `routes.d 3.ts` fails with TS6200/TS2300 duplicate-identifier errors that have nothing to do with the change being committed. CI never sees them (no `.next` there). `.next/` is gitignored build output, so the fix is to delete just the duplicates: `find .next -type f -name '* [0-9].*' -print -delete`. Look before widening that pattern past `.next` — a suffixed copy of a SOURCE file would be a different and worse problem, and on 2026-09-14 there were none. Also watch `echo "tsc-exit=$?"` after a pipe: it reports the last command in the pipe, not `tsc`, which is how a failing typecheck read as exit 0.
- **`venue_configs.brand_persona` vs `venues.brand_persona`.** It lives on `venue_configs`, not `venues`. Check `build-runtime-context.ts` for the canonical access pattern.
- **`response_review.reviewedVia` is optional with a read-time default (TAC-258).** `MessageReviewSchema` added `reviewedVia: z.enum(['cc_review', 'mobile_operator']).optional()` without bumping `schemaVersion`. Pre-TAC-258 cc-review rows don't carry the field. Readers MUST go through `getReviewedVia(review)` from `lib/schemas/message-review.ts`, which defaults missing values to `'cc_review'` (the only pre-existing channel). New writes on the cc-review path stamp the field explicitly; the mobile-operator edit path always stamps `'mobile_operator'`. Don't try to read `review.reviewedVia` directly — you'll get `undefined` on legacy rows and silently fall through wrong branches.
- **`response_review.originalAiBody` only set on the mobile-operator edit path (TAC-258).** For mobile-operator edits, the route overwrites `messages.body` with the operator's text (preserving the `body = canonical sent text` invariant cc-review depends on), and captures the pre-edit AI draft in `response_review.originalAiBody`. cc-review never sets `originalAiBody` — `messages.body` already carries the AI draft because cc-review is post-hoc. So a non-null `originalAiBody` is the strongest signal of "this row went through the mobile approval queue's edit path."
- **`voiceAntiPatterns` shape (THE-236).** Stored shape is `Array<{ text, source: 'auto' | 'manual', authorOperatorId?, addedAt? }>`. **The DB is in a dual-shape state in perpetuity:** legacy `string[]` entries from rows last written before THE-236 stay legacy until any writer touches them; new and migrated rows persist as struct. `BrandPersonaSchema` accepts both forms and normalizes string entries to `{text, source: 'manual'}` on parse, so all in-process code sees the struct shape uniformly — but the JSONB on disk does not. Three rules follow:
  - **Always go through `BrandPersonaSchema`.** Reading `venue_configs.brand_persona->'voiceAntiPatterns'` directly via SQL or a JSONB path expression breaks on the legacy half of the fleet — `jsonb_array_elements_text` works on legacy rows, `... ->> 'text'` works on migrated rows, neither works on both. If you need a SQL query that touches anti-patterns, write the union explicitly (`CASE WHEN jsonb_typeof(elem) = 'string' THEN elem #>> '{}' ELSE elem ->> 'text' END`) or load the row in app code and parse with the schema.
  - **Readers must reach for `.text` on each entry.** Code that previously rendered the entry directly (`${a}`) prints `[object Object]` after parse; the serializer is updated, any new UI is not exempt.
  - **Writers must take a `{ source, authorOperatorId? }` opts arg.** `dedupeAndAppendAntiPatterns` is the only current writer; any new helper that mutates this field must stamp the metadata so the migrated rows don't lose attribution.
- **A mocked behaviour flag must be asserted to match production, or the test certifies the bug instead of catching it (TAC-310, TAC-367 ×2).** When a test double hardcodes a flag that decides whether a code path RUNS — `shouldRetrieveKnowledge: () => false`, a feature toggle, a capability check — and production returns the opposite, the guarded path is unreachable in every test in that file. The suite then reports green *about behaviour it never executed*. This is not a coverage gap; it is a false negative that looks like proof.
  **Four instances, found in one sitting, three of them by going looking after the first** — which is why this is a rule and not an anecdote. `analog-operator`'s `api-queue.test.ts` asserted a request shape the server never accepted and kept certifying it on every green run while real operator sends failed (see *Cross-repo contracts → Test-assertion rule*). Then TAC-367 found `shouldRetrieveKnowledge: () => false` in THREE orchestrator test files — `handle-holding-message.test.ts`, `handle-followup.test.ts` and `handle-inbound.test.ts` — while the real predicate returns `true` for exactly the cases those files exercise. The inbound one is the starkest: `shouldRetrieveKnowledge`'s first line is `if (ctx.currentMessage !== null) return true`, and every test in that file is an inbound, so the stub was wrong **100% of the time** and the entire knowledge-retrieval branch of the repo's primary guest-facing path — span, degrade handling and all — had never once executed under test. Correcting it broke nothing, which is the tell: the branch had no coverage, not conflicting coverage.
  **The sweep that found the last two is the cheap part:** `grep -rn ": () => \(true\|false\)," --include='*.test.ts' .` lists every hardcoded behaviour-flag stub in the repo in one command. Run it when you add one. Two other hits were assessed at the same time: `isEmptyContextUpdate: () => true` is CORRECT (every fixture emits the `{}` no-op shape, for which production also returns true), and `shouldSendDraftFlaggedPush: () => false` in `handle-followup.test.ts` is WRONG (production is a deny-list returning true for everything except `previous_pending_held`) but **not free to fix** — flipping it fails two existing tests that pass only because the push path is suppressed, so it needs its own look rather than a drive-by.
  **The rule:** if a mock pins a boolean that gates a branch, either (a) set it to what production returns for the input under test and assert the resulting behaviour, or (b) add an explicit test that the real function returns that value for that input. Prefer (a). And **give the mock a return value that differs from the expected one** — a mock resolving `[]` under an assertion of `[]` cannot distinguish "the path was skipped" from "the path ran and found nothing," which is a second way the same test passes against a broken implementation.
- **Reproduce a CI permission failure with the exact strings the workflow uses, never equivalents.** A local test that differs from CI in the one dimension being tested proves nothing. On 2026-09-16 the build session for TAC-401 ran green with 30 permission denials and wrote nothing to Linear. The first local reproduction tested variable expansion with `-H "Authorization: $HOME"` instead of the workflow's `-H "Authorization: $LINEAR_API_KEY"`. Claude Code allows well-known variables like `$HOME` and denies expanding any other environment variable, so the stand-in passed a form CI denies, and a prompt teaching that denied command shipped in analog-guest #190. The next fixture run (35150207471) caught it: 4 denials, until the session found curl's `--variable`. Fixed in #191; run 35151034583 then had 0. **Before reproducing, copy the command, the allowlist string and the CLI version out of the failing run, and change nothing but the target host.** Same class as the mocked-flag entry above: a test whose input cannot exercise the failure certifies its absence.
- **In CI, an allowlisted shell tool is still refused on a file outside the checkout (TAC-444).** Claude Code checks the file arguments of a fixed set of commands (`jq`, `rg`, `grep`, `cat`, `wc`, `sed`, `head`, `tail`, `cd`, `ls`, `find`, `awk`, `diff`, `git` and more), and a path outside the working directories resolves to "ask", which an allow rule such as `Bash(jq:*)` cannot override. In a headless session "ask" is a refusal. An output redirect to a path outside the checkout is checked the same way. A pipeline or `&&` chain is refused whole if any part of it is refused. `curl`, `node` and `python3` are not in that set, which is why `curl -d @<file>` and `node scripts/linear.mjs comment <issue> <file>` pass on a file in the runner temp folder while `jq … <the same file>` is refused. `${{ runner.temp }}` (`/home/runner/work/_temp`) and the files Claude Code saves a large tool result to are both outside the checkout. Evidence: run 35271049290 refused `jq -Rs '{a:1}' /home/runner/work/_temp/…` with nothing else in the command, and three build runs (TAC-428, TAC-396, TAC-325) were refused the same way trying to JSON-encode a plan file with `jq`. Source: Claude Code 2.1.273's permission code, while CI ran 2.1.275, so re-check after a CLI upgrade. Related, from the same source: the Write tool fails when it overwrites an existing file outside the checkout that the session hasn't read, which is why both prompts say to give every write its own file name. **Before blaming a refused command's flags, pipe or `&&` shape, check whether any part of it is a path-checked tool touching a file outside the checkout, or a tool on neither allowlist (`wc`, `cat`).** Every Linear-related refusal that could be read in full on 2026-09-17 was one of the two; the taught curl read and write never were. Two older records cut at 200 characters (TAC-426, audit run 35256049709) still can't be read, so treat "the taught curl was refused" as unsettled rather than disproved.
- **A build run that goes over its turn limit is not always a session that was cut off (TAC-447).** `--max-turns` produces two different endings. The CLI stops the session with `subtype: error_max_turns` (audit runs 34949521131 and 34980172868: 61 turns against 60), and the work is incomplete. Or the session finishes with `subtype: success` and claude-code-action fails the step afterwards because `num_turns` is over the limit (`base-action/src/run-claude-sdk.ts:241-250` in the action's `v1` tag, which the workflow follows rather than pins, 2261fcf when read; run 35288433905: 128 against 120). In that second case nothing was lost: both tickets in the run got their comment and one got a PR, yet the run is red and TAC-447 was first written as if the second ticket had been starved. **Read the result's `subtype` before concluding anything from a red build run.** The check step now posts which one it was (`[TURN-LIMIT]` or `[OVER-LIMIT]`). Why the CLI let that session run to 128 against 120 is not established; the log does not print the transcript.
- **A build session's `git push` used the job's own token, not the Claude App token, until TAC-463.** `actions/checkout@v6` keeps the job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` as an `http.https://github.com/.extraheader` in a file the repo config includes with `includeIf.gitdir:<gitdir>.path`. claude-code-action's scrub (`replaceCheckoutCredentials`, unchanged at `v1` as of 2026-09-18) checks only the local config and `include.path`, logs `No existing authentication headers to remove`, and puts the App token in the remote URL. git sends the extra header on every request, so the URL token was never used. Every build push until then was recorded as `github-actions[bot]` while the same sessions' PRs opened as `app/claude`, and run 35299836324 was refused a push under `.github/workflows/`: the job token can never carry `workflows`, and GitHub offers no `workflows` key for a `permissions:` block. `build-ready.yml` now checks out with `persist-credentials: false` and asks the exchange for `workflows: write` through `additional_permissions`. **To see which token pushed, read the `actor` in `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/activity`. The commit author proves nothing**: the build commits as `41898282+claude[bot]@users.noreply.github.com`, 41898282 is github-actions' own user id, so GitHub links those commits to github-actions[bot] whichever token pushed them. analog-operator's `build-ready.yml` has the same setup.
- **A MUTANT IS ONLY EVIDENCE IF THE FILE COMPILED AND THE INTENDED LINE CHANGED (TAC-385).** Mutation testing is what this repo reaches for when a test might not be able to fail — and it has its own failure mode that reads as a finding and is not. Two ways a mutant reports SURVIVED while proving nothing, both hit in one sitting:
  1. **A syntax error makes the file collect ZERO tests**, so the runner prints nothing failing, which is indistinguishable from a real survivor. (Already logged once under TAC-394's `void (` mutant; hit again here replacing `waitUntil(` with `await (`.) **Check the run said `Tests N passed`, not `no tests`.**
  2. **The edit landed on a different line than intended.** A `perl -0pi -e 's/…/…/'` without `/g` replaces the FIRST match, and `now: new Date(),` at the same indentation appeared on a `createCommitmentFromPending` call sixty lines above the recording call. The mutant "survived" because the code under test was never touched. **Diff the file, or grep the changed line, before believing the result.**
  Both produce a clean survivor, and a false survivor is worse than no mutant at all: it sends you to write a test for coverage you already had, while the real gap stays open. **Confirm the compile and confirm the line.** The generalization is this project's own defect class arriving inside the technique built to catch it — same shape as a green suite certifying absence, one level up.
- **A by-path import to dodge a barrel mock has to hold for EVERY hop, not just the first (TAC-385).** `dispatch-operator-outbound.ts` imports `recordIntentionPrompts` by path, with a comment citing the documented barrel hazard — and then the module it reached imported `parseRenderedIntentions` from the `@/lib/schemas` BARREL, which `dispatch-operator-outbound.test.ts` factory-mocks for `PendingCommitmentSchema`. Under that mock the second hop throws `No "parseRenderedIntentions" export is defined on the "@/lib/schemas" mock`. **Dormant is not safe here**, and the reason is where the throw would land: the call is SYNCHRONOUS, outside any try/catch and outside `waitUntil`, and it runs AFTER the guest already has the message — so it would reject the dispatch and 500 the operator's approve on a send that succeeded. It stayed invisible only because that test file's mock chain stops at the optimistic state flip and never reaches the call, which is the "green suite certifies absence" shape this file keeps logging. Two rules: **trace the barrel hazard through the whole import chain, not one hop**, and **wrap any synchronous work that happens after a successful irreversible side effect**, however defensively its own branches are written. Verified by probe in both directions — barrel import throws under the mock, by-path import does not.
- **A partial-match assertion cannot pin a field SET, and when the defect IS a missing field the test reproduces the bug's own shape (TAC-318).** `toMatchObject` / `objectContaining` assert "at least these"; they pass while any field you did not name goes missing. That is fine for an incidental check and actively dangerous when the thing under test is *completeness* — TAC-318's `shouldUpgrade` promotes an open recommendation to a comp, and the defect it exists to fix was **a comp on the ledger with no verification code**. The first assertion for it was `toMatchObject({type, code, source_message_id})`, which would have passed with `code` silently dropped from the update payload: the test had the same hole as the bug. Fixed by `toEqual` on the exact payload, which pins the set from BOTH sides — dropping any of the four fields fails, and *adding* `created_at` / `status` / `created_by` / `description` fails too. **The rule: when a test's subject is which fields move, assert the whole object, then mutation-check in both directions** — drop each field, then add one that must not move. Neither half is optional; a drop-only check still passes when the payload quietly grows. Sibling of the mocked-flag rule above and of the fixture traps in the test-count entry: all three are ways a green suite certifies behaviour it never checked.
- **A source-level guard catches only the spellings it was written against, so run a second spelling as a mutant before trusting it (TAC-394).** When nothing runs the code for real under test, a regex over the source file is the only guard, and it certifies whichever form it matches. TAC-394's test that recent history is never filtered matched the literal `recentMessages.filter(`; a filter chained onto `groupIntoResponses(...)`, or applied inside its argument, passed every assertion, and only a reviewer trying other spellings found it. The fix pins the whole statement and counts every use of the input. The ruling on that ticket counted this as the ninth or tenth instance of the shape in one session, so the mutation habit alone has not been enough: when you write a source-level guard, write down the equivalent code it must reject and run that as a mutant in the same change.
- **`formatTimeDelta` is the AGENT'S PROMPT VOCABULARY — do not reuse it on an admin surface (TAC-381).** It lives in `lib/ai/prompts/serializers.ts` and renders the time deltas inside `## Recent conversation` and `## Visit history`. An admin page importing it couples two things that must move independently: a prompt-wording change would silently restyle the page, and a display tweak on the page would become an agent-runtime change. `app/admin/(authed)/venues/_lib/commitment-display.ts` has its own `formatAge` for exactly this reason, with the rationale written at the function and a source-level test banning the call — because this reads like duplication to anyone who greps, and the next person's instinct will be to "de-duplicate" it. The same logic applies in reverse: don't reach into an admin display helper from `lib/ai`.
- **Loyalty-program language is forbidden.** Points / rewards / tier / earn / badges / progress-bars in operator-facing or guest-facing surfaces violates the product principles at the top of this file. The voice critique system (THE-237/238) catches some drift; do not rely on it. Guests are *recognized*, not *enrolled*. If you write "this perk has been earned," the framing is wrong.
- **In-place mechanic edits without re-seed (THE-170).** `seed-venue` hard-fails on existing slugs and a full re-seed cascades through `venue_configs`/`mechanics`/`voice_corpus`/`voice_embeddings`/etc — destroying any Phase 5 voice corpus additions written by `ingest-response-review`. To update mechanic eligibility gates without losing voice training, run a SQL `UPDATE` directly in Supabase Studio:
  ```sql
  -- Set The Joey to regulars-only on mock-central-perk
  update mechanics
  set min_state = 'regular'
  where venue_id = (select id from venues where slug = 'mock-central-perk')
    and name = 'The Joey';

  -- Renewable mechanic example (free first drink each month)
  update mechanics
  set redemption_policy = 'renewable',
      redemption_window_days = 30
  where venue_id = (select id from venues where slug = 'mock-central-perk')
    and name = 'First Drink Free';

  -- TAC-212: require operator approval before a guest can be committed to
  -- this mechanic. The mechanic still appears in eligibility for qualifying
  -- guests; the difference is the agent must self-flag `requiresOperatorApproval=true`
  -- and the approval-policy gate routes the draft to the queue instead of
  -- auto-sending. Defaults false at the column level (migration 019).
  update mechanics
  set requires_operator_approval = true
  where venue_id = (select id from venues where slug = 'mock-central-perk')
    and name = 'The Joey';

  -- Mark a mechanic as redeemed for a specific guest. mechanic_id FK column is
  -- the source of truth; the data jsonb mirrors it for self-describing rows.
  insert into engagement_events (venue_id, guest_id, event_type, mechanic_id, data)
  values (
    (select id from venues where slug = 'mock-central-perk'),
    '<guest_uuid>',
    'mechanic_redeemed',
    '<mechanic_uuid>',
    jsonb_build_object(
      'mechanic_id', '<mechanic_uuid>',
      'source', 'operator_marked',
      'recorded_by_operator_id', '<operator_uuid>',
      'notes', 'Walk-in redemption on 2026-04-29'
    )
  );
  ```
  After updating, re-run `npm run run-test-scenarios mock-central-perk` to verify expected behavior. Per-guest data jsonb shape is documented by `MechanicRedeemedDataSchema` in `lib/recognition/eligibility.ts`. Proper upsert flow tracked in THE-196.
- **Granting analog admin access (THE-199).** `operators.is_analog_admin` defaults `false`. To grant access to `admin.theanalog.company`, run a SQL `UPDATE` directly in Supabase Studio:
  ```sql
  -- Grant admin access. Use the operator's email; do not interpolate the value
  -- into the query — type it manually so this template can't be copy-pasted
  -- against the wrong row.
  update operators
  set is_analog_admin = true
  where email = '<email>';
  ```
  No app code automates this — it's one operator at a time, by hand, with someone reviewing. Mirrors the friction-by-placeholder pattern from the seed-venue error template. Revoking is the same statement with `false`.
- **Flagging a demo guest (TAC-284).** `guests.is_demo` defaults `false`. Set it to give a specific guest the fast, ungated demo experience — the agent runtime bypasses the TAC-212 approval policy gate IN FULL (including the comp regex backstop — see "Demo guest bypass" under "Approval policy gates") and skips the read receipt and typing indicators. Since TAC-421 there is no pre-send sleep to skip, so the "fast" half of demo mode is now just the ungated send. Real guest traffic at the same venue is unaffected; the flag is per-guest. Flip it via a SQL `UPDATE` directly in Supabase Studio:
  ```sql
  -- Flag a guest for demo mode. Keyed on (phone_number, venue_id) so the same
  -- phone at a different venue is untouched. Type the values manually — do not
  -- interpolate — so this template can't be copy-pasted against the wrong row.
  -- WARNING: a demo guest's comps auto-send with NO operator review. Only flag
  -- a phone number you control (a teammate's own phone), never a real guest.
  update guests
  set is_demo = true
  where phone_number = '<e164>'
    and venue_id = (select id from venues where slug = '<slug>');
  ```
  No app code automates this — SQL-only via Studio for v1. The flag is read fresh every agent run (no runtime caching), so toggling it mid-session takes effect on the next inbound. Un-flagging is the same statement with `false`.
- **Backfilling commitment horizons (TAC-341).** Migration 038 adds `escalated_at` but writes no `expires_at` — every pre-TAC-341 obligation carries null, and a null horizon can never elapse, so the lifecycle cron's `expires_at IS NOT NULL` filter skips those rows forever. Backfill by hand in Studio AFTER applying 038. **Recommendations are NOT backfilled** — they have no horizon by design (TAC-380 owns them), and the `type` filter below is what keeps the statement honest about that.
  ```sql
  -- Comps and discounts: 60 days from creation, matching COMP_EXPIRY_DAYS in
  -- lib/guests/commitment-expiry.ts. KEEP THE TWO IN STEP — this has already
  -- drifted once: the first backfill ran at the original 2-year horizon and
  -- had to be corrected in place (see the next block).
  -- Scoped to status='open' because a terminal row's horizon is meaningless
  -- and writing one would be noise in the ledger, not data.
  update guest_commitments
  set expires_at = created_at + interval '60 days'
  where type in ('comp', 'discount')
    and status = 'open'
    and expires_at is null;

  -- Holds are NOT backfilled by formula. A hold's horizon is venue close on
  -- the day it was set aside, which needs that venue's hours and timezone —
  -- and a hold open long enough to need a backfill is one nobody claimed, so
  -- the honest action is to close it by hand rather than invent a horizon.
  -- (Zero hold rows exist fleet-wide as of 2026-09-14; verify before assuming.)
  select id, venue_id, guest_id, description, created_at
  from guest_commitments
  where type = 'hold' and status = 'open' and expires_at is null;
  ```
  **If a row was already backfilled at the 2-year horizon** (anything written before 2026-09-14), the constant change does NOT move it — `expires_at` is derived at creation and never recomputed, so an existing row keeps whatever it was given. Correct those in place, scoped by the horizon they carry rather than by date, so the statement is safe to re-run and cannot touch a correctly-derived row:
  ```sql
  -- Rows still carrying the retired 2-year horizon, rewritten to 60 days.
  ```
